The goal of this section is to construct a normalized gluing map
\varphi_{1}
on
{\partial P}
, so as to obtain a normalized triple
(P,X,\varphi_{1})
strongly isotopic to
(P_{0},X_{0},\varphi_{0})
in the sense of Definition
1.4.9
.

Proposition 2.7.1
.

There exists a normalized gluing map
\varphi_{1}
of
(P,X)
whose restriction to
{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
is strongly isotopic to
\hat{\varphi}
.

Recall that this means that
\varphi_{1}
satisfies Definition
2.1.13
, in other words coincides with the reflection
(x,x,\theta)\mapsto(-x,-x,\theta)
in normalized coordinate systems of the orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
, and
\varphi_{1}
maps the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
on a foliation transverse to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
on
{\partial P}
.

Summary of the proof

The organization of the proof is as follows.

•

In a first step, we show that we can make an isotopy among the strongly transverse incomplete gluing maps of
(P,X)
so that
\hat{\varphi}
maps the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
on a foliation transverse to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
in
P^{\mathrm{in}}
.
This step will allow us to obtain Item
2
of Definition
2.1.13
of a normalized gluing map.
This is Proposition
2.7.2
.

The key idea is to first locally straighten the image of the laminations in a neighborhood of the boundary
\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
, which are sublaminations with empty interior (Lemma
2.7.3
).
In a second step, we push the image of the laminations
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
transversally to the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,_{i}in}
in the complementary of an adapted neighborhood of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
(Lemma
2.7.8
), and similarly put the image of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
transverse to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
in the complementary of an adapted neighborhood of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
(Lemma
2.7.10
).
Once this is done, it remains to modify the mapped foliation on the complementary of
\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}\cup{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
.
By strong transversality of
\hat{\varphi}
, these complements are rectangles on which the foliations are trivial.

•

In a second step, we show that we can modify the gluing map
\hat{\varphi}
obtained in the previous step by strong isotopy so that
\hat{\varphi}
is equal to the reflection
(x,x,\theta)\mapsto(-x,-x,\theta)
in normalized coordinate systems of the periodic orbits of
X
contained in
{\partial P}
.
Such an incomplete gluing map then extends to a normalized gluing map
\varphi_{1}
of
(P,X)
.
This result is summarized in Proposition
2.7.11
.

The technique is to construct an isotopy which maps each leaf of
\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
to the “corresponding” leaf of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
for parameters given by the normalized coordinate system
\xi_{i}
in the neighborhood of
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
, and an analogous isotopy for the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s}
.
We then show that the composition extends trivially on
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
in coordinates
\xi_{i}
.

Step 1: Transverse form for an incomplete gluing map

A first step in the proof of Proposition
2.7.1
consists in making an isotopy of
\hat{\varphi}
among the strongly transverse incomplete gluing maps of
(P,X)
, so that the image of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
by the incomplete gluing map is transverse to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
.
In this paragraph, we show the following

Proposition 2.7.2
.

There exists an incomplete gluing map
\hat{\varphi}_{1}
of
(P,X)
strongly isotopic to
\hat{\varphi}
, such that the foliation
(\hat{\varphi}_{1})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
is transverse to the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
.

Figure 20
.
Foliations on a neighborhood of
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
in
P^{\mathrm{in}}

We repeat the proof of
[
BBY17
, Proposition 5.2]
, with the difference that we are working with laminations
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
with non-empty interior due to the attractors and repellers in
\Lambda
, on a non-compact set
{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
, and the isotopies we make on
{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
to straighten the image of the foliations will not extend on
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
.
The trick is to consider
\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
the boundary of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
respectively (Definition
2.1.8
), which is a sublamination with empty interior.
We first show the following

Lemma 2.7.3
.

There exist an incomplete gluing map
\hat{\varphi}^{\prime}
strongly isotopic to
\hat{\varphi}
such that

(1)

\hat{\varphi}^{\prime}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}={\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
in a neighborhood of
\hat{\varphi}^{\prime}_{*}({\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}})\cap\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}

(2)

\hat{\varphi}^{\prime}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}={\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
in a neighborhood of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}\cap\hat{\varphi}^{\prime}_{*}(\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}})

The key points of the proof are the following.
The transverse intersection of
\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
with the boundary
\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
has empty interior, and we can cover it by arbitrarily thin domains where the foliations are transverse.
We first straighten the image of the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
in the neighborhood of the compact leaves of
\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
along arbitrarily thin annuli by using the general Lemma
2.7.4
.
Then we cover the other connected components of the intersection by arbitrarily thin rectangles and (half)-bands and use the general Lemma
2.7.6
to straighten the image of the foliation in the neighborhood of the intersection, which proves Item
1
.
We can do it so as to preserves the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
, which allows to proves Item
2
in the same way by reversing in
P^{\mathrm{out}}
without destroying what have been done.

We first state two general lemmas.

Lemma 2.7.4
.

Let
A={\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}\times[-1,1]
an annulus, endowed with
3
foliations
{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}},{\mathcal{H}}
such that
{\mathcal{G}}
and
{\mathcal{H}}
are trivial foliations transverse to
{\mathcal{F}}
, and
{\mathcal{F}}
have a single compact leaf
f_{0}:={\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}\times\{0\}
with contracting holonomy.
Then there exist a diffeomorphism
\psi:A\to A
, isotopic to the identity via diffeomorphisms preserving each leaf of
{\mathcal{F}}
and equal to the identity in a neighborhood of
\partial A
, and such that leaves of
\psi_{*}{\mathcal{H}}
are leaves of
{\mathcal{G}}
in the neighborhood of
f_{0}
.

We refer to Figure
21
.

Figure 21
.
Action of the diffeomorphism
\psi:A\to A
on the foliation
{\mathcal{H}}

Proof.

Let
(\theta,x)
the coordinate of
{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}\times[-1,1]
.
Up to change the coordinate system, we can suppose that
{\mathcal{G}}
is the vertical foliation
\theta=\operatorname{\textit{cst}}
.
Let
\tilde{A}:={\mathbb{R}}\times[-1,1]
the universal cover of
A
with coordinate
(\tilde{\theta},x)
and
\pi:\tilde{A}\to A
the projection.
Denote
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
,
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
,
\tilde{\mathcal{H}}
the lifts of foliations in
\tilde{D}
.
Denote
\tilde{f}(\tilde{\theta},x)
the leaf of
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
through
(\tilde{\theta},x)
, and similarly
\tilde{g}(\tilde{\theta},x)
and
\tilde{h}(\tilde{\theta},x)
for
{\mathcal{G}}
and
{\mathcal{H}}
.

Claim 2.7.5
.

There exist
\epsilon>0
such that for all
|x|<\epsilon
and
\tilde{\theta}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
the leaf
\tilde{f}(\tilde{\theta},x)
intersect the leaf
\tilde{h}(0,\tilde{\theta})
at a unique point
p=p(\tilde{\theta},x)\in\tilde{A}
.

Proof.

The foliation
\tilde{\mathcal{H}}
is transverse to the leaf
\tilde{f}_{0}={\mathbb{R}}\times\{0\}
of
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
.
By continuity of the foliations, there exist
\epsilon>0
such that every leaf
\tilde{f}(0,x)
intersect
\tilde{h}(0,0)
for
|x|<\epsilon
.
As the holonomy of
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
along
\tilde{f}_{0}
is contracting, up to reduce
\epsilon
, we have that every leaf
\tilde{f}(0,x)
crosses from
\tilde{h}(0,0)
to
\tilde{h}(1,0)
for
|x|<\epsilon
(hence crosses every leaf
\tilde{h}(\tilde{\theta},0)
for
\tilde{\theta}\in[0,1]
) and this holonomy map is contracting.
By invariance by integer translation
\tilde{\theta}\to\tilde{\theta}+1
, we deduce that the leaf
\tilde{f}(\tilde{\theta},x)
intersect the leaf
\tilde{h}(\tilde{\theta},0)
for every
|x|<\epsilon
and any
\tilde{\theta}\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Now by transversality of the foliations, this intersection is unique.
∎

The map
p\colon{\mathbb{R}}\times(-\epsilon,\epsilon)\to{\mathbb{R}}\times[-1,1]
;
(\tilde{\theta},x)\mapsto p(\tilde{\theta},x)
thus defined is a smooth injective map (because the foliations are smoothly transverse), commuting with the integer translation along
\tilde{\theta}
, which preserves each leaf of
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
, and map
\tilde{\mathcal{H}}
to
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
.
It remains to do a barycentric isotopy in the leaves of
{\mathcal{F}}
away from
x=0
to get the identity in the neighborhood of
|x|=\epsilon
, and we extend
p
by the identity on
\tilde{A}
.
More precisely let
\tau\colon[0,\epsilon]\to[0,1]
be a smooth decreasing function which is equal to
1
in a neighborhood of
0
and
0
in a neighborhood of
\epsilon
.
Define
\hat{p}(x,\tilde{\theta})
the weight barycenter by
\tau(|x|)
between the point
p(x,\tilde{\theta})
and
(x,\tilde{\theta})
in the leaf
\tilde{f}(\tilde{\theta},x)
.
Extend
\hat{p}
by the identity on
\tilde{A}
.
This define a diffeomorphism
\hat{p}
, equal to the identity in the neighborhood of
\partial A
,
isotopic to the identity among diffeomorphisms which preserves the foliation
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
leaf-to-leaf,
and which maps leaves of
\tilde{\mathcal{H}}
to leaves of
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
on a neighborhood of
\tilde{f}_{0}
.
This diffeomorphism commutes with the integer translation along the coordinate
\tilde{\theta}
, so it is quotiented into a diffeomorphism
\psi:A\to A
which satisfies the desired properties.
∎

Lemma 2.7.6
.

Let
D
a connected and simply connected domain of the plan endowed with 3 foliations
{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}},{\mathcal{H}}
such that
{\mathcal{G}}
and
{\mathcal{H}}
are transverse to
{\mathcal{F}}
.
Let
{\mathcal{K}}
be a sublamination of
{\mathcal{F}}
with empty interior and
{\mathcal{L}}
a sublamination of
{\mathcal{H}}
.
Suppose the intersection
{\mathcal{K}}\cap{\mathcal{L}}
is disjoint from
\partial D
.
Then there exist a diffeomorphism
\psi:D\to D
, isotopic to the identity via diffeomorphisms preserving each leaf of
{\mathcal{F}}
and equal to the identity in the neighborhood of
\partial D
, and such that leaves of
\psi_{*}{\mathcal{H}}
are leaves
{\mathcal{G}}
in the neighborhood of
{\mathcal{K}}\cap{\mathcal{L}}
.

Figure 22
.
Foliated rectangle or half-band
R_{i}

Proof.

The intersection
\Delta:={\mathcal{K}}\cap{\mathcal{L}}
have empty interior and the connected components
0
-dimensional and
1
-dimensional submanifold of
D
.
As the foliations cannot have closed leaves by Poincaré-Hopf theorem, the connected components of
\Delta
are points, segments, lines and half-lines in
D
.
We can cover
\Delta
by a locally finite union
\{R_{i}\}
such that

•

the
R_{i}
are pariwise disjoint and diffeomorphic to either

(1)

a rectangle
[0,1]^{2}

(2)

a half-band
{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times[0,1]

(3)

a band
{\mathbb{R}}\times[0,1]

•

the boundary of
R_{i}
is disjoint from
\Delta

•

the vertical segment of
R_{i}
are on leaves of
{\mathcal{G}}

•

the horizontal segments of
R_{i}
are on leaves of
{\mathcal{F}}
and the horizontal boundary of
R_{i}
is disjoint from
{\mathcal{K}}

We refer to Figure
22
.
Consider Case
1
.
Denote
R=R_{i}
.
Take
\sigma
a connected component of
{\mathcal{K}}
.
As
{\mathcal{K}}
is of empty interior, we can take an arbitrarily thin horizontal subrectangle
R_{\sigma}
of
R
containing
\sigma
such that the horizontal boundary of
R_{\sigma}
is disjoint from
{\mathcal{K}}
.
Up to take
R_{\sigma}
thin, every leaf of
{\mathcal{H}}
crosses
R_{\sigma}
vertically in the neighborhood of
\Delta\cap R_{\sigma}
, because
\Delta
is disjoint from the horizontal boundary of
R_{\sigma}
.
One can locally straighten the foliation
{\mathcal{H}}
on the vertical foliation in a neighborhood of
\Delta\cap R_{\sigma}
.
More precisely, there exist a diffeomorphism
\psi_{\sigma}:R\to R
supported in the interior of
R_{\sigma}
such that
\psi
preserves the horizontal foliation
{\mathcal{F}}
and
{\mathcal{H}}
coincide with
{\mathcal{G}}
in the neighborhood of
\Delta\cap R_{\sigma}
.
We cover the connected components of
{\mathcal{K}}
by a finite disjoint union of such arbitrarily thin subrectangle
R_{\sigma_{j}}
and we take
\psi_{R}
as the product of the
\psi_{\sigma_{j}}
.
A barycentric isotopy in the leaves of
{\mathcal{F}}
shows that such a diffeomorphism is isotopic to the identity via diffeomorphism preserving each leaf of
{\mathcal{F}}
, and equal to the identity in the neighborhood of
D
.

We proceed the same in Case
2
or Case
3
. Denote
R=R_{i}
a band
{\mathbb{R}}\times[0,1]
or a half-band
{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times[0,1]
.
Take
\sigma
a connected component of
{\mathcal{K}}
. It is a horizontal line or half-line.
As
{\mathcal{K}}
is of empty interior, we can take an arbitrarily thin horizontal sub-(half-)band
R_{\sigma}
of
R
containing
\sigma
such that the horizontal boundary of
R_{\sigma}
is disjoint from
{\mathcal{K}}
.
Up to take
R_{\sigma}
thin, every leaf of
{\mathcal{H}}
crosses
R_{\sigma}
vertically in the neighborhood of
\Delta\cap R_{\sigma}
, because
\Delta
is disjoint from the horizontal boundary of
R_{\sigma}
.
The diffeomorphism
\psi_{R}
is then defined as for Case
1
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
2.7.3
.

We proceed in two steps.

Step 1: Push the image of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
on
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
.

The lamination
\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
is a sublamination of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
, hence of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
, with empty interior, and
\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
is transverse to
\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
.
The connected components of
\Delta:=\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}\cap\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
are points, segments, (half)-lines and circles.
There is a neighborhood of
\Delta
in
P^{\mathrm{in}}
where
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
and
\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
are transverse.
We first work in a neighborhood of the circle component, which are compact leaves of
\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
(hence of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
).
They are in finite number with contracting holonomy (Proposition
1.2.6
).
There exist a finite disjoint union of arbitrarily thin annuli
\{A_{i}\}
, covering each circle component
\gamma_{i}
of
\Delta
, such that
\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
is transverse to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
on
A_{i}
.
We use Lemma
2.7.4
with
{\mathcal{F}}={\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
,
{\mathcal{G}}={\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
and
{\mathcal{H}}=\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
in each
A_{i}
.
As the collection
\{A_{i}\}_{i}
is a finite collection of disjoint annuli, we can construct a diffeomorphism
\psi_{A}
on
{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
supported in the interior of the
A_{i}
’s, isotopic to the identity via diffeomorphism preserving each leaf of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
, such that
(\psi_{A}\circ\hat{\varphi})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
is transverse to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
on
A_{i}
and coincide with
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
in a neighborhood
V
of each
\gamma_{i}
.
Up to shrink
V
we can suppose that the boundary of
V
is transverse to both
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
and
(\psi_{A}\circ\hat{\varphi})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
.

The composition
\psi_{A}\circ\hat{\varphi}
is a strongly transverse incomplete gluing map, strongly isotopic to
\hat{\varphi}
.
We work now in the complementary of the neighborhood
V
of the closed connected component
\gamma_{i}
of
\Delta
.
Denote
\Delta^{\prime}=((\psi_{A}\circ\hat{\varphi})_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}){\smallsetminus}V
.
The connected components of
\Delta^{\prime}
are points, segments and (half)-lines.
One can cover
\Delta^{\prime}
by a disjoint union of arbitrarily thin, connected and simply connected domains
\{D_{i}\}
in
P^{\mathrm{in}}
such that the boundary of
D_{i}
is disjoint from
\Delta
, and the foliation
(\psi_{A}\circ\hat{\varphi})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
is transverse to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
on
D_{i}
.
We use Lemma
2.7.6
on each
D_{i}
, with
{\mathcal{F}}={\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
,
{\mathcal{K}}=\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
,
{\mathcal{G}}={\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
,
{\mathcal{H}}=(\psi_{A}\circ\hat{\varphi})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
,
{\mathcal{L}}=\check{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
, but we replace the intersection
{\mathcal{K}}\cap{\mathcal{L}}
by
({\mathcal{K}}\cap{\mathcal{L}}){\smallsetminus}V
, which does not change the proof.
As the collection
\{D_{i}\}
is a locally finite collection of disjoint domains, it gives a diffeomorphism
\psi_{D}
on
{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
supported in the interior of the
D_{i}
’s, isotopic to the identity via diffeomorphism preserving each leaf of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
, equal to the identity in
V
, and such that leaves of
(\psi_{D}\circ\psi_{A}\circ\hat{\varphi})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
are leaves of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
in a neighborhood of
(\psi_{D}\circ\psi_{A}\circ\hat{\varphi})_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
.
Define
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}:{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}\to{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
, equal to the identity on
P^{\mathrm{out}}
and to
\psi_{D}\circ\psi_{A}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
.
Define
\hat{\varphi}_{1}:=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{\varphi}
.
It is a strongly transverse incomplete gluing map, strongly isotopic to
\hat{\varphi}
, such that
(\hat{\varphi}_{1})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
coincide with
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
in a neighborhood of
(\hat{\varphi}_{1})_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
.

Step 2: Push the image of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
on
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
.

It remains now to push the image of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
on
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
in a neighborhood of the intersection
(\hat{\varphi}_{1})_{*}\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
, without destroying the previous work.
We consider
P^{\mathrm{out}}
and essentially do the same work, considering the foliations
{\mathcal{F}}={\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
,
{\mathcal{G}}={\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
and
{\mathcal{H}}=(\hat{\varphi}_{1})^{-1}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
, and the sublaminations
{\mathcal{K}}=\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
with empty interior and
{\mathcal{L}}=(\hat{\varphi}_{1})^{-1}_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
, and use Lemma
2.7.4
then Lemma
2.7.6
as previously.
We construct a diffeomorphism
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
supported in
P^{\mathrm{out}}
, such that
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
is isotopic the identity, preserves each leaf of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
, and maps leaves of
(\hat{\varphi}_{1})^{-1}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
to leaves of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
in a neighborhood of
(\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\circ\hat{\varphi}_{1}^{-1})_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}\cap\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
.
Define
\hat{\varphi}_{2}=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{\varphi}\circ(\psi^{\mathrm{out}})^{-1}
. It is a strongly transverse incomplete gluing map, strongly isotopic to
\hat{\varphi}
.
Moreover, we check that
(\hat{\varphi}_{2})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}=(\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{\varphi}_{1})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}={\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
in a neighborhood of
(\hat{\varphi}_{2})_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}=(\hat{\varphi}_{1})_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
, hence
\hat{\varphi}_{2}
satisfies Lemma
2.7.3
.
∎

Before proving proposition
2.7.2
we need the general lemma.

Lemma 2.7.7
.

Let
C
be either a square
[0,1]^{2}
, a half band
{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times[0,1]
or a band
{\mathbb{R}}\times[0,1]
, endowed with a pair of foliation
{\mathcal{F}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}
such that the horizontal boundary of
C
are leaves of
{\mathcal{F}}
and the vertical boundary of
C
(when existing) are leaves of
{\mathcal{G}}
.
We suppose that
{\mathcal{G}}
is transverse to
{\mathcal{F}}
in the neighborhood of
\partial C
and every leaf of
{\mathcal{G}}
crosses
C
from bottom to top.
Then there is a diffeomorphism
\psi:C\to C
, isotopic to the identity, and such that
\psi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}
is transverse to
{\mathcal{F}}
on
C
.

We refer to Figure
23
.

Figure 23
.

Proof.

The hypothesis imply that each of the foliations
{\mathcal{F}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}
are smoothly conjugated to trivial foliations, such that we may assume that
{\mathcal{F}}
is the horizontal foliation.
Now, there is a foliation
{\mathcal{H}}
on
C
transverse to the leaves of
{\mathcal{F}}
and coinciding with
{\mathcal{G}}
in a neighborhood of
\partial C
and having the same holonomy from the horizontal bottom to the horizontal top of
C
. The foliations
{\mathcal{G}}
and
{\mathcal{H}}
are smoothly conjugated by a diffeomorphism
\psi
which coincide with the identity close to
\partial C
, concluding.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.7.2
.

Up to make a strong isotopy among the incomplete gluing maps, we can assume that the strongly transverse incomplete gluing map
\hat{\varphi}
of
(P,X)
satisfies Lemma
2.7.3
. We proceed in two steps.

Lemma 2.7.8
.

There exists a diffeomorphism
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\colon{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}\to{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
, supported in
P^{\mathrm{in}}
, equal to the identity on a neighborhood of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
, such that
(\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{\varphi})_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
is transverse to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
.

Proof.

First remark that there is a neighborhood
U^{\mathrm{in}}
on
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
such that
\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
is transverse to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
.
Indeed, from Lemma
2.7.3
, there is a neighborhood
{\mathcal{O}}
of
\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}\cap\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
such that
\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
coincide with
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
.
We can choose a neighborhood of
\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
small enough such that the intersection with
\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
is contained in
{\mathcal{O}}
.
Now the union of a neighborhood of
\partial{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
with
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
is a neighborhood of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
, satisfying the property.
Up to reduce, we can choose
U^{\mathrm{in}}
to be an adapted neighborhood of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
(Definition
2.4.3
and Lemma
2.4.4
).
The complementary
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}U^{\mathrm{in}}
is a finite collection
\{C_{i}\}
of pairwise disjoint adapted squares and (half)-bands (see Figure
18
).

The foliation
\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
coincide with
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
in the neighborhood of
\partial C_{i}
.
Moreover,

Claim 2.7.9
.

Each leaf of
\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap C_{i}
is a segment crossing
C_{i}
from bottom to top.

Proof.

First remark that any leaf of
\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap C_{i}

crossing one horizontal boundary of
C_{i}
cannot cross it twice.
Indeed, by extending this leaf on the strip
B_{i}\subset P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
containing
C_{i}
, it would give us a bi-gon
G
with a boundary
g_{1}
on a leaf of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
and a boundary
g_{2}
on a leaf of
\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
, with interior disjoint from
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
.
Up to choose another smaller bi-gon inside
G
, either
G
is foliated by
\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
either it is disjoint from
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}\cup\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
.
The first case is impossible by transversality of
\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
with
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
and the second by strong transversality of
\hat{\varphi}
.
Suppose now the existence of a half-leaf
l
of
(\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}})\cap C_{i}
which remains in the interior of
C_{i}
, hence in the interior of the strip
B_{i}
containing
C_{i}
.
As every laminations are pre-foliations, there is no compact leaf in
B_{i}
therefore
l
is asymptotic to an end of
B_{i}
.
Recall that
\hat{\varphi}
is strongly isotopic to the restriction of a strongly quasi-transverse gluing map
\varphi
.
In order to keep notations light, we suppose that
\varphi
is a gluing map of the same building block
(P,X)
. The proof would be the same by considering an isotopic block.
By strong isotopy, there is a half-leave
l^{\prime}
of
\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
which is contained in the interior of the strip
B_{i}
and asymptotic to an end of
B_{i}
.
This end accumulates on a periodic orbit
{\mathcal{O}}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
, and let
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}:=\varphi({\mathcal{O}})
(where the orientation is preserved).
Half-leaves of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
in the neighborhood of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
in
P^{\mathrm{out}}
accumulate on
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
with a contracting holonomy (for the orientation of
{\mathcal{O}}
by the flow), so the same occurs for half-leaves of
\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
in the neighborhood of
{\mathcal{O}}=\varphi({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})
in
P^{\mathrm{in}}
.
On the other hand, the holonomy of
l^{\prime}
on a small transversal
\tau
to
{\mathcal{O}}
is bounded by the (expanding) holonomy of the leaves of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
bordering the strip
B_{i}
containing
C_{i}
, hence cannot be contracting which is a contradiction.
∎

We can use Lemma
2.7.7
on each pairwise disjoint
C_{i}
with
{\mathcal{F}}={\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}=\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
to construct a diffeomorphism
\psi_{C_{i}}
of
{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
supported in the interior of
C_{i}
.
The collection
\{C_{i}\}
is pairwise disjoint and locally compact in
P^{\mathrm{in}}
, which allows to define the diffeomorphism
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
by the product of the
\psi_{C_{i}}
.
∎

Denote
\hat{\varphi}_{1}=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\cap\hat{\varphi}
.

Lemma 2.7.10
.

There exists a diffeomorphism
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\colon{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}\to{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
, supported in
P^{\mathrm{out}}
, equal to the identity on a neighborhood of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
, such that
(\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\circ\hat{\varphi}_{1}^{-1})_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
is transverse to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
.

Proof.

The proof is the same as for Lemma
2.7.8
.
∎

Denote
\hat{\varphi}_{2}=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{\varphi}\circ(\psi^{\mathrm{out}})^{-1}:{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}\to{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
.
It is isotopic to
\hat{\varphi}
through strongly transverse incomplete gluing maps, and
(\hat{\varphi}_{2})_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}=(\hat{\varphi}_{1})_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
is transverse to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
and
(\hat{\varphi}_{2})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
is transverse to
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
.
So the foliations
\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
may fail to be transverse only in the interior of a connected component of
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}({\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}\cup(\hat{\varphi}_{2})_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}})
.
By strong transversality of
\hat{\varphi}_{2}
, the closure of those connected components are squares with two sides on leaves of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
and two sides on leaves of
(\hat{\varphi}_{2})_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
.
We conclude by using Lemma
2.7.7
with
{\mathcal{F}}={\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}=(\hat{\varphi}_{2})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
.
The result is a strongly transverse incomplete gluing maps, strongly isotopic to
\hat{\varphi}
because it coincides with
\hat{\varphi}
in a neighborhood of the boundary lamination
{\mathcal{L}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
, and which satisfies Proposition
2.7.2
.
∎

Step 2: Trivial extension of an incomplete gluing map.

We keep the notations of the beginning of Subsection
2.7
.
In this step, up to make a strong isotopy of incomplete gluing maps, we assume that
\hat{\varphi}
satisfies Proposition
2.7.2
, i.e.
\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
is transverse to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
.
For any
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
, there exists a unique orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{j}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
such that if
A
and
A^{\prime}
are connected components of
{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
adjacent to
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
, then
\hat{\varphi}(A)
and
\hat{\varphi}(A^{\prime})
are connected components of
{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
adjacent in
{\mathcal{O}}_{j}
.
This pairing defines an involution without fixed point
\sigma\colon\{1,\dots,n\}\to\{1,\dots,n\}
, which determines the combinatorial gluing of
\hat{\varphi}
.

We show the following proposition, which states that there exists a normalized gluing map
\varphi_{1}
of
(P,X)
whose restriction to the complementary of the orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
is strongly isotopic to
\hat{\varphi}
.
Recall that a normalized block
(P,X)
is equipped with normalized coordinate systems
({\mathcal{V}}_{i},\xi_{i}=(x,y,\theta))
on tubular neighborhoods of the periodic orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
contained in
{\partial P}
(Definition
2.1.4
, Item
4
).
In these coordinates, the boundary
\partial P
has equation
x=y
, the exit boundary
P^{\mathrm{out}}
is in the domain
\{x<0\}
, and the entrance boundary
P^{\mathrm{in}}
in the domain
\{x>0\}
.

Proposition 2.7.11
.

There exists a gluing map
\varphi_{1}\colon{\partial P}\to{\partial P}

such that

(1)

the restriction of
\varphi_{1}
to
{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
is strongly isotopic to
\hat{\varphi}

(in the sense of Definition
2.5.2
);

(2)

for any
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
, there exist normalized coordinate systems
({\mathcal{V}}_{i},\xi_{i}=(x,y,\theta))
and
({\mathcal{V}}_{j},\xi_{j}=(x,y,\theta))
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{j}=\varphi_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{i})
such that the expression of
\varphi_{1}
in these coordinates is
(x,x,\theta)\mapsto(-x,-x,\theta)
;

(3)

the foliation
(\varphi_{1})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
is transverse to the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
;

In other words,
\varphi_{1}
is a normalized gluing map of
(P,X)
(Definition
2.1.13
) whose restriction is strongly isotopic to
\hat{\varphi}
.

Let us start by fixing some notations.
Let
({\mathcal{V}}_{i},\xi_{i})
be a normalized coordinate system of the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
.
The coordinates
\xi_{i}=(x,y,\theta)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
on
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
induce the coordinates
\rho_{i}=(x,\theta)\in{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
on
\partial P\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
by forgetting the coordinate
y
.
Up to multiply the coordinates
x
and
y
by a constant and then shrink
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
, we can assume that
\rho_{i}(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{i})=]0,1]\times{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\rho_{i}(P^{\mathrm{out}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{i})=[-1,0[\times{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
.
According to the foliations equation (Remark
2.1.7
) each leaf of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
transversely intersects the section
\{x=1\}
once, and each leaf of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
transversely intersects the section
\{x=-1\}
once.
Denote
l^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{i,\theta}
and
l^{u,\mathrm{in}}_{i,\theta}
the leaves of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
containing
(1,\theta)
in coordinates
\rho_{i}
.
Similarly, denote
l^{s,\mathrm{out}}_{i,\theta}
and
l^{u,\mathrm{out}}_{i,\theta}
the leaves of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
containing
(-1,\theta)
in coordinates
\rho_{i}
(see Figure
24
).

Figure 24
.
Parameterization of the leaves of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
by the coordinate
\theta
on
{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}

We will show the following two lemmas, analogous to each other.

Lemma 2.7.12
.

There exists a diffeomorphism
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\colon{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}\to{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
with support in
P^{\mathrm{in}}
and a neighborhood
{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}
of
\partial P^{\mathrm{in}}
in
P^{\mathrm{in}}
such that

(1)

\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
is isotopic to the identity among the diffeomorphisms of
{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
which preserves the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
leaf-to-leaf

(2)

for any
i
and
j=\sigma(i)
, for any
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
, the leaf
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}(\hat{\varphi}(l^{u,\mathrm{out}}_{j,\theta}))
coincides with the leaf
l^{u,\mathrm{in}}_{i,\theta}
on
{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}

Lemma 2.7.13
.

There exists a diffeomorphism
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\colon{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}\to{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
with support in
P^{\mathrm{out}}
and a neighborhood
{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{out}}
of
\partial P^{\mathrm{out}}
in
P^{\mathrm{out}}
such that

(1)

\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
is isotopic to the identity among the diffeomorphisms of
{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
which preserves the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
leaf-to-leaf

(2)

for any
i
and
j=\sigma(i)
, for any
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
, the leaf
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}(\hat{\varphi}^{-1}(l^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{j,\theta}))
coincides with the leaf
l^{s,\mathrm{out}}_{i,\theta}
on
{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{out}}

Let us show that Proposition
2.7.11
follows from Lemmas
2.7.12
and
2.7.13
.

Proof of Proposition
2.7.11
.

Denote
\hat{\varphi}_{1}:=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{\varphi}\circ(\psi^{\mathrm{out}})^{-1}:{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}\to{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
.
According to Item
1
of Lemma
2.7.12
and its analogue of Lemma
2.7.13
, the diffeomorphism
\hat{\varphi}_{1}
is an incomplete gluing map strongly isotopic to
\hat{\varphi}
.
Moreover
\hat{\varphi}
maps the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
to a foliation transverse to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
by assumption, so it is the same for
\hat{\varphi}_{1}
, still according to Item
1
of Lemma
2.7.12
for
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
, and its analogue for
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
.
Let
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}:={\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}\cap\hat{\varphi}_{1}({\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{out}})
, where
{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{out}}
are the neighborhoods given by Lemmas
2.7.12
and
2.7.13
.
This is a neighborhood of
\partial P^{\mathrm{in}}
in
P^{\mathrm{in}}
.
Let
i
and
j=\sigma(i)
be
\theta_{s}\in{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\theta_{u}\in{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Then the following equalities are true on
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}
(see Figure
25
).

Figure 25
.
Image of the foliations by
\hat{\varphi}_{1}

\displaystyle\hat{\varphi}_{1}(l^{u,\mathrm{out}}_{j,\theta_{u}})

\displaystyle=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{\varphi}\circ(\psi^{\mathrm{out}})^{-1}(l^{u,\mathrm{out}}_{j,\theta_{u}})

\displaystyle=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{\varphi}(l^{u,\mathrm{out}}_{j,\theta_{u}})

(Lemma
2.7.13
, Item
1
)

\displaystyle=l^{u,\mathrm{in}}_{i,\theta_{u}}

(Lemma
2.7.12
, Item
2
)

\displaystyle\hat{\varphi}_{1}(l^{s,\mathrm{out}}_{j,\theta_{s}})

\displaystyle=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{\varphi}\circ(\psi^{\mathrm{out}})^{-1}(l^{s,\mathrm{out}}_{j,\theta_{s}})

\displaystyle=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{\varphi}(\hat{\varphi}^{-1}(l^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{i,\theta_{s}}))=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}(l^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{i,\theta_{s}})

(Lemma
2.7.13
, Item
2
)

\displaystyle=l^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{i,\theta_{s}}

(Lemma
2.7.12
, Item
1
)

Recall that the flow of
X
is linear in coordinates
\xi_{i}=(x,y,\theta)
on
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
, given by the expression
X^{t}(x,y,\theta)=(2^{-t}x,2^{t}y,\theta+t)
, and the boundary
\partial P
coincides with the diagonal
x=y
.
The leaf
l^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{i,\theta_{s}}
thus has equation
x=2^{-\theta_{s}+\tilde{\theta}}
and the leaf
l^{u,\mathrm{in}}_{i,\theta_{u}}
has equation
x=2^{-\theta_{u}-\tilde{\theta}}
in coordinates
\rho_{i}=(x,\theta)
, where
\tilde{\theta}\in{\mathbb{R}}
is a lift of
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
(Remark
2.1.7
).
So the intersection points of these two leaves in coordinates
\rho_{i}=(x,\theta)
are the following points, for
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, with
k
large enough :

p_{k}=\left(2^{\frac{\theta_{u}-\theta_{s}}{2}-\frac{k}{2}},\frac{\theta_{s}+\theta_{u}}{2}+\frac{k}{2}\right)

Similarly, the intersection points of the leaves
l^{s,\mathrm{out}}_{j,\theta_{s}}
and
l^{u,\mathrm{out}}_{j,\theta_{u}}
in coordinates
\rho_{j}=(x,\theta)
are the following points, for
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, with
k
large enough:

q_{k}=\left(-2^{\frac{\theta_{u}-\theta_{s}}{2}-\frac{k}{2}},\frac{\theta_{s}+\theta_{u}}{2}+\frac{k}{2}\right)

Therefore, for
x
close enough to
0
we have
\hat{\varphi}_{1}(x,\theta)=(-x2^{-\frac{k(x,\theta)}{2}},\,\theta+\frac{k(x,\theta)}{2})
in coordinates
\rho_{j}
and
\rho_{i}
, with
k(x,\theta)\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.
By continuity,
k(x,\theta)
is constant, and there exists
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
such that for
x
close enough to
0
, we have

\hat{\varphi}_{1}(x,\theta)=\left(-x2^{-\frac{k}{2}},\,\theta+\frac{k}{2}\right).

Let
\tau\colon[0,1]\to[0,1]
be a smooth decreasing plateau function, such that
\tau(x)=1
on a neighborhood of
0
and
\tau(x)=0
on a neighborhood of
1
.
Let
\psi\colon{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}\to{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
be the diffeomorphism with support in
P^{\mathrm{in}}
, equal to the identity outside
\cup_{i}{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
, and such that the expression of
\psi
in coordinates
\rho_{i}
on each
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}\cap P^{\mathrm{in}}
is

(x,\theta)\mapsto\left(x2^{\frac{k\tau(x)}{2}},\theta-\frac{k\tau(x)}{2}\right).

Then
\psi
is isotopic to the identity among the diffeomorphisms preserving
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
leaf-to-leaf, supported in a neighborhood of
\partial P^{\mathrm{in}}
where the foliations
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
and
\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
coincide (up to choose the support of
\tau
close to
0
).
Therefore, the composition

\hat{\varphi}_{1}:=\psi\circ\hat{\varphi}_{1}^{\prime}\colon{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}\to{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}

is an incomplete gluing map strongly isotopic to
\hat{\varphi}_{1}
,
which maps the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
on a foliation transverse to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
,
and for all
i
and
j=\sigma(i)
, the expression of
\hat{\varphi}_{1}^{\prime}
in coordinates
\rho_{j}
and
\rho_{i}
is
\hat{\varphi}_{1}^{\prime}(x,\theta)=(-x,\theta)
for all
x
close enough to
0
.
This diffeomorphism naturally extends on
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
by
(0,\theta)\mapsto(0,\theta)
.
The diffeomorphism
\hat{\varphi}_{1}^{\prime}\colon{\partial P}\to{\partial P}
is thus a gluing map of
(P,X)
, whose restriction to
{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
is strongly isotopic to
\hat{\varphi}
, hence satisfies Item
1
of Proposition
2.7.11
.
Recall the foliation
(\hat{\varphi}_{1}^{\prime})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
is transverse to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
, hence
\hat{\varphi}_{1}^{\prime}
satisfies Item
3
of Proposition
2.7.11
.
Finally,
\hat{\varphi}^{\prime}_{1}
satisfies Item
2
of Proposition
2.7.11
by construction, in other words it is equal to the reflection
(x,x,\theta)\mapsto(-x,-x,\theta)
in normalized coordinate systems
({\mathcal{V}}_{i}^{\prime},\xi_{i}=(x,y,\theta))
and
({\mathcal{V}}_{j}^{\prime},\xi_{j}=(x,y,\theta))
, for smaller normalized neighborhoods
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
and
{\mathcal{V}}_{j}^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{V}}_{j}
.
The proposition is shown.
∎

It remains to prove Lemma
2.7.12
, Lemma
2.7.13
being completely analogous.

Proof of Lemma
2.7.12
.

Let
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}^{\mathrm{in}}:={\mathcal{V}}_{i}\cap P^{\mathrm{in}}
, and
{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{j}:={\mathcal{V}}_{j}\cap P^{\mathrm{out}}
, where
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
and
{\mathcal{V}}_{j}
are tubular neighborhoods of the periodic orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{j}
contained in
{\partial P}
, with
j=\sigma(i)
.
It is sufficient to construct a diffeomorphism
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}\colon{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}\to{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
which satisfies the properties stated on
{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
, equal to the identity on the boundary of
{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
.
We will work in the universal covering.
Let
I=]0,1]
and
J=[-1,0[
.
Denote
\pi\colon{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
the projection.
Let
\pi^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i}^{\mathrm{in}}\to{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
be the universal covering of
{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
and
\tilde{\rho}_{i}=(x,\tilde{\theta})\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i}^{\mathrm{in}}\to I\times{\mathbb{R}}
the lift of coordinates
\rho_{i}
such that
\pi\circ\tilde{\rho}_{i}=\rho_{i}\circ\pi^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
.
We also define
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{j}^{\mathrm{out}}
the universal covering of
{\mathcal{V}}_{j}^{\mathrm{out}}
and
\tilde{\rho}_{j}=(x,\tilde{\theta})\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{j}\to J\times{\mathbb{R}}
the lift of coordinates
\rho_{j}
.
Denote
(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
,
(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}})
the lifts of the foliations in
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
,
and
(\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
,
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}})
the lifts of the corresponding foliations in
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{i}
.
For any
\tilde{\theta}\in{\mathbb{R}}
, denote
\tilde{l}^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta}
and
\tilde{l}^{u,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta}
the leaves of
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
containing
(1,\tilde{\theta})
in the coordinate system
\tilde{\rho}_{i}
.
Similarly, for any
\tilde{\theta}\in{\mathbb{R}}
, denote
\tilde{l}^{s,\mathrm{out}}_{\theta}
and
\tilde{l}^{u,\mathrm{out}}_{\theta}
the leaves of
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
containing
(-1,\tilde{\theta})
in the coordinate system
\tilde{\rho}_{j}
.
The incomplete gluing map
\hat{\varphi}
lift to a diffeomorphism
\tilde{\varphi}
, which is well defined from a neighborhood of
\{x=0\}
in
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{j}
into a neighborhood of
\{x=0\}
in
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
.
For any
p\in\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}\cap\tilde{\varphi}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{j})
, let us denote
\tilde{\theta}_{u}(p)
and
\tilde{\theta}_{s}(p)
the real numbers such that
p\in\tilde{l}^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta_{s}(p)}\cap\tilde{\varphi}(\tilde{l}^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta_{s}(p)})
.

Lemma 2.7.14
.

There exists
\epsilon>0
such that for any
p\in\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}\cap\tilde{\varphi}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{j})
with coordinates
\tilde{\rho}_{i}(p)=(x(p),\tilde{\theta}(p))
with
0<x(p)<\epsilon
, the leaves
\tilde{l}^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta_{s}(p)}
and
\tilde{l}^{u,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta_{u}(p)}
intersect at a single point
q\in\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
.

Proof.

Recall that, for every
\tilde{\theta}_{s}
and
\tilde{\theta}_{u}
, the leaf
\tilde{l}^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta_{s}}
has equation

\tilde{\theta}=\log_{2}(x)+\tilde{\theta}_{s}
,
the leaf
\tilde{l}^{u,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta_{u}}
has equation

\tilde{\theta}=-\log_{2}(x)+\tilde{\theta}_{u}
, and
the leaf
\tilde{l}^{u,\mathrm{out}}_{\theta_{u}}
has equation

\tilde{\theta}=-\log_{2}(x)+\tilde{\theta}_{u}
(in the corresponding coordinate systems
\rho_{i}
and
\rho_{j}
).
Note that
\tilde{l}^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta_{s}}
intersects
\tilde{l}^{u,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta_{u}}
if and only if
\tilde{\theta}_{s}<\tilde{\theta}_{u}
.
Orient each leaf of
\tilde{l}^{u,\mathrm{out}}_{\theta_{u}}
in the increasing direction of the coordinate
\tilde{\theta}
in
\rho_{j}
.
The leaf is then oriented in the direction of the lift of the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{j}=\partial{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{j}
.
Orient the leaf
\tilde{\varphi}_{*}(\tilde{l}^{u,\mathrm{out}}_{\theta_{u}})
with the mapped orientation.
Each leaf
\tilde{l}^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta_{s}}
of
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
disconnects the plane
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
into two components.
We call
left component of
\tilde{l}^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta_{s}}
the component containing the leaves of
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
parametrized by
\tilde{\theta}<\theta_{s}
, and
right component of
\tilde{l}^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta_{s}}
the other one.
With this orientation, the oriented leaves of
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
intersect the leaves of
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
from left to right.
Likewise,

Claim 2.7.15
.

The oriented leaves of
\tilde{\varphi}_{*}\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
transversely intersect the oriented leaves of
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
from their left to their right on
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
.

Proof.

By assumption
\hat{\varphi}
is strongly isotopic to the restriction of a strongly quasi-transverse gluing map
\varphi_{0}
of an isotopic building block
(P_{0},X_{0})
.
We denote
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}
and
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}
the boundary laminations on the entrance boundary
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}
and the exit boundary
P^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}
of
(P_{0},X_{0})
.
Let
{\mathcal{O}}_{0,*}={\mathcal{O}}_{0,1},\dots,{\mathcal{O}}_{0,n}
be the periodic orbits of
X_{0}
contained in
{\partial P}_{0}
, numbered compatibly with the numbering of the orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
(via block isotopy).
Then
\varphi_{0}
maps the oriented orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{0,j}
to the oriented orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{0,i}
, and maps
P^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}
to
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}
.
It follows that the leaves of
(\varphi_{0})_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{0}
accumulate on the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{0,i}
in a contracting way on
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}
, hence transversely intersect the leaves of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{0}
from their left to their right, for the same orientation conventions.
By strong isotopy of
\hat{\varphi}
with the restriction of
\varphi_{0}
to
{\partial P}_{0}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{0,*}
, it is the same for the intersection of oriented leaves of
\hat{\varphi}_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
with oriented leaves of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
.
By transversality of the foliations, it is the same for the leaves of
\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
with
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
.
This property remains true for the universal covering.
∎

We don’t know a priori if the leaf
\tilde{l}^{u,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta_{u}(p)}
intersects the leaf
\tilde{l}^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta_{s}(p)}
, but we have the following

Claim 2.7.16
.

If
x(p)\to 0
, then
(\tilde{\theta}_{s}(p)-\tilde{\theta}_{u}(p))\to+\infty
and this convergence is uniform in
\tilde{\theta}(p)
.

Proof.

According to Claim
2.7.15
, if
p=(x(p),\tilde{\theta}(p))
is a coordinate for the oriented leaf
\tilde{\varphi}(\tilde{l}^{u,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta_{u}(p)})
, in other words
\tilde{\theta}_{u}(p)
is fixed and
x(p)\to 0
, then
\tilde{\theta}_{s}(p)\to+\infty
.
By continuity (the foliations
\tilde{\varphi}_{*}\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
are transverse), the convergence is locally uniform in
\tilde{\theta}(p)
.
Since everything commutes with the translation
\tilde{\theta}\mapsto\tilde{\theta}+1
, we deduce that the convergence is globally uniform.
∎

It follows from Claim
2.7.16
the existence of
\epsilon>0
such that for all
p=(x,\theta)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
with
x<\epsilon
, we have
\tilde{\theta}_{s}(p)<\tilde{\theta}_{u}(p)
.
As noticed previously, the explicit equations of the leaves
\tilde{l}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
and
\tilde{l}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
imply that for all
p=(x,\tilde{\theta})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
with
x<\epsilon
, the leaves

\tilde{l}^{u,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta_{u}(p)}
and
\tilde{l}^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta_{s}(p)}
intersect at a single point

q(p)=\left(2^{\frac{\tilde{\theta}_{u}(p)-\tilde{\theta}_{s}(p)}{2}},\frac{\tilde{\theta}_{u}(p)+\tilde{\theta}_{s}(p)}{2}\right).

∎

We can now prove Lemma
2.7.12
.
Lemma
2.7.14
gives a map

p\mapsto q(p)
, such that, given a point
p\in{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
such that
x(p)<\epsilon
, it associates the point
q(p)
of intersection of the leaf
l^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta_{s}(p)}
with the leaf
\tilde{l}^{u,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta_{u}(p)}
.
This map is well defined from
]0,\epsilon[\times{\mathbb{R}}
(in the coordinate system
\rho_{i}=(x,\theta)
) on its image
{\mathcal{V}}
where
{\mathcal{V}}\subset{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}
contains a neighborhood
]0,\eta]\times{\mathbb{R}}
(
\eta<\epsilon
), and injective.
By smooth transversality of the foliations, it is differentiable.
Let
\tau\colon[0,1]\to[0,1]
be a smooth decreasing function which is equal to
1
on
[0,\frac{\eta}{2}]
and
0
on
[\eta,1]
.
Define
\tilde{\psi}^{\mathrm{in}}\colon\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}\to{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
in the following way (Figure
26
).
Let
p=(x,\tilde{\theta})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
.
If
x\geq\eta
, define
\tilde{\psi}^{\mathrm{in}}(x,\tilde{\theta})=(x,\tilde{\theta})
.
If
x\leq\eta
, let
q=q(p)\in{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}
given by Lemma
2.7.14
.
Then
p
and
q
belong to the same leaf
l^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta_{s}}
of
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
, and define
\tilde{\psi}^{\mathrm{in}}(p)
the weighted barycenter by
\tau(x)
between the points
p
and
q
in the leaf
l^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta_{s}}
, which we denote by use of notation

\tilde{\psi}^{\mathrm{in}}(p=(x,\tilde{\theta}))=\tau(x)q+(1-\tau(x))p
.
This expression define a diffeomorphism
\tilde{\psi}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
, equal to the identity in the neighborhood of the boundary
\{x=1\}
of
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
,
isotopic to the identity among diffeomorphisms which preserves the foliation
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
leaf-to-leaf,
and which maps the leaf
\tilde{\varphi}(\tilde{l}^{u,\mathrm{out}}_{\theta_{u}})
to the leaf
\tilde{l}^{u,\mathrm{in}}_{\theta_{u}}
on a neighborhood of the boundary
\{x=0\}
of
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
.
This diffeomorphism commutes with the integer translation along the coordinate
\tilde{\theta}
, so it is quotiented into a diffeomorphism
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\colon{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}\to{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
which satisfies the desired properties.
∎

Figure 26
.
Action of
\tilde{\psi}^{\mathrm{in}}
on
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}

Conclusion

Proof of Proposition
2.7.1
.

It is sufficient to apply successively Propositions
2.7.2
and
2.7.11

∎

2.8.
Proof of Proposition
2.1.16

Proof of Proposition
2.1.16
.

Let
(P_{0},X_{0})
be a filled building block, and
\varphi_{0}
be a strongly quasi-transverse gluing map of
(P_{0},X_{0})
.

•

Propositions
2.2.2
and
2.3.1
applied successively to
(P_{0},X_{0})
give the existence of a building block
(P,X)
which satisfies Items
1
and
2
of the definition of a normalized block
2.1.4
, in other words it admits an affine section and the multipliers of the orbits of
X
in
{\partial P}
are equal to
\{\frac{1}{2},2\}
,
and it is isotopic to
(P_{0},X_{0})
among the orbit equivalent building blocks.
The block
(P,X)
satisfies the assumptions of Proposition
2.4.1
.
Let
({\mathcal{G}}^{s},{\mathcal{G}}^{u})
be a pair of affine invariant foliations on
(P,X)
given by Proposition
2.4.1
.
The block
(P,X)
equipped with the pair of foliations
({\mathcal{G}}^{s},{\mathcal{G}}^{u})
satisfies Items
1
,
2
and
3
of Definition
2.1.4
.
It follows from Proposition
2.5.5
the existence of a strongly transverse incomplete gluing map
\hat{\varphi}
of
(P,X)
, strongly isotopic to the restriction of the gluing map
\varphi_{0}
to the complementary of the periodic orbits of
X_{0}
in
{\partial P}_{0}
.

•

Proposition
2.6.1
applied to
(P,X)
gives the existence of a filled normalized block
(P_{1},X_{1})
isotopic to
(P,X)
.
Lemma
2.6.3
applied to the incomplete gluing map
\hat{\varphi}
of
(P,X)
gives the existence of a strongly transverse incomplete gluing map
\hat{\varphi}_{1}
of
(P_{1},X_{1})
and strongly isotopic to
\hat{\varphi}
and thus to the restriction of the strongly quasi-transverse gluing map
\varphi_{0}
to the complementary of the periodic orbits of
X_{0}
in
{\partial P}_{0}
.

•

The block
(P_{1},X_{1})
is a filled normalized block with a strongly transverse incomplete gluing map
\hat{\varphi}_{1}
, strongly isotopic to the restriction of the strongly quasi-transverse gluing map
\varphi_{0}
of
(P_{0},X_{0})
.
One can apply Proposition
2.7.1
, which gives the existence of a normalized gluing map
\varphi^{\prime}_{1}

of
(P_{1},X_{1})
, whose restriction is strongly isotopic to
\hat{\varphi}_{1}
and thus to the restriction of
\varphi_{0}
.
To show that the triple
(P_{1},X_{1},\varphi^{\prime}_{1})
is strongly isotopic to the triple
(P_{0},X_{0},\varphi_{0})
, it remains to show that
\varphi^{\prime}_{1}
is isotopic to
\varphi_{0}
(Item
2
of Definition
1.4.9
).
The blocks
(P_{0},X_{0})
and
(P_{1},X_{1})
are isotopic.
Up to make an orbit equivalence
(P_{0}^{\prime},X_{0}^{\prime})
of
(P_{0},X_{0})
, they are contained in a common minimal continuation (Proposition
1.4.4
), and the boundaries
{\partial P}^{\prime}_{0}
and
{\partial P}_{1}
are isotopic relatively to the periodic orbits contained in the boundary.
Let
\varphi_{0}^{\prime}
be the conjugate of
\varphi_{0}
by the orbit equivalence and
\varphi_{1}
be the push of
\varphi_{0}^{\prime}
on
{\partial P}_{1}
by the isotopy between
{\partial P}_{0}^{\prime}
and
{\partial P}_{1}
.
Then
\varphi_{1}
is a gluing map, a priori only topological, that we will use only to adjust the isotopy class of
\varphi_{1}^{\prime}
.
Up to make a small perturbation, we can assume that it is differentiable.
It is isotopic to
\varphi_{0}
in the sense of Definition
1.4.9
.

Let us show that we can adjust
\varphi^{\prime}_{1}
to make it isotopic
to
\varphi_{1}
without destroying the strong isotopy.
If
T
is a connected component of
{\partial P}
that does not intersect the collection
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
, the restriction of
\hat{\varphi}_{1}
to
T
is a complete gluing map.
The restrictions to
T
of
\hat{\varphi}_{1}
and
\varphi_{0}
, and of
\varphi^{\prime}_{1}
and
\hat{\varphi}_{1}
are then isotopic.
The result follows by transitivity.
Suppose
T
contains a collection of periodic orbits of
X
, and let
T^{\prime}
be the connected component of
{\partial P}
matched with
T
by
\varphi_{1}
.
Let
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
be a periodic orbit contained in
T
, and
({\mathcal{V}}_{i},\xi_{i}=(x,y,\theta))
an normalized coordinate system of
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
.
As
\varphi^{\prime}_{1}
is a normalized gluing map, up to shrink
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
, we can assume that

(\varphi^{\prime}_{1})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}={\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
and
(\varphi^{\prime}_{1})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}={\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
on
{\partial P}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
.
Let
A:={\partial P}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
and
\rho_{i}=(x,\theta)\in I\times{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
the coordinates induced on
A
.
There exists
\tau\colon T\to T
a Dehn twist on the annulus
A
, equal to the identity on the boundary of
A
, and such that
\tau\circ\varphi^{\prime}_{1}\colon T^{\prime}\to T
is isotopic to
\varphi_{1}\colon T^{\prime}\to T
.
For the composition
\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{1}:=\tau\circ\varphi^{\prime}_{1}
to still be a normalized gluing map, it is necessary and sufficient that:

(1)

\tau
is equal to the identity in the neighborhood of
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}

(2)

\tau
maps the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
to a foliation transverse to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
,

(3)

\tau
maps the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
to a foliation transverse to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
.

Let
\sigma:=\{\theta=0\}
and
n
be the integer such that
\tau([\sigma])=[\sigma]+n[{\mathcal{O}}]
, where
[\cdot]
denotes the homology class of the curve.
We have
\tau(x,\theta)=(x,\theta+f(x)\operatorname{mod}{\mathbb{Z}})
, with
f\colon[-1,1]\to{\mathbb{R}}
a smooth monotone function, equal to
O
on a neighborhood of
-1
, and equal to
n
on a neighborhood of
1
.
According to Remark
2.1.7
there exists a constant
c>0
such that, if
∗
denotes
\mathrm{in}
or
\mathrm{out}
, the tangent directions to the foliations
({\mathcal{G}}^{s,*},{\mathcal{G}}^{u,*})
at a point
p=(x,\theta)\in A
are

T_{(x,\theta)}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,*}\colon dx=cx.d\theta,\noindent\quad T_{(x,\theta)}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,*}\colon dx=-cx.d\theta

–

If
n\geq 0
, we choose the support of
\tau
in a compact set of
A\cap P^{\mathrm{out}}
.
The direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
at the point
p=(x,\theta)
is mapped on the direction
(cx.f^{\prime}(x)+1)dx=cx.d\theta
.
Since
f^{\prime}(x)\geq 0
and
x\geq 0
, the direction of the image is transverse to the direction
dx=-cx.d\theta
tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
at the point
\tau(p)=(x,\theta+f(x))
, which shows that Items
1
and
3
are true.
Item
2
is trivially true because
\tau
is the identity on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
.

–

If
n\leq 0
, we choose the support of
\tau
in a compact set of
A\cap P^{\mathrm{in}}
.
The direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
at the point
p=(x,\theta)
is mapped on the direction
(-cx.f^{\prime}(x)+1).dx=-cx.d\theta
.
Since
f^{\prime}(x)\leq 0
and
x\leq 0
, the direction of the image is transverse to the direction
dx=cx.d\theta
tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
at the point
\tau(p)=(x,\theta+f(x))
, which shows that Items
1
and
2
are true.
Item
3
is trivially true because
\tau
is the identity on
P^{\mathrm{out}}
.

It follows that the triple
(P_{1},X_{1},\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{1})
is a normalized triple strongly isotopic to the triple
(P_{0},X_{0},\varphi_{0})
.∎

3.
Crossing map

In this section
(P,X)
denotes a normalized filled building block.
We refer to Section
1
and
2
for notations, definitions and associated properties.
By definition of a normalized building block (Definition
2.1.4
), each orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
contained in the boundary is provided with a normalized coordinate system which we denote
({\mathcal{V}}_{i},\xi_{i}=(x,y,\theta))
.
For the rest of the proof, we fix
g=\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
a Riemannian metric on a minimal continuation
\tilde{P}
,
adapted to the hyperbolic splitting of the maximal invariant set
\Lambda
, and such that in a normalized coordinate system
({\mathcal{V}}_{i},\xi_{i}=(x,y,\theta))
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
, we have

g=dx^{2}+dy^{2}+d\theta^{2}
.

3.1.
Definition and statement of the main proposition

In this section, we will study some properties of the crossing map
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}
of the flow of
X
from
P^{\mathrm{in}}{}
to
P^{\mathrm{out}}{}
.
For any
p\in P
, let
\tau(p)
be the
exit time
(when well defined) of the orbit of
p
by the flow of
X
, in other words such that
X^{\tau(p)}(p)\in P^{\mathrm{out}}{}
.

Definition 3.1.1

(Crossing map)
.

The map
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}{}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}\to P^{\mathrm{out}}
defined by
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}(p)=X^{\tau(p)}(p)

is a diffeomorphism from
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
to
P^{\mathrm{out}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
, called
the crossing map of
(P,X)
.

Indeed the time
\tau
is well defined if and only if
p
is in the complementary of the stable manifold
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}
of
\Lambda
.
The image of
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}
corresponds to the set of points of
P^{\mathrm{out}}
whose orbit intersects
P^{\mathrm{in}}
, and thus coincides with the complementary of the unstable manifold
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}
of
\Lambda
in
P^{\mathrm{out}}
.
A crossing map of a
{\mathcal{C}}^{1}
-flow between (smooth) surfaces quasi-transverse to the vector field is
{\mathcal{C}}^{1}
.

3.2.
Projective action of the crossing map

Cone fields

3.3.
Expansion of the crossing map

The purpose of this section is to prove the following main proposition, which states that
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}
expands the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
arbitrarily strongly in a small neighborhood of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
, and the analogous result for the inverse map along the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
.

Proposition 3.3.1

(Expansion of the crossing map)
.

(1)

For any
\lambda>1
, there exists a neighborhood
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}
of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
in
P^{\mathrm{in}}
,
such that the crossing map
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}{}
expands by a factor
\lambda
the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
on
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
:

\forall p\in{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}},\quad\forall v\in T_{p}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}},\quad\|(f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}})_{*}v\|>\lambda\|v\|

(2)

For any
\lambda>1
, there exists a neighborhood
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{\lambda}
of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
in
P^{\mathrm{out}}
,
such that the inverse
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}^{-1}
expands by a factor
\lambda
the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
on
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{\lambda}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
:

\forall p\in{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{\lambda}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}},\quad\forall v\in T_{p}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}},\quad\|(f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}^{-1})_{*}v\|>\lambda\|v\|

Remarks

This result is valid for any normalized building block, not necessary filled.
However, in order to have pictures in mind and because it corresponds to our setting, let us describe the crossing map for a filled building block.
The boundary lamination
{\mathcal{L}}
is filling hence the connected components of
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
P^{\mathrm{out}}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
are
strips
, i.e. topological disks whose accessible boundary is formed by two non-compact leaves asymptotic to each other at both end (Definition
1.2.12
).
The map
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}{}
maps a strip
B^{s}\subset P^{\mathrm{in}}
to a strip
B^{u}=f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}(B^{s})\subset P^{\mathrm{out}}{}
(Figure
27
).
If
B^{s}
is a strip of
P^{\mathrm{in}}
, there are two possible cases for each end of
B^{s}
:

(1)

Either the given end of
B^{s}
accumulate on a compact leaf of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
in
P^{\mathrm{in}}

(2)

Either the given end of
B
accumulates on a component of
\partial P^{\mathrm{in}}
, in other words a periodic orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
for the flow of
X
.

Let
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}
be the neighborhood given by Proposition
3.3.1
.

If
B^{s}
is a connected component of
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
, then
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}\cap B^{s}
is a neighborhood of the accessible boundary of
B^{s}
.

(1)

Either we are in the previous case 1), and
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}\cap B^{s}
contains a neighborhood of the corresponding end of
B^{s}
.

(2)

Either we are in the previous case 2), and
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}\cap B^{s}
does not contain any neighborhood of the corresponding end of
B^{s}
.

Up to shrink
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}
, we can suppose it is an adapted neighborhood of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
(Definition
2.4.3
, Item
2
) and we can refer to Figure
18
.

Figure 27
.
Action of the crossing map from a strip
B^{s}\subset P^{\mathrm{in}}
to a strip
B^{u}\subset P^{\mathrm{out}}
.

Proof summary

Let us give the main ideas for the proof of the first item of Proposition
3.3.1
. The second item is symmetrical.

•

We can extend the hyperbolic structure of
\Lambda^{s}
on a small filtrating neighborhood
{\mathcal{U}}
of
\Lambda^{s}
, in the sense that there are directions
E^{ss}
and
E^{uu}
in
{\mathcal{U}}
, invariant by the differential of
X^{t}
in
{\mathcal{U}}
, and which are respectively contracted and expanded in the future (Lemma
3.4.1
).
According to the
\lambda
-lemma (
[
KH95
]
), a vector transverse to the plane
E^{ss}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X
carried by the flow for large enough positive times is exponentially expanded and tends to the strong unstable direction
E^{uu}
.

•

A point in
P^{\mathrm{in}}{}
close to the lamination
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
in a connected component of
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
is close to the stable manifold of the maximal saddle invariant set (Lemma
2.1.12
) thus has an orbit which spends a long time in the neighborhood
{\mathcal{U}}
before intersecting
P^{\mathrm{out}}{}
.
It follows that a vector
v
on a point close to the lamination
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
and far enough from the orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
, tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
, is expanded by the differential of
X^{t}
after a uniformly bounded time.

•

The image of a vector
v
by the differential of the crossing map
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}{}
is the projection
\pi\colon TP\to TP^{\mathrm{out}}
onto the tangent space
TP^{\mathrm{out}}{}
parallel to
{\mathbb{R}}.X
of the vector
X^{\tau}_{*}v
pushed by the flow on
P^{\mathrm{out}}
, i.e.
(f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}})_{*}v=\pi(X^{\tau}_{*}v)

where
\tau
is the exit time of the orbit.
We show that the projection of a vector
v=v^{uu}+v^{X}+v^{ss}\in E^{uu}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X\oplus E^{ss}
on a surface
S
on
{\mathcal{U}}
parallel to
{\mathbb{R}}.X
does not contract the strong unstable component
v^{uu}
(Lemma
3.4.4
).

•

The tricky point occurs in the neighborhood of an orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
.
Indeed a vector
v
tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
at a point
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
very close to an orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
has a strong unstable component
v^{uu}
very small compared to its component
v^{X}
tangent to
{\mathbb{R}}.X
, on which the action of the flow is isometric.
It is therefore necessary to spend a very long time in a hyperbolic neighborhood for the vector to be exponentially expanded by the
X^{t}
flow.
However, the trajectory of an orbit of a point
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
very close to an orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
spends a very long time in a neighborhood of
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
, where the dynamics of the flow is hyperbolic.
We will see that this competition balances.
To do so, we decompose the crossing map
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}
from
P^{\mathrm{in}}
to an “intermediate” surface
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
close to
P^{\mathrm{in}}
and uniformly transverse to the vector field, then from an “intermediate” surface
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
close to
P^{\mathrm{out}}
and uniformly transverse to the vector field.
We study the intermediate maps explicitly in a normalized coordinate system
({\mathcal{V}}_{i},\xi_{i})
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
.

3.4.
Preliminary lemmas

Before proving the proposition, we will need a number of lemmas which we now state and prove.

Hyperbolic filtrating neighborhood

A neighborhood of
\Lambda_{s}
in
P
is said to be
filtrating
if the intersection of this neighborhood with any orbit of the flow of
X
in
P
is connected.
The hyperbolic structure of a locally maximal invariant set extend on small filtrating neighborhood.
More precisely we have:

Lemma 3.4.1
.

There exists a filtrating neighborhood
{\mathcal{U}}
of
\Lambda_{s}
in
P
,
and an
X^{t}
-invariant hyperbolic splitting
{\mathcal{U}}
, still denoted
TP|_{{\mathcal{U}}}=E^{uu}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X\oplus E^{ss}
, which coincides with the hyperbolic splitting on
\Lambda_{s}
.
There exist constants
\lambda_{0}>1
and
C_{0}>0
such that if the orbit of
p\in{\mathcal{U}}
is in
{\mathcal{U}}
at time
t>0
then

•

\forall v\in E^{uu}(p)
,
\|(X^{t})_{*}v\|\geq C_{0}\lambda_{0}^{t}v\|
;

•

\forall v\in E^{ss}(p)
,
\|(X^{t})_{*}v\|\leq C_{0}^{-1}\lambda_{0}^{-t}\|v\|
.

Moreover, the direction
E^{ss}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X
is tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s}
and the direction
E^{uu}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X
is tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.2.5
, there exists a Markov partition
{\mathcal{R}}=(R,\Sigma)
of
\Lambda_{s}
on
\Sigma
, such that the intersection of any orbit of
X
in
P
with the suspension neighborhood
{\mathcal{U}}:=\cup_{t\in[0,1]}X^{t}(R)
is connected (recall that the return time of
R
on
\Sigma
is equal to
1
).
Let
(\zeta^{s},\zeta^{u})
be the pair of
f
-invariant foliations on
\Sigma
, such that
f
uniformly expands the direction tangent to
\zeta^{u}
and uniformly contracts the direction tangent to
\zeta^{s}
.
Let us show that the neighborhood
{\mathcal{U}}
satisfies Lemma
3.4.1
.
According to the properties of the Markov partition, it is a filtrating neighborhood of
\Lambda_{s}
.
We can push the decomposition
T\Sigma=T\zeta^{s}\oplus T\zeta^{u}
on
{\mathcal{U}}
by the differential of the flow for
t\in[0,1]
.
By invariance and hyperbolicity of
f
, they are invariant and respectively contracted and expanded in the future by the flow, and extend the hyperbolic structure of
\Lambda^{s}
on
{\mathcal{U}}
. ∎

We will need the following fact.

Claim 3.4.2
.

The segments of
X
-orbit from
P^{\mathrm{in}}
to
P^{\mathrm{out}}
which never enters
{\mathcal{U}}
have uniformly bounded length.
The segment of
X
-orbit from
P^{\mathrm{in}}
to
{\mathcal{U}}
and from
{\mathcal{U}}
to
P^{\mathrm{out}}
have uniformly bounded length.

Proof.

Let us show the first assertion.
If this were not the case, we would have a sequence of points
p_{n}\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
and increasing times
t_{n}\to+\infty
such that the orbit segments
[p_{n},X^{t_{n}}p_{n}]
are in the complementary of
{\mathcal{U}}
and
X^{t_{n}}p_{n}\in P^{\mathrm{out}}
.
Up to take a subsequence the sequence
(p_{n})_{n}
converges to a point
q
in the closure
\overline{P^{\mathrm{in}}}
.
Suppose that there exists a time
T>0
such that
X^{T}(q)
is in the interior of
{\mathcal{U}}
.
By continuity of flow, for an arbitrarily large
n
, the distance
\operatorname{dist}(X^{T}(p_{n}),X^{T}(q))
is arbitrarily small, and
X^{T}(p_{n})
is in
{\mathcal{U}}
for
n
large enough, which is a contradiction as soon as
t_{n}>T
.
Suppose now that there exists a time
T>0
such that
X^{T}(q)
crosses
P^{\mathrm{out}}
.
The distance
\operatorname{dist}(X^{T}(p_{n}),X^{T}(q))
is arbitrarily small, but a point in
P
arbitrarily close to
P^{\mathrm{out}}{}
has a positive orbit which exits
P
in arbitrarily small time, which is a contradiction for
t_{n}
big in front of
T
.
Suppose now that the positive orbit of
q
accumulate on an attractor in
{\mathfrak{A}}
. Hence
q
belongs to the basin of the attractor, which is an open set, and so do
p_{n}
for
n
large enough, which is a contradiction.
We deduce that the positive orbit of
q
neither enters
{\mathcal{U}}
, neither exits through
P^{\mathrm{out}}{}
, and neither accumulates on an attractor.
This is impossible.
The other assertions are shown similarly.
∎

Remark 3.4.3
.

We can push the hyperbolic splitting of
{\mathcal{U}}
from Lemma
3.4.1
in the future along the orbits of
{\mathcal{U}}
exiting through
P^{\mathrm{out}}
and in the past along the orbits of
{\mathcal{U}}
entering through
P^{\mathrm{in}}
because the neighborhood
{\mathcal{U}}
is filtrating.
Moreover, the hyperbolic properties are still satisfied along these orbits segments according to Claim
3.4.2
.

Projection of a center-unstable vector onto a transverse surface

We will need the following lemma, which states that the projection parallel to
{\mathbb{R}}.X
of a vector
v\in E^{uu}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X
onto a surface
S
transverse to
X
, at a point
p\in{\mathcal{U}}
has a norm essentially less than the norm of the strong unstable component in
E^{uu}
of the vector
v
.

Lemma 3.4.4
.

Let
S
be a surface embedded in
P
transverse to the vector field
X
.
Let
p\in S\cap{\mathcal{U}}
and
\pi_{p}\colon T_{p}P\to T_{p}S
be the projection on
T_{p}S
parallel to
{\mathbb{R}}.X
in the neighborhood
{\mathcal{U}}
provided with the hyperbolic splitting.
There exists a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
such that for any vector
v=v^{uu}+v^{X}\in E^{uu}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X
tangent to the center unstable bundle on a point
p\in S
, we have

\|\pi_{p}(v)\|\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\|v^{uu}\|.

Proof.

Recall the following

Claim 3.4.5
.

Let
F,G
be two vector planes in
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
, and
H
be a supplementary line of
F
and
G
, and
\|\cdot\|_{2}
the Euclidean norm.
Let
\pi\colon F\to G
be the projection of
F
onto
G
parallel to
H
, linear invertible. Then we have

\dfrac{\|\pi(v)\|_{2}}{\|v\|_{2}}=\left|\dfrac{\sin\measuredangle(H,{\mathbb{R}}.v)}{\sin\measuredangle(H,{\mathbb{R}}.\pi(v))}\right|.

If
\|\cdot\|_{2}
is the Euclidean norm associated to a basis of tangent vectors
(\partial_{uu},\partial_{ss},X)
where
\partial_{uu}\in E^{uu}
and
\partial_{ss}\in E^{ss}
,
then we have

\frac{\|\pi(v)\|_{2}}{\|v\|_{2}}\geq\sin(\measuredangle(v,X))=\frac{\|v^{uu}\|_{2}}{\|v\|_{2}}

By equivalence of norms, we deduce the existence of the constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
which satisfies the lemma.
∎

Angles between the foliations and vector field on
\partial P

The vector field
X
becomes more and more tangent to the surface
{\partial P}
as we approach the set of periodic orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
.
Similarly the
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
laminations on
P^{\mathrm{in}}{}
are increasingly tangent to each other as one approaches the set
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}\subset{\partial P}
.
Using the expression for the vector field
X
and the foliations in the normalized coordinates
({\mathcal{V}}_{i},\xi_{i})
, we show that the closing velocity of the angles is essentially the same, of the order of the distance to the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
.
This property will be useful in the rest of the proof.

Lemma 3.4.6
.

There exists a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
such that for all
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{}
,

(1)

\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}\operatorname{dist}(p,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})\leq\measuredangle({\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}},{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}})_{p}\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\operatorname{dist}(p,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})

(2)

\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}\operatorname{dist}(p,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})\leq\measuredangle(X,P^{\mathrm{in}}{})_{p}\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\operatorname{dist}(p,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})

(3)

\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}\operatorname{dist}(p,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})\leq\measuredangle(X,{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}})_{p}\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\operatorname{dist}(p,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})

Proof.

It is sufficient to check this in the neighborhood of a periodic orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
in the
({\mathcal{V}}_{i},\xi_{i}=(x,y,\theta))
.
Let
(\partial_{x},\partial_{y},\partial_{\theta})
be the basis of vector fields associated with the coordinates
(x,y,\theta)
.
Recall that the norm
\|\cdot\|
on
P
coincides with the Euclidean norm in these coordinates.
By definition of a normalized block (Definition
2.1.4
and Corollary
2.1.6
), the surface
P^{\mathrm{in}}{}
has local equation
x=y
and, if
c:=\ln(2)
, the vector field
X
in these coordinates is

X(x,y,\theta)=-cx\partial_{x}+cy\partial_{y}+\partial_{\theta}.

The neighborhoods
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
are contained in the neighborhood
{\mathcal{U}}
provided with a hyperbolic splitting of the tangent space
TP|_{{\mathcal{U}}}=E^{uu}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X\oplus E^{ss}
(Lemma
3.4.1
), up to restrict
{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
, which we will always suppose.
The direction
E^{uu}
is generated by
\partial_{y}
and the direction
E^{ss}
is generated by
\partial_{x}
.
Note that the distance from a point
p=(x,x,\theta)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{}
to
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
is equal to
x
.
We show each of the three items.

(1)

The foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
is generated by the vector field
\partial_{s}=cx\,(\partial_{x}+\partial_{y})+\partial_{\theta}
and the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
is generated by the vector field
\partial_{u}=-cx\,(\partial_{x}+\partial_{y})+\partial_{\theta}
(Figure
28
).
As the couple
(\partial_{x}+\partial_{y},\partial_{\theta})
forms an orthogonal basis on
P^{\mathrm{in}}{}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
, the angle between the two tangent directions at a point
p=(x,x,\theta)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{}
is proportional to
x
, hence to the distance from
p
to
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
.

Figure 28
.
Coordinates of the vector fields
\partial_{u}
and
\partial_{s}
generating the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
at a point
p=(x,x,\theta)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}

(2)

The vector field
X
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}{}
is

X(x,x,\theta)=cx\left(\partial_{y}-\partial_{x}\right)+\partial_{\theta}
.
It is orthogonal to
\partial_{x}+\partial_{y}
at any point of the annulus and the angle between
X
and
\partial_{\theta}
at a point
p=(x,x,\theta)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
is proportional to
x
, thus to the distance from
p
to
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
(Figure
29
).

Figure 29
.
Coordinates of the vector
X(p)
at point
p=(x,x,\theta)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}

(3)

The direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
at the point
p=(x,x,\theta)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
is generated by the vector field

\partial^{u}=-cx\,(\partial_{x}+\partial_{y})+\partial_{\theta}=-2cx\,\partial_{y}+X(x,x,\theta)

Since the vector
X
is uniformly transverse to
\partial_{y}
, we deduce that the angle between
X
and the vector
\partial^{u}
at a point
p=(x,x,\theta)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
is proportional to
x
, hence to the distance from
p
to
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
.

∎

3.5.
Decomposition of the crossing map

In order to explain the crossing of an orbit close to the set
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
,
we will consider surfaces
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}{}
and
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}{}
which coincide with
P^{\mathrm{in}}{}
and
P^{\mathrm{out}}{}
except in the neighborhood of the orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
and are uniformly transverse to the vector field
X
.
Let
{\mathcal{V}}_{*}=\cup_{i}{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
be the union of the normalized neighborhoods of the orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
in a minimal continuation
\tilde{P}
.
The expression of the flow in the coordinate system
({\mathcal{V}}_{i},\xi_{i}=(x,y,\theta)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}})
is

X^{t}(x,y,\theta)=(2^{-t}x,2^{t}y,\theta+t)
,
and the boundary of
P
is identified with
\{x=y\}
(Definition
2.1.4
).
Let
r_{0}>0
so that the point
(r_{0},r_{0},0)
is in the image of
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
in coordinates
\xi_{i}
for all
i
.

Notation 3.5.1
.

Let
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
be a smooth surface such that

\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}{}
coincides with
P^{\mathrm{in}}{}
outside
{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
,

\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}{}=\{y=r_{0},x>0\}
close to
x=0
in
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
, and
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}{}
