is uniformly transverse to the vector field
X
.
Similarly, let
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
be a smooth surface such that
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}{}
coincides with
P^{\mathrm{out}}{}
outside
{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
,
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}{}=\{x=-r_{0},y<0\}
close to
y=0
in
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
,
and
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}{}
is uniformly transverse to the vector field
X
.
We refer to Figure
30
.

Figure 30
.
Decomposition of the crossing map
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}{}
in a normalized neighborhood
({\mathcal{V}}_{i},\xi_{i})
.

Remark 3.5.2
.

•

Each connected component of
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
(respectively
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
) is either a closed surface or the interior of a surface whose boundary is formed by closed curves in the unstable (respectively stable) manifold of periodic orbits of
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
.

•

Each point on
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
has a past orbit which enters via
P^{\mathrm{in}}
, each point on
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
has a future orbit which exits via
P^{\mathrm{out}}
.
The time is not bounded because one can be arbitrarily close to the boundary of
P^{\mathrm{in}}
and
P^{\mathrm{out}}
, in which case the orbit spends an arbitrarily long time in the neighborhood of an orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
before intersecting
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
or
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
.

•

By transversality of
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
with the vector field
X
, the lamination
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}
induces a lamination of dimension 1 on
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
and the lamination
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}
induces a lamination of dimension 1 on
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
.
Denote
\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}={\mathcal{W}}^{s}\cap\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}={\mathcal{W}}^{u}\cap\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
.

We write

f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}=\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out}}\circ\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in}}

where

\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}{}\to\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
,
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\colon\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}\to\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}{\smallsetminus}\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
and
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out}}{}\colon\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}\to P^{\mathrm{out}}

are the crossing maps for the flow of
X
between the corresponding transverse surfaces (see Figure
30
).
The composition map is well defined on
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
with image in
P^{\mathrm{out}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
.
The local crossings
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in}}
and
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out}}
are trivial outside the normalized neighborhoods
{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
, on which we have explicit expressions.
The crossing map
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}
from
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
to
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
is a crossing map between two sections uniformly transverse to the vector field
X
.

Intermediate transit time

For any
p
in
P
, recall that
\tau(p)
is the exit time (if well defined) of the orbit of the point
p
on
P^{\mathrm{out}}{}
, in other words such that
X^{\tau(p)}(p)\in P^{\mathrm{out}}{}
. We also define

•

\hat{\tau}
the time of intersection of the orbit (positive or negative) of a point with the surface
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
(when it is well defined), in other words such that
X^{\hat{\tau}(p)}(p)\in\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
.
This time is unique because an orbit which intersects
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
never passes through
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
again.
This time is well defined on the complementary of
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}
in
P
.
Indeed, any point on
P
has an orbit which intersects
P^{\mathrm{out}}
.
Moreover, any orbit that intersects
P^{\mathrm{out}}
intersects
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
.

•

\tau_{\mathrm{in}}
the time of intersection of the orbit (positive or negative) of a point with the surface
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
(when it is well defined), in other words such that
X^{\tau_{\mathrm{in}}(p)}(p)\in\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
.
This time is unique because an orbit which intersects
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
never passes through
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
again.
This time is well defined on the complementary of
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}
in
P
.
Indeed, any point on
P
has an orbit which intersects
P^{\mathrm{in}}
.
Moreover, any orbit that intersects
P^{\mathrm{in}}
intersects
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
.

3.6.
Proof of Proposition
3.3.1

We are now able to show Proposition
3.3.1
.
We are reduced to the study of the expanding effects on the vectors tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u}
of the three maps
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in}}
,
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}
,
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out}}
.
First, we show that
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in}}
does not contract too much the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u}
(Lemma
3.6.1
) on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
.
In a second step, we show that the map
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out}}\circ\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}
arbitrarily strongly expands the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u}
in a small neighborhood of
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}
on
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
(Lemma
3.6.2
and
3.6.3
).
In a conclusion, we assemble the previous results to show that the crossing map
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}
expands arbitrarily strongly the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u}
in a small neighborhood of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
.

Step 1: Lower bound for the contraction of
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in}}
in the unstable direction

Lemma 3.6.1
.

The derivative of
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in}}
is uniformly bounded from below in the direction tangent to the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
.
In other words, there exists a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
such that

\forall p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{},\noindent\quad\forall v\in T_{p}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}},\noindent\quad\|(\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in}})_{*}v\|\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\|v\|

This lemma tells us that the competition balances between the expansion of an unstable vector
v\in E^{uu}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X
under the effect of the flow, and the contraction under the effect of the projection on the surface
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
.
More precisely, a point on
P^{\mathrm{in}}{}
close to
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
has an orbit whose local crossing from
P^{\mathrm{in}}{}
to
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}{}
is not trivial.
A vector tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
at this point has a small strong unstable component compared to its tangent component at
X
.
The projection parallel to
X
of such a vector pushed by the flow on the transverse surface
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}{}
has a strong contracting effect on the tangent component to
X
and a uniformly bounded effect on the strong unstable component.
But the time spent in the neighborhood of the hyperbolic periodic orbit during the transition from
P^{\mathrm{in}}
to
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
is long enough for the flow to expand the strong unstable component sufficiently so that the projection on
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}{}
of the vector pushed by the flow is not (too) contracting.

Proof.

We have
(\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in}})_{*}=\pi\circ X^{\tau_{\mathrm{in}}}_{*}

where
\pi\colon TP\to T\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
is the projection on the tangent space of
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
parallel to
{\mathbb{R}}.X
.
The map
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in}}
is equal to the identity everywhere outside
{\mathcal{V}}_{*}=\cup_{i}{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
, there is nothing to show.
Let us show the property on
{\mathcal{V}}
, which admits an
X^{t}
-invariant hyperbolic splitting.
Let
({\mathcal{V}}_{i},\xi_{i}=(x,y,\theta))
be a normalized coordinate system of
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
, provided with the hyperbolic splitting
E^{uu}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X\oplus E^{ss}
of the tangent space (Lemma
3.4.1
).
The flow in these coordinates is

X^{t}(x,y,\theta)=(2^{-t}\,x,2^{t}\,y,\theta+t)
.
The lemma is based on the following two points.

(1)

Let
v=v^{uu}+v^{X}\in E^{uu}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X
a tangent vector to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
above a point
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
.
Then there exists a uniform constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}
such that

\|v^{uu}\|\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\,\operatorname{dist}(p,{\mathcal{O}}_{i})\,\|v\|

(2)

The transit time
\tau_{\mathrm{in}}
is well defined on
P^{\mathrm{in}}{}
and satisfied :
there exists a uniform constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}{}>0
such that for any
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{}
,

\tau_{\mathrm{in}}(p)\geq\log_{2}(\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\operatorname{dist}(p,{\mathcal{O}}_{i})^{-1}).

A vector in
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
pushed by the (linear) flow during a time
\tau_{\mathrm{in}}(p)
has thus its strong unstable component expanded by a factor bounded below by
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\operatorname{dist}(p,{\mathcal{O}}_{i})^{-1}
.

The first point is a direct consequence of Lemma
3.4.6
, Item
3
, which states that the angle
\measuredangle(X,{\mathcal{G}}^{u}_{\mathrm{in}})
is of order of the distance to the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
.
For the second point, the orbit of a point
p=(x,x,\theta)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{}
intersects the section
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}{}
at time
\tau_{\mathrm{in}}(p)=-\log_{2}\left(2x\right)
.
We conclude by noticing that
x
is equal to the distance
\operatorname{dist}(p,{\mathcal{O}}_{i})
.

Let
v=v^{uu}+v^{X}\in E^{uu}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X
a tangent vector to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
above a point
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{}
.
Then up to change the constant at each line, we have

\begin{array}[]{l@{\hspace{2cm}}r}\|(\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in}})_{*}v\|=\|\pi\circ(X^{\tau_{\mathrm{in}}(p)})_{*}v\|\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\,\|(X^{\tau_{\mathrm{in}}(p)})_{*}v^{uu}\|\hfil\hskip 56.9055pt&\text{(Lemma\penalty\ \ref{lem: projection bounded effect on strong unstable})}\\
\|(\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in}})_{*}v\|\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\,2^{\tau_{\mathrm{in}}(p)}\|v^{uu}\|\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\,\dfrac{1}{\operatorname{dist}(p,{\mathcal{O}}_{i})}\|v^{uu}\|\hfil\hskip 56.9055pt&\text{(Item\penalty\ \ref{it: crossing time Pin tPin})}\\
\|(\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in}})_{*}v\|\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\,\|v\|\hfil\hskip 56.9055pt&\text{(Item\penalty\ \ref{it: relation uu X on Pin})}\end{array}

∎

Step 2: Expansion of the composition
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out}}\circ\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}
in the unstable direction

We show that the composition
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out}}\circ\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}
expands arbitrarily strongly a vector tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u}\cap\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
in a small neighborhood of
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}\cap\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
.
For this we study the expansion property of
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}
on the one hand (Lemma
3.6.2
) and of
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out}}
on the other hand (Lemma
3.6.3
).

Lemma 3.6.2
.

There exists a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
such that for any
p\in\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{W}}^{s}
and for any vector
v
tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u}\cap\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
on
p
,

\|(\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}})_{*}v\|\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\,\lambda_{0}^{\hat{\tau}(p)}\|v\|
.

Proof.

We have

(\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}})_{*}=\hat{\pi}\circ X^{\hat{\tau}}_{*}

where
\hat{\pi}\colon TP\to T\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
is the projection on the tangent space of
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}{}
parallel to
{\mathbb{R}}.X
.
This lemma is based on the following key points.
Let
{\mathcal{U}}
be the filtrating neighborhood, equipped with a hyperbolic splitting (Lemma
3.4.1
).
Let
p\in\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}{}
, disjoint from
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}
, and
v
be a vector tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u}\cap\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
at point
p
.
There are two situations.

(1)

Either the orbit of
p
never enters
{\mathcal{U}}
.
The orbit of
p
enters through
P^{\mathrm{in}}
and escapes through
P^{\mathrm{out}}
at time
\tau(p)
, and the total length, greater than
\tau(p)
, is uniformly bounded by a constant say
T_{0}
from Claim
3.4.2
.
The set
P^{\mathrm{out}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{U}}
is contained in
P{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
, where the surfaces
P^{\mathrm{out}}
and
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
coincide.
We deduce that the time
\hat{\tau}=\tau
is uniformly bounded on the set of point of
\hat{}P^{\mathrm{in}}
whose orbit never crosses
{\mathcal{U}}
.
Then

(a)

The action of the differential
X^{\hat{\tau}}_{*}
is uniformly bounded,

(b)

For any vector
v
tangent to
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
, the angle between the vector
w=X^{\hat{\tau}}_{*}(v)
and the direction tangent to
X
is uniformly bounded from below,

(c)

If the angle between a vector
w
of norm 1 and the direction tangent to
X
at a point
p\in\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
is uniformly bounded from below, then the norm of the projected vector
\hat{\pi}(w)\in T\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
is uniformly bounded.

Item
1b
is a consequence of the uniform transversality of
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
to
X
, and Item
1c
is a consequence of the uniform transversality of
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
to
X
.
We deduce that there exists a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{\prime}>0
uniform in
p
such that

\displaystyle\|d_{p}\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}(v)\|

\displaystyle=\|\hat{\pi}_{p}\circ d_{p}X^{\hat{\tau}(p)}(v)\|

\displaystyle\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{\prime}\|d_{p}X^{\hat{\tau}(p)}(v)\|

(Item
1b
and
1c
)

\displaystyle\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{\prime}\|v\|

(Item
1a
)

Lemma
3.6.2
is satisfied by setting
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}=\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{\prime}(\lambda_{0}^{T_{0}})^{-1}
.

(2)

Either the orbit of
p
enters
{\mathcal{U}}
.
The orbit enters through
P^{\mathrm{in}}
and exit through
P^{\mathrm{out}}
, hence according to
3.4.3
, we can extend the splitting
E^{uu}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X\oplus E^{ss}
along the orbit, and the action of the flow is still uniformly hyperbolic because the length of segment of orbit disjoint from
{\mathcal{U}}
are uniformly bounded.
The tangent vector
v\in T_{p}\cap T_{p}{\mathcal{G}}^{u}
decomposes into the sum

v=v^{uu}+v^{X}\in E^{uu}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X
.
By transversality of
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
with
X
, there exists a uniform constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
such that
\|v^{uu}\|\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\,\|v\|
.
By hyperbolicity, the direction
E^{uu}
is preserved and the component
v^{uu}
is expanded by the differential of the flow along the orbit of
p
, i.e. we have
w=X^{\hat{\tau}}_{*}(v)=w^{uu}+w^{X}\in E^{uu}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X
, and
\|w^{uu}\|\geq C_{0}\lambda_{0}^{\hat{\tau}}\|v^{uu}\|\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\lambda_{0}^{\hat{\tau}}\|v\|
.
Moreover, we know from Lemma
3.4.4
that the norm of the projection
\hat{\pi}(w)
is essentially bounded from below by the strong unstable component
w^{uu}
, hence

\displaystyle\|d_{p}\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}(v)\|

\displaystyle=\|\hat{\pi}_{p}\circ d_{p}X^{\hat{\tau}(p)}(v)\|=\|\hat{\pi}_{p}(w^{uu}+w^{X})\|

\displaystyle\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\|w^{uu}\|

\displaystyle\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\lambda_{0}^{\hat{\tau}}\|v\|.

∎

Finally, we show that the exit crossing map
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out}}{}
exponentially expands the unstable vectors as a function of the transit time from
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
to
P^{\mathrm{out}}
.

Lemma 3.6.3
.

There exists a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
such that for any
p\in\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}{}
, for any vector
v
tangent to the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u}\cap\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
on
p
, we have
\|(\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out}}{})_{*}v\|\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\,\lambda_{0}^{\tau(p)}\|v\|
.

In this sense, there is no competition between expansion and contraction.
Indeed,
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out}}
is a crossing map of the flow starting from a surface
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
uniformly transverse to
X
.
The projection on the destination
P^{\mathrm{out}}{}
will not be too contracting.
Moreover, the transport by the flow in the neighborhood of
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
contributes to exponentially expand an unstable vector.

Proof.

We have
(\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out}}{})_{*}=\pi\circ X^{\tau}_{*}

where
\pi
is the projection on
P^{\mathrm{out}}{}
parallel to
{\mathbb{R}}.X
, and the transit time
\tau
is well defined everywhere on
P^{\mathrm{out}}
.
The argument is the same as the proof of the previous lemma.
We stand in a domain where the map
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out}}
is not trivial, in other words a neighborhood
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}\subset{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
of a periodic orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
.
There exists an invariant hyperbolic splitting
E^{uu}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X\oplus E^{ss}
on
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
.
For
p\in\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
, a vector tangent to
v\in T_{p}\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap T_{p}{\mathcal{G}}^{u}
decomposes into the sum

v=v^{uu}+v^{X}\in E^{uu}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X
.
By transversality of
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}
with
X
, there exists a uniform constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
such that

\|v^{uu}\|\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\,\|v\|
.
The orbit of
p
is entirely contained in
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
, and the intersection time
\tau
with
P^{\mathrm{out}}
is strictly positive.
By hyperbolicity, the direction
E^{uu}
is preserved and the component
v^{uu}
is expanded by the differential
X^{\tau}_{*}
of the flow along the orbit of
p
, i.e. we have
w=X^{\tau}_{*}(v)=w^{uu}+w^{X}\in E^{uu}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X
, and
\|w^{uu}\|\geq C_{0}\lambda_{0}^{\tau}\|v^{uu}\|\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\lambda_{0}^{\tau}\|v\|
.
Moreover, we know from Lemma
3.4.4
that the norm of the projection
\hat{\pi}(w)
is essentially bounded below by the strong unstable component
w^{uu}
, hence

\displaystyle\|d_{p}\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out}}(v)\|

\displaystyle=\|\hat{\pi}_{p}\circ d_{p}X^{\tau(p)}(v)\|=\|\hat{\pi}_{p}(w^{uu}+w^{X})\|

\displaystyle\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\|w^{uu}\|

\displaystyle\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\lambda_{0}^{\tau}\|v\|

∎

Conclusion : Expansion of the crossing map
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}
in the unstable direction

Proof of Proposition
3.3.1
.

.
We show the first item.
Let
\lambda>1
, and
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
be constants satisfying Lemmas
3.6.1
,
3.6.2
and
3.6.3
.

Claim 3.6.4
.

There exists
\hat{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}
a neighborhood of
\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}={\mathcal{W}}^{s}\cap\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
on
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
such that the orbit of any point
p\in\hat{\mathcal{W}}_{\lambda}
is defined for a time at least equal to

(3.1)

T_{\lambda}=\log_{\lambda_{0}}(\lambda)-3\log_{\lambda_{0}}(\operatorname{\textit{cst}})

Proof.

This neighborhood exists because a point arbitrarily close to
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}
has an orbit which spends an arbitrarily long time in the neighborhood of
\Lambda
, thus in the minimal continuation
\tilde{P}
, whose boundary is disjoint from
\Lambda
.
Moreover, an orbit of a point in
\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}
defined at time
T
in
\tilde{P}
is defined at time
T^{\prime}\geq\frac{T}{2}
in
P
.
Indeed, the complementary
\tilde{P}{\smallsetminus}P
is contained in the neighborhood
{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
by definition of a minimal continuation (Definition
1.4.3
), and it is clear that an orbit that enters a neighborhood
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
spends a time greater than half of
T
in
P
, which concludes.
∎

Let
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}=(\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}(\hat{{\mathcal{W}}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda})
.
This is a neighborhood of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
in
P^{\mathrm{in}}
(Figure
31
).
Let

p\in{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
,
q=\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in}}(p)\in\hat{P}^{\mathrm{in}}{}
and
s=\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}(q)\in\hat{P}^{\mathrm{out}}{}
.
Let
v
be a vector tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
at
p
.
Let us note that
\tau(s)=\tau(q)-\hat{\tau}(q)
and
\tau(q)\geq T_{\lambda}
by Claim
3.6.4
.
Then

\begin{array}[]{lcl@{\hspace{1cm}}r}\|(f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}})_{*}v\|&=&\|(\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out}}{}\circ\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in}})_{*}v\|\hfil\hskip 28.45274pt\\
&\geq&\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\,\lambda_{0}^{\tau(s)}\,\|(\hat{f}_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in}})_{*}v\|\hfil\hskip 28.45274pt&\text{(Lemma\penalty\ \ref{lem: expansion exit crossing})}\\
&\geq&\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{2}\,\lambda_{0}^{\tau(q)-\hat{\tau}(q)}\,\lambda_{0}^{\hat{\tau}(q)}\,\|(\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in}})_{*}v\|\hfil\hskip 28.45274pt&\text{(Lemma\penalty\ \ref{lem: expansion middle crossing})}\\
&\geq&\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{3}\,\lambda_{0}^{T_{\lambda}}\|v\|\hfil\hskip 28.45274pt&\text{(Lemma\penalty\ \ref{lem: non-contraction of enter crossing})}\\
&\geq&\lambda\,\|v\|\hfil\hskip 28.45274pt&\text{(according to Equation\penalty\ \eqref{eq: T_lambda})}\end{array}

This concludes the proof of the first item of Proposition
3.3.1
. The second item is completely symmetrical.

fact

Figure 31
.

∎

4.
Spread the hyperbolicity

In the previous section we have shown that the expansion of the crossing map
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}{}\to P^{\mathrm{out}}{}
in the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
is arbitrarily strong close to the lamination
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
(Proposition
3.3.1
).
We refer to the beginning of Section
3
for a reminder of the notations.
We will now “spread” this expansion.
The goal is that the map
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}
expands the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
, not only in the neighborhood of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
, but almost all over
P^{\mathrm{in}}
.
This spreading is done with a diffeomorphism
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\colon{\partial P}\to{\partial P}
, supported on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
, which we can think of as a change of coordinates on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
.
The diffeomorphism
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
will depend on three parameters
\lambda,\epsilon,\delta
.
Let us present them informally:

•

\lambda>1
is a lower bound of the expansion factor of the composition
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
in the direction
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
outside a neighborhood of the orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
;

•

\epsilon>0
measures the perturbation of the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
induced by the action of
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
;

•

\delta>0
is the size of the neighborhood of the orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
on which we no longer control the expansion of the composition
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
.

In an analogous way, the expansion of the inverse crossing map
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}^{-1}\colon P^{\mathrm{out}}{}\to P^{\mathrm{in}}{}
in the direction
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
is arbitrarily strong close to the lamination
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
(Proposition
3.3.1
).
The symmetrical operation consists in “spreading” the expansion of
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}^{-1}
in the direction
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
far from the lamination
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
, by means of a diffeomorphism
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}:{\partial P}\to{\partial P}
supported in
P^{\mathrm{out}}
, which one can think of as a change of coordinates on
P^{\mathrm{out}}
.
These two symmetrical steps must be done in such a way that one does not destroy the other, in other words so that the composition
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\circ f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
continues to expand the direction
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
and its inverse continues to expand the direction
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
.
This will be possible thanks to the control of the perturbation of the foliation by the diffeomorphisms
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
.

Let us translate this result into the framework of the gluing procedure of the block
(P,X)
.
If
\varphi
is a normalized gluing map of the block
(P,X)
, then :

•

Replacing
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}
by
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\circ f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
amounts to replacing the gluing map
\varphi
by
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\circ\varphi\circ\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
.
Indeed the first return map of the flow of
X_{\varphi}
in the glued manifold
P/\varphi
on the projection of the surface
\partial P
is the composition of the crossing map
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}\to P^{\mathrm{out}}
and the gluing map
\varphi
.
If
\psi=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\circ\varphi\circ\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
is another gluing map, then the first-return map of
X_{\psi}
on
\partial P
is (up to conjugate) the map
\varphi\circ(\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\circ f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\psi^{\mathrm{in}})
.

•

Since
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
have their support contained in the complementary of a neighborhood of the orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
, the composition
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\circ\varphi\circ\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
is a dynamical gluing map, because it satisfies Item
1
of Definition
2.1.13
.
Moreover, the properties of
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
imply that the composition
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\circ\varphi\circ\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
is isotopic to
\varphi
among the strongly quasi-transverse gluing maps, which implies that the triples are strongly isotopic (Definition
1.4.9
)

4.1.
Statement of the main proposition

We will use cone fields on the surface
\partial P
.
We will talk about the slope of a cone field on a surface relatively to a pair of transverse foliations on the surface (Figure
32
).
Formally:

Definition 4.1.1

(Cones)
.

Let
S
be a surface equipped with a Riemannian norm
\|\cdot\|
, and
{\mathcal{G}}^{1}
and
{\mathcal{G}}^{2}
be two transverse foliation on
S
, and
K\geq 0
.

•

We say that
C\in T_{p}S
is a (closed)
(K,{\mathcal{G}}^{1}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{2})
-cone
if

C=\{v_{1}+v_{2}\in T_{p}{\mathcal{G}}^{1}\oplus T_{p}{\mathcal{G}}^{2}\mid\|v_{2}\|\leq K\|v_{1}\|\}
8
8

8

The order is important: a
(K,{\mathcal{G}}^{1}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{2})
-cone contains the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{1}
and does not contain the tangent direction to
{\mathcal{G}}^{2}
.

where
T_{p}{\mathcal{G}}^{1}\oplus T_{p}{\mathcal{G}}^{2}
is the direct sum of the transverse directions tangent respectively to the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{1}
and
{\mathcal{G}}^{2}
on
p
.
We say that
K
is
the slope of
C
.

•

The
interior of a cone
is the set

\operatorname{int}C:=\{v_{1}+v_{2}\in T_{p}{\mathcal{G}}^{1}\oplus T_{p}{\mathcal{G}}^{2}\mid\|v_{2}\|<K\|v_{1}\|\}\cup\{O\}
.

Le be a cone field
C=\{C(p)\in T_{p}S\}_{p\in S}
.

•

We say that
C
is a
(K(p),{\mathcal{G}}^{1}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{2})
-cone field if each
C(p)
is a
(K(p),{\mathcal{G}}^{1}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{2})
-cone on
T_{p}S
.

•

If
K(p)=K
is constant, we will say that
C
is a
(K,{\mathcal{G}}^{1}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{2})
-cone field.

•

The
(1,{\mathcal{G}}^{1}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{2})
-cone field is called the
bisector
cone field of
({\mathcal{G}}^{1}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{2})
.

Figure 32
.
A
(K,{\mathcal{G}}^{1}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{2})
-cone field

The main result of the chapter is the following.

Proposition 4.1.2

(Spreading of the expansion)
.

For any
\lambda>1
,
\epsilon>0
and
\delta>0
, there exists a diffeomorphism
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}\colon{\partial P}\to{\partial P}
such that

(1)

\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
is the identity outside a compact contained in a finite number of connected components of
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
,

(2)

\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
preserves the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
leaf-to-leaf,

(3)

\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
slightly perturb the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
: the direction tangent to
(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}^{-1}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
lies inside a
(\epsilon,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}/{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field,

(4)

the derivative of
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
in the direction
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
is uniformly bounded by a constant depending only on
\lambda
: for all
\lambda>1
, there exists
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}=\operatorname{\textit{cst}}(\lambda)>0
, such that for all
{\epsilon,\delta}{}>0
we have

\forall p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{},\noindent\quad\forall v\in T_{p}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}},\noindent\quad\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}\,<\frac{\|\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}v\|}{\|v\|}<\operatorname{\textit{cst}}

(5)

if
{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}\subset{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
denotes the
\delta
-neighborhood of the periodic orbits of
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
, then the composition
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
expands by a factor
\lambda
the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
in the complementary of
{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
:

\forall p\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}){\mathcal{V}}_{\delta},\noindent\quad\forall v\in T_{p}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}},\noindent\quad\|(f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}v\|\geq\|\lambda\|

(6)

\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
does not depend on
\delta
on the complementary of
{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
:
for any
\lambda>1
,
\epsilon>0
, the family of diffeomorphisms
\{\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}\}_{\delta>0}
coincide on
{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
.

Figure 33
.
Action of the composition
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}

We refer to Figure
33
to illustrate the properties of
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
.
We state the following symmetric result which is proved in an analogous way.

Proposition 4.1.3

(Spreading of the expansion)
.

For any
\lambda>1
,
\epsilon>0
and
\delta>0
, there exists a diffeomorphism
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}=\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}\colon{\partial P}\to{\partial P}
such that

(1)

\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
is the identity outside a compact contained in a finite number of connected components of
P^{\mathrm{out}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
,

(2)

\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
preserves the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
leaf-to-leaf,

(3)

\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
slightly perturb the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
: the direction tangent to
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
lies inside a
(\epsilon,{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}/,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}})
-cone field,

(4)

the derivative of
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
in the direction
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
is uniformly bounded by a constant depending only on
\lambda
: for all
\lambda>1
, there exists
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}=\operatorname{\textit{cst}}(\lambda)>0
, such that for all
{\epsilon,\delta}{}>0
we have

\forall p\in P^{\mathrm{out}}{},\noindent\quad\forall v\in T_{p}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}},\noindent\quad\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}\,<\frac{\|\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{*}v\|}{\|v\|}<\operatorname{\textit{cst}}

(5)

if
{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}\subset{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
denotes the
\delta
-neighborhood of the periodic orbits of
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
, then the composition
(\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\circ f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}})^{-1}
expands by a factor
\lambda
the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
on
P^{\mathrm{out}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
in the complementary of
{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
:

\forall p\in(P^{\mathrm{out}}{}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}){\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta},\noindent\quad\forall v\in T_{p}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}},\noindent\quad\|(\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\circ f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}})^{-1}_{*}v\|\geq\lambda\|v\|

(6)

\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
does not depend on
\delta
on the complementary of
{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
:
for any
\lambda>1
,
\epsilon>0
, the family of diffeomorphisms
\{\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}\}_{\delta>0}
coincide on
{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
.

We follow closely the proof of
[
BBY17
, Proposition 6.2]
to spread the expansion on the interior of the strips.
The difference is that the strips in our blocks have ends which can accumulate on periodic orbits.
The spreading in the neighborhood of a strip end which accumulates on a periodic orbit will have to be controlled, which justifies the new parameter
\delta
.

Construction of the diffeomorphism on a strip

In the previous section, we proved (Proposition
3.3.1
) that for any
\lambda>1
, there exists a neighborhood
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}
of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
such that the crossing map
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}{}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}\to P^{\mathrm{out}}{}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
expands the direction
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
by a factor
\lambda
on
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}
.
Therefore the identity diffeomorphism
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}=\operatorname{Id}
satisfies all items of Proposition
4.1.2
on the neighborhood
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}
, for the parameter
\lambda
and for any parameter
\epsilon>0
and
\delta>0
.
To prove Proposition
4.1.2
, it is therefore sufficient to work on the complementary of
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}
in
P^{\mathrm{in}}
.
The complementary
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}
is contained in a finite number of connected components of
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
which we denote
B_{1},\dots,B_{n}
.
Recall that the boundary lamination
{\mathcal{L}}
of
(P,X)
is filling, so the connected components of
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
are strips bounded by two noncompact leaves of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
which are asymptotic to each other at both ends.
The proof of Proposition
4.1.2
consists in building a diffeomorphism
\psi_{i}:B_{i}\to B_{i}
on each strip
B_{i}
which satisfies the items of Proposition
4.1.2
on this strip, and equal to the identity outside a compact of the strip.
We can extend
\psi_{i}
by the identity on
{\partial P}
and their product
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}=\prod_{i}\psi_{i}
defines a diffeomorphism which satisfies the proposition.
In summary, it suffices to prove the following

Lemma 4.1.4
.

Let
B\subset P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
be a strip.
For all
\lambda>1
,
\epsilon>0
and
\delta>0
,
there exists a diffeomorphism
\psi=\psi_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}{}\colon B\to B
, such that

(1)

\psi
is the identity outside a compact subset of
B
,

(2)

\psi
preserves
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
leaf-to-leaf,

(3)

the direction tangent to
\psi^{-1}_{*}({\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
lies inside a
(\epsilon,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}/,{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field,

(4)

the derivative of
\psi
in the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
is uniformly bounded by a constant depending only on
\lambda
: for all
\lambda>1
, there exists
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}=\operatorname{\textit{cst}}(\lambda)>0
, such that for all
{\epsilon,\delta}{}>0
we have

\forall p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{},\noindent\quad\forall v\in T_{p}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}},\noindent\quad\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}\,<\frac{\|\psi_{*}v\|}{\|v\|}<\operatorname{\textit{cst}}

(5)

if
{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}\subset{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
denotes the
\delta
-neighborhood of
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
orbits, then the composition
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\psi
expands by a factor
\lambda
the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
on
B{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
:

\forall p\in B{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta},\noindent\quad\forall v\in T_{p}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}},\noindent\quad\|(f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\psi)_{*}v\|\geq\|\lambda\|

(6)

\psi
does not depend on
\delta
on the complementary of
{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
:
for any
\lambda>1
,
\epsilon>0
, the family of diffeomorphisms
\{\psi_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}\}_{\delta>0}
coincide on
B{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
.

Let us draw attention to one point.
The support of
\psi\colon B\to B
is a compact contained in
B
, but the complementary
B{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}
is not necessarily a compact contained in
B
.
A strip
B
accumulates at both ends on a compact leaf of the boundary lamination
{\mathcal{L}}
.
If one of these compact leaves is a periodic orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{j}
, then the neighborhood
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}
does not overlap the corresponding end of the strip
B
,
because there is no
\delta
-neighborhood of the
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
periodic orbits contained in
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}
.
Therefore the expansion property of the composition
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
along
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
is no longer true on a
\delta
-neighborhood of
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
.

Let us show that Proposition
4.1.2
follows from Lemma
4.1.4
.

Proof of Proposition
4.1.2
.

Let
\lambda>1
,
\epsilon>0
,
\delta>0
.
We define the diffeomorphism
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}{}\to P^{\mathrm{in}}{}
as follows.
Let
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}
be the neighborhood given by Proposition
3.3.1
.
The complementary of
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}
is contained in a finite number of connected components
B_{1},\dots,B_{n}
of
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
(the integer
n
depends on
\lambda
).
For
i=1,\dots,n
, let be the diffeomorphism
\psi_{i}=(\psi_{i})_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}\colon B_{i}\to B_{i}
given by Lemma
4.1.4
.
Item
1
of Lemma
4.1.4
allows us to extend each
\psi_{i}
by the identity on
{\partial P}
, and we define

\psi_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}^{\mathrm{in}}=\underset{i=1}{\overset{m}{\prod}}{\psi_{i}}\colon{\partial P}\to{\partial P}.

The support of
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}
is contained in a finite number of strips
B_{i}
of the entrance boundary, and is compact in each of these strips, which gives Item
1
.
Items
2
,
3
and
6
are direct consequences of Items
2
,
3
and
6
of Lemma
4.1.4
.
Item
4
is true because the
\psi_{i}
diffeomorphisms are in finite number.

Let us show Item
5
.
On a strip
B_{i}
, we have
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}=\psi_{i}
. We use Item
5
of Lemma
4.1.4
.
On the complementary of the strips
{}^{c}{(B_{1}\cup\dots\cup B_{m})}
and by Proposition
3.3.1
,
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\psi^{\mathrm{in}}=f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}
expands by a factor
\lambda
the direction
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}

∎

The rest of the chapter is devoted to the proof of Lemma
4.1.4
.
Let
B
be a connected component of
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
.
Recall that
B
is a strip bordered by two non-compact leaves
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
of the lamination
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
, which are asymptotic to each other at both ends (Figure
34
).
Each of the two ends of the strip
B
accumulates on a compact leaf
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
of the boundary lamination
{\mathcal{L}}
of
(P,X)
(possibly the same in the non orientable case).
There are two possible cases for the compact leaf
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
: either it is a compact leaf of the lamination
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
, or it is a periodic orbit of
(P,X)
.

Figure 34
.
A strip which accumulates on one end on a periodic orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
and on the other end on a compact leaf of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}

Remark 4.1.5
.

To fix the ideas, we make the choice to represent in our figures a strip
B
with one end accumulating on a periodic orbit and the other on a compact leaf of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
.
This strip is contained in a connected component
A
of the complementary
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}\Gamma
, where
\Gamma
is the union of compact leaves of
{\mathcal{L}}
, this component
A
is an annulus, and the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
on
A
is a suspension foliation.
Of course, one should keep in mind that the following situations are also possible:

•

A
is an annulus and the boundary compact leaves are two leaves of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
, or two periodic orbits;

•

A
is a Moebius strip bordered by a unique compact leaf of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
, or a periodic orbit;

•

the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
forms a Reeb component on
A
.

4.2.
Action of the holonomy

Before constructing the diffeomorphism
\psi\colon B\to B
, we will need some general results on the holonomy maps of foliations on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
and
P^{\mathrm{out}}
.

Bound for the distortion of the holonomy of the stable foliation in a strip on the entrance boundary

Let
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
be leaves of the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
.
These are open arcs contained in a leaf of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
, and which cross the strip
B
from (accessible) boundary to (accessible) boundary, i.e. the two ends of
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
lie on the two leaves
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
which form the accessible boundary of
B
.
Denote the holonomy map of the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
between the leaves
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
by
H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}\colon\sigma\to\sigma^{\prime}
.
These maps are well defined because each leaf of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
in
B
intersects one and only one time each leaf
\sigma
of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
.
Since the leaves are
{\mathcal{C}}^{1}
, the holonomy maps are diffeomorphisms.
We will need to control the derivatives of these holonomies.
The derivatives of the elements of the family
\{H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}\}_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}}
are not uniformly bounded.
Indeed, the width of the strip
B
tends to 0 at the ends, and the map
H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}
is very contracting when
\sigma^{\prime}
tends to an end of
B
which accumulates on a compact leaf of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
.
However, the following lemma states that one can uniformly bound the distortion of the holonomy of the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
between two leaves of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
.

Figure 35
.
Holonomy of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
on a strip

Lemma 4.2.1
.

There exists a constant
\vartheta>0
such that for any strip
B\subset P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
, for any
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
, for any
p,q\in\sigma

\vartheta^{-1}<\frac{|(H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma})^{\prime}(p)|}{|(H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma})^{\prime}(q)|}<\vartheta

We begin with the following fact, which is true on both
P^{\mathrm{in}}
and
P^{\mathrm{out}}
.
In order not to repeat it, we state it with the notation
∗
which means
\mathrm{in}
or
\mathrm{out}
.

Claim 4.2.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
be an orbit in
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
and
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
be a normalized neighborhood of
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
.
Then

(1)

there exists a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
such that for all leaves
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,*}
, if
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
are two arcs of leaves of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,*}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
whose ends are on
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
, then

\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}\leq{l(\sigma)}/{l(\sigma^{\prime})}\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}
.

(2)

the derivatives of the holonomy maps
\{H^{s,*}_{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}\}_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}}
are uniformly bounded for
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{G}}^{u,*}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
.

We refer to Figure
36
which illustrates Claim
4.2.2
, Item
1
.

Figure 36
.
Arcs of leaves of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,*}
between two leaves
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,*}
in a normalized neighborhood of
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}

Proof.

Let us show this fact on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
, the proof is identical on
P^{\mathrm{out}}
.
Let
({\mathcal{V}}_{i},\xi_{i}=(x,y,\theta))
be a normalized coordinate system of
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
.
Let
\rho_{i}=(x,\theta)\in{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}/\,{\mathbb{Z}}
be the normalized coordinate system induced by
\xi_{i}
on the boundary of
P
(Remark
2.1.7
).
Let two leaves
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
of the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
in
{\mathcal{V}}
, and
p=(x,\theta)\in l_{1}
.
The lifts of the leaves in the lift
(x,\tilde{\theta})\in{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}
of the coordinates
(x,\theta)
have equation
x=c_{i}2^{\tilde{\theta}}
.
It follows that the distance
\operatorname{dist}(p,l_{2})
decreases linearly in
x
.
Moreover the angle between the foliations
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
also decreases linearly in
x=\operatorname{dist}(p,{\mathcal{O}}_{i})
according to Lemma
3.4.6
.
We conclude that the ratio of the lengths
{l(\sigma^{\prime})}/{l(\sigma)}
of two leaf arcs
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
joining
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
in
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
is uniformly bounded away from
0
and
+\infty
(Figure
37
).

Figure 37
.
The distance between
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
and the angle between the foliations
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
decreases at the same rate

.

The second item is a consequence of the first.
Indeed, if
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
are two arcs of leaves of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
in
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
, we can choose
\sigma_{0}
an arc of leaf contained in
\sigma
of arbitrarily small length around a point
p
, and
\sigma_{0}^{\prime}=H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}
its image contained in
\sigma^{\prime}
.
Then the ratio of the lengths
{l(\sigma_{0}^{\prime})}/{l(\sigma_{0})}
tends to the derivative of
H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}
at point
p
when the length of
\sigma_{0}
tends to 0.
According to Item
1
, this ratio is uniformly bounded.
We deduce Item
2
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.2.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{Q}}
be a compact leaf of
{\mathcal{L}}={\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}\cup{\mathcal{O}}_{*}\cup{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
on which the strip
B
accumulates.
Then
{\mathcal{Q}}
is a compact boundary leaf (Definition
2.1.8
).
Let
W
be a neighborhood of
{\mathcal{Q}}
on which the holonomy maps of the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
are conjugate to affine maps (Proposition
2.1.9
, Item
3
).
If
{\mathcal{Q}}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
is a periodic orbit,
W
is contained in a normalized coordinate system
({\mathcal{V}}_{i},\xi_{i})
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}={\mathcal{Q}}
.
We use the fact that the holonomy maps
H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}
have a uniformly bounded derivative in this neighborhood.
If
{\mathcal{Q}}
is a compact leaf of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
, the foliations
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
are uniformly transverse on
W
.
By definition of
W
the holonomy maps of a compact leaf of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
are conjugate to affine maps, hence the distortion of the holonomy maps
H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}
is uniformly bounded for
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}
in
W
.

Finally, it is enough to see that we can compose a holonomy map in the following way (Figure
38
).
There exists a constant
l>0
, such that if
I,J
are two leaves of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
, then we can write

H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{I,J}=H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{I,I_{1}}\circ H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}\circ H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{I_{2},J}

with
H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{I,I_{1}}
and
H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{I_{2},J}
holonomy maps in the neighborhood
W
,
and such that the leaves of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
have a length uniformly bounded by
l
between
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
and are uniformly transverse to the leaves of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
.
The derivatives of
H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}
are uniformly bounded, and so is the composition.
Each map of the composition has a uniformly bounded distortion, and the bound is independent of the strip
B
.
The same is true for the composition.

Figure 38
.
Decomposition of
H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{J,I}
in
B

∎

Let us quote the following lemma which is completely analogous to Lemma
4.2.1
, where
H^{u,\mathrm{out}}_{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}
denotes the holonomy map of the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
between two leaves
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}
of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}\cap B
.

Lemma 4.2.3
.

There exists a constant
\vartheta>0
such that for any strip
B\subset P^{\mathrm{out}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
, for any
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}\cap B
, for any
p,q\in\sigma

\vartheta^{-1}<\frac{|(H^{u,\mathrm{out}}_{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma})^{\prime}(p)|}{|(H^{u,\mathrm{out}}_{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma})^{\prime}(q)|}<\vartheta

Bound for the derivative of the holonomy of the stable foliation on a strip on the exit boundary

.
Let
\varsigma
and
\varsigma^{\prime}
be leaves of the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
on
P^{\mathrm{out}}
in a strip
B\subset P^{\mathrm{out}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
.
We define the holonomy map of the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}\cap B
between the leaves
\varsigma
and
\varsigma^{\prime}
(Figure
39
) by
H^{s,\mathrm{out}}_{\varsigma^{\prime},\varsigma}\colon\varsigma\to\varsigma^{\prime}
.
These maps are well defined because each arc of leaf of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}\cap B
intersects each leaf
\varsigma
of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
.
These maps are diffeomorphisms.
Let us show that the derivatives of the holonomy maps
\{H^{s,\mathrm{out}}_{\varsigma^{\prime},\varsigma}\}
for
{\varsigma^{\prime},\varsigma}
leaves of
P^{\mathrm{out}}
in a same strip
B\subset P^{\mathrm{out}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
are uniformly bounded.

Lemma 4.2.4
.

There exists a constant
\alpha>0
such that for any strip
B\subset P^{\mathrm{out}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
, for any
\varsigma,\varsigma^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}\cap B
, for any
p\in\varsigma
, we have

\alpha^{-1}<|(H^{s,\mathrm{out}}_{\varsigma^{\prime},\varsigma})^{\prime}(p)|<\alpha
.

Figure 39
.
Holonomy map of the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
on a strip

Proof.

The family
\{H^{s,\mathrm{out}}_{\varsigma^{\prime},\varsigma}\}_{\varsigma^{\prime},\varsigma}
is a relatively compact family of diffeomorphisms.
Indeed, let
I
be a leaf of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}\cap B
.
Each leaf
\varsigma
of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
intersects
I
once.
We can therefore parameterize the family
\{H^{s,\mathrm{out}}_{\varsigma^{\prime},\varsigma}\}_{\varsigma^{\prime},\varsigma}
by the product
I\times I
.
We conclude by noticing that
I
is a relatively compact arc.
It is then sufficient to show that the derivative of a diffeomorphism
H^{s,\mathrm{out}}_{\varsigma^{\prime},\varsigma}
is bounded on
\varsigma
.
On the complementary
P^{\mathrm{out}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
of the normalized neighborhoods of the periodic orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
, the angle of the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
with
\varsigma
is bounded, and the distance between
\varsigma
and
\varsigma^{\prime}
is increased.
On a normalized neighborhood
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
of an orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
, the derivative of
H^{s,\mathrm{out}}_{\varsigma^{\prime},\varsigma}
is bounded according to Claim
4.2.2
.
We deduce that the derivative of
H^{s,\mathrm{out}}_{\varsigma^{\prime},\varsigma}
is bounded on
\varsigma
.
Finally, let us note that we can uniformly bound the derivatives of
\{H^{s,\mathrm{out}}_{\varsigma^{\prime},\varsigma}\}_{\varsigma^{\prime},\varsigma}
independently of the strip
B
.
Indeed, there exists a relatively compact leaf arc
I
of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
such that each leaf
\varsigma
of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
intersects
I
only once.
It follows that the family
\{H^{s,\mathrm{out}}_{\varsigma^{\prime},\varsigma}\}_{\varsigma^{\prime},\varsigma}
for
\varsigma,\varsigma^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
is a relatively compact family of diffeomorphisms, and the previous proof works.
∎

Let us quote the following lemma which is completely analogous to Lemma
4.2.4
, where
H^{u,\mathrm{in}}_{\varsigma^{\prime},\varsigma}
denotes the holonomy of the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
between two leaves of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
.

Lemma 4.2.5
.

There exists a constant
\alpha>0
such that for any strip
B\subset P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
, for any
\varsigma,\varsigma^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
, for any
p\in\varsigma
, we have

\alpha^{-1}<|(H^{u,\mathrm{in}}_{\varsigma^{\prime},\varsigma})^{\prime}(p)|<\alpha
.

4.3.
Proof of Lemma
4.1.4

Let
B
be a strip on
P^{\mathrm{in}}{}
. Let
\lambda>1
,
\epsilon>0
and
\delta>0
be fixed.
Let
l_{1},l_{2}
be the leaves of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
forming the accessible boundary of
B
.
The proof will be done in three steps.

•

In Step 1, we fix a leaf
\sigma
of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
that crosses the strip
B
and we construct a diffeomorphism
h:\sigma\to\sigma

equal to the identity at the ends of the leaf,
such that the composition
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ h:\sigma\to f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}(\sigma)
has a derivative bounded from below by a given factor proportional to
\lambda
.
This is Lemma
4.3.1
.

•

In Step 2, we push the diffeomorphism
h
along the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
by the holonomy maps
H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}
to obtain a diffeomorphism
\hat{\psi}\colon B\to B
, equal to the identity on a neighborhood of
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
.
This diffeomorphism naturally preserves the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
(leaf-to-leaf) and the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
(globally) on
B
.

Moreover, the expansion of the composition
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{\psi}
in the direction
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
on
B
is essentially equal to the expansion of the composition of
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ h
.
Indeed, this is a consequence of the uniform bounds on the distortion and the derivative of the holonomy maps of the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s}
between two leaves of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u}
from Lemmas

4.2.1
and
4.2.4
.
We chose
h
in order to compensate the contraction of these holonomy maps.
Thus the composition
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{\psi}
expands by a factor
\lambda
the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
on the strip
B
.
The diffeomorphism thus constructed on
B
satisfies all items of Lemma
4.1.4
,
except that its support is not a compact of the strip
B
(Item
1
).

•

Step 3 consists in “slowing down” the diffeomorphism
\hat{\psi}
in order to obtain a diffeomorphism
\psi
equal to the identity at the ends of
B
.
This is where the parameters
\epsilon
and
\delta
come in.

–

In order not to destroy what has been done previously we slow down by a leaf-to-leaf barycentric isotopy along
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
.

–

The speed of the “slowing down” controls the perturbation of the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
, and will therefore be chosen according to the parameter
\epsilon
.

–

If one end of
B
is a periodic orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
, we lose control on the expansion of the composition with
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}
at the location of the slowdown.
We make the slowing down in a neighborhood of the periodic orbit of size
\delta
.
Otherwise, the ends of the strip
B
accumulates on a compact leaf
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
, and we make the slowdown in a neighborhood of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
small enough so that the “natural” expansion of
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}
obtained by Proposition
3.3.1
compensates the contracting effects of the slowdown.
This preserves the expansion of the composition by a factor
\lambda
on this end.

Step 1: On a leaf

Let
\sigma
be a leaf of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
, of
quasi-maximal
length among all the leaves of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
, more precisely we ask that for any leaf
\sigma^{\prime}
of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
, we have
l(\sigma)\geq\frac{1}{2}l(\sigma^{\prime})
.
This is possible because the leaves of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
are of uniformly bounded length.
We fix
\sigma
for the rest of the section.
It is an arc whose ends are on the non-compact leaves
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
which border
B
.
Let
\vartheta>0
and
\alpha>0
be the constants which satisfy Lemmas
4.2.1
and
4.2.4
.

Lemma 4.3.1
.

There exists a diffeomorphism
h\colon\sigma\to\sigma
, equal to the identity on a neighborhood of the ends of
\sigma
such that

\forall p\in\sigma,\noindent\quad\forall v\in T_{p}\sigma,\noindent\quad\|(f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ h)_{*}v\|>2\vartheta\alpha\lambda\|v\|

Proof.

As
\partial\sigma\in{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
, we know from Proposition
3.3.1
that the derivative of
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}|_{\sigma}
tends to infinity at the ends of
\sigma
.
The unit-speed parametrization identifies
\sigma
to
]0,l[
for some
l>0
, and
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}(\sigma)
to
{\mathbb{R}}
by isometries.
We come to the following result, whose proof is left to the reader.

Claim 4.3.2
.

If
f\colon\,]0,l[\,\to{\mathbb{R}}
is a diffeomorphism such that

|f^{\prime}(x)|\underset{x\to 0,\,l}{\longrightarrow}+\infty
then for all
A>1
, there exists a diffeomorphism
f_{A}\colon\,]0,l[\,\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that

•

f_{A}=f
on a neighborhood of
0
and
l
,

•

|f_{A}^{\prime}(x)|>A
for all
x
in
]0,l[
.

We apply this result with
A=2\vartheta\alpha\lambda>1
, and we put

h:=f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}^{-1}\circ(f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}})_{A}
.
∎

Step 2: On the strip

We extend the diffeomorphism
h
to the strip
B
by pushing through the holonomy of the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
between the leaves of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
.
More precisely, we define for any
p\in B

(4.1)

\hat{\psi}(p)=H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\sigma_{p},\sigma}\circ h\circ H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\sigma,\sigma_{p}}(p)

where
\sigma_{p}
denotes the leaf of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
passing through
p
.
This expression defines a diffeomorphism on
B
.
It satisfies the following properties:

Claim 4.3.3
.

(1)

\hat{\psi}
is equal to the identity on a neighborhood of the leaves
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
which border
B
,

(2)

\hat{\psi}
preserves the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
leaf-to-leaf,

(3)

\hat{\psi}
preserve the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
globally,

(4)

the composition
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{\psi}
expands the norm of the vectors in the direction
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
by a factor
\lambda
:

\forall p\in B,\quad\forall v\in T_{p}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}},\quad\|(f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{\psi})_{*}v\|\geq\lambda\|v\|

Proof.

(1)

The neighborhood in question is the saturated set of the complementary of the support of
h
by the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
.
It is a neighborhood of the leaves
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
by Lemma
4.3.1
.

(2)

Obvious.

(3)

Obvious.

(4)

Let
\varsigma=f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}(\sigma)
.
The map
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}
commutes with the holonomy maps of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
because it maps the pair
({\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}},{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}})
on the pair
({\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}},{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}})
on each strip.
We deduce that

(f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{\psi})(p)=H^{s,\mathrm{out}}_{\varsigma_{p},\varsigma}\circ\left(f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ h\right)\circ H^{s,in}_{\sigma,\sigma_{p}}(p)

where
\varsigma_{p}=f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}(\sigma_{p})
denotes the leaf of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
passing through
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}(p)
.
We refer to Figure
40
.

Figure 40
.
Action of
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{\psi}

Let
\vartheta
be the constant given by Lemma
4.2.1
.
Then, if
l(\sigma)
denotes the length of the leaf
\sigma
, we have

|(H^{s,in}_{\sigma,\sigma_{p}})^{\prime}|\ >\ \vartheta^{-1}\frac{l(\sigma)}{l(\sigma_{p})}\ \geq\ \frac{1}{2}\vartheta^{-1}

because
\sigma
is of quasi-maximal length among the leaves of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
(see Step 1).
Moreover, by Lemma
4.2.4
,
we have
|(H^{s,\mathrm{out}}_{\varsigma,\varsigma_{p}})^{\prime}|>\alpha^{-1}
.
So for all
p\in B
, for all
v\in T_{p}\sigma_{p}
, if
q=H_{\sigma,\sigma_{p}}(p)
,

\|(f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{\psi})_{*}v\|>\frac{1}{2}\vartheta^{-1}\alpha^{-1}|(f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ h)^{\prime}(q)|\|v\|

We conclude with Lemma
4.3.1
.

∎

Step 3: Slowing down at the ends of the strip

The diffeomorphism
\hat{\psi}\colon B\to B
(Claim
4.3.3
) satisfies all items of Lemma
4.1.4
on the strip
B
, except Item
1
: it is not compactly supported, because it is not equal to the identity at the ends of
B
.
To solve this problem, we will “slow down”
\hat{\psi}
on a neighborhood of the ends of
B
.
We can choose the slowing down area in order to keep the expansion of the composition
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{\psi}
on
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
at the end of
B
if it accumulates on a compact leaf of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
.
If the end accumulates on a periodic orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
, one loses the expansion of
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\hat{\psi}
on the slowing down zone.
We will then choose this zone in a
\delta
-neighborhood of
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
.
In fact, we will operate the slowing down on the
\hat{\psi}^{-1}
diffeomorphism in order to control the perturbation of the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
by
\hat{\psi}^{-1}

(Item
3
of Lemma
4.1.4
).
Recall that
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
are the non-compact leaves of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
which form the accessible boundary of
B
.
Let
s\colon{\mathbb{R}}\to l_{1}
be a parameterization by length of the leaf
l_{1}
,
where
s(0)=l_{1}\cap\sigma
.
Denote
\sigma_{t}
the leaf of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
such that
\sigma_{t}\cap l_{1}=s_{1}(t)
.
We have
\sigma=\sigma_{0}
.
As
\hat{\psi}
preserves the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
leaf-to-leaf on
B
, it induces the existence of a continuous one-parameter family of diffeomorphisms
\hat{\psi}_{t}=\hat{\psi}|_{\sigma_{t}}\colon\sigma_{t}\to\sigma_{t}
.

Let
W
be a neighborhood of the ends of
B
on which the holonomy maps of the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
are conjugate to affine maps (Remark
2.1.10
).
For any leaf
\sigma_{t}
contained in
W
, we define
\Psi=\{\Psi_{t,\tau}\colon\sigma_{t}\to\sigma_{t}\tau\in[0,1],t\in{\mathbb{R}}\}
a family of barycentric isotopies between
\hat{\psi}_{t}^{-1}
and the identity on
\sigma_{t}
on each leaf
\sigma_{t}
:

(4.2)

\forall p\in\sigma_{t},\noindent\quad\forall\tau\in[0,1],\noindent\quad\Psi_{t,\tau}(p)=\tau\hat{\psi}_{t}^{-1}(p)+(1-\tau)p

This formula makes sense because the leaf
\sigma_{t}
contained in
W
has an affine structure, and defines a family
\Psi
of diffeomorphisms.

Let
t_{1}<0
,
t_{2}>0
, such that
\sigma_{t}\in W\cap B
for all
t\in\ ]-\infty,t_{1}[\ \cup\ ]t_{2},+\infty[
.
Let
s>0
.
Let
\tau=\tau(t_{1},t_{2},s)\colon{\mathbb{R}}\to[0,1]
be a plateau function of class
{\mathcal{C}}^{1}
satisfying the following properties (Figure
41
):

(1)

\tau=1
on
[t_{1},t_{2}]
and
\tau=0
on
{\mathbb{R}}{\smallsetminus}[t_{1}-s,t_{2}+s]

(2)

\tau
is increasing on
{\mathbb{R}}_{-}
and decreasing on
{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
, and the norm of its derivative is upper bounded by
2s^{-1}
.

Figure 41
.
Graph of the function
\tau\colon{\mathbb{R}}\to[0,1]

The parameters
t_{i}
determine the area of the beginning of the isotopy in the strip, and the parameters
s
determines the speed of the isotopy (Figure
42
).
For
p\in B
, if
\sigma_{t}
is the leaf of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
passing through
p
, we define

(4.3)

\psi(p)=\left(\Psi_{t,\tau(t)}(p)\right)^{-1}

This expression defines a diffeomorphism
\psi\colon B\to B
.

Figure 42
.
Area of the start of the isotopy (light) and the end of the isotopy (dark) on the strip
B

Proof of Lemma
4.1.4
.

Let us show each item for the diffeomorphism
\psi\colon B\to B
defined by Equation (
4.3
).

(1)

By definition of
\tau
(Item
1
above), the support of
\psi
is a compact of the strip
B
equal to :

\operatorname{Supp}(\psi)=\operatorname{Supp}(\hat{\psi}){\smallsetminus}\left(\left(\underset{-\infty}{\overset{t_{1}-s}{\bigcup}}\sigma_{t}\right)\cup\left(\underset{t_{2}+s}{\overset{+\infty}{\bigcup}}\sigma_{t}\right)\right)

(2)

It is clear that this diffeomorphism preserves the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
leaf-to-leaf.

(3)

Let
(\partial_{u},\partial_{s})
be a pair of two unit vector fields tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
respectively.
Let
p\in B
, let
t
be a parameter such that
\sigma_{t}
is the leaf of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
passing through
p
, let
q=\psi^{-1}(p)
.
Then the image of
\partial_{s}
by the differential of
\psi^{-1}
is

d_{p}\psi^{-1}(\partial_{s})=\tau^{\prime}(t)\,\partial_{u}+(H^{u,\mathrm{in}}_{\varsigma_{p},\varsigma_{q}})^{\prime}(p)\,\partial_{s}.

According to Lemma
4.2.5
, the derivatives of these holonomy maps are uniformly bounded.
It suffices to choose a function
\tau
such that the derivative is close enough to 0, in other words a parameter
s
large enough (Item
2
of the definition of
\tau
above), so that

|\tau^{\prime}(t)|\leq\epsilon\alpha^{-1}

where
\alpha
is the constant given by Lemma
4.2.5
which bounds from below the derivative of
H^{u,\mathrm{in}}_{\varsigma_{p},\varsigma_{q}}
.
Thus the image
\psi^{-1}_{*}\partial_{s}
is in a
(\epsilon,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field (Definition
4.1.1
). This proves Item
3
.

(4)

According to Equation (
4.2
) of the barycentric isotopy in each leaf of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
, the derivative of
\psi^{-1}
in the direction of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
is bounded by the upper and lower bounds of the derivatives of the family of diffeomorphisms
\{\hat{\psi}_{t}\}_{t}
, in other words by the upper and lower bounds of the derivative of
\hat{\psi}
in the direction
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
.
Recall that
\hat{\psi}
(Equation (
4.1
)) is the conjugate by
H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}}
of the diffeomorphism
h\colon\sigma\to\sigma
(Lemma
4.3.1
).
Since the distortion of the holonomies
H^{s,\mathrm{in}}_{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}
is uniformly bounded by a constant
\vartheta>0
, (Lemma
4.2.1
), we deduce that the derivative of
\hat{\psi}
in the direction of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
is uniformly controlled by the derivative of
h
:

\forall v\in T{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}},\noindent\quad\vartheta^{-1}|h^{\prime}|\leq\|\hat{\psi}_{*}v\|\leq\vartheta|h^{\prime}|

Since
h
is a diffeomorphism with compact support, we conclude that the differential of
\psi
in the direction
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
is uniformly bounded by a constant which only depends on
\lambda
.
We thus obtain Item
4
.

(5)

Let us show that we can choose
t_{1},t_{2}
in order to obtain Item
5
.
Let
c>0
be a constant which bounds from below the derivative of
\psi
in the direction
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
.
This constant exists according to the previous Item
4
and is independent of
t_{i}
.
Denote
B_{1}
the neighborhood of the end of
B
corresponding to
t\leq t_{1}
, and
B_{2}
the neighborhood of the end of
B
corresponding to
t\geq t_{2}
.

(a)

If the end
B_{i}
accumulates on a periodic orbit
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
, then choose
t_{i}
such that
\sigma_{t_{i}}
is contained in a
\delta
-neighborhood
{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}\subset{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
, where we recall that
{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
is the union of the normalized neighborhoods of the periodic orbits of
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
.

(b)

If the end
B_{i}
accumulates on a compact leaf
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
then choose
t_{i}
such that
\sigma_{t_{i}}
is contained in the neighborhood
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda c^{-1}}
given by Proposition
3.3.1
.
9
9

9

we can have
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}={\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
in the non-orientable case

Such a choice is always possible because
\sigma_{t}\to{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
when
t\to\pm\infty
, and because the neighborhoods
{\mathcal{W}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda}
of Proposition
3.3.1
are neighborhoods of compact leaves of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
.
Then we have

•

On
\underset{[t_{1},t_{2}]}{\bigcup}\sigma_{t}
, we have
\psi=\hat{\psi}
and the expansion of the composition
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\psi
in the direction
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
is satisfied according to Claim
4.3.3
, Item
4
.

•

By construction, we have

B{\smallsetminus}\left(\left(\underset{[t_{1},t_{2}]}{\bigcup}\sigma_{t}\right)\cup{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}\right)=\left(\underset{{\mathbb{R}}{\smallsetminus}[t_{1},t_{2}]}{\bigcup}\sigma_{t}\right){\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}\ \subset\ {\mathcal{W}}_{\lambda c}.

According to the previous Item
2
, the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
is preserved by
\psi
.
Hence for any
v\in T{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
at a point of this set, we have by Proposition
3.3.1
and the previously shown Item
4
of Lemma
4.1.4
:

\|(f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\psi)_{*}v\|>\lambda c^{-1}\|\psi_{*}v\|>\lambda\|v\|
.

(6)

By construction the diffeomorphism
\psi
coincides with
\hat{\psi}
on the complementary of the normalized
{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
neighborhoods of the tangent
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
periodic orbits in
B
, hence is independent of
\delta
in this region.
Item
