is thus verified.

∎

5.
Choice of the parameters and cone criterion

In this section and for the rest of the proof,
\varphi
denotes a normalized gluing map of
(P,X)
(Definitions
2.1.13
).
We refer to the beginning of Section
3
for a reminder of the notations.
By definition of a normalized block (Definition
2.1.4
, Item
1
), the block
(P,X)
is provided with an affine section
\Sigma
10
10

10

Such a section is defined in an extension of
(P,X)
, but we consider in this chapter its restriction to
P
, in other words a transverse local section of
\Lambda_{s}
such that the first return map of the flow of
X
is affine in the neighborhood of
\Sigma\cap\Lambda_{s}
.

In the previous section we built a family of diffeomorphisms
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}\colon{\partial P}\to{\partial P}
with support in
P^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}\colon{\partial P}\to{\partial P}
with support in
P^{\mathrm{out}}
indexed by parameters
\lambda>1
,
\epsilon>0
,
\delta>0
, which satisfy the hypotheses of Proposition
4.1.2
and Proposition
4.1.3
.
Recall that:

•

\lambda>1
is a lower bound for the expansion factor of the composition
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
in the direction
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
outside a neighborhood of the orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
,

•

\epsilon>0
measures the perturbation of the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
under the action of
(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}
,

•

\delta>0
is the size of the neighborhood of the orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
on which we no longer control the dilation of the composition
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\circ\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
.

Symmetrically:

•

\lambda>1
is a lower bound for the expansion factor of the composition
(\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\circ f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}})^{-1}
in the direction
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
outside a neighborhood of the orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
,

•

\epsilon>0
measures the perturbation of the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
under the action of
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
,

•

\delta>0
is the size of the neighborhood of the
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
orbits on which we no longer control the dilation of the composition
(\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\circ f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}})^{-1}
on
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
.

5.1.
Statement of the main proposition and summary of the proof

Gluing map

For any parameter
\lambda,\epsilon,\delta
, define the composition

\psi_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}:=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}\,\varphi\,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}\,\colon{\partial P}\to{\partial P}

where
\varphi
is a normalized gluing map of
(P,X)
.
For any
\lambda,\epsilon,\delta
,
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
are equal to the identity on a neighborhood of
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
in
{\partial P}
, so the same holds for
\psi
and
\psi
is a dynamical gluing map of
(P,X)
(Definition
1.3.3
and Proposition
2.1.15
).
Moreover,
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
are isotopic to the identity via diffeomorphisms equal to the identity on a neighborhood of the boundary lamination
{\mathcal{L}}
, so
\psi_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}
and
\varphi
are isotopic via strongly transverse gluing maps.

Claim 5.1.1
.

For all
{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}
, if
\psi=\psi_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}
, then

(P,X,\psi)
is a triple strongly isotopic to
(P,X,\varphi)
, and the quotient space
P_{\psi}:=P/\,\psi
is a closed manifold of dimension three with a vector field of class
{\mathcal{C}}^{1}
induced by
X
on
P_{\psi}
which is denoted by
X_{\psi}

Proof.

Indeed,
\psi=\psi_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}
is a gluing map of
(P,X)
isotopic to
\varphi
.
Moreover, there is an isotopy
h_{t}\colon{\partial P}\to{\partial P}
which joins
h_{0}=\operatorname{Id}
and
h_{1}=\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\circ(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}
, which preserves the boundary lamination
{\mathcal{L}}
, and such that
h_{1}
maps leaves of
\psi_{*}{\mathcal{L}}
on leaves of
\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{L}}
.
The triples are thus strongly isotopic in the sense of Definition
1.4.9
.
The last affirmation relies on the fact that
\psi
is a dynamical gluing map and Claim
1.3.4
.
∎

Let
\pi_{\psi}
be the projection
P\to P_{\psi}
.
In order not to complicate the notations, denote
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}=\pi_{\psi}(P^{\mathrm{in}})
,
\Sigma_{\psi}=\pi_{\psi}(\Sigma)
,
\pi_{\psi}({\mathcal{O}}_{*})=({\mathcal{O}}_{*})_{\psi}
, etc.
More generally the index
ψ
indicates that one considers an element projected in the quotient manifold
P_{\psi}
.

Local section in
P_{\psi}

The projection

\pi_{\psi}(\Sigma\cup P^{\mathrm{in}}\cup P^{\mathrm{out}})=\pi_{\psi}(\Sigma\cup P^{\mathrm{in}})=\pi_{\psi}(\Sigma\cup P^{\mathrm{out}})

is a non-compact, immersed surface in
P_{\psi}
, transverse to the orbits of
X_{\psi}
.
Denote

S_{0,\psi}:=\pi_{\psi}(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cup\Sigma)
.
Let
f_{0,\psi}\colon S_{0,\psi}\to S_{0,\psi}
the first return map of the flow of
X_{\psi}
on
S_{0,\psi}
.
Notice that we have
S_{0,\psi}=P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}\cup\Sigma_{\psi}
.
The section
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
is a union of closed surfaces transverse to
X_{\psi}
and closed surfaces quasi-transverse to
X_{\psi}
minus the periodic orbits
({\mathcal{O}}_{*})_{\psi}
.
It follows that the section
S_{0,\psi}
is not uniformly transverse to the vector field
X_{\psi}
.
Note that we have the equality
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}=P^{\mathrm{out}}_{\psi}
, but we will make the arbitrary choice to consider
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
in the following.

Figure 43
.
The section
S_{0,\psi}
in the neighborhood of a periodic orbit of
({\mathcal{O}}_{*})_{\psi}

The cone fields condition

Definition 5.1.2

(Cone fields condition)
.

Let
f\colon S\to S
be a diffeomorphism.
We say that
f
satisfies the
cone fields condition
if there exists a pair of cone fields
(C^{u},C^{s})
on
S
and constants
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
,
\lambda>1
such that for all
p\in S
:

•

f_{*}(C^{u}(p))\subset\operatorname{int}C^{u}(f(p))
, and for all
v\in C^{u}(p)
,
n\geq 0
,

\|f^{n}_{*}v\|\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\lambda^{n}\|v\|
.

•

f^{-1}_{*}(C^{s}(f(p)))\subset\operatorname{int}C^{s}(p)
,
and for all
v\in C^{s}(f(p))
,
n\geq 0
,

\|f^{-n}_{*}v\|\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\lambda^{n}\|v\|
.

Analogously, we say that
(C^{u},C^{s})
is a
pair of
f
-invariant cone fields
.
We say that
C^{u}
is an
unstable cone field
and
C^{s}
is a
stable cone field
for
f
.

Goal

The goal of this section is to choose parameters
\lambda,\epsilon,\delta
such that if
\psi=\psi_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}
, the first return map
f_{0,\psi}
has hyperbolic properties in the neighborhood of its maximal invariant set.
This set captures all the
X_{\psi}
-orbit in
P_{\psi}
which do not accumulate on the attractors
\pi_{\psi}({\mathfrak{A}})
in the past or on the repellers
\pi_{\psi}({\mathfrak{R}})
in the future (the projection is trivial in the neighborhood of the attractors and repellers).
We will deal with those orbit separately in the next section.

We will show that an iterate (which depends on the starting point) of
f_{0,\psi}
satisfies the cone fields condition in the neighborhood of certain points.
However, the non-uniform transversality of the section
f_{0,\psi}
with the vector field
X_{\psi}
prevents the first return (or an iterate) from being uniformly hyperbolic.
In the following Section
6
, we will show that for such a choice of parameters, we can truncate the section
S_{0,\psi}
by removing a well-chosen neighborhood of the orbits
\pi_{\psi}({\mathcal{O}}_{*})
to make it compact and uniformly transverse to the vector field
X_{\psi}
while catching the same set of orbits in uniformly bounded time, and such that the maximal invariant set is a hyperbolic set.
We have to specify some technical aspects before stating the result.

Self-intersections

Notice that
S_{0,\psi}
has double or triple self-intersection points (Figure
43
).
Indeed the intersection
\Sigma\cap P^{\mathrm{in}}
is a double point locus in
P
so likewise for the image in
P_{\psi}
. Moreover, a point of
\pi_{\psi}(\Sigma)
can be in the image of two disjoint disks of
\Sigma
in the neighborhood of two periodic orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{j}
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
.
The projections by
\pi_{\psi}
of these disks intersect at some points and are not necessarily equal everywhere.
Then the surface
S_{0,\psi}
has two or three tangent planes at its self-intersection points.

•

If
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}\subset S_{0,\psi}
, we denote
T^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}\subset T_{p}S_{0,\psi}
the tangent plane induced by
T_{\tilde{p}}P^{\mathrm{in}}
, where
\tilde{p}\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
is the lift of
p
in
P^{\mathrm{in}}

•

If
q\in\Sigma_{\psi}\subset S_{0,\psi}
, we denote
T^{\Sigma}_{q}\subset T_{p}S_{0,\psi}
the tangent plane(s) induced by
T_{\tilde{q}}\Sigma
, where
\tilde{q}\in\Sigma
is a lift of
q
in
\Sigma
.

The differential of
f_{0,\psi}
in
p
is well defined for a choice of tangent plane on
p
and
f_{0,\psi}(p)
.
More precisely, for
p\in S_{0,\psi}
, once a tangent plane
T_{p}^{*}
to
S_{0,\psi}
has been chosen at point
p
and
T^{*}_{f(p)}
, the differential
d_{p}f_{0,\psi}\colon T_{p}^{*}\to T_{f(p)}^{*}
is well defined.

Remark 5.1.3
.

One might want to adjust the section
\Sigma
to avoid triple points of
S_{0,\psi}
.
However, the choice of the gluing maps
\psi=\psi_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}
determined by the parameters
{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}
will depend on the data of
\Sigma
, and one cannot proceed in this way.

Metric on the section

The main proposition of this section involves expansion results on non-invariant sets.
We must first choose a metric on
S_{0,\psi}
.
The metric
g
on
P
(see beginning of Section
3
) induces a metric
g^{\mathrm{in}}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
and a metric
g^{\Sigma}
on
\Sigma
.
For technical reasons, we will consider the metric
\hat{g}^{\mathrm{in}}:=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}g^{\mathrm{in}}
which is the push-forward by
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
of the metric
g^{\mathrm{in}}
.
We then consider the quotient of
\hat{g}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
g^{\Sigma}
onto
\pi_{\psi}(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cup\Sigma)
, denoted
g_{\psi}
.
These metrics do not coincide along self-intersections and the image
g_{\psi}
does not define a continuous metric.
However, one can always lift everything in
P
by the local homeomorphism
\pi_{\psi}
and “pretend” that there are no intersections and all the pieces coming from
\Sigma
and
P^{\mathrm{in}}
are disjoint in
P_{\psi}
.
We don’t write it but all these objects depend on the choice of the parameters
{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}{}
.

Cone field

A cone field
C
on
S_{0,\psi}
is the data, for each
p\in S_{0,\psi}
and for each tangent plane
T^{*}
to
S_{0,\psi}
at the point
p
, of a cone
C(p,T^{*})\subset T^{*}
.

•

When we specify that a point
p
in
S_{0,\psi}
belongs to
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
, we consider the tangent space
T^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}\subset T_{p}\Sigma_{\psi}
induced by
T_{\tilde{p}}P^{\mathrm{in}}
in
p
, and the metric induced by
\hat{g}^{\mathrm{in}}
, in other words we lift by the local homeomorphism
\pi_{\psi}
in the neighborhood of the unique lift
\tilde{p}\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
.

•

When we specify that a point
p\in S_{0,\psi}
belongs to
\Sigma_{\psi}
, we consider the tangent space
T^{\Sigma}_{p}\subset T_{p}\Sigma_{\psi}
induced by
T_{\tilde{p}}\Sigma
in
p
, and the metric induced by
g^{\Sigma}
,
in other words we lift by the local homeomorphism
\pi_{\psi}
in the neighborhood of an (arbitrary) lift
\tilde{p}\in\Sigma
.

Statement of the main proposition

The following proposition states that one can choose parameters
\lambda,\epsilon,\delta
and cones on
\Sigma
and on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
such that a (non-uniform) iterate of the first return map
f_{0,\psi}\colon S_{0,\psi}\to S_{0,\psi}
of the flow of
X_{\psi}
satisfies the cone fields condition in the neighborhood of some points.
Denote
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{\psi}=\pi_{\psi}({\mathcal{W}}^{s})
the projection of the stable lamination of
\Lambda
for
X
in
P_{\psi}
, and similarly
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}_{\psi}=\pi_{\psi}({\mathcal{W}}^{u})
for the projection of the unstable of
\Lambda
in
P_{\psi}
.
We show:

Proposition 5.1.4
.

There are parameters
\lambda_{0}>1
,
\epsilon_{0}>0
,
\delta_{0}>0
, with the following properties.
We denote

\psi^{\mathrm{in}}=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda_{0},\epsilon_{0},\delta_{0}}
,

\psi^{\mathrm{out}}=\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\lambda_{0},\epsilon_{0},\delta_{0}}
,

\psi=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
.
Denote
{\mathcal{V}}_{0}:=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}({\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}})
.
There exists a pair
(C^{u}_{\psi},C^{s}_{\psi})
of cone fields on
S_{0,\psi}
such that

(1)

C^{u}_{\psi}
contains the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}_{\psi}\cap S_{0,\psi}
in the interior and does not contain the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{\psi}\cap S_{0,\psi}
;

C^{s}_{\psi}
contains the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{\psi}\cap S_{0,\psi}
in the interior does not contains the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}_{\psi}\cap S_{0,\psi}
.

There exists integers
N_{0}
,
N_{1}
and
N_{\Sigma}
which satisfy the following properties. Let
p\in S_{0,\psi}
, and
n\geq 1
such that
q:=f_{0,\psi}^{n}(p)
is well defined.

(2)

Suppose that
p\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
. Then
f^{k}_{0,\psi}(p)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
for
k=-N_{0},\dots,-1,1,\dots,N_{0}
.

(3)

Suppose
p\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
,
q\in\Sigma_{\psi}
,
n\geq N_{1}
, and
f^{k}_{0,\psi}(p)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
for
k=1,\dots,n-1
.
Then:

•

(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\mathrm{in}}_{p})\subset\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T^{\Sigma}_{q})
,
and for all
v\in C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\mathrm{in}})
,
\|(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|
,

•

(f_{0,\psi}^{n})^{-1}_{*}C^{s}_{\psi}(q,T_{q}^{\Sigma})\subset\operatorname{int}C^{s}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\mathrm{in}})
,
and for all
v\in C^{s}_{\psi}(q,T_{q}^{\Sigma})
,
\|(f_{0,\psi}^{n})^{-1}_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|
.

(4)

Suppose
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
,
q\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
and
f^{k}_{0,\psi}(p)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
for
k=1,\dots,n-1
.
Then:

•

(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\mathrm{in}})\subset\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T_{q}^{\mathrm{in}})
,

•

(f_{0,\psi}^{n})^{-1}_{*}C^{s}_{\psi}(q,T_{q}^{\mathrm{in}})\subset\operatorname{int}C^{s}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\mathrm{in}})
,

Moreover, if both
p,q\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
, then

•

for all
v\in C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\mathrm{in}})
,
\|(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|
,

•

for all
v\in C^{s}_{\psi}(q,T_{q}^{\mathrm{in}})
,
\|(f_{0,\psi}^{n})^{-1}_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|
.

(5)

Suppose that
p\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
,
q\in\Sigma_{\psi}
,
n\geq N_{0}+1
,
there exists
1\leq m\leq n-N_{0}
such that
f^{m}_{0,\psi}(p)\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
and
f^{k}_{0,\psi}(p)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
for
k=1,\dots,m-1,m+1,\dots,n
.
Then:

•

(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\mathrm{in}})\subset\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T_{q}^{\Sigma})
,
and for all
v\in C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\mathrm{in}})
,
\|(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|
,

•

(f_{0,\psi}^{n})^{-1}_{*}C^{s}_{\psi}(q,T_{q}^{\Sigma})\subset\operatorname{int}C^{s}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\mathrm{in}})
,
and for all
v\in C^{s}_{\psi}(q,T_{q}^{\Sigma})
,
\|(f_{0,\psi}^{n})^{-1}_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|
.

(6)

Suppose
p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
,
q\in\Sigma_{\psi}
, and
f^{k}_{0,\psi}(p)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
for
k=1,\dots,n-1
.
Then:

•

(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\Sigma})\subset\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T_{q}^{\Sigma})
,

•

(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}^{-1}C^{s}_{\psi}(q,T_{q}^{\Sigma})\subset\operatorname{int}C^{s}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\Sigma})
.

Moreover if
n\geq N_{\Sigma}
, then :

•

for all
v\in C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\Sigma})
,
\|(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|
,

•

for all
v\in C^{s}_{\psi}(q,T_{q}^{\Sigma})
,
\|(f_{0,\psi}^{n})^{-1}_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|
.

(7)

Suppose
p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
,
q\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
and
f^{k}_{0,\psi}(p)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
for
k=1,\dots,n-1
.
Then:

•

(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\Sigma})\subset\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T_{q}^{\mathrm{in}})
and,

•

(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}^{-1}C^{s}_{\psi}(q,T_{q}^{\mathrm{in}})\subset\operatorname{int}C^{s}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\Sigma})
.

(8)

Suppose that
p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
,
q\in\Sigma_{\psi}
,
n\geq N_{0}+1
, there exists
1\leq m\leq n-N_{0}
such that
f_{0,\psi}^{m}(p)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
, and
f^{k}(p)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
for
k=1,\dots,m-1,m+1,\dots,n-1
.
Then:

•

(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\Sigma})\subset\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T_{q}^{\Sigma})
,
and for all
v\in C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\Sigma})
,
\|(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|
,

•

(f_{0,\psi}^{n})^{-1}_{*}C^{s}_{\psi}(q,T_{q}^{\Sigma})\subset\operatorname{int}C^{s}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\Sigma})
, and
for all
v\in C^{s}_{\psi}(q,T_{q}^{\Sigma})
,
\|(f_{0,\psi}^{n})^{-1}_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|
.

(9)

Suppose
p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
,
q\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
, and there exist
m\geq N_{1}
, and integers
1\leq k_{1}<\dots<k_{m}=n
such that
and
f^{k_{1}}(p),\dots,f^{k_{m}}(p)\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}

and
f^{k}(p)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
for
k\neq k_{1},\dots,k_{m}
,
1\leq k\leq n-1
.
Then:

•

(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\Sigma})\subset\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T_{q}^{\mathrm{in}})
, and
for all
v\in C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\Sigma})
,
\|(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|
,

•

(f_{0,\psi}^{n})^{-1}_{*}C^{s}_{\psi}(q,T_{q}^{\mathrm{in}})\subset\operatorname{int}C^{s}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\Sigma})
, and
for all
v\in C^{s}_{\psi}(q,T_{q}^{\mathrm{in}})
,
\|(f_{0,\psi}^{n})^{-1}_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|
.

Lifting the return map in
P

For the proof, we will study lifts in
P
of the iterates
f_{0,\psi}^{n}
.
If a lift
\tilde{f}_{0,\psi}^{n}
maps a cone field
C
on
\Sigma\cup P^{\mathrm{in}}
on its interior in the neighborhood of a point
\tilde{p}
, and expands the norm of the vectors of
C
by a factor
2
for the metric
g
, then we can project into
P_{\psi}
and we have the same result for the action
f_{0,\psi}^{n}
on the cone field
C_{\psi}
for the metric
g_{\psi}
in
P_{\psi}
, in the neighborhood of
p=\pi_{\psi}(\tilde{p})
.
The lifts
\tilde{f}_{0,\psi}^{n}
are compositions of the crossing and return maps of the
X
-flow and the gluing map
\psi
.

Notation 5.1.5
.

Define

•

f_{\Sigma}\colon\Sigma\to\Sigma
the first return map of the flow of
X
on
\Sigma

•

f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}\to\Sigma
the crossing map of the flow of
X
from
P^{\mathrm{in}}
to
\Sigma

•

f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\colon\Sigma\to P^{\mathrm{out}}
the crossing map of the flow of
X
from
\Sigma
to
P^{\mathrm{out}}

•

f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}\to P^{\mathrm{out}}
the crossing map of the flow of
X
from
P^{\mathrm{in}}
to
P^{\mathrm{out}}

We have the following

Claim 5.1.6
.

•

If
p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
and
f_{0,\psi}(p)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
then a lift of
f_{0,\psi}
is
f_{\Sigma}\colon\Sigma\to\Sigma
.

•

If
p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
and
f_{0,\psi}(p)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
then a lift of
f_{0,\psi}
is
\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}:\Sigma\to P^{\mathrm{in}}
.

•

If
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
and
f_{0,\psi}(p)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
then a lift of
f_{0,\psi}
is
f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}:P^{\mathrm{in}}\to\Sigma
.

•

If
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
and
f_{0,\psi}(p)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
then a lift of
f_{0,\psi}
is
\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}:P^{\mathrm{in}}\to P^{\mathrm{in}}

Section summary

We will look for cones on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
and on
\Sigma
such that the lifts of the iterates
f^{n}_{0,\psi}
satisfy the cone fields condition in the neighborhood of some points.
The cones of Proposition
5.1.4
will be the projections in
P_{\psi}
of the cones on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
and on
\Sigma
.
The difficulty of the proof is to fix the parameters and the cones in the right order according to their relative dependence, in order to have the different compatibility conditions.
The steps are done in the following order.
Denote
\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}({\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})
.

•

In Subsection
5.2
,
we show the existence of the parameters
\epsilon=\epsilon_{0}
,
\lambda=\lambda_{0}
and the cone fields
(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}},C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}})
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
which will allow us to obtain the expansion property of Item
4
of Proposition
5.1.4
, and this uniformly in
\delta
.
Notice that we will deal separately with the invariance property of
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
and the expansion property on
(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})_{\psi}
of Item
4
.
The last one requires to choose correctly the cones on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
and the parameters
\lambda
and
\epsilon
(uniformly in
\delta
), which is the work of this subsection.
The first one requires to choose correctly
\delta
, which will be the work of Subsection
5.5
.

We consider here the points
p\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})_{\psi}
whose future orbit by
X_{\psi}
intersects again
(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})_{\psi}
in
q=f^{n}_{0,\psi}(p)
(Figure
44(c)
).
We therefore consider a well chosen restriction of the return map of the flow of
X_{\psi}
on the section
(P^{\mathrm{in}})_{\psi}
.
A lift in
P
of
f^{n}_{0,\psi}
in the neighborhood of
p
is
\psi f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}\to P^{\mathrm{in}}
, but for practical reasons we will study the composition

f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}=\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}\to P^{\mathrm{in}}

which is its conjugate by
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
.
We show that
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}
satisfies the cone fields condition for a good choice of cones
(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}},C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}})
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
and of parameters
\lambda
and
\epsilon
on the complementary of
{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
, and this for all
\delta
.
The result is summarized in Proposition
5.2.1
.
The key point is that one can choose
\lambda
large enough to have arbitrarily strong expansion and contraction of two transverse directions by
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}
, uniformly in the parameters
\epsilon
and
\delta
.

The cone fields
(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}},C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}})
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
and the parameters
\epsilon=\epsilon_{0}
,
\lambda=\lambda_{0}
will be fixed for the rest of the proof.

•

In Subsection
5.3
, we show the existence of cone fields
(C^{u}_{\Sigma},C^{s}_{\Sigma})
on
\Sigma
which will allow us to obtain Item
7
, and which determine an integer
N_{\Sigma}
which satisfies Item
6
of Proposition
5.1.4
, uniformly in
\delta
.

–

Item
7
concerns points
p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
whose future orbit by
X_{\psi}
intersects a first time
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
in
q=f^{n}_{0,\psi}(p)
(Figure
45(c)
).
A lift in
P
of
f^{n}_{0,\psi}
in the neighborhood of
p
is the composition
\psi f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}f_{\Sigma}^{i}\colon\Sigma\to P^{\mathrm{in}}
.
We will study

\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}f_{\Sigma}^{i}

which is its conjugate by
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
(
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
is the identity outside
P^{\mathrm{in}}
).
We show the existence of two cone fields
(C^{u}_{\Sigma},C^{s}_{\Sigma})
on
\Sigma
such that a compatibility criterion between the cones on
\Sigma
and the cones on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
is satisfied for the composition
\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}f_{\Sigma}^{i}
, and this uniformly in
\delta
.
This is Proposition
5.3.3
.
The key point is that the projection
\pi\colon T\Sigma\to TP^{\mathrm{out}}
parallel to
{\mathbb{R}}.X
between
\Sigma
and
P^{\mathrm{out}}
(or
P^{\mathrm{in}}
) has a uniformly bounded effect on the slope of the cones (Lemma
5.3.5
).

–

Item
6
concerns the points
p
in
\Sigma_{\psi}
whose future orbit by
X_{\psi}
intersects
N_{\Sigma}
consecutive times
\Sigma_{\psi}
(Figure
45(b)
).
A lift in
P
of
f^{n}_{0,\psi}
in the neighborhood of
p
is
f^{n}_{\Sigma}\colon\Sigma\to\Sigma
.
Once the cones are fixed on
\Sigma
containing the suitable tangent directions, the hyperbolicity of
f_{\Sigma}
ensures that an iterate greater than a uniform integer
N_{\Sigma}
satisfies the cone fields condition.

The cones
(C^{u}_{\Sigma},C^{s}_{\Sigma})
on
\Sigma
and the integer
N_{\Sigma}
will be fixed for the rest of the proof.
The pairs
(C^{u}_{\Sigma},C^{s}_{\Sigma})
and
(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}},C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}})
are built so as to check Item
1
of Proposition
5.1.4
.

•

In Subsection
5.4
, we show the existence of an integer
N_{0}
which will allow us to obtain Items
8
and
5
of Proposition
5.1.4
, and this uniformly in
\delta
.

–

Item
8
concerns points
p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
whose future orbit by
X_{\psi}
intersects
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
once and then
N_{0}
consecutive times
\Sigma_{\psi}
(Figure
46(a)
).
A lift in
P
of
f^{n}_{0,\psi}
in the neighborhood of
p
is the composition map

f_{\Sigma}^{j},f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{i}\colon\Sigma\to\Sigma
.

We show that the pair
(C^{u}_{\Sigma},C^{s}_{\Sigma})
on
\Sigma
fixed in the previous section form a pair of strictly invariant cone fields under this map, for an integer
j
greater than a certain uniform integer
N_{0}
, uniformly in
\delta
.
This property is summarized in Proposition
5.4.1
.
The key point is that the contraction of the composition map

f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}

is bounded below uniformly in
\delta
(Lemma
5.4.3
).

–

Item
5
is shown uniformly in
\delta
.
It concerns points
p\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})_{\psi}
whose future orbit by
X_{\psi}
intersects once
(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})_{\psi}
and then
N_{0}
consecutive times
\Sigma_{\psi}
(Figure
45(a)
).
A lift in
P
of
f^{n}_{0,\psi}
in the neighborhood of
p
is the composition map

f_{\Sigma}^{j}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{i}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}\to\Sigma
.
We will study the composition

f_{\Sigma}^{j}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{i}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}

which is its conjugate by
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
.
We show that it satisfies the cone fields condition for the pair
(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}\cup C^{u}_{\Sigma},C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}\cup C^{s}_{\Sigma})
for an integer
j
greater than a certain uniform integer
N_{0}
, and this uniformly in
\delta
.
This is Proposition
5.4.4
.
The key result is that the contraction along an orbit of the flow of
X
from
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
to
\Sigma\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
is bounded below, uniformly in
\delta
(Lemma
5.4.5
).

The integer
N_{0}
is now fixed for the rest of the proof.

•

In Subsection
5.5
, we show the existence of a parameter
\delta_{0}
which will allow us to obtain Item
2
and Item
4
of Proposition
5.1.4
.

–

Item
2
concerns the
X_{\psi}
orbits crossing
({\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}=(\psi^{\mathrm{in}}({\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}))_{\psi}
(Figure
44(a)
).
We will study the composition maps

f_{\Sigma}^{n}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
and
f_{\Sigma}^{-n}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}^{-1}\,(\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}})^{-1}
from
P^{\mathrm{in}}
to
\Sigma
.
We show that for
\delta
small enough, the maps restricted to
P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
are well defined for arbitrarily big
n
(Claim
5.5.2
).
A key argument is that a point
p
close enough to
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
in
P
has an orbit which intersects the section
\Sigma
an arbitrarily large number of times in the past and in the future, as long as it is well defined.
We will use a key lemma which states that the action of
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
on the distance to the set
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
is bounded uniformly in
\delta
(Lemma
5.3.6
).
It then suffice to choose
\delta
small enough depending on the integer
N_{0}
fixed at the last section.

–

Item
4
concerns points
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
whose future orbits by
X_{\psi}
intersects
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
a first time at point
q=f^{n}_{0,\psi}(p)
.
We know from Subsection
5.2
that if
p
and
q
are in
(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})_{\psi}
then the cones are invariant and expanded by
f^{n}_{0,\psi}
(uniformly in
\delta
).
It remains to check the cone invariance if
p
or
q
are in
(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})_{\psi}
.
We will study the map
\varphi\,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{n}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}\to P^{\mathrm{in}}
.
We show that for a uniformly large
n
, the cones
(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}},C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}})
are invariant (Lemma
5.5.3
), and this uniformly in
\delta
.
We then use our last point which states that the map restricted to
P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
at the start or at the destination are well defined for a given
n
if
\delta
is small enough.

The parameter
\delta_{0}
is now fixed for the rest of the proof.

•

In Subsection
5.6
, we show the existence of an integer
N_{1}
which will allow us to have Items
3
and
9
of Proposition
5.1.4
.

At this point, the parameters
({\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}{})=(\lambda_{0},\epsilon_{0},\delta_{0})
are now fixed as well as the cone fields
(C^{u}_{\Sigma},C^{s}_{\Sigma})
and
(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}},C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}})
on
\Sigma
and on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
.
Denote
{\mathcal{V}}_{0}=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}({\mathcal{V}}_{0})
.

–

Item
3
concerns the points
p\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
whose future orbit by
X_{\psi}
intersects
N_{1}
consecutive times the section
\Sigma_{\psi}
(Figure
44(b)
).
A lift in
P
of
f^{n}_{0,\psi}
in the neighborhood of
p
is the composition map
f^{j}_{\Sigma},f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}\to\Sigma
.
We will study the composition map

f^{j}_{\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}

which is its conjugate by
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
.
We show that it satisfies the cone fields condition for the pair
(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}\cup C^{u}_{\Sigma},C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}\cup C^{s}_{\Sigma})
for an integer
j
greater than some uniform integer
N_{1}
.
The key points are the uniform transversality between the vector field
X
and the surface
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0}
, the hyperbolicity of
f_{\Sigma}
, and the fact that the cone fields
(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}},C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}})
and
(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}\cup C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}})
contain “compatible” directions.

–

Item
9
concerns the points
p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
whose future orbit by
X_{\psi}
intersects
(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
a number
N_{1}
of times (Figure
46(b)
).
A lift in
P
of
f^{n}_{0,\psi}
in the neighborhood of
p
is the composition maps
(f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\psi)^{j}f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}f_{\Sigma}^{i}\colon\Sigma\to P^{\mathrm{in}}{}
.
We will study the conjugate by
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
.
We show that it satisfies the cone fields condition for the pair
(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}\cup C^{u}_{\Sigma},C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}\cup C^{s}_{\Sigma})
, for an integer
j
greater than some uniform integer
N_{1}
.
The key points are the hyperbolicity of
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}
, and the bounded effect of the composition map
\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}f_{\Sigma}^{i}
on the slope of the cones and on the norm of the vectors.

The integer
N_{1}
will be fixed for the rest of the proof.

•

In Subsection
5.7
, we show each item of Proposition
5.1.4
using the results of the previous subsections.
Each previous result is true for the parameter choice
\lambda=\lambda_{0}
,
\epsilon=\epsilon_{0}
and
\delta=\delta_{0}
.
We have to restore the conjugation defect by
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
and then project into
P_{\psi}
.
In other words, we push by
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
the cones and the metric on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
and project on
P_{\psi}
these cones and this metric, as well as the cones and the metric on
\Sigma
.
All the results follow afterwards.

5.2.
Parameters
\lambda_{0}
,
\epsilon_{0}
and cone fields on
P^{\mathrm{in}}

In this subsection
we show the existence of the parameters
\lambda=\lambda_{0}
,
\epsilon=\epsilon_{0}
and the cone fields on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
which will allow us to obtain the expansion property of Item
4
of Proposition
5.1.4
, and this uniformly in
\delta
.
This item concerns the points
p\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})_{\psi}
whose future orbit by
X_{\psi}
intersects again
(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})_{\psi}
.
Define the following composition map:

(5.1)

f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}:=\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}\to P^{\mathrm{in}}

Let us show the following

Proposition 5.2.1
.

There exist parameters
\lambda_{0}>1
,
\epsilon_{0}>0
,
a pair of continuous (closed) cone fields
(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}},C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}})
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
,
there exist
K>0
,
k>0
,
k^{\prime}>0

such that for all
\delta>0
,
if
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}=\psi_{\lambda_{0},\epsilon_{0},\delta}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}=\psi_{\lambda_{0},\epsilon_{0},\delta}^{\mathrm{out}}
are the diffeomorphisms given by Proposition
4.1.2
and
4.1.3
, then

(1)

(directions)

the cone field
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
:

•

is contained in a
(K,{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/\,(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field,

•

contains a
(k,{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/\,(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field,

•

contains a
(k^{\prime},\ (\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}/\,\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}})
-cone field.

and the cone field
C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}
:

•

is contained in a
(K,\ (\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field.

•

contains a
(k,\ (\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field.

•

contains a
(k^{\prime},\ \varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}/\,(\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}})
-cone field.

(2)

(invariance and expansion)

For any
p\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})\cap f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{-1}(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})
,

•

\left(f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}\right)_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(p)\subset\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}(p))
, and for all
v\in C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(p)
,
\|(f_{\mathrm{in},\psi})_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|

•

\left(f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{-1}\right)_{*}C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}(f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}(p))\subset\operatorname{int}C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}(p)
, and for all
v\in C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}(f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}(p))
,
\|(f_{\mathrm{in},\psi})^{-1}_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|

Figure 47
.
Decomposition of the map
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}
and action on the cone field
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
on
(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})

See Figure
47
.
We will only show the existence of the unstable cone field
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
.
The proof to find a stable cone field is symmetric up to reversing the flow.
We do not require that these cones are disjoint, or complementary, so the two proofs are independent.
To show the existence of an unstable cone field for
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}
it is enough to find two transverse directions (not necessarily invariant), one uniformly expanded and the other uniformly contracted by
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}
.
A cone field
C
which contains the expanded direction and its image by
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}
in its interior, and whose adhesion does not contain the contracted direction is a good candidate.
A strong enough expansion and contraction factor ensures that such a cone field will be an unstable cone field, in other words strictly invariant and expanded by
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}
.

The following lemma gives us the two transverse directions which are respectively expanded and contracted by a factor proportional to
\lambda
(respectively
\lambda^{-1})
, on the complementary of a
\delta
-neighborhood of
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
and its reciprocal image, and this uniformly in
\lambda,\epsilon,\delta
.

Lemma 5.2.2
.

There exists a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}{}>0
such that for any
\lambda,\epsilon,\delta
,

(1)

for all
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
, for all
v\in T_{p}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
,

\|(f_{\mathrm{in},\psi})_{*}v\|>\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\lambda\|v\|
;

(2)

for all
p\in f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{-1}(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})
, for all
v\in T_{p}(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
,

\|(f_{\mathrm{in},\psi})_{*}v\|<(\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\lambda)^{-1}\|v\|
.

We deduce the following corollary

Corollary 5.2.3
.

There exists a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
, such that for any
\lambda,\epsilon,\delta
, for any

p\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})\cap f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{-1}(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})
,
if
C\subset T_{p}P^{\mathrm{in}}
is a
(K,{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/\,(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
-cone, and
q=f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}(p)
, then:

(1)

(f_{\mathrm{in},\psi})_{*}(C)\subset\operatorname{int}C^{\prime}
where
C^{\prime}
is a
\left(\frac{K}{(\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\,\lambda)^{2}},\ \varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}/\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}\right)
-cone in
T_{q}P^{\mathrm{in}}
,

(2)

\|(f_{\mathrm{in},\psi})_{*}v\|\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\lambda K^{-1}\|v\|
for all
v\in C
.

Demonstration of Corollary
5.2.3
.

It is enough to check that the mapped directions are indeed those announced.
Recall that
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
preserves
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
(Proposition
4.1.2
, Item
2
),
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}\to P^{\mathrm{out}}
maps the pair
({\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}},{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
to the pair
({\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}},{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}})
, and
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
preserves
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
.
Thus we have:

\displaystyle(f_{\mathrm{in},\psi})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=(\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=(\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=(\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}

and

\displaystyle(f_{\mathrm{in},\psi})_{*}(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=(\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}\,(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=(\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}

The rest follows directly from Lemma
5.2.2
when standing on the intersection of
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
and its reciprocal image by
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}
.
∎

To show Lemma
5.2.2
, we need the following lemma, which states that the derivative of
\psi_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}^{\mathrm{out}}
in the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
is uniformly (in
\lambda,\epsilon,\delta
) bounded, and symmetrically, the derivative of
\psi_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}^{\mathrm{in}}
in the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
is uniformly (in
\lambda,\epsilon,\delta
) bounded.

Lemma 5.2.4
.

There exists a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
, such that for any
\lambda,\epsilon,\delta
, if
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}=\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}
and
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}
, then

•

for all
p\in P^{\mathrm{out}}
and
v\in T_{p}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
, we have

\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}\|v\|\leq\|\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{*}v\|\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\|v\|
;

•

for all
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
and
v\in T_{p}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
, we have

\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}\|v\|\leq\|\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}v\|\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\|v\|
.

Proof.

Let us show the lemma for
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
.
Let
v^{u}
be a tangent vector to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
and

w:=\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{*}(v^{u})=w^{s}+w^{u}\in T{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}\oplus T{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
.
From Proposition
4.1.3
, Item
3
, we know that
w
is in a
(\epsilon,{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}/,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}})
-cone, which means that

\|w^{s}\|\leq\epsilon\|w^{u}\|
.
From Item
2
, Proposition
4.1.3
, we know that
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
preserves the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
leaf-to-leaf, and it follows that
w^{u}=(H^{s,\mathrm{out}}_{\varsigma_{q},\varsigma_{p}})_{*}(v^{u})

where
H^{s,\mathrm{out}}_{\varsigma_{q},\varsigma_{p}}
is the holonomy map of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
between the leaf
\varsigma_{p}\in{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
passing through
p
and the leaf
\varsigma_{q}\in{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
passing through
q=\psi^{\mathrm{out}}(p)
.
As the support of
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
is contained in strips
B_{i}\subset P^{\mathrm{out}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
(Item
1
, Proposition
4.1.3
), we have
p,q\in B_{i}
and the maps
H^{s,\mathrm{out}}_{\varsigma_{q},\varsigma_{p}}
are holonomy maps in the strips
B_{i}
, we can apply Lemma
4.2.4
which states that the ratio
\frac{\|w^{u}\|}{\|v^{u}\|}
is uniformly bounded.
It follows that the ratio
\frac{\|w\|}{\|v^{u}\|}
is bounded uniformly in the parameters
\lambda,\epsilon,\delta
for
\epsilon
small enough.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.2.2
.

Recall that
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}=\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
.
From Proposition
4.1.2
, Item
5
and
2
, the composition
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
expands by a factor
\lambda
the vectors tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
and maps the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
to the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
.
According to the previous Lemma
5.2.4
, the derivative of
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
in the direction
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
is bounded by a constant uniform in
\lambda,\epsilon,\delta
.
Moreover,
\varphi
is a diffeomorphism with compact support, its differential is therefore uniformly bounded.
We deduce that there exists a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
uniform in
\lambda,\epsilon,\delta
which satisfies Item
1
of Lemma
5.2.2
.

Let us show Item
2
.
Let
p\in f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{-1}(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})
and
v\in T_{p}(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
.
Then
w=(f_{\mathrm{in},\psi})_{*}v
is a vector tangent to
T_{q}\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
at the point
q=f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}(p)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
.
This item is equivalent to showing that
(f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{-1})_{*}
expands the vector
w
by a factor uniformly proportional to
\lambda
.
We have
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{-1}=(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}\psi^{\mathrm{out}}(\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}})^{-1}\varphi^{-1}
.
We study the effect of each map on the vector
w\in T_{q}\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
.

•

\varphi^{-1}
is a compactly supported diffeomorphism, so its differential is uniformly bounded and maps the direction tangent to
\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
to the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
.
As
\varphi
is a normalized gluing map, it maps
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
on
P^{\mathrm{out}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
(Definition
2.1.13
, Item
1
).

•

According to Proposition
4.1.3
, Item
5
and
2
, the composition
(\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}})^{-1}
expands by a factor
\lambda
the vectors tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
on
P^{\mathrm{out}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
and maps the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
to the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
.

•

By the previous Lemma
5.2.4
, the derivative of
(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}
in the direction
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
is bounded by a constant uniform in
\lambda,\epsilon,\delta
,

We deduce that
(f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{-1})_{*}
expands the vector
w
by a factor uniformly proportional to
\lambda
, so there exists a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
uniform in
\lambda,\epsilon,\delta
which satisfies Item
2
of Lemma
5.2.2
.
∎

Let us show the following lemma, which allows us to separate the suitable directions by a cone field (Figure
48
).

Lemma 5.2.5
.

There exists a closed cone field
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
and a parameter
\epsilon_{0}
such that for any
\lambda>1
,
\delta>0
, if
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}=\psi_{\lambda,\epsilon_{0},\delta}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}=\psi_{\lambda,\epsilon_{0},\delta}^{\mathrm{out}}
,
then

•

\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
contains the directions tangent to
{\psi^{\mathrm{in}}}^{-1}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
and
\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
,

•

C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
does not contain the direction tangent to
(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
,

•

C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
is the bisector
({\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field on
P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
.

Figure 48
.
Cone field
C^{u,\mathrm{in}}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
in the neighborhood of
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}

Let us start by showing the following

Lemma 5.2.6
.

There exists a closed cone field
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
such that :

•

\operatorname{int}(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}})
contains the directions tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
and
\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
,

•

C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
does not contain the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
,

•

C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
is the bisector
({\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field on
P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
.

Proof of Lemma
5.2.6
.

Recall that the gluing map
\varphi
is normalized for the normalized block
(P,X)
equipped with the pair of affine invariant foliations
({\mathcal{G}}^{s},{\mathcal{G}}^{u})
, hence
\varphi_{*}({\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}})
is transverse to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
(Item
2
, Definition
2.1.13
).
Moreover
\varphi
is the reflection in the normalized coordinate systems
({\mathcal{V}}_{i},\xi_{i})
, which implies that we have the relation
\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}={\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
and
\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}={\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
.
According to this remark and by transversality of
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
, there exists an open (thin) cone field around the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
, which does not contain either the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
or the direction tangent to
\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
.
Moreover, we can choose this cone field as a bisector of
({\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}})
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
.
The cone field
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
is the complementary cone field.
∎

Corollary 5.2.7
.

There exists
\hat{K}>0
,
\hat{k}>0
,
\hat{k}^{\prime}>0
, such that the field
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
:

•

is contained in a
(\hat{K},\ {\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field,

•

contains a
(\hat{k},\ {\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field.

•

contains a
(\hat{k}^{\prime},\ \varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}/\,\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}})
-cone field.

Proof.

P^{\mathrm{in}}
is not compact, but on
{\mathcal{V}}_{*}\cap P^{\mathrm{in}}
, the cone field
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
coincides with the
(1,{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})=(1,\ \varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}/,\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}})
-cone field.
The complementary of these neighborhoods is compact in
P^{\mathrm{in}}
, which allows us to conclude.
∎

We can now show Lemma
5.2.5
.

Proof of Lemma
5.2.5
.

According to Propositions
4.1.2
and
4.1.3
, we have
({\psi^{\mathrm{in}}})_{*}^{-1}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}={\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
, and the direction tangent to
({\psi^{\mathrm{in}}})_{*}^{-1}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
is in a
(\epsilon,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field.
Similarly
(\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}=\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
, and the direction tangent to
(\psi^{\mathrm{out}})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
is in a
(\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}},\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}})
-cone field of slope uniformly proportional to
\epsilon
(the uniform constant comes from the action of
\varphi
which is a compactly supported diffeomorphism).
Let
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
be the cone field given by Lemma
5.2.6
.
According to Corollary
5.2.7
,
it suffices to choose
\epsilon_{0}
small before
\hat{k}
and
\hat{k}^{\prime}
.
∎

Let
\epsilon=\epsilon_{0}
satisfying Lemma
5.2.5
.
Then

Corollary 5.2.8
.

There exists
K>0
,
k>0
,
k^{\prime}>0
, such that for any
\lambda>1,\delta>0
, if
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}=\psi_{\lambda,\epsilon_{0},\delta}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}=\psi_{\lambda,\epsilon_{0},\delta}^{\mathrm{out}}
, then
the cone field
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
:

•

is contained in a
(K,\ {\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/\,(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field,

•

contains a
(k,\ {\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/\,(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field,

•

contains a
(k^{\prime},\ (\varphi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}/\,\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}})
-cone field.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of the first two items of Lemma
5.2.5
, which states that the directions tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}=(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
and to
(\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
are contained in the interior of
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
and the direction tangent to
(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}
is everywhere disjoint from
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
.
∎

The cone field
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
contains the suitable directions for
\epsilon=\epsilon_{0}
, and this is true for any choice of
\lambda
and
\delta
.
It remains to show that we can choose
\lambda
large enough so that the cone field
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
is
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}
-invariant and its vectors are expanded by
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}
on the well-chosen domain, and this uniformly in
\delta
.
We refer to Figure
49
for an image of the action of
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}
on the cone field
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
.

Figure 49
.
Directions in the cone field
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
and
(f_{\mathrm{in},\psi})_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}

Proof of Proposition
5.2.1
.

We fix
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
given by Lemma
5.2.5
.
Item
1
of Proposition
5.2.1
is satisfied by Corollary
5.2.8
and let
K>0
and
k^{\prime}>0
be the constants of the corollary.
Let
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
be a constant which satisfies Corollary
5.2.3
.
Then for
\lambda
large enough compared to
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}Kk^{-1}
, the field
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
is strictly invariant by
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}
and its vectors are uniformly expanded on
(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})\cap f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{-1}(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})
, and this uniformly in
\delta
.
The proof for the existence of a stable cone field
C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}
is symmetric.
∎

We fix the parameters
\lambda_{0}
,
\epsilon_{0}
and a pair of cone fields
(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}},C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}})
satisfying Proposition
5.2.1
.

5.3.
Cone fields on
\Sigma
and integer
N_{\Sigma}

In this section we show the existence of cone fields on
\Sigma
which will allow us to obtain Item
7
, and which determine an integer
N_{\Sigma}
which satisfies Item
6
of Proposition
5.1.4
.
Recall that Item
7
concerns the points
p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
whose future orbit by
X_{\psi}
intersects a first time
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
in
q=f^{n}_{0,\psi}(p)
,
and Item
6
concerns the points
p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
whose future orbit by
X_{\psi}
intersects
n
consecutive times
\Sigma_{\psi}
.
Recall that the parameters
\lambda=\lambda_{0}
and
\epsilon=\epsilon_{0}
were fixed in the previous Subsection
5.2
, as well as the cone fields
(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}},C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}})
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
. The results will be uniform in
\delta
.
We study the maps:

f^{i}_{\Sigma}\colon\Sigma\to\Sigma,\noindent\quad\mbox{ and }\noindent\quad\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}f_{\Sigma}^{i}\colon\Sigma\to P^{\mathrm{in}}

Recall that
\Sigma
is an affine section of
(P,X)
(Definition
2.1.4
, Item
1
).
Up to restrict
\Sigma
, there exists a coordinate system on
\Sigma
in which the map
f_{\Sigma}\colon\Sigma\to\Sigma
is an hyperbolic diagonal affine map, wherever it is well defined.
Let
T\Sigma=E^{uu}\oplus E^{ss}
be the hyperbolic splitting of
f
on
\Sigma
, and

(\zeta^{s},\zeta^{u})
the pair of stable and unstable foliation of
f_{\Sigma}
on
\Sigma
.
The metric
g_{\Sigma}
induced by the ambient metric
g
on
\Sigma
is adapted to the hyperbolic splitting of
f_{\Sigma}
.

Notation 5.3.1
.

Let
\mu>1
be a uniform factor of expansion of
f_{\Sigma}
, in other words such that

•

for all
v\in E^{uu}
,
n\geq 0
,
\|(f_{\Sigma}^{n})_{*}v\|\geq\mu^{n}\|v\|

•

for all
v\in E^{ss}
,
n\geq 0
,
\|(f_{\Sigma}^{-n})_{*}v\|\geq\mu^{n}\|v\|

It follows that any closed cone field
C^{u}
of constant slope whose interior
\operatorname{int}C^{u}
contains the direction
E^{uu}
and is disjoint from the direction
E^{ss}
is strictly invariant under the action of
f_{\Sigma}
(at the first iteration) and its vectors are uniformly expanded by a (uniformly) large enough iteration
f^{n}_{\Sigma}
, which depends on the slope of the cone.
In other words, we have:

Claim 5.3.2
.

For any
K
, if
C^{u}
is a
(K,\ \zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field on
\Sigma
, then

•

(f_{\Sigma})_{*}C^{u}
is contained in a
(K\mu^{-2},\ \zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field,

•

there exists a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}=\operatorname{\textit{cst}}(K)>0
such that
\forall v\in C^{u}
,
\forall n\geq 0
,
\|(f^{n}_{\Sigma})_{*}v\|\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\mu^{n}\|v\|
.

Similarly, if
C^{s}
is a
(K,\ \zeta^{s}/\,\zeta^{u})
-cone field on
\Sigma
, then

•

(f_{\Sigma})^{-1}_{*}C^{s}
is contained in a
(K\mu^{-2},\ \zeta^{s}/\,\zeta^{u})
-cone field,

•

there exists a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}=\operatorname{\textit{cst}}(K)>0
such that
\forall v\in C^{s}
,
\forall n\geq N
,
\|(f^{-n}_{\Sigma})_{*}v\|\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\mu^{n}\|v\|
.

We will choose the slope
K
of a cone field
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
of
(\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field such that we have a compatibility of cones on
\Sigma
and cones on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
for the composition map
\varphi,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{n}:\Sigma\to P^{\mathrm{in}}
, for any
n\geq 0
, when it is well defined (Figure
50
).
It will then suffice to choose an iterate of
f_{\Sigma}
large enough for the vectors of
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
to be uniformly expanded.
Denote
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\delta}=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda_{0},\epsilon_{0},\delta}
and
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta}=\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\lambda_{0},\epsilon_{0},\delta}
.
This is sum up in the following main proposition.

Proposition 5.3.3
.

There exist a pair of cone field
(C^{u}_{\Sigma},C^{s}_{\Sigma})
on
\Sigma

(1)

(direction)

There exist constant
K^{u}>0
and
K^{s}>0
such that:

C^{u}_{\Sigma}
is a
(K^{u},\ \zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field, and
C^{s}_{\Sigma}
is a
(K^{s},\ \zeta^{s}/\,\zeta^{u})
-cone field

(2)

(compatibility on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
)

for all
\delta>0
, if
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}=\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta}
, and for all
p\in\Sigma
such that the point
q=\varphi\,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{n}\,(p)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
is well defined for some integer
n\geq 0
, then:

(\varphi\,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{n}\,)_{*}C^{u}_{\Sigma}(p)\subset\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(q)
, and

(\varphi\,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{n}\,)^{-1}_{*}C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}(q)\subset\operatorname{int}C^{s}_{\Sigma}(p)

(3)

(invariance and expansion on
\Sigma
)

there exists an integer
N_{\Sigma}\geq 0
such that

(f_{\Sigma})_{*}C^{u}_{\Sigma}\subset\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\Sigma},\ \mbox{and}\ \forall v\in C^{u}_{\Sigma}
,
\forall n\geq N_{\Sigma}
,
\|(f_{\Sigma}^{n})_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|
,

(f_{\Sigma})^{-1}_{*}C^{s}_{\Sigma}\subset\operatorname{int}C^{s}_{\Sigma},\ \mbox{and}\ \forall v\in C^{s}_{\Sigma}
,
\forall n\geq N_{\Sigma}
,
\|(f_{\Sigma}^{n})^{-1}_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|

Figure 50
.
Trajectory of a cone
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
under the map
\varphi,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{n}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}

Remark 5.3.4
.

There is no requirement that the fields
C^{s}_{\Sigma}
and
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
be complementary to each other.
For the lemma to be true, the slope
K^{s}
of
C^{s}_{\Sigma}
will be large in front of 1, and the two cone fields can have a large intersection.
The important point of this lemma is that the slope
K^{s}
and
K^{u}
is independent of
\delta
.

Recall that the map
f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}:\Sigma\to P^{\mathrm{out}}
is the flow of
X
from the section
\Sigma
to the boundary
P^{\mathrm{out}}
.
In order to show this lemma we need to show that we keep a control on the slope of the cones when we apply
f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\colon\Sigma\to P^{\mathrm{out}}
.
This is the following lemma, which is symmetric for
f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}
and
f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}
.

Lemma 5.3.5
.

There exists a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
such that for all
K>0
,

•

if
C^{u}
is a
(K,\ \zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field on
\Sigma
, then
(f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma})_{*}C^{u}
is contained in a
(\operatorname{\textit{cst}}K,\ {\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}})
-cone field on
P^{\mathrm{out}}
.

•

if
C^{s}
is a
(K,\ {\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}})
-cone field on
P^{\mathrm{out}}
, then
(f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma})^{-1}_{*}C^{s}
is contained in a
(\operatorname{\textit{cst}}K,\ \zeta^{s}/\,\zeta^{u})
-cone field on
\Sigma
.

Likewise,

•

if
C^{u}
is a
(K,\ {\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
, then
(f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}})_{*}C^{u}
is contained in a
(\operatorname{\textit{cst}}K,\ \zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field on
\Sigma
.

•

if
C^{s}
is a
(K,\ \zeta^{s}/\,\zeta^{u})
-cone field on
\Sigma
, then
(f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}})^{-1}_{*}C^{s}
is contained in a
(\operatorname{\textit{cst}}K,\ {\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field on
P^{\mathrm{out}}
.

We will just show the result for
f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}
.
This result is not trivial because
P^{\mathrm{out}}
is not uniformly transverse to the vector field, so the projection
TP\to TP^{\mathrm{out}}
parallel to
{\mathbb{R}}.X
has an unbounded effect on the norm of vectors.
The key point is that the surface
P^{\mathrm{out}}
is invariant by a symmetry which exchanges the strong stable direction
E^{ss}
and strong unstable direction
E^{uu}
in a neighborhood of
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
.
As a result, the effect of the projection on the norm of the vectors of
E^{uu}
and
E^{ss}
is the same, which ensures that the slope of a
(E^{uu}/\,E^{ss})
-cone is preserved.

Proof of Lemma
5.3.5
.

Let us show the first item. The proof of the others are analogous.
This lemma concerns the segments of orbits which intersect
\Sigma
then
P^{\mathrm{out}}
.
They are endowed with a hyperbolic splitting
E^{ss}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X\oplus E^{uu}
for the flow of
X
(Remark
3.4.3
).

First observe that the lemma is true for all orbits intersecting
P^{\mathrm{out}}
outside
{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
.
Indeed the sections
\Sigma
and
P^{\mathrm{out}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
are uniformly transverse to the vector field
X
, the flow of
X
is hyperbolic along these orbits, and the cones in question are uniformly far from the center-stable bundle.
It remains to check this for the orbits intersecting
P^{\mathrm{out}}
in a normalized coordinate system
({\mathcal{V}}_{i},\xi_{i})
.

Let
C^{u}
be a
(K,\ \zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone on
\Sigma
, and
v
a vector tangent to
\Sigma
at a point
p
.
Then
v=v^{uu}+v^{ss}+0\in E^{uu}\oplus E^{ss}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X

with
\|v^{ss}\|\leq K\|v^{uu}\|
.
The transport by the (linear) flow
X^{t}
during a time
t>0
contributes to expand exponentially the component
v^{uu}
and contract exponentially the component
v^{ss}
, and has no effect on the component
v^{X}=0
.
So the action of
X^{t}
contributes to close a
(E^{uu}/\,E^{ss})
-cone field exponentially fast, in other words to contract the slope of the cones exponentially fast.

It is therefore sufficient to look at the effect of the projection on
TP^{\mathrm{out}}
parallel to
X
of a
(K,\ E^{uu}/\,E^{ss})
-cone field transverse to the vector field
X
(contained in the plane
E^{uu}\oplus E^{ss}
) in a normalized coordinate system
({\mathcal{V}}_{i},\xi_{i}=(x,y,\theta))
.
Let us note this projection
\pi\colon E^{uu}\oplus E^{ss}\to TP^{\mathrm{out}}
.
To say that
\pi
maps a
(K,\ E^{uu}/\,E^{ss})
-cone onto a
(K,\ {\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}})
-cone is equivalent to saying that

\pi
maps the decomposition
E^{uu}\oplus E^{ss}
onto
T{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}\oplus T{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
and that

|||\pi|_{E^{uu}}|||=|||\pi|_{E^{ss}}|||
.
The first assertion is true because by definition we have
T{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}=(E^{uu}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X)\cap TP^{\mathrm{out}}
, and
T{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}=(E^{ss}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X)\cap TP^{\mathrm{out}}
.
The second one is shown with the following argument.
By definition of a normalized block (Definition
2.1.4
, Item
4
), the sets
\Sigma
,
P^{\mathrm{out}}
and
{\mathbb{R}}.X
are invariant in
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
by the symmetry
s\colon(x,y,\theta)\mapsto(y,x,\theta)
, so
we have the commutation relation
\pi\circ s_{*}=s_{*}\circ\pi
.
We have
\operatorname{Vect}(\partial_{x})=E^{ss}
and
\operatorname{Vect}(\partial_{y})=E^{uu}
,
and
s_{*}\partial_{x}=\partial_{y}
, so
s_{*}
permutes the directions
E^{uu}
and
E^{ss}
.
Finally,
s
is an isometry for the metric
g
which coincides with
dx^{2}+dy^{2}+d\theta^{2}
on
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
.
We conclude by writing that
\pi|_{E^{uu}}=\pi\circ s_{*}|_{E^{ss}}=s_{*}\circ\pi|_{E^{ss}}
.
∎

To measure the action of
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta}
, we need the following lemmas.
The first lemma states that the action of
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta}
on the distance to the orbit is bounded uniformly in
\delta
.

Lemma 5.3.6
.

There exists a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>1
such that for all
\delta>0
,

•

\forall\,p\in P^{\mathrm{out}}{}
,

\ \operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}\,\operatorname{dist}(p,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})\leq\operatorname{dist}(\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta}(p),{\mathcal{O}}_{*})\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\,\operatorname{dist}(p,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})
;

•

\forall\,p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{}
,

\ \operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}\,\operatorname{dist}(p,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})\leq\operatorname{dist}(\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\delta}(p),{\mathcal{O}}_{*})\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\,\operatorname{dist}(p,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})

Proof.

We show the lemma for
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta}
.
The diffeomorphism
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta}
is independent of the parameter
\delta
on the complementary of the union
{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
(Proposition
4.1.3
, Item
6
).
The complementary of
{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
in
P^{\mathrm{out}}
is compact, so the property is true for any point
p\in P^{\mathrm{out}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{*}
.
It is sufficient to check this property in a normalized coordinate system
({\mathcal{V}}_{i},\xi_{i}=(x,y,\theta))
.
Let
(x,\theta)
be the coordinates induced by
\xi_{i}
on
P^{\mathrm{out}}
.
A point
p=(x,\theta)
is in the closure of a strip
B
which is a connected component of
P^{\mathrm{out}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
.
According to Items
1
and
2
of Proposition
4.1.3
, the diffeomorphism
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
moves the point
p
along the leaf arc of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
inside
B
and this for any parameter
{\lambda,\epsilon,\delta}
.
Let
\sigma^{s}
be the leaf of
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}\cap B
passing through
p=(x,\theta)
.
Then
\sigma^{s}
intersects one of the boundary leave of strip
B
at a point
(x_{1},\theta_{1})
with
x_{1}>x/\sqrt{2}
and the other boundary leave of
B
at a point
(x_{2},\theta_{2})
with
x_{2}<\sqrt{2}x
(Remark
2.1.7
)
We conclude that the variation of the coordinate
x
of a point
p\in B
under the action of
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
is uniformly bounded.
The same is true for the distance to the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
.
∎

The second lemma states that the differentials of the maps
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta}
and
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\delta}
are bounded uniformly in
\delta
.

Lemma 5.3.7
.

There exists
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
, such that for all
\delta>0
and for all
v\in TP^{\mathrm{out}}{}
,

•

for all
v\in TP^{\mathrm{out}}
,
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}\|v\|\leq\|(\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta})_{*}v\|\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\,\|v\|

•

for all
v\in TP^{\mathrm{in}}
,
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}\|v\|\leq\|(\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\delta})_{*}v\|\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\,\|v\|
.

Proof.

We show the lemma for
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
.
Let
(\partial_{u},\partial_{s})
be a basis of unit vectors tangent to
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
respectively on
P^{\mathrm{out}}
.
The following fact is a direct consequence of Item
4
of Proposition
4.1.3
and of Lemma
5.2.4
.

Claim 5.3.8
.

The norm of the matrix of
(\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta})_{*}
in the basis
(\partial_{u},\partial_{s})
is uniformly (in
\delta
) bounded.

This fact is not enough to bound the action of the differential because the angle between the vectors
(\partial_{u},\partial_{s})
tends to 0 as we approach a periodic orbit of the boundary.
If
B
is a field of basis on
P^{\mathrm{out}}
, and
f\colon P^{\mathrm{out}}\to P^{\mathrm{out}}
is a diffeomorphism, we denote
M_{B}(d_{p}f)
the matrix of the differential from the basis
B(p)
on
T_{p}P^{\mathrm{out}}
to the basis
B(f(p))
on
T_{f(p)}P^{\mathrm{out}}
.
Let
M^{B^{\prime}}_{B,p}
be the change of basis matrix from basis
B
to basis
B^{\prime}
on
T_{p}P^{\mathrm{out}}
.
Let
B:=(\partial_{u},\partial_{s})
and
B^{\prime}
be a field of orthonormal basis on
TP^{\mathrm{out}}{}
.
We want to bound uniformly in
\delta
the operator

P^{\mathrm{out}}{}\ni p\mapsto M_{B^{\prime}}\left(d_{p}\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta}\right)\in GL_{2}({\mathbb{R}})
.
We have the following change of basis relation in
GL_{2}({\mathbb{R}})
, where
q:=\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta}(p)
:

\displaystyle{M_{B^{\prime}}\left(d_{p}\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta}\right)}={M^{B^{\prime}}_{B,q}}\times{M_{B}\left(d_{p}\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta}\right)}\times{M^{B}_{B^{\prime},p}}

As the operator

p\mapsto{M_{B}(d_{p}\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta})}

is bounded on
P^{\mathrm{out}}{}
uniformly in
\delta
(Claim
5.3.8
), it is sufficient to show that

p\mapsto{M^{B^{\prime}}_{B,q}}\times{M^{B}_{B^{\prime},p}}

is bounded on
P^{\mathrm{out}}{}
.
We can assume without loss of generality that the vector
\partial_{u}
is the first vector of the orthonormal basis
B^{\prime}
.
Therefore the product
{M^{B^{\prime}}_{B,q}}\times{M^{B}_{B^{\prime},p}}
is a
GL_{2}({\mathbb{R}})
matrix of determinant

\frac{\sin\theta_{B}(p)}{\sin\theta_{B}(q)}
,
where
\sin\theta_{B}
denotes the sine of the angle of the vectors of the basis
B
.
We want to bound this ratio uniformly.
We compare the angle

\measuredangle({\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}},{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}})
at the point
p\in P^{\mathrm{out}}
and the point
q=\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta}(p)
.

\displaystyle\frac{\measuredangle({\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}},{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}})_{p}}{\measuredangle({\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}},{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}})_{q}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\measuredangle({\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}},{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}})_{p}}{\operatorname{dist}(p,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})}\times\frac{\operatorname{dist}(q,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})}{\measuredangle({\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}},{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}})_{q}}\times\frac{\operatorname{dist}(p,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})}{\operatorname{dist}(\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta}(p),{\mathcal{O}}_{*})}

According to Lemma
3.4.6
and Lemma
5.3.6
, each of these ratios is bounded uniformly in
\delta
.
As explained above, Lemma
5.3.7
follows from this.
∎

We deduce the following lemma:

Lemma 5.3.9
.

There exists a constant
\hat{K}
, such that for any
\delta
,

•

(\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\delta})_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
is contained in a
(\hat{K},{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field.

•

(\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta})^{-1}_{*}C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}
is contained in a
(\hat{K},\ {\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}})
-cone field.

Proof.

Let us show the first item.
The differential of
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\delta}
is bounded uniformly in
\delta
(Lemma
5.3.7
) and

C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
is contained in a
(K,{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/\,(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}^{-1}({\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}))
-cone field (Proposition
5.2.1
, Item
1
).
As
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\delta}
preserves the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
(Proposition
4.1.2
, Item
2
), we conclude that
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\delta}
maps
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
inside a
({\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field of uniformly increased slope in
\delta
.

The second item is similar.
The field
C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}
is contained in a
(K,\ \varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}/\,(\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}})
-cone field
(Proposition
5.2.1
, Item
1
).
The diffeomorphism
\varphi^{-1}
is supported on a compact set so it maps a
(K,\ \varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}/\,(\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}})
-cone field of
P^{\mathrm{in}}
inside a
(K^{\prime},\ {\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}/\,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}})
-cone field where
K^{\prime}
is a uniform constant.
According to Lemma
5.3.7
,
the differential of
(\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta})^{-1}
is bounded on
P^{\mathrm{out}}
uniformly in
\delta
.
Since
(\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta})^{-1}
preserves the foliation
{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}
(Proposition
4.1.3
, Item
2
), we deduce that it maps a
(K^{\prime},\ {\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}/\,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}})
-cone field inside a
({\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}})
-cone field of slope bounded below, uniformly in
\delta
.
∎

We are now able to show Proposition
5.3.3
.

Proof of Proposition
5.3.3
.

Let us show the existence of the cone field
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
satisfying Item
2
.
The effects of the differential of
\varphi\,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{n}
on the slope of a
(\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone of
\Sigma
are the following.

•

f_{\Sigma}^{n}
maps a
(K,\ \zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field on a
(K,\ \ \zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field for
n\geq 0
(Claim
5.3.2
),

•

f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}
maps a
(K,\ \zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field on
\Sigma
inside a
({\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}})
-cone field, of slope
K_{1}\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}K
on
P^{\mathrm{out}}
(Lemma
5.3.5
),

•

the differential of
\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
is bounded on
P^{\mathrm{out}}
uniformly in
\delta
(Lemma
5.3.7
), so it maps a
(K_{1},{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}},{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field inside a
(K_{2},\ (\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}/\,\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}})
-cone field on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
,
K_{2}\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}K_{1}
and the constant is uniform in
\delta
.

To summarize, a
(K,\ \zeta^{u}/\zeta^{s})
-cone field on
\Sigma
is mapped by
\varphi\,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{n}
inside a
(K^{\prime},\ (\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}/\,\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}})
-cone field on
P^{\mathrm{out}}
, with
K^{\prime}\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}K
, where the constant is uniform in
\delta>0
.
Finally, according to Item
1
of Proposition
5.2.1
, it suffices to choose
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
a
(K^{u},\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field of small enough slope
K^{u}
(in front of the constant
k^{\prime}
given by the proposition).

The existence of the
C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}
field is shown symmetrically, and requires to choose a
(K^{s},\ \zeta^{s}/\,\zeta^{u})
-cone field of fairly large
K^{s}
opening.
Indeed:

•

(\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}})^{-1}
maps
C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}
inside a
(\hat{K},{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}})
-cone field (Lemma
5.3.9
),

•

f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}^{-1}
maps a
(\hat{K},{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{out}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}})
-cone field on
P^{\mathrm{out}}
inside a
(K_{1},\ \zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field on
\Sigma
,
K_{1}\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\hat{K}
(Lemma
5.3.5
),

•

f_{\Sigma}^{-n}
maps a
(K_{1},\zeta^{s}/\,\zeta^{u})
-cone field on a
(K_{1},\zeta^{s}/\,\zeta^{u})
-cone field for
n\geq 0
(Claim
5.3.2
)

To summarize, the field
C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}
is mapped by
(\varphi,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}f_{\Sigma}^{n})^{-1}
inside a
(K^{\prime},\ \zeta^{s}/\,\zeta^{u})
-cone field on
\Sigma
, where
K^{\prime}
is a constant uniform in
\delta>0
. So it suffices to choose
K^{s}=K^{\prime}
and
C^{s}_{\Sigma}
is the
(K^{s},\zeta^{s}/\,\zeta^{u})
-cone field.
The cone fields
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
and
C^{s}_{\Sigma}
have constant slope
K^{u}
and
K^{s}
, i.e. satisfies Item
1
.
Item
3
follows directly from Claim
5.3.2
.
∎

We fix a pair of cone fields
(C^{u}_{\Sigma},C^{s}_{\Sigma})
on
\Sigma
and integer
N_{\Sigma}\geq 0
which satisfies Proposition
5.3.3
.

Figure 51
.
Trajectory of a cone
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
under
f_{\Sigma}^{n}

5.4.
Integer
N_{0}

In this subsection, we show the existence of an integer
N_{0}
which will allow us to obtain Items
8
and
5
of Proposition
5.1.4
and this uniformly in
\delta
.
We recall that Item
8
concerns the points
p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
whose future orbit by
X_{\psi}
intersects once
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
and then
N_{0}
consecutive times
\Sigma_{\psi}
,
and Item
5
concerns points
p\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})_{\psi}
whose future orbit by
X_{\psi}
intersects a single time
(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})_{\psi}
then
N_{0}
consecutive times
\Sigma_{\psi}
.

From
\Sigma
to
\Sigma
through
P^{\mathrm{in}}

Let
p\in\Sigma
and
i,j\geq 0
such that the following map is well defined in the neighborhood of
p
:

f_{\Sigma}^{j}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi_{\delta}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{i}(p)\colon\Sigma\to\Sigma

We look for an integer
N_{0}
, such that if
j\geq N_{0}
, the composition map above satisfies the cone fields condition on
\Sigma
for the pair
(C^{u}_{\Sigma},C^{s}_{\Sigma})
.
We want to prove the following main proposition:

Proposition 5.4.1
.

There exists
N_{0}
such that for any
\delta>0
, if

f_{\Sigma}^{j}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{i}
is well defined in
p\in\Sigma
for some
i\geq 0
and
j\geq N_{0}
, then, denoting
q
the image of
p
,

•

\left(f_{\Sigma}^{j}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{i}\right)_{*}
maps
C^{u}_{\Sigma}(p)
inside
C^{u}_{\Sigma}(q)
and expands the norm of the vectors of
C^{u}_{\Sigma}(p)
by a factor 2,

•

\left(f_{\Sigma}^{j}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{i}\right)_{*}^{-1}
maps
C^{s}_{\Sigma}(q)
inside
C^{s}_{\Sigma}(p)
and expands the norm of the vectors of
C^{s}_{\Sigma}(q)
by a factor 2

Figure 52
.
Trajectory of a cone
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
under the map
f_{\Sigma}^{N_{0}}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{i}

We know that the iterates of
f_{\Sigma}
contribute to close
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
and expand its vectors exponentially (Claim
5.3.2
).
We will first show a result (Lemma
5.4.3
) which gives a lower bound for the contraction of the composition map
f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi_{\delta}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\colon\Sigma\to\Sigma
on the vectors of
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
and on the slope of a
(\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone, and this uniformly in
\delta
.
Let us start by estimating the effect of the crossing maps
f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\colon\Sigma\to P^{\mathrm{out}}
and
f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}\to\Sigma
.

Lemma 5.4.2
.

There exists a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
such that:

•

if
p\in\Sigma
such that
q=f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}(p)\in P^{\mathrm{out}}
is well defined, then for any
v\in T_{p}\Sigma

\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}\operatorname{dist}(q,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})^{-1}\leq\frac{\|(f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma})_{*}v\|}{\|v\|}\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\operatorname{dist}(q,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})^{-1}

•

if
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
such that
q=f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}(p)\in\Sigma
is well defined, then for any
v\in T_{p}P^{\mathrm{in}}

\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}\operatorname{dist}(p,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})\leq\frac{\|(f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}})_{*}v\|}{\|v\|}\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\operatorname{dist}(p,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})

Proof.

Let
p\in\Sigma
and
v\in T_{p}\Sigma
. Let
\tau=\tau(p)
be the time such that

X^{\tau}(p)=f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}(p)=q\in P^{\mathrm{out}}
.
The time
\tau
is uniformly bounded.
Let
\pi\colon TP\to TP^{\mathrm{out}}
be the projection on
TP^{\mathrm{out}}
parallel to
{\mathbb{R}}.X
.
We have
\left(f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\right)_{*}v=\pi(X^{\tau}_{*}v)
.
Since the time
\tau
is bounded, the norm
\|X^{\tau}_{*}v\|
and the angle
\measuredangle(X^{\tau}_{*}v,X)_{q}
are proportional to the norm
\|v\|
and the angle
\measuredangle(X,v)_{p}
, respectively, and the proportionality constant is uniform in
p
.
From this observation and from the general Claim
3.4.5
, there exists a uniform constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
such that

\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}\left|\frac{\sin\measuredangle(v,X)_{p}}{\sin\measuredangle(f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma})_{*}v,X)_{q}}\right|\leq\frac{\|\left(f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\right)_{*}v\|}{\|v\|}\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\left|\frac{\sin\measuredangle(v,X)_{p}}{\sin\measuredangle(f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma})_{*}v,X)_{q}}\right|

On the other hand, by uniform transversality we know that the angle between a vector
v\in T\Sigma
and
X
is uniformly bounded,
and that the angle between a vector
v\in T_{q}P^{\mathrm{out}}
and
X
is bounded below by a factor uniformly proportional to the distance
\operatorname{dist}(\cdot,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})
(Lemma
3.4.6
).
We deduce the existence of a constant such that

\frac{\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}}{\operatorname{dist}(q,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})}\leq\frac{\|\left(f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\right)_{*}v\|}{\|v\|}\leq\frac{\operatorname{\textit{cst}}}{\operatorname{dist}(q,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})}

The same arguments applied to
f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}^{-1}
allow us to prove the second item.
∎

The following lemma bounds uniformly in
\delta
the differential of the composition map
f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi_{\delta}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}
