(when well defined) and its action on the slope of the cone field
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
and
C^{s}_{\Sigma}
.

Lemma 5.4.3
.

(1)

the differentials of

f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi_{\delta}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\colon\Sigma\to\Sigma

are bounded uniformly in
\delta
;

(2)

there exists a constant
K>0
uniform in
\delta
such that

•

(f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi_{\delta}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma})_{*}C^{u}_{\Sigma}

is inside a
(K,\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field,

•

(f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi_{\delta}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma})^{-1}_{*}C^{s}_{\Sigma}

is inside a
(K,\zeta^{s}/\,\zeta^{u})
-cone field.

Proof.

Let
\psi=\psi_{\delta}
.
Let
p\in\Sigma
such that
q=f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\in\Sigma
is well defined.
Let us write

\frac{\|(f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma})_{*}v\|}{\|v\|}=\underset{(a)}{\underbrace{\frac{\|(f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma})_{*}v\|}{\|(\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma})_{*}v\|}}}\ \underset{(b)}{\underbrace{\frac{\|(\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma})_{*}v\|}{\|(f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma})_{*}v\|}}}\ \underset{(c)}{\underbrace{\frac{\|(f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma})_{*}v\|}{\|v\|}}}

Let us note
p_{1}=f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}(p)\in P^{\mathrm{out}}
and
p_{2}=\psi(p_{1})\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
.
There exists a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
such that

•

from Lemma
5.4.3
:
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}\operatorname{dist}(p_{2},{\mathcal{O}}_{*})\leq(a)\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\operatorname{dist}(p_{2},{\mathcal{O}}_{*})
;

•

from Lemma
5.3.7
:
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}\leq(b)\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}
;

•

from Lemma
5.4.2
:
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}\operatorname{dist}(p_{1},{\mathcal{O}}_{*})^{-1}\leq(c)\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\operatorname{dist}(p_{1},{\mathcal{O}}_{*})^{-1}
.

Up to change the constant, we have

\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}\frac{\operatorname{dist}(p_{2},{\mathcal{O}}_{*})}{\operatorname{dist}(p_{1},{\mathcal{O}}_{*})}\leq\frac{\|(f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma})_{*}v\|}{\|v\|}\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\frac{\operatorname{dist}(p_{2},{\mathcal{O}}_{*})}{\operatorname{dist}(p_{1},{\mathcal{O}}_{*})}

Since
p_{2}=\psi(p_{1})
, according to Lemma
5.3.6
, up to change the constant, we have a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}
uniform in
\delta
such that

\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}\leq\frac{|(f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma})_{*}v|}{|v|}\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}.

Let us show the second item.
We prove only the statement concerning the image of the cone field
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
, the one for
C^{s}_{\Sigma}
being symmetric.

•

We know from Proposition
5.3.3
that the composition map
\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\colon\Sigma\to P^{\mathrm{in}}
maps
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
inside
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
, and this for any
\delta>0
.

•

The differential of
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\delta}
is bounded uniformly in
\delta
(Lemma
5.3.7
) and

C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
is contained in a
(K,{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/,(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}^{-1}({\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}))
-cone field (Proposition
5.2.1
, Item
1
).
Since
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\delta}
preserves
{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}
, we deduce that
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\delta}
maps
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
inside a
({\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field with a slope bounded uniformly in
\delta
.

•

Using Lemma
5.3.5
, we deduce that the crossing map
f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}
maps the cone field
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
inside a
(\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field on
\Sigma
with a slope bounded uniformly in
\delta
.
∎

We are now ready to prove Proposition
5.4.1
.

Proof of proposition
5.4.1
.

We decompose
f_{\Sigma}^{j}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{i}
in three pieces:

•

f_{\Sigma}^{i}
leaves the cones
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
invariant and does not contract the vectors of
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
for
i\geq 0
(Proposition
5.3.3
, Item
3
),

•

f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}
maps
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
to a
(\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone on
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
, of uniformly bounded slope, and contracts the vectors of
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
by a factor uniformly bounded below, and the constants are uniform in
\delta
(Lemma
5.4.3
),

•

f_{\Sigma}^{j}
maps a
(\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field of bounded slope inside a
(\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field of arbitrarily small slope for
j
large enough, and expands its vectors arbitrarily strongly (Claim
5.3.2
).

We deduce the existence of an integer
N_{0}
which satisfies the first item if
j\geq N_{0}
.
The proof of the second item is symmetric.
∎

From
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
to
\Sigma
through
P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}

Now we want to show that the composition map

(5.2)

f_{\Sigma}^{j}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{i}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}\to\Sigma

satisfies the cone fields condition for the pair
(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}\cup C^{u}_{\Sigma},C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}\cup C^{s}_{\Sigma})

on the set of points
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
whose image by
\varphi\,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{i}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
belongs to
P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
.
We refer to Figure
53
.
This is the following proposition.

Figure 53
.
Trajectory of a cone
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
under the map
f_{\Sigma}^{j}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{i}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}\to\Sigma
crossing
P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}

Proposition 5.4.4
.

There exists
N_{0}
such that
for all
\delta>0
small enough,
the following condition is satisfied.
Let
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
such that the following are well defined for
i\geq 0
and
j\geq N_{0}

•

p^{\prime}=\varphi\,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{i}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}(p)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
, and

•

q=f_{\Sigma}^{j}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{i}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}(p)\in\Sigma
.

Then

•

f_{\Sigma}^{j}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{i}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
maps
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(p)
inside
C^{u}_{\Sigma}(q)
and expands the norm of the vectors of
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(p)
by a factor
2
,

•

(f_{\Sigma}^{j}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{i}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}
maps
C^{s}_{\Sigma}(q)
inside
C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}(p)
and expands the norm of the vectors of
C^{s}_{\Sigma}(q)
by a factor
2
.

The key result is that the contraction along an orbit of the flow of
X
from
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
to
\Sigma\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
is bounded below, uniformly in
\delta
(Lemma
5.4.5
).
More precisely, let us show that the composition
f_{\Sigma}^{n},f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
restricted to
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
at the domain and
\Sigma\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
at the destination has a contraction bounded below on the norm of the vectors of
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
, and the bound is uniform in
\delta
, moreover the map contributes to close a
(\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field.

Lemma 5.4.5
.

There exists a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
such that for any
\delta>0
uniformly small enough, if
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\delta}
,
for any
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
such that there exists
n\geq 1
with the image

q=f_{\Sigma}^{n}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}(p)\in\Sigma\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
well defined, then

•

(f_{\Sigma}^{n}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(p)\,\subset\,\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\Sigma}(q),\ \mbox{and}\ \forall\,v\in C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(p)
,
\|(f_{\Sigma}^{n}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}v\|\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\|v\|

•

(f_{\Sigma}^{n}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}_{*}C^{s}_{\Sigma}(q)_{,}\subset\,\operatorname{int}C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}(p),\ \mbox{and}\ \forall v\in C^{s}_{\Sigma}(q)
,
\ \|(f_{\Sigma}^{n}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}_{*}v\|\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\|v\|

Proof.

Let us show the first item, the proof of the second being similar.
In order not to introduce new constants, we can assume that each normalized neighborhood
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
contains a
1
-neighborhood of
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
.
We denote
{\mathcal{V}}_{1}
the union of
1
-neighborhoods of
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
in
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
.
The proof relies on the following key points.

•

The differential of
f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}
is bounded below by a factor uniformly proportional to
\operatorname{dist}(\cdot,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})
from Lemma
5.4.2
, so greater than
\delta
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
.

•

The orbit of any point
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
exits a
1
-neighborhood of
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
before exiting
P
.

•

The number
n
of crossings through
\Sigma
of the orbit of a point crossing from
{\mathcal{V}}_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{i})
to
{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}({\mathcal{O}}_{i})
is of the order of
\log_{2}(\delta^{-1})
.
The corresponding iteration
f_{\Sigma}^{n}
of the return map on
\Sigma
expands the norm of the vectors
v\in E^{uu}
by a factor greater than
\delta^{-1}
(up to a uniform multiplicative constant), which will compensate for the contraction of
f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}
.

Let us insist on the fact that we will have an exact compensation of a contracting effect of order
\delta
and a expanding effect of order
\delta^{-1}
.
The contraction of the composition map
f_{\Sigma}^{n},f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}\to\Sigma\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
will thus be bounded below by a constant
independent of
\delta
.
We formally state the last two items.
Let
l
be the minimum diameter of the disks of section
\Sigma
and
0<\delta<l
.

Claim 5.4.6
.

Let
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
. Then there exists
T\geq 0
such that for all
t\leq T
,
X^{t}(p)\in P{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
and
X^{T}(p)\in P{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{1}
.

Proof.

If
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{1}
, the fact is true for
T=0
.
Otherwise,
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
, where
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
is the normalized neighborhood of the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
for some
i
, given coordinates
\xi_{i}=(x,y,\theta)
.
We have
p=(x,x,\theta)
, with
|x|>\delta
and
X^{t}(x,y,\theta)=(2^{-t}x,2^{t}x,\theta+t)
.
The distance of
X^{t}(p)
to the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
is therefore greater than
2^{t}\delta
.
We deduce that the orbit of
p
leaves
{\mathcal{V}}_{1}
in time
t=\log_{2}(\delta^{-1})
without intersecting
{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
.
∎

Claim 5.4.7
.

Let
q\in\Sigma\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
be such that there exists
T\geq 0
with
X^{-T}(q)\in P{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{1}
.
Then the orbit segment
[X^{-T}(q),q]
intersects the section
\Sigma
a number
n_{0}\geq\log_{2}(l\delta^{-1})-1
of times in a normalized neighborhood
{\mathcal{V}}_{j}
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{j}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
.

Proof.

Let
({\mathcal{V}}_{j},\xi_{j})
be an normalized coordinate system of
{\mathcal{O}}_{j}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
such that
q\in{\mathcal{V}}_{j}
.
Let
{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}\subset{\mathcal{V}}_{j}
be the
\delta
-neighborhood of
{\mathcal{O}}_{j}
.
By definition of
l
, and by Definition
2.1.4
, Item
1
, the section
\Sigma
contains the set
R_{l}:=\{\theta=0,\,|x\leq l,\,|y|\leq l\}
.
Let
q=(x,y,\theta)
with
x^{2}+y^{2}\leq\delta^{2}
and we have
X^{-t}(p)=(2^{t}x,2^{-t}y,\theta-t)
for
t\geq 0
.
Then the negative orbit
X^{-t}(p)
intersects the set
R_{l}\subset\Sigma
a number greater than
n=\log_{2}(l\delta^{-1})-1
times before exiting
{\mathcal{V}}_{1}
.
∎

Now let
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
such that
q=f^{n}_{\Sigma}f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}(p)
is well defined in
\Sigma\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
.
According to Claim
5.4.6

the orbit of
p
exits a
1
-neighborhood at a point
p^{\prime}=X^{T}(p)
.
According to Claim
5.4.7
,
the orbit segment from
p^{\prime}
to
q
intersects
n_{0}\geq\log_{2}(l\delta^{-1})-1
times the
\Sigma
section in a normalized
{\mathcal{V}}_{j}
neighborhood of a
{\mathcal{O}}_{j}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
orbit.
In the neighborhood of
p
we have the following equality

f^{n}_{\Sigma}f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}=f^{n_{0}}_{\Sigma}f^{n-n_{0}}_{\Sigma}f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}

with
n-n_{0}\geq 0
and the map
f^{n_{0}}_{\Sigma}
is the iterate
n_{0}
of the restriction of
f_{\Sigma}
to
\Sigma\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{j}
.
We can study the effect on the norm of the vectors of each map of the composition
f^{n}_{\Sigma}f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
in the neighborhood of a point
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
.

(1)

The diffeomorphism
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\delta}
maps
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
inside a
(\hat{K},{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field, where
\hat{K}
is a constant uniform in
\delta
(Lemma
5.3.9
).
Its differential is bounded (below) by a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}
uniform in
\delta
(Lemma
5.3.7
).

(2)

The crossing map
f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}
maps a
(\hat{K},{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field inside a
(\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field on
\Sigma
of slope
K_{1}\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\hat{K}
(Lemma
5.3.5
).
Its differential at a point
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}(p)
is bounded below by
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\operatorname{dist}(\psi^{\mathrm{in}}(p),{\mathcal{O}}_{*})

(Lemma
5.4.2
).
According to
5.3.6
, this factor is bounded below by
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\operatorname{dist}(p,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})>\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\delta
.

(3)

The iteration
f_{\Sigma}^{n-n_{0}}\colon\Sigma\to\Sigma
maps a
(K_{1},\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field to a
(K_{1},\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field (Claim
5.3.2
) and contracts the vectors of a
(K_{1},\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone by a factor bounded below by a
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
which depends only on
K_{1}
.

(4)

Finally, we can use Claim
5.3.2
with an expansion factor
\mu=2
and an iterate
n_{0}\geq\log_{2}(l\delta^{-1})-1
to show that
f_{\Sigma}^{n_{0}}
maps a
(K_{1},\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone inside a
(\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field of slope
K_{2}\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\,\delta^{2}\,K_{1}
, where the constant depends only on
l
.
It expands the vectors of a
(K_{1},\ \zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone by a factor greater than
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\delta
, where the constant depends only on
K_{1}
and
l
.

We deduce that one can choose
\delta
small enough so that the image
(f^{n}_{\Sigma}f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
is contained inside
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
.
Moreover, up to change the constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}
which is uniform in
\delta
, we have in the neighborhood of
p
:

\displaystyle\left\|\left(f_{\Sigma}^{n}f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\right)_{*}v\right\|=\left\|\left(f_{\Sigma}^{n_{0}}f_{\Sigma}^{n-n_{0}}f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\right)_{*}v\right\|

\displaystyle\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\,\delta^{-1}\left\|\left(f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\right)_{*}v\right\|

(Item
3
and
4
)

\displaystyle\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\,\delta^{-1}\delta\|(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}v\|

(Item
2
)

\displaystyle\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\,\|v\|

(Item
1
)

∎

Remark 5.4.8
.

It is easy to check that Lemma
5.4.5
is still true (up to change the constant) for any orbit trajectory starting from
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
and arriving at

q=f_{\Sigma}^{n}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}(p)\in\Sigma\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{r.\delta}
, where
r>0
is a uniform constant previously fixed.

We are now ready to prove Proposition
5.4.4
.

Proof of Proposition
5.4.4
.

Let us show the first item concerning unstable cones, the proof of the second is similar.
The fact that the point
p^{\prime}=\varphi\,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{i}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}(p)
belongs to
P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
(by assumption of the corollary) implies that the point
f_{\Sigma}^{i}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}(p)
belongs to
\Sigma\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{r.\delta}
, in other words the positive orbit of
p
intersects the
\Sigma
section one last time in the neighborhood
{\mathcal{V}}_{r.\delta}
before exiting through
P^{\mathrm{out}}
, where
r>0
is a (uniform) constant which satisfies Lemma
5.3.6
.

We decompose the map

f_{\Sigma}^{j}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{i}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}

in the neighborhood of
p
in three pieces:

•

The map
f_{\Sigma}^{i}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}\to\Sigma\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{r.\delta}
is addressed in Lemma
5.4.5
and Remark
5.4.8
.
For
\delta
small enough, it maps the cone
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(p)
inside
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
and contracts the vectors of
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
by a factor bounded below by a constant
c_{0}>0
uniform in
\delta
.

•

The map
f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\colon\Sigma\to\Sigma
is addressed in Lemma
5.4.3
. Its differential is bounded by a constant
c_{1}>0
and it maps the cone field
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
inside a
(K_{1},\ \zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field, with
c_{1}
and
K_{1}
uniform in
\delta
.

•

From Claim
5.3.2
, there exists an integer
N_{0}
, such that if
j\geq N_{0}
, then
f_{\Sigma}^{j}
maps a
(K_{1},\ \zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field inside
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
and expands the norm of the vectors of a
(K_{1},\ \zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone by a factor greater than
2(c_{1}c_{0})^{-1}
, which completes the proof.
∎

We fix the integer
N_{0}
which satisfies Propositions
5.4.1
and
5.4.4
.

5.5.
Parameter
\delta_{0}

In this Subsection, we show the existence of a parameter
\delta_{0}
which will allow us to obtain Item
2
and Item
4
of Proposition
5.1.4
.

•

Item
2
concerns the
X_{\psi}
orbits crossing
({\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}=(\psi^{\mathrm{in}}({\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}))_{\psi}
(Figure
44(a)
).
We will study the composition maps

f_{\Sigma}^{n}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
and
f_{\Sigma}^{-n}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}^{-1}\,(\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}})^{-1}
from
P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
to
\Sigma
.

•

Item
4
concerns points
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
whose future orbits by
X_{\psi}
intersects
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
a first time at point
q=f^{n}_{0,\psi}(p)
(Figure
54
).
We know from Subsection
5.2
that if
p
and
q
are in
(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})_{\psi}
then the cones are invariant and expanded by
f^{n}_{0,\psi}
(uniformly in
\delta
).
It remains to check the cone invariance if
p
or
q
are in
(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})_{\psi}
.
We will study the map
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}=\varphi\,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{n}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}\to P^{\mathrm{in}}
.

Figure 54
.
Trajectory of a cone
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
under the map
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}

The conditions are resumed in the following

Proposition 5.5.1
.

There exists
\delta_{0}
such that:

(1)

the maps
f_{\Sigma}^{N_{0}}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
and
f_{\Sigma}^{-N_{0}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}^{-1}\,(\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}})^{-1}
are well defined from
P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}
to
\Sigma
.

(2)

for all
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
such that
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}(p)=:q
is well defined in
P^{\mathrm{in}}
, the map
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}
maps
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(p)
inside
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(q)
, and the map
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{-1}
maps
C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}(q)
inside
C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}(p)
.

Proof.

Let us start with the following

Claim 5.5.2
.

For every integer
n>0
, there exist
\delta(n)>0
such that for all
\delta\leq\delta(N)
the maps
f_{\Sigma}^{n}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
and
f_{\Sigma}^{-n}=\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}^{-1}\,(\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}})^{-1}
are well defined from
P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
to
\Sigma
.

Proof.

A point
p\in\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
is in a
\delta
-neighborhood of a periodic orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{i}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
.
For arbitrarily small
\delta
, the orbit by the flow of
X
of a point
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
spends an arbitrarily long time in the neighborhood of
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}\subset\Lambda_{s}
so intersects an arbitrarily large number of times the section
\Sigma
before intersecting
P^{\mathrm{out}}
.
It suffices to recall that the diffeomorphisms
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
and
\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
have a bounded effect on the distance
\operatorname{dist}(\cdot,{\mathcal{O}}_{*})
, uniformly in
\delta
(Lemma
5.3.6
).
∎

We now state a lemma about the projective action of
f_{\Sigma}^{n}f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}:P^{\mathrm{in}}\to\Sigma
.

Lemma 5.5.3
.

There exist an integer
M_{0}>0
such that for all
\delta>0
, if
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
and
q=f_{\Sigma}^{n}f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\in\Sigma
, with
n\geq M_{0}
, then

f_{\Sigma}^{n}f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
maps
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(p)
inside
C^{u}_{\Sigma}(q)
and the inverse
(f_{\Sigma}^{n}f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}
maps
C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}(q)
inside
C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}(p)
.

Proof.

We write the proof for the unstable cone field.
We study each part of the composition in the following way.

•

The diffeomorphism
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\delta}
maps
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
inside a
(\hat{K},{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field, where
\hat{K}
is a constant uniform in
\delta
(Lemma
5.3.9
).

•

The crossing map
f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}
maps a
(\hat{K},{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field inside a
(\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field on
\Sigma
of slope
K_{1}\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\hat{K}
(Lemma
5.3.5
).

•

f_{\Sigma}^{n}
maps a
(\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field of bounded slope inside a
(\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field of arbitrarily small slope for
n
large enough (uniformly in
\delta
) (Claim
5.3.2
).

We deduce the existence of an integer
M_{0}
such that the lemma is satisfied.
∎

Now we can prove Proposition
5.5.1
.
From Claim
5.5.2
we can choose
\delta_{0}<\min(\delta(N_{0}),\delta(M_{0}))
where
M_{0}
is given by Lemma
5.5.3
and
N_{0}
is the integer of the Proposition.
Item
1
is then a direct consequence of Claim
5.5.2
with
N=N_{0}
.

Now let us prove Item
2
.
If
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}
and
q\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}
, it follows from Proposition
5.2.1
, Item
2
.
If
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}
or
q\in P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}
.
then we write

f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}(p)=\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}f_{\Sigma}^{n}f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}(p)
, and we know that
n\geq M_{0}
from Claim
5.5.2
.
Define
p^{\prime}=f_{\Sigma}^{n}f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\in\Sigma
.
We decompose in the following way

•

f_{\Sigma}^{n}f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
maps
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(p)
inside
C^{u}_{\Sigma}(p^{\prime})
according to Lemma
5.5.3
.

•

\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}
maps
C^{u}_{\Sigma}(p^{\prime})
to
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(q)
according to Proposition
5.3.3
, Item
2
.

This completes the proof.
∎

We fix the parameter
\delta_{0}
which satisfies Proposition
5.5.1
.

The parameters
\lambda=\lambda_{0}
,
\epsilon=\epsilon_{0}
and
\delta=\delta_{0}
are fixed, as well as the cones
(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}},C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}})
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
and
(C^{u}_{\Sigma},C^{s}_{\Sigma})
on
\Sigma
.
From now on we call
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda_{0},\epsilon_{0},\delta_{0}}
,
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}=\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\lambda_{0},\epsilon_{0},\delta_{0}}
the diffeomorphisms of
{\partial P}
given by Proposition
4.1.2
and
4.1.3
.

5.6.
Integer
N_{1}

The parameters
\lambda=\lambda_{0}
,
\epsilon=\epsilon_{0}
and
\delta=\delta_{0}
are fixed, as well as the cones
(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}},C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}})
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
and
(C^{u}_{\Sigma},C^{s}_{\Sigma})
on
\Sigma
.
From now on we call
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda_{0},\epsilon_{0},\delta_{0}}
,
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}=\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\lambda_{0},\epsilon_{0},\delta_{0}}
the diffeomorphisms of
{\partial P}
given by Proposition
4.1.2
and
4.1.3
.
Let
\psi:=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
be the composition map.
Let
{\mathcal{V}}_{0}:=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}({\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}})
.
In this section, we show the existence of an integer
N_{1}
which will allow us to have Items
3
and
9
of Proposition
5.1.4
.
Recall that Item
3
concerns the points
p\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
whose future orbit by
X_{\psi}
intersects
\Sigma_{\psi}
a number
N_{1}
of consecutive times,
and Item
9
concerns the points
p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
whose future orbit by
X_{\psi}
intersects
(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
a number
N_{1}
of times.

Let us start by showing that the map
f^{j}_{\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}\to\Sigma

restricted to
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}
satisfies the cone fields condition for the pair
(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}\cup C^{u}_{\Sigma},C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}\cup C^{s}_{\Sigma})
for a sufficiently large iterate
j
.
We refer to Figure
55
.

Figure 55
.
Cone
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
mapped by
f^{j}_{\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\si^{\mathrm{in}}
restricted to
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}

Lemma 5.6.1
.

There exists
N_{1}
such that for any
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}
if the image
q=f^{n}_{\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}(p)\in\Sigma
is well defined for a
n\geq N_{1}
,
then

•

(f^{n}_{\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(p)\subset\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\Sigma}(q)
and

\|(f^{n}_{\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|
for all
v\in C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(p)

•

(f^{n}_{\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}_{*}C^{s}_{\Sigma}(q)\subset\operatorname{int}C^{s}_{\Sigma}(p)
and

\|(f^{n}_{\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|
for all
v\in C^{s}_{\Sigma}(p)

Proof.

We show the existence of an integer
N_{1}
such that the first item is true.
The second item is shown in a similar way.
Let us study each map of the composition.

•

The cone field
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}})
is inside a
(\hat{K},{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field (Lemma
5.3.9
) and
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
has a differential uniformly bounded by a constant
c_{0}>0
.

•

The crossing map
f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}\to\Sigma
maps a
(\hat{K},{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{in}}/\,{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}})
-cone field on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
inside a
(\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field on
\Sigma
of uniform slope
K_{1}
(Lemma
5.3.5
).
Moreover, the differential of
f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}
restricted to
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}
is uniformly bounded by a constant
c_{1}>0
because it is a crossing map of the flow
X^{t}
between a compact surface
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}
transverse to the vector field
X
and a compact surface
\Sigma
transverse to the vector field
X
and the time of the crossing is uniformly bounded.

•

By hyperbolicity of
f_{\Sigma}
(Claim
5.3.2
), there exists an integer
N_{1}
such that if
n\geq N_{1}
, then
f_{\Sigma}^{n}
maps a
(K_{1},\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field inside
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
and expands the norm of the vectors of a
(K_{1},\zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone by a factor greater than
2(c_{1}c_{0})^{-1}

Putting together we obtain the existence of an integer
N_{1}
such that the composition
f^{j}_{\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}\to\Sigma
with
n\geq N_{1}
maps the cone
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(p)
inside
C^{u}_{\Sigma}(q)
and expands the vectors of
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
by a factor
2
.
∎

We recall the definition in Subsection
5.2
of the following composition

f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}=\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}\to P^{\mathrm{in}}
.
Denote
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in},\psi}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}\to P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}

the restriction of
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}
to the set
P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}
at the domain and destination.
Let us show that the composition map

\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{j}\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}f_{\Sigma}^{i}\colon\Sigma\to P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}

satisfies the cone fields condition for the pair
(C^{u}_{\Sigma}\cup C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}},C^{s}_{\Sigma}\cup C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}})
if
j
is large enough.

Figure 56
.
Trajectory of a cone
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
under the map
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{j}\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}f_{\Sigma}^{i}

Lemma 5.6.2
.

There exists an integer
N_{1}\geq 0
such that for all

p\in\Sigma
, if the image
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{j}\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}f_{\Sigma}^{i}(p)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}
is well defined for
j\geq N_{1}
and
i\geq 0
, in other words
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{k}\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}f_{\Sigma}^{i}(p)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
for all
k=0,\dots,j
,
then

•

\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{j}\,\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{i}
maps
C^{u}_{\Sigma}(p)
inside
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(q)
and expands the norm of the vectors of
C^{u}_{\Sigma}(p)
by a factor
2
,

•

(\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{j}\,\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{i})^{-1}
maps
C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}(p)
inside
C^{s}_{\Sigma}(q)
and expands the norm of the vectors of
C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}(p)
by a factor
2
,

Let us insist on the fact that each crossing of the orbit of such a point
p
on the surface
P^{\mathrm{in}}
takes place on the complementary of
{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}
by assumption.

Proof.

The composition is decomposed into three pieces.

•

f_{\Sigma}^{i}
maps
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
on
C^{u}_{\Sigma}
and the contraction of the norm of the vectors is greater than a uniform constant
c_{0}>0
for
i\geq 0
(Proposition
5.3.3
, Item
3
).

•

We know that
\left(\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\right)_{*}C^{u}_{\Sigma}\subset\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
(Proposition
5.3.3
, Item
2
).
Moreover, by hypotheses the codomain of
f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}
for our restriction is contained in
P^{\mathrm{out}}{\smallsetminus}(\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}})^{-1}({\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}})
, so uniformly far from the set
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
.
Using Lemma
5.4.2
, we deduce that the differential of
f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}
is bounded, uniformly in
p
.
The diffeomorphisms
\varphi
,
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
are compactly supported so the differential of
\varphi\,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}
is bounded by a constant
c_{1}>0
(uniform in
p
).

•

The map
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in},\psi}\colon{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}\to P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}
, which coincides with the restriction of
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}
to the set
(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})\cap f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{-1}(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta})
maps the field
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
inside
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
and expands the norm of the vectors of
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
by a factor
2
(Proposition
5.2.1
).
It is enough to choose
N_{1}\geq-\log_{2}(c_{0}c_{1})
so that the expansion of
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in},\psi}
compensates the contraction of the previous maps.
This integer is uniform in
p
.

The second item is shown in a similar way.
∎

We fix an integer
N_{1}
which satisfies Lemmas
5.6.1
and
5.6.2
.

5.7.
Proof of Proposition
5.1.4

We are now able to prove Proposition
5.1.4
.
We have fixed in the previous sections the parameters
\lambda=\lambda_{0}
,
\epsilon=\epsilon_{0}
(Subsection
5.2
),
\delta=\delta_{0}
(Subsection
5.5
),
a pair of cone fields
(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}},C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}})
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
(Subsection
5.2
) and a pair of cone fields
(C^{u}_{\Sigma},C^{s}_{\Sigma})
on
\Sigma
(Subsection
5.3
),
and we defined integers
N_{\Sigma}
(Subsection
5.3
),
N_{0}
(Subsection
5.4
),
N_{1}
(Subsection
5.6
).
These choices have been made in the “right order”, which means that all the results from Subsections
5.2
to
5.6
are true for the parameters
\lambda_{0},\epsilon_{0},\delta_{0}
.
Denote
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda_{0},\epsilon_{0},\delta_{0}}
and
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}=\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\lambda_{0},\epsilon_{0},\delta_{0}}
.
We use the notations introduced in Section
5
.
Let
\psi=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
be the modified gluing map,
P_{\psi}=P/\,\psi
the closed manifold and
X_{\psi}
the vector field induced by
X
on
P_{\psi}
. The projection is
\pi_{\psi}\colon P\to P_{\psi}
. Define the section
S_{0,\psi}=\pi_{\psi}(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cup\Sigma)
,
f_{0,\psi}
the first-return map of the flow of
X_{\psi}
on
S_{0,\psi}
and
{\mathcal{V}}_{0}=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}({\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}})
.

Define the following pair of cone fields on
S_{0,\psi}
:

(C^{u}_{\psi},C^{s}_{\psi})=\left(\pi_{\psi}(\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}(C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}})\sqcup C^{u}_{\Sigma}),\ \pi_{\psi}(\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}(C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}})\sqcup C^{s}_{\Sigma})\right)

and the metric

g_{\psi}=\pi_{\psi}(\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}g^{\mathrm{in}})\sqcup\pi_{\psi}(g^{\Sigma})
on
P_{\psi}
,
where
g^{\Sigma}
and
g^{\mathrm{in}}
are restrictions of the metric fixed on
P
.
Recall that the metric
g
is the metric fixed at the beginning of Section
3
, which coincides with
dx^{2}+dy^{2}+d\theta^{2}
in each
({\mathcal{V}}_{i},\xi_{i}=(x,y,\theta))
, and which is adapted to the hyperbolic splitting of the flow.
Let us note
\|\cdot\|_{\psi},\|\cdot\|_{\Sigma},\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{in}}
the associated norms.
Recall that for practical reasons, we have studied lifts in
P

conjugated by
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
of the map
f^{n}_{0,\psi}
.
This explains that we compose the projection
\pi_{\psi}
by
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
to fit.
Recall that :

•

S_{0,\psi}
has points of self-intersections
p
, at these points it has two or three tangent planes
T^{*}=T^{*}_{p}\subset T_{p}S_{0,\psi}
.
The differential of
f_{0,\psi}
in
p
is well defined for a choice of tangent plane on
p
and on
f_{0,\psi}(p)
.
A cone field
C
on
S_{0,\psi}
is a collection of cones
C(p,T^{i})
on each of the tangent planes
T^{i}\subset T_{p}S_{0,\psi}
.

•

If
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}\subset S_{0,\psi}
, we denote
T^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}\subset T_{p}S_{0,\psi}
the tangent plane induced by
T_{\tilde{p}}P^{\mathrm{in}}
, where
\tilde{p}\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
is the lift of
p
in
P^{\mathrm{in}}
, and we denote
(C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}),C^{s}_{\psi}(p,T^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}))
the pair
(C^{u}_{\psi},C^{s}_{\psi})
on
T^{\mathrm{in}}_{p}
in
p
.

If
q\in\Sigma_{\psi}\subset S_{0,\psi}
, we denote
T^{\Sigma}_{\psi}\subset T_{p}S_{0,\psi}
the (or one of the two) tangent plane(s) induced by
T_{\tilde{q}}P^{\Sigma}
, where
\tilde{q}\in\Sigma
is a lift of
q
in
\Sigma
, and we denote
(C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T^{\Sigma}_{q}),C^{s}_{\psi}(q,T^{\Sigma}_{q})
the pair
(C^{u}_{\psi},C^{s}_{\psi})
on
T^{\Sigma}_{q}
in
q
.

Lifts in
P

We summarize the lifts in
P
of the points, cones, vectors and norms that we will use.

Claim 5.7.1
.

(1)

(maps)

•

If
p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
and
f_{0,\psi}(p)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
then a lift of
f_{0,\psi}
is
f_{\Sigma}\colon\Sigma\to\Sigma
.

•

If
p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
and
f_{0,\psi}(p)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
then a lift of
f_{0,\psi}
is
\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}:\Sigma\to P^{\mathrm{in}}
.

•

If
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
and
f_{0,\psi}(p)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
then a lift of
f_{0,\psi}
is
f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}:P^{\mathrm{in}}\to\Sigma
.

•

If
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
and
f_{0,\psi}(p)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
then a lift of
f_{0,\psi}
is
\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}:P^{\mathrm{in}}\to P^{\mathrm{in}}

(2)

(points)

•

If
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
, there exists a unique lift
\tilde{p}
of
p
in
P^{\mathrm{in}}
, then denote
\check{p}:=(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}(\tilde{p})
, in other words we have
\pi_{\psi}(\psi^{\mathrm{in}}(\check{p}))=p
.
Moreover if
p\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
then
\check{p}\in P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}
by definition of
{\mathcal{V}}_{0}=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}({\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}})
.

•

If
q\in\Sigma_{\psi}
, there exists
\tilde{q}\in\Sigma
a lift of
q
in
P
.

(3)

(cones)

•

If
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
, a lift of the cone
C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\mathrm{in}}_{p})
is the cone
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{p})
.

•

If
q\in\Sigma
, a lift of the cone
C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T^{\Sigma}_{q})
is the cone
C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{q})
.

(4)

(vectors)

•

If
v\in TP^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
, there exists a unique lift of
v
in
TP
of the form
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}\check{v}
with
\check{v}\in TP^{\mathrm{in}}
.
Moreover, if
v\in C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\mathrm{in}}_{p})
, then
\check{v}\in C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{p})
.

•

If
w\in T\Sigma_{\psi}
, there exists
\tilde{w}\in T\Sigma
a lift of
w
in
P
.
Moreover, if
w\in C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T^{\Sigma}_{q})
, then
\tilde{w}\in C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{q})
.

(5)

(norm)

•

If
v\in TP^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
, then
\|v\|_{\psi}=\|\check{v}\|_{\mathrm{in}}=\|\check{v}\|
where
\check{v}
is given by the previous item and
\|\cdot\|
is the norm from the Riemannian metric on
P
.

•

If
w\in T\Sigma_{\psi}
, then
\|w\|_{\psi}=\|\tilde{w}\|_{\Sigma}=\|\tilde{w}\|
where
\tilde{w}
is given by the previous item and
\|\cdot\|
is the norm from the Riemannian metric on
P
.

All the above items are direct consequences of the definitions of the cones and the metric on
P_{\psi}
.
Let us show Proposition
5.1.4
.

Proof of Proposition
5.1.4
.

Let
p\in S_{0,\psi}
,
n\geq 1
and
q=f_{0,\psi}^{n}(p)
.
Let us show each item of the proposition.
We only show the properties for the unstable cones
C^{u}_{\psi}
.
The proofs for the stable cones
C^{s}_{\psi}
are symmetric.

1
.

Suppose
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
and let
\tilde{p}:=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\check{p}
be a lift of
p
in
P
with
\check{p}\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
(Claim
5.7.1
, Item
2
). Then

\displaystyle\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\mathrm{in}})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi})_{*}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}(\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{p}))

\displaystyle\supset(\pi_{\psi})_{*}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}(\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}

(Proposition
5.2.1
, Item
1
)

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi})_{*}\psi_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}=(\pi_{\psi})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{u,\mathrm{out}}

\displaystyle\supset(\pi_{\psi})_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}={\mathcal{W}}^{u}_{\psi}\cap P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}

\displaystyle\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\mathrm{in}})\cap{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{\psi}

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi})_{*}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}(\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{p}))\cap(\pi_{\psi})_{*}{\mathcal{W}}^{s}

\displaystyle\subset(\pi_{\psi})_{*}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}(\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{p}))\cap(\pi_{\psi})_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi}\circ\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}\left(\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{p})\cap(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}_{*}{\mathcal{G}}^{s,\mathrm{in}}\right)

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi}\circ\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}\{O\}=\{O\}

(Proposition
5.2.1
, Item
1
)

Suppose now
p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
and let
\tilde{p}:=\check{p}
be a lift of
p
in
P
with
\check{p}\in\Sigma
(Claim
5.7.1
, Item
2
).

\displaystyle\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\Sigma})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi})_{*}(C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\check{p}))

\displaystyle\supset(\pi_{\psi})_{*}\zeta^{u}

(Proposition
5.3.3
, Item
1
)

\displaystyle\supset(\pi_{\psi})_{*}({\mathcal{W}}^{u}\cap\Sigma)={\mathcal{W}}^{u}_{\psi}\cap\Sigma_{\psi}

\displaystyle\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\Sigma})\cap{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{\psi}

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi})_{*}(\pi_{\psi})_{*}(C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\check{p}))\cap(\pi_{\psi})_{*}{\mathcal{W}}^{s}

\displaystyle\subset(\pi_{\psi})_{*}(C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\check{p}))\cap(\pi_{\psi})_{*}\zeta^{s}

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi})_{*}\left(C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\check{p})\cap\zeta^{s}\right)

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi})_{*}\{O\}=\{O\}

(Proposition
5.3.3
, Item
1
)

2
.

Suppose that
p\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
.

Let
\tilde{p}:=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\check{p}
be a lift of
p
in
P
with
\check{p}\in P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}
(Claim
5.7.1
, Item
2
).

According to Proposition
5.5.1
, the maps

f_{\Sigma}^{N_{0}},f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}\to\Sigma
and

f_{\Sigma}^{-N_{0}}f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}^{-1}\,(\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}})^{-1}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}\to\Sigma
are well defined in
\check{p}
.
From Claim
5.7.1
, Item
1
, the lifts of the iterates
f^{k}_{0,\psi}(p)
in
P
are the iterates :

•

f_{\Sigma}^{k-1}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}(\tilde{p})=f_{\Sigma}^{k-1}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}(\check{p})
for
k=1,\dots,N_{0}
, and

•

f_{\Sigma}^{k-1}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}^{-1}\,\psi^{-1}(\tilde{p})=f_{\Sigma}^{k-1}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}^{-1}\,(\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}})^{-1}(\check{p})
for
k=-N_{0},\dots,-1
.

We deduce that the iterates
f^{k}_{0,\psi}(p)
belong to
\Sigma_{\psi}
for
k=-N_{0},\dots,-1,1,\dots,N_{0}
.

3
.

Suppose
p\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
,
q\in\Sigma_{\psi}
,
n\geq N_{1}
, and
f^{k}_{0,\psi}(p)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
for
k=1,\dots,n-1
.

Let
\tilde{p}:=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\check{p}
be a lift of
p
in
P
with
\check{p}\in P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}
(Claim
5.7.1
, Item
2
).
According to Claim
5.7.1
, Item
1
,
f^{n-1}_{\Sigma},f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}
is a lift of
f^{n}_{0,\psi}
in the neighborhood of
p
.
Let
\tilde{q}:=f^{n-1}_{\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}(\check{p})\in\Sigma
.
This is a lift of
q=f^{n}_{0,\psi}(p)
in
P
.

According to Item
3
, Claim
5.7.1
, a lift of
C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\mathrm{in}}_{p})
is
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{p})
and a lift of
C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T^{\Sigma}_{q})
is
C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{q})

We use Lemma
5.6.1
.
We have

\displaystyle(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\mathrm{in}}_{p})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi})_{*}(f^{n-1}_{\Sigma},f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{p})

\displaystyle\subset(\pi_{\psi})_{*}\,\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{q})

(Lemma
5.6.1
)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T^{\Sigma}_{q})

Let
v\in C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\mathrm{in}}_{p})
.
Let
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}\check{v}
be a lift of
v
in
TP^{\mathrm{in}}
, with
v\in C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{p})
(Claim
5.7.1
Item
4
).
Then
(f^{n-1}_{\Sigma}f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}\check{v}
is a lift of
(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}v
in
T\Sigma
, and we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{\|(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}v\|_{\psi}}{\|v\|_{\psi}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\|(f^{n-1}_{\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}\check{v}\|}{\|\check{v}\|}

(Claim
5.7.1
, Item
5
)

\displaystyle\geq 2

(Lemma
5.6.1
)

4
.

Suppose
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
,
q\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
and
f^{k}_{0,\psi}(p)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
for
k=1,\dots,n-1
.

Let
\tilde{p}:=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\check{p}
be a lift of
p
in
P
with
\check{p}\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
(Claim
5.7.1
, Item
2
).
According to Claim
5.7.1
, Item
1
,
\psi f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}
is a lift of
f^{n}_{0,\psi}
in the neighborhood of
p
.
Let
\tilde{q}:=\psi f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}(\psi^{\mathrm{in}}(\check{p}))=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\,f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}(\check{p})\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
(Equation (
5.1
)).
This is a lift of
q=f^{n}_{0,\psi}(p)
in
P
.
Let
\check{q}:=(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}\tilde{q}=f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}(\check{p})
.
From Item
3
, Claim
5.7.1
,
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{p})
is a lift of
C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\mathrm{in}}_{p})
and
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{q})
is a lift of
C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T^{\mathrm{in}}_{q})
.
We use Proposition
5.5.1
, Item
2
.
We have

\displaystyle(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\mathrm{in}}_{p})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi})_{*}(\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\,\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{p})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi}\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}(f_{\mathrm{in},\psi})_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{p})

\displaystyle\subset(\pi_{\psi}\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}(\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{q}))

(Proposition
5.5.1
, Item
2
)

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi})_{*}(\operatorname{int}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{q})))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T^{\mathrm{in}}_{q})

Now suppose
p
and
q
are in
(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
, and
q\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
.
Then
\check{p}
and
\check{q}
are in
P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}
(Claim
5.7.1
, Item
2
).
Let
v\in C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\mathrm{in}}_{p})
.
Let
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}\check{v}
be a lift of
v
in
TP^{\mathrm{in}}
, with
v\in C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{p})
(Claim
5.7.1
Item
4
).
Then
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}(f_{\mathrm{in},\psi})_{*}\check{v}
is a lift of
(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}v
in
TP^{\mathrm{in}}
, and we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{\|(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}v\|_{\psi}}{\|v\|_{\psi}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\|(f_{\mathrm{in},\psi})_{*}\check{v}\|}{\|\check{v}\|}

(Claim
5.7.1
, Item
5
)

\displaystyle\geq 2

(Proposition
5.2.1
)

5
.

Suppose that
p\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
,
q\in\Sigma_{\psi}
,
n\geq N_{0}+1
,
there exists
1\leq m\leq n-N_{0}
such that

and

for

.

Let
\tilde{p}:=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\check{p}
be a lift of
p
in
P
with
\check{p}\in P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}
(Claim

, Item
2
).
From Claim
2
, Item
1
,
f_{\Sigma}^{n-m}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{m-1}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}
is a lift of
f^{n}_{0,\psi}
in the neighborhood of
p
.
Let
\tilde{q}:=f_{\Sigma}^{n-m}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{m-1}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}(\check{p})\in\Sigma
.
This is a lift of
q=f^{n}_{0,\psi}(p)
in
P
.

According to Item
3
, Claim
5.7.1
, a lift of
C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\mathrm{in}}_{p})
is
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{p})
and a lift of
C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T^{\Sigma}_{q})
is
C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{q})
.
We have
n-m\geq N_{0}
.
By Proposition
5.4.4
:

\displaystyle(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\mathrm{in}}_{p})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi})_{*}(f_{\Sigma}^{n-m}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{m-1}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{p})

\displaystyle\subset(\pi_{\psi})_{*}\,\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{q})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T^{\Sigma}_{q})

Let
v\in C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\mathrm{in}}_{p})
.
Let
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}\check{v}
be a lift of
v
in
TP^{\mathrm{in}}
, with
v\in C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{p})
(Claim
5.7.1
Item
4
).
Then
f_{\Sigma}^{n-m}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{m-1}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}\check{v}
is a lift of
(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}v
in
T\Sigma
, and we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{\|(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}v\|_{\psi}}{\|v\|_{\psi}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\|(f_{\Sigma}^{n-m}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{m-1}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}\check{v}\|}{\|\check{v}\|}

(Item
5
)

\displaystyle\geq 2

(Proposition
5.4.4
)

6
.

Suppose
p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
,
q\in\Sigma_{\psi}
, and
f^{k}_{0,\psi}(p)\in\Sigma
for

.

Let
\tilde{p}\in\Sigma
be a lift of

in

.
According to Claim

, Item

,
f_{\Sigma}^{n}
is a lift of

.

.

in
P
.

According to Item

, Claim
5.7.1
, a lift of
C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\Sigma}_{p})
is
C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{p})
and a lift of
C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T^{\Sigma}_{q})

.
We use Proposition
5.3.3
.
We have

\displaystyle(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\Sigma}_{p})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi})_{*}(f_{\Sigma}^{n})_{*}C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{p})

\displaystyle\subset(\pi_{\psi})_{*}(\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{q}))

(Proposition
5.3.3
)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T^{\Sigma}_{q})

Moreover, if
n\geq N_{\Sigma}
:
Let
v\in C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\Sigma}_{p})
and
\tilde{v}\in C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{p})
be a lift of

.
We have

\displaystyle\dfrac{\|(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}v\|_{\psi}}{\|v\|_{\psi}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\|(f_{\Sigma}^{n})_{*}\check{v}\|}{\|\check{v}\|}

(Claim
5.7.1
, Item
5
)

\displaystyle\geq 2

(Proposition
5.3.3
)

7
.

Suppose
p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
,
q\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
and
f^{k}_{0,\psi}(p)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
for

.

Let
\tilde{p}\in\Sigma
be a lift of
p
in

.
From Claim
5.7.1
, Item
1
,
\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{n}

in the neighborhood of

.

\tilde{q}:=\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{n}\,(\tilde{p})\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
.
This is a lift of

in
P
.
Denote
\check{q}:=(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}\tilde{q}=\varphi\,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{n}(\tilde{p})
.

According to Item
3
, Claim
5.7.1
, a lift of
C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\Sigma}_{p})

.

5.3.3
.

\displaystyle(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\Sigma}_{p})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi})_{*}(\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{n})_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\tilde{p})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi}\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}(\varphi\,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{n})_{*}C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{p})

\displaystyle\subset(\pi_{\psi}\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}(\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{q}))

(Proposition
5.3.3
)

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi})_{*}\operatorname{int}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{q}))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T^{\mathrm{in}}_{q})

8
.

Suppose that
p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
,
q\in\Sigma_{\psi}
,
n\geq N_{0}+1
, and there exists
1\leq m\leq n-N_{0}
such that
f_{0,\psi}^{m}(p)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
, and
f^{k}(p)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
for
k=1,\dots,m-1,m+1,\dots,n-1
.

Let
\tilde{p}\in\Sigma
be a lift of
p
in
P
.
From Claim
5.7.1
, Item
1
,
f_{\Sigma}^{n-m}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{m-1}
is a lift of
f^{n}_{0,\psi}
in the neighborhood of
p
.
We set
\tilde{q}:=f_{\Sigma}^{n-m}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{m-1}(\tilde{p})
.
This is a lift of
q=f^{n}_{0,\psi}(p)
in
P
.

According to Item
3
, Claim
5.7.1
, a lift of
C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\Sigma}_{p})
is
C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{p})
and a lift of
C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T^{\Sigma}_{q})
is
C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{q})
.
Since
n-m\geq N_{0}
, we can use Proposition
5.4.1
.
We have

\displaystyle(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\Sigma}_{p})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi})_{*}(f_{\Sigma}^{n-m}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{m-1})_{*}C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{p})

\displaystyle\subset(\pi_{\psi})_{*}(\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{q}))

(Proposition
5.4.1
)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T^{\Sigma}_{q})

Let
v\in C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\Sigma}_{p})
and
\tilde{v}\in C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{p})
be a lift of
v
.
We have

\displaystyle\dfrac{\|(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}v\|_{\psi}}{\|v\|_{\psi}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\|(f_{\Sigma}^{n-m}\,f_{\Sigma,\mathrm{in}}\,\psi\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{m-1})_{*}\check{v}\|}{\|\check{v}\|}

(Claim
5.7.1
, Item
5
)

\displaystyle\geq 2

(Proposition
5.4.1
)

9
.

p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
,
q\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
, and there exist
m\geq N_{1}
, and integers
1\leq k_{1}<\dots<k_{m}=n
such that
and
f_{0,\psi}^{k_{1}}(p),\dots,f_{0,\psi}^{k_{m}}(p)\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}

and
f_{0,\psi}^{k}(p)\in\Sigma
for
k\neq k_{1},\dots,k_{m}
,
1\leq k\leq n-1
.

Let
\tilde{p}\in\Sigma
be a lift of
p
in
P
.
According to Claim
5.7.1
, Item
1
,
(f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\psi)^{m-1}f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}f_{\Sigma}^{k_{1}-1}
is a lift of
f^{n}_{0,\psi}
in the neighborhood of
p
, and we write in the following decomposition :

(f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}\psi)^{m-1}f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}f_{\Sigma}^{k_{1}-1}=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{m-1}\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}f_{\Sigma}^{k_{1}-1}

with
\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in},\psi}\colon P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}}\to P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
is the restriction of
f_{\mathrm{in},\psi}
to the corresponding sets.

Let
\tilde{q}:=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{m-1}\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}f_{\Sigma}^{k_{1}-1}\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
.
This is a lift of
q=f^{n}_{0,\psi}(p)
in
P
.
We set
\check{q}:=(\psi^{\mathrm{in}})^{-1}\tilde{q}=(\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in},\psi})^{m-1}\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}f_{\Sigma}^{k_{1}-1}(\tilde{p})
.

According to Item
3
, Claim
5.7.1
, a lift of
C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\Sigma}_{p})
is
C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{p})
and a lift of
C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T^{\mathrm{in}}_{q})
is
\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{q})
.
We use Lemma
5.6.2
.
We have

\displaystyle(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\Sigma}_{p})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi})_{*}(\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{m-1}\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}f_{\Sigma}^{k_{1}-1})_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\tilde{p})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi}\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}(\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{m-1}\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}f_{\Sigma}^{k_{1}-1})_{*}C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{p})

\displaystyle\subset(\pi_{\psi}\psi^{\mathrm{in}})_{*}(\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{q}))

(Lemma
5.6.2
)

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\psi})_{*}\operatorname{int}\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(\check{q}))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T^{\mathrm{in}}_{q})

Let
v\in C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T^{\Sigma}_{p})
, and
\tilde{v}\in C^{u}_{\Sigma}(\tilde{p})
be a lift of

in
T\Sigma
.
Then

in

, and we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{\|(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}v\|_{\psi}}{\|v\|_{\psi}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\|(\hat{f}_{\mathrm{in},\psi}^{m-1}\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}f_{\Sigma}^{k_{1}-1})_{*}\tilde{v}\|}{\|\tilde{v}\|}

(Claim

, Item

)

\displaystyle\geq 2

(Lemma

)
