5.8.
Additional result

We can improve Proposition
5.1.4
by adding (for the same parameters) the existence of another pair of cone fields on
S_{0,\psi}
, denoted
(\hat{C}^{u}_{\psi},\hat{C}^{s}_{\psi})
, and which will have a complementary role to the one of the pair
(C^{u}_{\psi},C^{s}_{\psi})
in the next section. Let us explain.

In the next section, we will show (Proposition
6.1.2
) that we can remove a neighborhood of the boundary of
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
in
S_{0,\psi}
to obtain a compact section
S_{\psi}
with boundary, transverse to the vector field
X
, and catching the same orbit in uniformly bounded time.
The first return map
f_{\psi}
which is a restriction of
f_{0,\psi}
then satisfies the following property.
For
any
p\in S_{\psi}
, there exists an integer
n^{\star}=n^{\star}(p)\geq 0
, such that
f^{n}_{\psi}
satisfies the cone fields condition in the neighborhood of
p
for the pair
(C^{u}_{\psi},C^{s}_{\psi})
(restricted on
S_{\psi}
).
Since the integer
n^{\star}=n^{\star}(p)
depends on the point
p
, this result is not symmetric by inversion of the time direction, in other words it does not follow that there exists an integer
\hat{n}^{\star}=\hat{n}^{\star}(p)\leq 0
such that
f^{\hat{n}^{\star}}_{\psi}
satisfies the cone fields condition in the neighborhood of
p
for the pair
(C^{s}_{\psi},C^{u}_{\psi})
.
Now this is necessary to prove the existence of a hyperbolic splitting on
S_{\psi}
using the invariant cone fields criterion, which will then give the Anosov structure for
X_{\psi}
on
P_{\psi}
(Proposition
6.2.1
).
In fact, this is not the case for the pair
(C^{s}_{\psi},C^{u}_{\psi})
given by Proposition
5.1.4
. Let us explain.

We chose the pair
(C^{s}_{\psi},C^{u}_{\psi})
so as to satisfy Item
7
of Proposition
5.1.4
, which gives a compatibility of cones from
\Sigma_{\psi}
to
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
at the first crossing, and allows us to keep the control of the image of a cone during the trajectory of an orbit crossing
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
several times.
On the other hand, there is no compatibility of cones from
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
to
\Sigma_{\psi}
on the first crossing.
To get around this problem, we use a pair
(\hat{C}^{u}_{\psi},\hat{C}^{s}_{\psi})
which satisfies this property, i.e. such that we have strict invariance of the cones by
f_{\psi}
at the first crossing from
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
to
\Sigma_{\psi}
.
This pair will also continue to satisfy all the items of Proposition
5.1.4
, except that of Item
7
.

Let us state the result. Let the parameters
\lambda_{0}>1
,
\epsilon_{0}>0
,
\delta_{0}>0
and the integers
N_{0}
,
N_{1}
and
N_{\Sigma}
be given by Proposition
5.1.4
.
Let

\psi^{\mathrm{in}}=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\lambda_{0},\epsilon_{0},\delta_{0}}
,

\psi^{\mathrm{out}}=\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\lambda_{0},\epsilon_{0},\delta_{0}}
,

\psi=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
,
the diffeomorphisms of
{\partial P}
associated with this choice of parameters.

Proposition 5.8.1
.

There exists a pair
(\hat{C}^{u}_{\psi},\hat{C}^{s}_{\psi})
of cone fields on
S_{0,\psi}
which satisfy the following properties.
Let
p\in S_{0,\psi}
, let
n\geq 1
and let
q=f_{0,\psi}^{n}(p)
.

(1)

All the items of Proposition
5.1.4
except Item
7
are satisfied.

(2)

Suppose
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
,
q\in\Sigma_{\psi}
and
f^{k}_{0,\psi}(p)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
for
k=1,\dots,n-1
.
Then:

•

(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}\hat{C}^{u}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\mathrm{in}})\subset\operatorname{int}\hat{C}^{u}_{\psi}(q,T_{q}^{\Sigma})
and,

•

(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}^{-1}\hat{C}^{s}_{\psi}(q,T_{q}^{\Sigma})\subset\operatorname{int}\hat{C}^{s}_{\psi}(p,T_{p}^{\mathrm{in}})
.

The proof is the same, except for Section
5.3
, Proposition
5.3.3
(this is the part which allows us to obtain Item
7
of Proposition
5.1.4
in the conclusion).
The analogous of Proposition
5.3.3
is

Proposition 5.8.2
.

There exist a pair of cone field
(\hat{C}^{u}_{\Sigma},\hat{C}^{s}_{\Sigma})
on
\Sigma

(1)

(direction)

There exist constant
\hat{K}^{u}>0
and
\hat{K}^{s}>0
such that:

\hat{C}^{u}_{\Sigma}
is a
(\hat{K}^{u},\ \zeta^{u}/\,\zeta^{s})
-cone field, and
\hat{C}^{s}_{\Sigma}
is a
(\hat{K}^{s},\ \zeta^{s}/\,\zeta^{u})
-cone field

(2)

(compatibility on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
)

for all
\delta>0
, if
\psi^{\mathrm{out}}=\psi^{\mathrm{out}}_{\delta}
, and for all
p\in\Sigma
such that the point
q=\varphi\,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{n}\,(p)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}
is well defined for some integer
n\geq 0
, then:

(\varphi\,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{n}\,)_{*}\hat{C}^{u}_{\Sigma}(p)\subset\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}(q)
, and

(\varphi\,\psi^{\mathrm{out}}\,f_{\mathrm{out},\Sigma}\,f_{\Sigma}^{n}\,)^{-1}_{*}C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}(q)\subset\operatorname{int}\hat{C}^{s}_{\Sigma}(p)

(3)

(invariance and expansion on
\Sigma
)

there exists an integer
N_{\Sigma}\geq 0
such that

(f_{\Sigma})_{*}\hat{C}^{u}_{\Sigma}\subset\operatorname{int}\hat{C}^{u}_{\Sigma},\ \mbox{and}\ \forall v\in\hat{C}^{u}_{\Sigma}
,
\forall n\geq N_{\Sigma}
,
\|(f_{\Sigma}^{n})_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|
,

(f_{\Sigma})^{-1}_{*}\hat{C}^{s}_{\Sigma}\subset\operatorname{int}\hat{C}^{s}_{\Sigma},\ \mbox{and}\ \forall v\in\hat{C}^{s}_{\Sigma}
,
\forall n\geq N_{\Sigma}
,
\|(f_{\Sigma}^{n})^{-1}_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|

The proof is based on the same arguments.
We have to choose a slope
\hat{K}^{u}
of
\hat{C}^{u}_{\Sigma}
big enough and a slope
\hat{K}^{s}
of
\hat{C}^{s}_{\Sigma}
small enough to satisfy the compatibility of Item
2
. Essentially, the slope
\hat{K}^{u}
is of the order of the slope
K^{s}
and conversely the slope
\hat{K}^{s}
is of the order of the slope
K^{u}
, hence the integer
N_{\Sigma}
is indeed the same in both proposition.
The cone fields
(\hat{C}^{u}_{\psi},\hat{C}^{s}_{\psi})
will be the quotient by
\pi_{\psi}
of cone fields on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
and on
\Sigma
in
P
.
The cone fields on
P^{\mathrm{in}}
are the same as for the pair
(C^{u}_{\psi},C^{s}_{\psi})
, namely
(\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}},\psi^{\mathrm{in}}_{*}C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}})
where
C^{u}_{\mathrm{in}}
and
C^{s}_{\mathrm{in}}
are the cone fields given in Proposition
5.2.1
.
We replace the cones
(C^{u}_{\Sigma},C^{s}_{\Sigma})
by the cones
(\hat{C}^{u}_{\Sigma},\hat{C}^{s}_{\Sigma})
on
\Sigma
.
By reproducing point by point the previous proof and replacing the cone fields
(C^{u}_{\Sigma},C^{s}_{\Sigma})
by the cone fields
(\hat{C}^{u}_{\Sigma},\hat{C}^{s}_{\Sigma})
we obtain the result announced in Proposition
5.8.1
.

6.
Modification of the gluing map and proof of Theorem
A

In Section
5
, we showed that there exist parameters
\lambda_{0},\epsilon_{0},\delta_{0}
which satisfy Proposition
5.1.4
.
We refer to the notations introduced in Section
5
.
Let
\psi=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}\varphi\psi^{\mathrm{out}}
be the modified gluing map for such parameters and
X_{\psi}
be the vector field induced by
X
on
P_{\psi}=P/\,\psi
.
Recall that
S_{0,\psi}=\pi_{\psi}(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cup\Sigma)
and
f_{0,\psi}
is the first-return map of the flow of
X_{\psi}
on
S_{0,\psi}
.
According to Proposition
5.1.4
, there exists a pair of cone fields

(C^{u}_{\psi},C^{s}_{\psi})
on
S_{0,\psi}
for which a (non-uniform) iterate of
f_{0,\psi}
satisfies the cone fields condition in the neighborhood of some points,
outside a small neighborhood
({\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
of the periodic orbits
({\mathcal{O}}_{*})_{\psi}
in
S_{0,\psi}
.

Indeed, the section is not uniformly transverse and the expansion of the vector of the cones is missing along some orbits when approaching the periodic orbits
({\mathcal{O}}_{*})_{\psi}
contained in
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
(Item
7
and Item
4
).
The goal of this section is therefore to remove a well-chosen neighborhood of the orbits
({\mathcal{O}}_{*})_{\psi}
in
S_{0,\psi}
in order to have the hyperbolicity of the return map everywhere while catching the same set of orbit.

6.1.
Gluing procedure and invariant cone fields on a global section

Compact local section

We remove from
S_{0,\psi}
the projection of the neighborhood
{\mathcal{V}}_{0}:=\psi^{\mathrm{in}}({\mathcal{V}}_{\delta_{0}})
of the orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
. We obtain the surface

(6.1)

S_{\psi}:=\pi_{\psi}\left((P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})\cup\Sigma\right)=(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}\cup\Sigma_{\psi}\subset S_{0,\psi}

It is an immersed compact section catching the same set of
X_{\psi}
-orbit.
Indeed, according to Item
2
of Proposition
5.1.4
, an orbit of
X_{\psi}
which intersects
(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
intersects the section
\Sigma_{\psi}\subset S_{\psi}
in the past and in the future in uniformly bounded time.

Figure 57
.
Section
S_{\psi}

We recall (see Subsection
5.1
) that as for the section
S_{0,\psi}
, the surface
S_{\psi}
admits points
p
of self-intersection on which it can have two or three tangent planes
T^{*}=T^{*}_{p}\subset T_{p}S_{\psi}
.
Denote
T^{\Sigma}_{p}
the tangent plane(s) to
\Sigma_{\psi}
at
p
and
T^{\mathrm{in}}_{q}
the tangent plane to
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
at
q
.
The surface
S_{\psi}
has a metric
g_{\psi}
, which is a collection of metrics
g_{\psi}(p,T^{*})
on each of the tangent planes
T^{*}\subset T_{p}S_{\psi}
.
A cone field
C
on
S_{\psi}
is the data for each
p\in S_{\psi}
and for each tangent plane
T^{*}
to
S_{\psi}
at the point
p
of a cone
C(p,T^{*})\subset T^{*}
.

Statement and proof of the proposition

If
f_{\psi}
is the first return map of the flow of
X_{\psi}
on
S_{\psi}
,
then we show that it has a hyperbolic behavior in the
future
of the orbit of the points
p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
.
We will deal separately with the maximal
positive
invariant set in this section and the maximal
negative
invariant set in the next subsection. More precisely

Definition 6.1.1

(Positive, Negative, Maximal invariant set)
.

Define

•

K_{\psi}^{+}:=\bigcap_{n\geq 0}f_{\psi}^{n}(S_{\psi})
the
maximal positive invariant set for
f_{\psi}
in
S_{\psi}
.

•

K_{\psi}^{-}:=\bigcap_{n\geq 0}f_{\psi}^{n}(S_{\psi})
the
maximal negative invariant set for
f_{\psi}
in
S_{\psi}
.

•

K_{\psi}:=\bigcap_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}f_{\psi}^{n}(S_{\psi})
the
maximal invariant set for
f_{\psi}
in
S_{\psi}
.

The following proposition gives the existence of a
positive
integer
n^{\star}(p)\geq 0
for any point
p\in K_{\psi}^{+}
, uniformly bounded from above, such that the iterate
f^{n^{\star}}_{\psi}
satisfies the cone fields condition in the neighborhood of
p
for the pair
(C^{s}_{\psi},C^{u}_{\psi})
of Proposition
5.1.4
.

Proposition 6.1.2
.

There exists an integer
{\mathbf{N}}\geq 0
such that,
for all
p\in K_{\psi}^{+}
, if
T_{p}
is (one of) the tangent plane(s) to
S_{\psi}
at
p
,
there exists an integer
n^{\star}=n^{\star}(p,T_{p})
with
0\leq n^{\star}\leq{\mathbf{N}}
,
and a tangent plane
T_{q}=T_{q}(p,T_{p})
to
S_{\psi}
in

q=f_{\psi}^{n^{\star}}(p)\in S_{\psi}

such that:

(1)

(f_{\psi}^{n^{\star}})_{*}C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T_{p})\subset\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T_{q})
,
and for all
v\in C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T_{p})
,
\|(f_{\psi}^{n^{\star}})_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|

(2)

(f_{\psi}^{n^{\star}})^{-1}_{*}C^{s}_{\psi}(q,T_{q})\subset\operatorname{int}C^{s}_{\psi}(p,T_{p})
,
and for all
v\in C^{s}_{\psi}(q,T_{q})
,
\|(f_{\psi}^{n^{\star}})^{-1}_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|

(a)

Dichotomy diagram of orbits in Case
1.

Figure 58
.
Dichotomy diagram of
f_{\psi}
-orbits on
S_{\psi}

(a)

Dichotomy diagram of orbits in Case
2.

Figure 59
.
Dichotomy diagram of
f_{\psi}
-orbits on
S_{\psi}

Proof.

Note that
f_{\psi}
is a restriction of
f_{0,\psi}
.
We can use exclusively the results of Proposition
5.1.4
on the return map
f_{0,\psi}
of the flow of
X_{\psi}
on the section
S_{0,\psi}
.
If
p\in K_{\psi}^{+}
, then there exist
n
and
m
such that
f^{n}_{\psi}(p)=q=f^{m}_{0,\psi}(p)
and
n\leq m
.
Indeed
f_{\psi}
does not “see” the crossings on
(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
.
We will make dichotomy according to the starting point
p
on the set
K_{\psi}^{+}
, and the trajectory of its orbit.
We represent the dichotomy by the diagrams of Figure
58
.

1.

Suppose
p\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
and
T_{p}=T_{p}^{\mathrm{in}}
.

Two cases are possible depending on whether the future orbit of
p
by the flow of
X_{\psi}
crosses the section
\Sigma_{\psi}
enough times without crossing
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
to have strict invariance and expansion of the cones, or whether it crosses
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
before.
This return number is the integer
N_{1}
which satisfies Item
3
of Proposition
5.1.4
.

We make the following dichotomy:

1.1.

Either the orbit of
p
by the flow of
X_{\psi}
crosses
N_{1}
consecutive times
\Sigma_{\psi}
without crossing
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
.

In other words
f_{0,\psi}^{k}(p)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
for
k=1,\dots,N_{1}
.
Then according to Item
3
Proposition
5.1.4
,
Proposition
6.1.2
is satisfied for
n^{\star}(p)=N_{1}
and
T_{q}=T_{q}^{\Sigma}
.

1.2.

Either the orbit of
p
by the flow of
X_{\psi}
crosses
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
before crossing
N_{1}
times
\Sigma_{\psi}
.

In other words there exists
k_{0}\leq N_{1}
minimal such that
f_{0,\psi}^{k_{0}}(p)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
.
Then two cases are possible.

1.2.1.

Either
f_{0,\psi}^{k_{0}}(p)\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
.

According to Item
4
of Proposition
5.1.4
, Proposition
6.1.2
is satisfied for
n^{\star}(p)=k_{0}\leq N_{1}
and
T_{q}=T_{q}^{\mathrm{in}}
.

1.2.2.

Either
f_{0,\psi}^{k_{0}}(p)\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
.

Then according to Item
2
of Proposition
5.1.4
, the future orbit of
f_{0,\psi}^{k_{0}}(p)
by the flow of
X_{\psi}
crosses
N_{0}
consecutive times the section
\Sigma_{\psi}
without crossing
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
.
In other words
f^{K}_{\psi}\left(f^{k_{0}}_{0,\psi}(p)\right)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
for
k=1,\dots,N_{0}
.

Then according to Item
5
of Proposition
5.1.4
, Proposition
6.1.2
is satisfied for
n^{\star}(p)=N_{0}+k_{0}-1\leq N_{0}+N_{1}-1
and
T_{q}=T_{q}^{\Sigma}
.

Note that the endpoint

q
on
\Sigma_{\psi}
corresponds to the iteration
N_{0}+k_{0}
of the orbit of
p
by
f_{0,\psi}
, and the iteration
N_{0}+k_{0}-1
of the orbit of
p
by
f_{\psi}
because in the second case we do not count the crossing of the orbit through
(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
.

2.

Suppose
p\in\Sigma_{\psi}
, and
T_{p}=T_{p}^{\Sigma}
.

We make a dichotomy depending on whether the orbit of
p
by the flow of
X_{\psi}
crosses
\Sigma_{\psi}
enough times without crossing
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
to have strict invariance and expansion of the cones, or if it crosses
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
before.

This return number is the integer
N_{\Sigma}
which satisfies Item
6
of Proposition
5.1.4
.

2.1.

Either the orbit of
p
by the flow of
X_{\psi}
crosses
N_{\Sigma}
consecutive times without crossing
P^{\mathrm{in}}
.

In other words
f^{K}_{\psi}(p)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
for
k=0,\dots,N_{\Sigma}
.
Then according to Item
6
of Proposition
5.1.4
, Proposition
6.1.2
is satisfied for
n^{\star}(p)=N_{\Sigma}
and
T_{q}=T_{q}^{\Sigma}
.

2.2.

Either the orbit of
p
by the flow of
X_{\psi}
crosses
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
before
.

In other words there exists
k_{1}\leq N_{\Sigma}
minimal such that
f^{k_{1}}_{0,\psi}(p)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
.
Then two cases are possible, depending on whether this intersection point is in the neighborhood
({\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
or not.

2.2.1.

Either
f^{k_{1}}_{0,\psi}(p)\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
.

Then according to Item
2
of Proposition
5.1.4
, the future orbit of
f^{k_{1}}(p)
by the flow of
X_{\psi}
crosses
N_{0}
consecutive times the section
\Sigma_{\psi}
without crossing
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
.
In other words
f^{K}_{\psi}\left(f^{k_{1}}_{0,\psi}(p)\right)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
for
k=1,\dots,N_{0}
.

Then according to Item
8
of Proposition
5.1.4
, Proposition
6.1.2
is satisfied for
n^{\star}(p)=N_{0}+{k_{1}}-1\leq N_{0}+N_{\Sigma}-1
and
T_{q}=T_{q}^{\Sigma}
.

Note that the endpoint
q
on
\Sigma_{\psi}
corresponds to the iteration
N_{0}+k_{1}
of
p
by
f_{0,\psi}
, and the iteration
N_{0}+k_{1}-1
of
p
by
f_{\psi}
, because in the second case we do not count the crossing of the orbit by
(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
.

2.2.2.

Either
f^{k_{1}}_{0,\psi}(p)\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
.

Let
q_{1}=f^{k_{1}}_{0,\psi}(p)=f^{k_{1}}_{\psi}(p)
.
Either the orbit of
q_{1}
crosses the
\Sigma
section enough consecutive times without crossing
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
.
This return number is the integer
N_{0}
which satisfies Item
9
of Proposition
5.1.4
, and we will have strict invariance and expansion of the cones.
Either the orbit of
q_{1}
crosses
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
at a point
q_{2}
with less than
N_{0}
crossing through
\Sigma_{\psi}
between each.
According to Item
2
of Proposition
5.1.4
, the point of intersection
q_{2}
is in
(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
.
In this case, we can reiterate this dichotomy by replacing
q_{2}
by
q_{1}
.
This recurrence is finite because if the orbit of
q_{1}
crosses
(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
enough times without crossing
(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
, then we have the strict invariance and expansion of cones. This return number is the integer
N_{1}
which satisfies Proposition
5.1.4
, Item
9
.
Formally the dichotomy is the following.

2.2.2.1

Either the orbit of
p
crosses
N_{1}
times
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
with strictly less than
N_{0}
crossings through
\Sigma_{\psi}
between each
.
Then each crossings through
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
occurs in
(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
.
There exists
k_{1}<\dots<k_{N_{1}}
such that

•

k_{i}\leq i.N_{0}

•

f_{0,\psi}^{k_{i}}(p)\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}

•

f^{k}_{0,\psi}(p)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
for
k\leq k_{N_{1}}
and
k\neq k_{1},\dots,k_{N_{1}}

According to Item
9
of Proposition
5.1.4
, Proposition
6.1.2
is satisfied for
n^{\star}(p)=k_{N_{1}}\leq N_{1}\times(N_{0}-1)
and
T_{q}=T_{q}^{\mathrm{in}}
.

2.2.2.2

Either the orbit of
p
crosses strictly less than
N_{1}
times
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
with strictly less than
N_{0}
crossings through
\Sigma_{\psi}
between each,
then its orbit crosses
N_{0}
consecutive times the section
\Sigma_{\psi}
without crossing
P^{\mathrm{in}}
.

Then each crossing through
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
occurs in
(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
.
There exists
m\leq N_{1}-1
and
k_{1}<\dots<k_{m}
such that

•

k_{i}\leq i.N_{0}\in(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}

•

f^{k}_{0,\psi}(p)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
for
k\leq k_{m}
and
k\neq k_{1}\dots,k_{m}

•

f_{0,\psi}^{k}\left(f_{0,\psi}^{k_{m}}(p)\right)\in\Sigma_{\psi}
for
k=1,\dots,N_{0}

We set
q_{i}=f^{k_{i}}(p)
for
i=1,\dots,m
.
Note that according to Item
7
,
f^{k_{1}}_{\psi}
maps
C^{u}_{\psi}(p)
inside
C^{u}_{\psi}(q_{1})
and its inverse maps
C^{s}_{\psi}(q_{1})
inside
C^{s}_{\psi}(p)
.
Then, each crossing of the orbit from
(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
to
(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
contributes to map the cone
C^{u}_{\psi}(q_{i})
inside the cone
C^{u}_{\psi}(q_{i+1})
and expand the norm of the vectors by a factor of 2 according to Item
4
.
So it is enough to show that an orbit of a point of
\Sigma_{\psi}
which crosses
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
and then crosses
N_{0}
times the section
\Sigma_{\psi}
without crossing
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
leaves the cones of
C^{u}_{\psi}
strictly invariant and expanded, and similarly for the action of the inverse on the cones
C^{s}_{\psi}
.
This is Item
8
of Proposition
5.1.4
.
We deduce that Proposition
6.1.2
is satisfied for
n^{\star}(p)=N_{0}+k_{m}\leq N_{0}+(N_{1}-1)\times N_{0}
, and
T_{q}=T_{q}^{\Sigma}
.

∎

Additional Result

As announced in Subsection
5.8
, we have an additional complementary result on the the maximal negative invariant set
K_{\psi}^{-}
for
f_{\psi}
in
S_{\psi}
(Definition
6.1.1
).
It gives the existence of an integer
\hat{n}^{\star}(p)\leq 0
for any point
p\in K_{\psi}^{-}
, uniformly bounded from below, such that the iterate
f^{\hat{n}^{\star}}_{\psi}
satisfies the cone fields condition in the neighborhood of
p
for the pair of cone fields
(\hat{C}^{s}_{\psi},\hat{C}^{u}_{\psi})
given by Proposition
5.1.4
.
This means that the behavior of the map
f^{\hat{n}^{\star}}
is hyperbolic in the
past
orbits.

Proposition 6.1.3
.

There exists an integer
{\mathbf{N}}\geq 0
such that,
for all
p\in K_{\psi}^{-}
, if
T_{p}
is (one of) the tangent plane(s) to
S_{\psi}
in
p
,
there exists an integer
\hat{n}^{\star}=\hat{n}^{\star}(p,T_{p})
with
-{\mathbf{N}}\leq\hat{n}^{\star}\leq 0
,
and a tangent plane
T_{q}=T_{q}(p,T_{p})
to
S_{\psi}
in

q=f_{\psi}^{\hat{n}^{\star}}(p)\in S_{\psi}

such that :

(1)

(f_{\psi}^{\hat{n}^{\star}})_{*}\hat{C}^{s}_{\psi}(p,T_{p})\subset\operatorname{int}\hat{C}^{s}_{\psi}(q,T_{q})
,
and for all
v\in\hat{C}^{s}_{\psi}(p,T_{p})
,
\|(f_{\psi}^{\hat{n}^{\star}})_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|

(2)

(f_{\psi}^{\hat{n}^{\star}})^{-1}_{*}\hat{C}^{u}_{\psi}(q,T_{q})\subset\operatorname{int}\hat{C}^{u}_{\psi}(p,T_{p})
,
and for all
v\in\hat{C}^{u}_{\psi}(q,T_{q})
,
\|(f_{\psi}^{\hat{n}^{\star}})^{-1}_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|

The proof is identical using Proposition
5.8.1
.

6.2.
Hyperbolic splitting on the local section

Propositions
6.1.2
and
6.1.3
will allow us to construct a hyperbolic splitting of the tangent bundle of
S_{\psi}
on the maximal invariant set
K_{\psi}
for the return map
f_{\psi}\colon S_{\psi}\to S_{\psi}
.
Let us show the following proposition which gives the existence of a hyperbolic splitting for the return map
f_{\psi}
on
K_{\psi}
.
More precisely, we construct the stable direction on all of
K_{\psi}^{+}
, and the unstable direction on all of
K_{\psi}^{-}
.
We will also need that the fibers of the invariant direction on two different tangent planes generates the same sum with
{\mathbb{R}}.X_{\psi}
.
A line bundle
F
on
S_{\psi}
is the data, for any
p\in S_{\psi}
and any tangent plane
T^{*}=T^{*}_{p}
to
S_{\psi}
at
p
, of a 1-dimensional vector space
F(p,T^{*})\subset T^{*}
.

Proposition 6.2.1
.

There exists a
f_{\psi}
-invariant direction
F^{s}\subset TS_{\psi}
on
K_{\psi}^{+}
, and a transverse
f_{\psi}^{-1}
-invariant direction
F^{u}\subset TS_{\psi}
on
K_{\psi}^{-}
, and constants
\lambda>1
and
C>0
such that :

(1)

F^{s}\subset\operatorname{int}C^{s}_{\psi}
and
F^{u}\subset\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}
.

(2)

For all
p\in K_{\psi}^{+},\ \forall n\geq 0,\ \forall v\in F^{s}(p),\ \|(f^{n}_{\psi})_{*}v\|\leq C\lambda^{-n}\|v\|
.

For all
p\in K_{\psi}^{-},\ \forall n\leq 0,\ \forall v\in F^{u}(p),\ \|(f^{n}_{\psi})_{*}v\|\leq C\lambda^{n}\|v\|
.

(3)

If
T
and
T^{\prime}
are two planes tangent to
S_{\psi}
at
p\in K_{\psi}^{+}
, then
F^{s}(p,T)\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X_{\psi}=F^{s}(p,T^{\prime})\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X_{\psi}
.

If
T
and
T^{\prime}
are two planes tangent to
S_{\psi}
at
p\in K_{\psi}^{-}
, then
F^{u}(p,T)\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X_{\psi}=F^{u}(p,T^{\prime})\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X_{\psi}
.

Proof.

In the proof we will just show the existence of the stable bundle
F^{s}
on
K_{\psi}^{-}
, the proof for
F^{u}
being similar.

Step 1: Infinite intersection of stable cones

Let
p
be a point of
K_{\psi}^{+}
and
T
(one of) the tangent plane(s) to
S_{\psi}
at
p
.
Then there exists a sequence of particular points on the positive orbit of
p
by
f_{\psi}
defined as follows.
Let
(p_{k},T_{k})
and
n_{k}
, defined by recurrence by :

(6.2)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(p_{0},T_{0})=(p,T),\\
(p_{k+1},T_{k+1})=\left(f_{\psi}^{n^{\star}(p_{k},T_{k})}(p_{k}),\ T_{p_{k+1}}(p_{k},T_{k})\right)\end{array}\right.,\ \left\{\begin{array}[]{l}n_{0}=n^{\star}(p,T),\\
n_{k+1}=n^{\star}(p_{k},T_{k})\end{array}\right.

where
n^{\star}(p,T)
is the integer and
T_{q}(p,T)
is the tangent plane satisfying Proposition
6.1.2
for the given
(p,T)
.
Recall that by Proposition
6.1.2
the sequence
n_{k}
is bounded by an integer
{\mathbf{N}}
.
Moreover,
f_{\psi}^{n_{k}}
maps the cone
C^{u}_{\psi}(p_{k},T_{k})
inside
C^{u}_{\psi}(p_{k+1},T_{k+1})
and expands the norm of the vectors of
C^{u}_{\psi}(p_{k},T_{k})
by a factor 2, and
\left(f_{\psi}^{n_{k}}\right)^{-1}
maps the cone
C^{s}_{\psi}(p_{k+1},T_{k+1})
inside
C^{s}_{\psi}(p_{k},T_{k})
and expands the norm of the vectors of
C^{s}_{\psi}(p_{k+1},T_{k+1})
by a factor 2.
Define
L_{k}
to be the differential of
f_{\psi}^{n_{k}}
from the plane
T_{k}
to the plane
T_{k+1}
, in other words

(6.3)

L_{k}:=d_{p_{k}}f_{\psi}^{n_{k}}\colon T_{k}\to T_{k+1}

It is a family of invertible linear maps. Define

(6.4)

F^{s}(p,T):=\underset{k=0}{\overset{+\infty}{\bigcap}}\left(L_{0}^{-1}\circ\dots\circ L_{k}^{-1}\right)C^{s}_{\psi}(p_{k},T_{k})\subset C^{s}_{\psi}(p,T)

By strict invariance of the stable cones
C^{s}_{\psi}
under the iterates
f_{\psi}^{-n^{\star}}
, this infinite intersection is a cone contained in
C^{s}_{\psi}(p,T)
.
Repeating this operation for each point
p
of
K_{\psi}
and tangent plane
T
of
S_{\psi}
in
p
, we obtain a cone field
F^{s}=\{F^{s}(p,T)\}
on
S_{\psi}
on
K_{\psi}^{+}
.
Then we have for all
k\geq 0
:

(6.5)

F^{s}(p_{k+1},T_{k+1})=L_{k}F^{s}(p_{k},T_{k})\noindent\quad\mbox{and}\noindent\quad\forall v\in F^{s}_{k},\ \|L_{k}v\|\leq\frac{1}{2}\|v\|

The first equality follows from Equation (
6.4
) defining
F^{s}
by an infinite intersection.
The following inequality is true because
F^{s}(p_{k},T_{k})\subset L_{k}^{-1}C_{\psi}^{s}(p_{k+1},T_{k+1})
by (
6.4
), and
L_{k}^{-1}
expands the norm of the vectors of
C_{\psi}^{s}(p_{k+1},T_{k+1})
by a factor 2.

Step 2: Line bundle

Claim 6.2.2
.

F^{s}
is a line bundle.

Proof.

Let
(p,T)
be fixed,
(p_{k},T_{k},n_{k})
the sequence associated by Equation (
6.2
).
Let
F^{s}_{k}=F^{s}(p_{k},T_{k})
be the family of cones defined by Equation (
6.4
), and
L_{k}\colon T_{k}\to T_{k+1}
the family of invertible linear maps defined by Equation (
6.3
).
According to Equation (
6.5
),
\{F^{s}_{k}\}_{k}
is a collection of cones (exactly) invariant by
\{L_{k}\}_{k}
, and whose vectors are uniformly contracted by
\{L_{k}\}_{k}
.
Suppose by contradiction that
F^{s}_{0}
is not reduced to a line.
Then the same is true for
F^{s}_{k}
and we denote
F^{1}_{k}
and
F^{2}_{k}
the two disjoint lines which constitute the boundary of
F^{s}_{k}
, and such that
L_{k}F^{i}_{k}=F^{i}_{k+1}
.
Therefore, any vector
v\in T
decomposes uniquely as
v=v_{1}+v_{2}\in F^{1}\oplus F^{2}
, and we have by iteration of the inequality in Equation (
6.5
)

(6.6)

\|L_{n}\circ L_{n-1}\circ\dots\circ L_{0}(v)\|\leq 2^{-n}(\|v_{1}\|+\|v_{2}\|)\underset{n\to+\infty}{\longrightarrow}0

In other words, the norm of any vector tends to 0 under the action of
\{L_{k}\}_{k}
.
Now there is an invariant direction expanded by
\{L_{k}\}_{k}
: it is enough to iterate any direction in the unstable field
C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T)
.
More precisely, according to Proposition
6.1.2
, and by definition of
(p_{k},T_{k},n_{k})
to Equation (
6.2
) and
L_{k}
to Equation (
6.3
), we have
L_{k}\,C^{u}_{\psi}(p_{k},T_{k})\subset\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}(p_{k+1},T_{k+1})
and for all
v_{k}\in C^{u}_{\psi}(p_{k},T_{k})
,
\|L_{k}v_{k}\|\geq 2\|v\|
.
We conclude by iterating the inequality for
v\in C^{u}_{\psi}(p,T)\subset T
:

\|L_{n}\circ L_{n-1}\circ\dots\circ L_{0}(v)\|\geq 2^{n}\|v\|\underset{n\to+\infty}{\longrightarrow}+\infty

This is a contradiction with Equation (
6.6
).
∎

Step 3: Exponential contraction

Let us show that the line bundle
F^{s}
is contracted by a uniform iteration of
f_{\psi}
.

Claim 6.2.3
.

There exists an integer
{\mathbf{N}}_{0}
, such that for any
p\in K_{\psi}
and
T\subset T_{p}S_{\psi}
a tangent plane in
p
,

\forall v\in F^{s}(p,T),\noindent\quad\|(f^{{\mathbf{N}}_{0}}_{\psi})_{*}v\|\leq\frac{1}{2}\|v\|

Proof.

Let
(p,T)
be fixed,
(p_{k},T_{k},n_{k})
the sequence associated by Equation (
6.2
).
Let
F^{s}_{k}=F^{s}(p_{k},T_{k})
be the family lines defined by Equation (
6.4
), and
L_{k}\colon T_{k}\to T_{k+1}
be the family of invertible linear maps defined by Equation (
6.3
).
Let
{\mathbf{N}}
be the uniform integer which bounds the sequence
\{n_{k}\}_{k}
.
There exists a constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
which bounds the differential of
f_{\psi}^{i}
for
0\leq i\leq{\mathbf{N}}
.
Let be an integer
k_{0}
satisfying

(6.7)

k_{0}>\log_{2}\operatorname{\textit{cst}}+1

and let
{\mathbf{N}}_{0}
be an integer greater than
k_{0}.{\mathbf{N}}
.
Then we can decompose
f_{\psi}^{{\mathbf{N}}_{0}}
in the neighborhood of
p
in the form

f_{\psi}^{{\mathbf{N}}_{0}}=f_{\psi}^{i}\circ f_{\psi}^{n_{k}}\circ\dots\circ f_{\psi}^{n_{0}}
,
with
k\geq k_{0}
and
0\leq i\leq{\mathbf{N}}
.
Then by iterating the inequality of Equation (
6.5
) (with
L_{k}=d_{p_{k}}f^{n_{k}}_{\psi}
) for a
v\in F^{s}
, we have

\displaystyle\|(f_{\psi}^{{\mathbf{N}}_{0}})_{*}v\|

\displaystyle=\|(f_{\psi}^{i}\circ f_{\psi}^{n_{k}}\circ\dots\circ f_{\psi}^{n_{0}})_{*}v\|

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\|(f_{\psi}^{n_{k}}\circ\dots\circ f_{\psi}^{n_{0}})_{*}v\|

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}2^{-k}\|v\|

Equation (
6.5
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|v\|

Equation (
6.7
)

∎

Step 4: Invariance

Let us show that
F^{s}
is an
f_{\psi}
-invariant bundle.

Claim 6.2.4
.

For any
p\in K_{\psi}^{+}
and
T\subset T_{p}S_{\psi}
a tangent plane at
p
, for any
j\geq 0
, if
q=f_{\psi}^{j}(p)
and
T^{\prime}\subset T_{q}S_{\psi}
is a tangent plane at
q
, then
F^{s}(q,T^{\prime})=(f_{\psi}^{j})_{*}F^{s}(p,T)
.

Proof.

Suppose by contradiction that
F^{s}(q,T^{\prime})\neq(f_{\psi}^{j})_{*}F^{s}(p,T)
.
Let
F^{1}=F^{s}(q,T^{\prime})
and
F^{2}=(f_{\psi}^{j})_{*}F^{s}(p,T)
.
These are two lines in
T^{\prime}
.
We can assume without loss of generality that
0\leq j\leq{\mathbf{N}}_{0}
.
According to Claim
6.2.3
, we have for
v_{1}\in F^{1}

and
n\geq 0
,

\|(f^{n{\mathbf{N}}_{0}})_{*}v_{1}\|\leq 2^{-n}\|v_{1}\|
,
and for
v_{2}\in F^{2}
and
n\geq 0
,

\|(f_{\psi}^{n{\mathbf{N}}_{0}})_{*}v_{2}\|=\|f^{j}_{\psi}(f_{\psi}^{{\mathbf{N}}_{0}})^{n}_{*}(f^{-j}_{\psi})_{*}v_{2}\|\leq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}2^{-n}\|v_{1}\|
.
Any vector
v\in T^{\prime}
decomposes uniquely as
v=v_{1}+v_{2}\in F^{1}\oplus F^{2}
.
We deduce that the norm of any vector
v\in T^{\prime}
under the action of
\{(f^{n{\mathbf{N}}_{0}}_{\psi})_{*}\}
tends to 0.

Now there exists an invariant direction expanded by the family
\{(f_{\psi}^{n{\mathbf{N}}_{0}})_{*}\}_{n}
, it is enough to iterate any direction in the unstable cone field
C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T^{\prime})
.
More precisely, let
(q_{k},T^{\prime}_{k},n_{k})
be the sequence defined by Equation (
6.2
) from
(q,T^{\prime})
.
According to Proposition
6.1.2
we have for all
k\geq 0
,
(f_{\psi}^{n_{k}})_{*}\,C^{u}_{\psi}(q_{k},T^{\prime}_{k})\subset\operatorname{int}C^{u}_{\psi}(q_{k+1},T^{\prime}_{k+1})
, and for all
v\in C^{u}_{\psi}(q_{k},T^{\prime}_{k})
,
\|(f_{\psi}^{n_{k}})_{*}v\|\geq 2\|v\|
.
For all
n\geq 0
, there exists a unique decomposition of
f_{\psi}^{n{\mathbf{N}}_{0}}
in the neighborhood of
q^{\prime}
of the form

f_{\psi}^{n{\mathbf{N}}_{0}}=f_{\psi}^{i}\circ f_{\psi}^{n_{k_{n}}}\circ\dots\circ f_{\psi}^{n_{0}}
with
0\leq i<{\mathbf{N}}_{0}
.
Moreover,
k_{n}\underset{n\to\infty}{\longrightarrow}+\infty
.
There is also a uniform constant
\operatorname{\textit{cst}}>0
which bounds the differential of
f_{\psi}^{i}
for
0\leq i\leq{\mathbf{N}}_{0}
.
We conclude that for
v\in C^{u}_{\psi}(q,T^{\prime})\subset T^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle\|(f_{\psi}^{n{\mathbf{N}}_{0}})_{*}v\|

\displaystyle=\|(f_{\psi}^{i}\circ f_{\psi}^{n_{k_{n}}}\circ\dots\circ f_{\psi}^{n_{0}})_{*}v\|

\displaystyle\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}\|(f_{\psi}^{n_{k_{n}}}\circ\dots\circ f_{\psi}^{n_{0}})_{*}v\|

\displaystyle\geq\operatorname{\textit{cst}}^{-1}2^{k_{n}}\|v\|

\displaystyle\underset{n\to+\infty}{\longrightarrow}+\infty

This is a contradiction.
∎

Step 5: Generated tangent planes.

We have shown that
F^{s}
is a
f_{\psi}
-invariant line bundle of
S_{\psi}
on the subset
K_{\psi}^{+}
, and exponentially contracted by
f_{\psi}
, i.e.
F^{s}
satisfies Item
2
of Proposition
6.2.1
.
Let us show that
F^{s}
satisfies Item
3
of Proposition
6.2.1
.

Claim 6.2.5
.

Let
T
and
T^{\prime}
be two tangent planes at
p\in K_{\psi}^{+}
in
T_{p}S_{\psi}
, then
F^{s}(p,T)\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X_{\psi}=F^{s}(p,T^{\prime})\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X_{\psi}
.

Proof.

Let
q=f_{\psi}(p)
and
T_{q}
be a tangent plane to
S_{\psi}
at
q
.
Let
L\colon T\to T_{q}
and
L^{\prime}\colon T^{\prime}\to T_{q}
be the two restrictions of the differential
d_{p}f_{\psi}
.
Then by Claim
6.2.4
,

L(F^{s}(p,T))=F^{s}(q,T_{q})=L(F^{s}(p,T^{\prime}))
.
Now
L
and
L^{\prime}
differ at a projection parallel to
{\mathbb{R}}.X_{\psi}
, in other words if
\pi\colon T\to T^{\prime}
is the projection on
T^{\prime}
parallel to
{\mathbb{R}}.X_{\psi}
, then we have
L=L^{\prime}\circ\pi
.
Since
L^{\prime}
is invertible, we deduce that
\pi F^{s}(p,T)=F^{s}(p,T^{\prime})
.
The equality of sums with
{\mathbb{R}}.X
is therefore true.
∎

It follows that the line bundle
F^{s}
on
S_{\psi}
satisfies Proposition
6.2.1
.
The proof for the line bundle
F^{u}
is symmetric.
We have to use Proposition
6.1.3
, construct a sequence in the past orbit of a point
p\in K_{\psi}^{-}
, the iteration is given by the integer
\hat{n}^{\star}
, and the action of the inverse differential on the pair
(\hat{C}^{s}_{\psi},\hat{C}^{u}_{\psi})
.
∎

6.3.
Proof of Theorem
A

We want to show that the vector field
X_{\psi}
on
P_{\psi}
is Anosov.
We will use the following general criterion, see for example
[
FH19
, Theorem 6.2.24]
.

Lemma 6.3.1

(Mañé Criterion)
.

A
{\mathcal{C}}^{1}
flow on a compact manifold is an Anosov flow if and only if the chain-recurrent set is hyperbolic, the stable and unstable manifolds intersect transversely at one (hence every) point of each orbit, and their dimension is constant.

To use the Mañé Criterion, we will need to prove that the chain-recurrent set of
X_{\psi}
in
P_{\psi}
is hyperbolic.
To do so, we use the following general

Lemma 6.3.2
.

Let
\Lambda
be a compact invariant set for a
{\mathcal{C}}^{1}
flow
Y
on a compact 3-manifold
M
.
Suppose that the differential of the flow of
Y
preserves two plane fields
E^{cs}
and
E^{cu}
on
P
on
\Lambda
, tangent to
Y
, and transverse to each other, such that
Y^{t}
contracts exponentially the area of
E^{cs}
and expands exponentially the area of
E^{cu}
for
t\geq 0
.
Then
\Lambda
is a hyperbolic set of index (1,1).

Proof.

Let us show the existence of the strong unstable direction
E^{uu}\subset E^{cu}
.
Let
V
be a vector field tangent to
E^{cu}
and transverse to the vector field
Y
on
E^{cu}
, such that the pair
(V,Y)
forms a vector field basis of
E^{cu}
.
Let
L^{t}:=Y^{t}_{*}|_{E^{cu}}
.
The matrix of
L^{t}
in the basis
(V,Y)
is

M_{(V,Y)}L^{t}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{t}&0\\
b_{t}&1\end{pmatrix}

where
a_{t}
and
b_{t}
are continuous functions of
{\mathcal{M}}
and bounded at
t
fixed.
Let us show that
L^{t}
satisfies the cone fields condition.
By hypothesis, there exist constants
C>0
and
\lambda>1
such that

\operatorname{Jac}(L^{t})=|a_{t}|\geq C\lambda^{t}
.
Up to change the metric, we can suppose that

\inf|a_{1}|>1
.
We suppose that
b_{1}
is not uniformly zero, otherwise the lemma is true : the strong unstable direction
E^{uu}
is the direction generated by
V
.
We define

(6.8)

K:=2\,\frac{\sup|b_{1}|}{\inf|a_{1}|-1}>0

Let
C^{u}
be the
K
-cone field on
E^{cu}
in the
(V,Y)
basis.

Claim 6.3.3
.

For all
n\geq 1
, we have

L^{n}C^{u}\subset\operatorname{int}C^{u}
,
and
L^{n}
exponentially expands the norm of the vectors of
C^{u}
.

Proof.

Let
v=v^{u}V+v^{Y}Y\in C^{u}
, then we have
|v^{Y}|\leq K|v^{u}|
.
Then
L^{1}v=a_{1}v^{u}V+(b_{1}v^{u}+v^{Y})Y
and

\frac{|b_{1}v^{u}+v^{Y}|}{|a_{1}v^{u}|}\leq\frac{|b_{1}|K^{-1}+1}{|a_{1}|}K<K

So
L^{1}v
is inside
C^{u}
.
We deduce the property for
L^{n}
by writing
L^{n}=L^{1}\circ\dots\circ L^{1}
.

Let
n
be a positive integer and
L^{n}v=v^{u}_{n}V+v^{Y}_{n}Y=a_{n}v^{u}V+(b_{n}v^{u}+v^{Y})Y
.
We have

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl l}|v^{u}_{n}|&\geq&(\inf|a_{1}|)^{n}|v^{u}|>2|v^{u}|,&\forall n\geq\log_{\inf|a_{1}|}(2)\\
|v^{u}_{n}|&\geq&(\inf|a_{1}|)^{n}\frac{1}{K}|v^{Y}|>2|v^{Y}|,&\forall n\geq\log_{\inf|a_{1}|}(2K)\end{array}\right.

Therefore
{\operatorname{max}}(|v_{1}^{n}|,|v_{Y}^{n}|)>2{\operatorname{max}}(|v_{1}|,|v_{Y}|)
for
n
(uniformly) large enough, which proves that
L^{n}
expands the norm of the vectors of
C^{u}
by a factor of
2
for
n
greater than some integer
n_{0}
.
Since the operator
L^{i}
is uniformly bounded for
0\leq i\leq n_{0}
,
we deduce that
L^{n}
expands exponentially the norm of the vectors of
C^{u}
by iterating
L^{kn_{0}+i}=L^{i}\circ L^{n_{0}}\circ\dots\circ L^{n_{0}}
.
∎

Define

E^{uu}(p):=\underset{n\geq 0}{\bigcap}L^{n}C^{u}(Y^{-n}(p))\subset C^{u}(p)
.

Claim 6.3.4
.

E^{uu}
is an
L^{t}
-invariant line bundle, exponentially expanded by
L^{t}
for
t\geq 0
.

Proof.

Indeed, by construction
E^{uu}
is a cone field in the plane field
E^{cu}
on
K
, invariant by
L^{-n}
and exponentially contracted by
L^{-n}
for
n\geq 0
.
Now we know that
L^{-n}
acts isometrically on the direction generated by
Y
, so there exists an
L^{-n}
-invariant direction which is not contracted.
Since
\{L^{-n}\}_{n}
is a family of invertible linear maps in dimension two,
we deduce that
E^{uu}
cannot contain two distinct invariant line fields (these are the same arguments as the proof of Proposition
6.1.2
).
It is therefore a line bundle exponentially contracted by
L^{-n}
.

Let us assume by contradiction that
E_{1}:=E^{uu}(p)
and
E_{2}:=L^{t}E^{uu}(Y^{-t}(p))
are distinct for a certain
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
(we can assume
|t|\leq 1
),
then the families
\{L^{-n}E_{1}\}_{n}
and
\{L^{-n}E_{2}\}_{n}
are two line bundles invariant by
L^{-n}
and exponentially contracted for
n\geq 0
.
Indeed, for the second family, it suffices to write that if
v\in E^{2}
then

L^{-n}v=L^{-t}(L^{-n}v^{\prime})
with
v^{\prime}\in E^{uu}(Y^{-t}(p))

and use the fact that
L^{t}
is uniformly bounded for
|t|\leq 1
and
L^{-n}
contracts exponentially
E^{uu}
.
By the same argument as in the previous paragraph, we have a contradiction because the
{\mathbb{R}}.Y
-direction is an
L^{n}
-invariant direction which is not contracted.
So
E^{uu}
is a line bundle
L^{t}
-invariant for
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
, and exponentially expanded for
t\geq 0
.
Indeed, it suffices to write for
v\in E^{uu}
,

L^{t}v=L^{r}\circ L^{E(t)}v=L^{r}\circ(L^{-E(t)})^{-1}v
(
E(t)
is the integer part of
t
) and use that

L^{r}
is uniformly bounded for
r\in[0,1]
and
L^{-n}
contracts exponentially
E^{uu}
for
n\geq 0
.
∎

We conclude that
E^{uu}
is the strong unstable bundle of
\Lambda
for
Y
.
We show in the same way the existence of the strong stable bundle
E^{ss}
of
\Lambda
for
Y
.
We deduce that
\Lambda
is a hyperbolic set for
Y
.
∎

Now is the final step of the proof of Theorem
1
.

Proof of Theorem
1
.

Let
(P_{0},X_{0},\varphi_{0})
be a filled building block
with a strongly quasi-transverse gluing map.
Let
(P,X,\varphi)
be the normalized strongly isotopic triple given by Proposition
2.1.16
.
Let
\lambda_{0},\epsilon_{0},\delta_{0}
, be the parameters which satisfy Proposition
5.1.4
, and

\psi=\psi_{\lambda_{0},\epsilon_{0},\delta_{0}}\colon{\partial P}\to{\partial P}

the modified gluing map.
According to Claim
5.1.1
, the triple

(P,X,\psi)
is strongly isotopic to
(P,X,\varphi)
thus to
(P_{0},X_{0},\varphi_{0})
, and
P_{\psi}=P/\psi
is a compact manifold of dimension 3 equipped with a vector field
X_{\psi}
of class
{\mathcal{C}}^{1}
induced by
X
on
P_{\psi}
.

Let us show that
X_{\psi}
is an Anosov vector field.
Recall that
{\mathfrak{A}}
and
{\mathfrak{R}}
denotes the union of, respectively, attracting and repelling basic pieces of
\Lambda
.
Denote
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\psi}
and
{\mathfrak{R}}_{\psi}
the projection in
P_{\psi}
. Note that the projection is trivial in the neighborhood of
{\mathfrak{A}}
and
{\mathfrak{R}}
, because uniformly away from the boundary, hence
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\psi}
and
{\mathfrak{R}}_{\psi}
are union of hyperbolic attracting and repelling basic pieces respectively for the flow of
X_{\psi}
.
A filtration adapted to the splitting
\Lambda={\mathfrak{R}}\cup\Lambda_{s}\cup{\mathfrak{A}}
in
P
for the flow of
X
gives a filtration for the flow of
X_{\psi}
in
P_{\psi}
(see Figure
60
).

Figure 60
.
Filtration in
P
adapted for the decomposition
\Lambda={\mathfrak{R}}\cup\Lambda_{s}\cup{\mathfrak{A}}
for the flow
X^{t}

Notation 6.3.5
.

Define
P^{\prime}_{\psi}
the complementary of the filtrating neighborhoods of
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\psi}
and
{\mathfrak{R}}_{\psi}
in
P_{\psi}
for
X_{\psi}
, and
\Lambda^{\prime}_{\psi}
the maximal invariant set of
P^{\prime}_{\psi}
for the flow of
X_{\psi}
.

Let us show the following

Claim 6.3.6
.

The set
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\psi}\,\cup\,\Lambda^{\prime}_{\psi}\,\cup\,{\mathfrak{R}}_{\psi}
is hyperbolic for
X_{\psi}
.

Proof.

It is clear that
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\psi}
and
{\mathfrak{R}}_{\psi}
are hyperbolic sets for
X_{\psi}
because they inherits the hyperbolic structure of
{\mathfrak{A}}
and
{\mathfrak{R}}
for
X
in
P
(the projection restricted to small neighborhoods of
{\mathfrak{A}}
and
{\mathfrak{R}}
is trivial, because the sets are contained in the interior of
P
).
Let us show that
\Lambda^{\prime}_{\psi}
is a hyperbolic set.
Let
S_{\psi}
the section
given by Equation (
6.1
) and
f_{\psi}
the first return map of
X_{\psi}
on
S_{\psi}
.
First remark that
S_{\psi}
intersect every orbit of
\Lambda^{\prime}_{\psi}
in uniformly bounded time.
Indeed the filtration adapted to
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\psi}\cup\Lambda^{\prime}_{\psi}\cup{\mathfrak{R}}_{\psi}
is the projection of a filtration adapted to

{\mathfrak{R}}\cup\Lambda_{s}\cup{\mathfrak{A}}
.
Hence an orbit
\Lambda^{\prime}_{\psi}
is either an orbit of
(\Lambda_{s})_{\psi}
, or crosses
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
, and never escapes to
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\psi}
in the future of
{\mathfrak{R}}_{\psi}
in the past.
It follows that every segment of this orbit will cross
\Sigma_{\psi}\cup P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
, hence
\Sigma_{\psi}\cup(P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}=S_{\psi}
, in uniformly bounded time, .
Hence it suffices to use the fact that
K_{\psi}
is a hyperbolic set for
f_{\psi}
.

Let
TS_{\psi}|_{K_{\psi}}=F^{u}\oplus F^{s}
be the hyperbolic splitting of
S_{\psi}
for
f_{\psi}
given by Proposition
6.2.1
above
K_{\psi}=\Lambda^{\prime}_{\psi}\cap S_{\psi}
.
We define at any point
p\in K_{\psi}
the tangent plane in
T_{p}P_{\psi}
by

E^{cu}(p):=F^{u}(p)\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X_{\psi}(p)
.
Proposition
6.2.1
ensures that this definition does not depend on the choice of a tangent plane
T^{*}\subset T_{p}S_{\psi}
in
p
.
Let
p\in\Lambda^{\prime}_{\psi}
.
Then the orbit of
p
intersects
S_{\psi}
in uniformly bounded time, and let
\tau(p)\geq 0
be the first positive time such that
q=X^{\tau(p)}_{\psi}(p)\in S_{\psi}
.
Define for all
p\in P_{\psi}
,

E^{cu}(p):=(X_{\psi}^{-\tau(p)})_{*}E^{cu}(q)
.
This definition gives a plane field
E^{cu}
at any point of
\Lambda^{\prime}_{\psi}
.
Let us show that

E^{cu}
is a
X_{\psi}^{t}
-invariant plane field, and whose area is exponentially expanded by the differential of the flow of
X_{\psi}^{t}

Indeed, the invariance of
E^{cu}
by the differential of the flow of
X_{\psi}
is ensured by the invariance of
F^{u}
by the first return map
f_{\psi}
on
S_{\psi}
according to Proposition
6.2.1
.
Let
V
be a unit vector field tangent to
F^{u}
at any point of
S_{\psi}
.
By transversality of
S_{\psi}
and
X_{\psi}
, the pair
(V,X_{\psi})
forms a basis of vector fields on the plane field
E^{cu}
at any point of
S_{\psi}
.
The matrix of the differential of
X^{t}_{\psi}
restricted to
E^{cu}
in the basis
(V,X)
is of the form

M_{(V,X_{\psi})}(X^{t}_{\psi})_{*}|_{E^{cu}}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{t}&0\\
b_{t}&1\end{pmatrix}

where
a_{t}
and
b_{t}
are continuous functions on
S_{\psi}
.
By compactness, these functions are uniformly bounded at
t
.
By Proposition
6.2.1
,
there exist constants
C>0
and
\lambda>0
such that
for all
p\in S_{\psi}
, if
\tau
is the time of first return on the section
S_{\psi}
, then

|a_{\tau^{n}(p)}|>C\lambda^{n}
.
The flow returns in uniformly bounded time on
S_{\psi}
, so the differential of
X^{t}_{\psi}
is uniformly bounded between two crossings on the section.
We deduce that
for all
p\in P_{\psi}
and
t\geq 0
, up to change the constant
C>0
, we have

|a_{t}|>C\lambda^{t}
.
So the differential of
X^{t}_{\psi}
expands exponentially the area of
E^{cu}
for all
t\geq 0
.
We similarly show the existence of a plane field
E^{cs}
on
\Lambda^{\prime}_{\psi}
, invariant by the differential of the flow of
X_{\psi}
, transverse to
E^{cu}
and whose area is exponentially contracted by
X^{t}_{\psi}
for
t\geq 0
.
It follows that
\Lambda^{\prime}_{\psi}
is a hyperbolic set (of index
(1,1)
) for the flow of
X_{\psi}
in
P_{\psi}
, from Lemma
6.3.2
.
∎

The existence of a filtration adapted to
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\psi}\,\cup\,{\mathfrak{R}}_{\psi}\,\cup\,\Lambda^{\prime}_{\psi}
implies that the chain-recurrent set of
X_{\psi}
in
P_{\psi}
is contained in

{\mathfrak{A}}_{\psi}\,\cup\,{\mathfrak{R}}_{\psi}\,\cup\,\Lambda^{\prime}_{\psi}
, hence hyperbolic.
It is clear that the dimension of the stable and unstable manifolds of these sets are of constant dimension 2.
To use Mañé Criterion, it remains to show

Claim 6.3.7
.

The stable and unstable manifolds of
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\psi}\,\cup\,{\mathfrak{R}}_{\psi}\,\cup\,\Lambda^{\prime}_{\psi}
intersects transversally

Proof.

We consider orbits
O
which are not contained in
\Lambda_{\psi}
, where the intersection is naturally transverse (via lift in
P
).
Every such orbit intersects at most once
P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
.
We study the following disjoint cases.

(1)

Let
O
be an
X_{\psi}
-orbit in
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{X_{\psi}}({\mathfrak{A}}_{\psi})\cap{\mathcal{W}}^{u}_{X_{\psi}}({\mathfrak{R}}_{\psi})
.
There is a first intersection point
p\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
, and a last intersection point
q=X_{\psi}^{\tau}(p)=f_{0,\psi}^{n}(p)\in P^{\mathrm{in}}_{\psi}
in
O
.
The segment of
X_{\psi}
-orbit in the past of
p
lift to a segment of
X
-orbit, so the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}_{X_{\psi}}
on

coincide with the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}_{\psi}
.
The trace on

is contained in the interior of

according to Proposition

, Item
1
.
Similarly, the segment of
X_{\psi}
-orbit in the future of
q
lift to a segment of
X
-orbit, so the direction tangent to

on

coincide with the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{\psi}
.
The trace on

is disjoint from the closure of
C^{u}_{\psi}
according to Proposition

, Item
1
.
Moreover, we know from Proposition
5.1.4
, Item

that
(f_{0,\psi}^{n})_{*}(C^{u}_{\psi})
is contained in the interior
C^{u}_{\psi}
.
As
f_{0,\psi}^{n}
maps the trace of the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}_{X_{\psi}}
on
p
to the trace of the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}_{X_{\psi}}
on
q
to, it follows that
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{X_{\psi}}
is transverse to
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}_{X_{\psi}}
in
q
, hence everywhere in
O
.

(2)

Let
O
an
X_{\psi}
-orbit in
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{X_{\psi}}(\Lambda^{\prime}_{\psi})\cap{\mathcal{W}}^{u}_{X_{\psi}}({\mathfrak{R}}_{\psi})
.
There is a first intersection point
p\in S_{0,\psi}
.
Remark that this point necessarily belong to
S_{\psi}=S_{0,\psi}{\smallsetminus}(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{0})
.
Indeed, if it belongs to
(P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{0})_{\psi}
, then the orbit of

has crossed
\Sigma_{\psi}
before by Item

of Proposition

, hence it is not the first intersection point.
Moreover

belongs to
K_{\psi}^{+}
.
The direction tangent to
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}_{X_{\psi}}
on
p
coincide with the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}_{\psi}
.
The trace on
S_{\psi}
of the direction tangent to
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{X_{\psi}}
on coincide with
F^{s}
and is contained in the interior of
C^{s}_{\psi}
from Proposition
6.2.1
, Item
1
.
We know in addition that the closure of
C^{s}_{\psi}
is disjoint from
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}_{\psi}
from Proposition
5.1.4
, Item
1
.
It follows that
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{X_{\psi}}
is transverse to
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}_{X_{\psi}}
in
p
, hence everywhere in
O
.

(3)

Let
O
an
X_{\psi}
-orbit in
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{X_{\psi}}({\mathfrak{A}}_{\psi})\cap{\mathcal{W}}^{u}_{X_{\psi}}(\Lambda^{\prime}_{\psi})
.
There is a last intersection point
q\in S_{0,\psi}
.
Remark that this point necessarily belong to
S_{\psi}
, as for the previous item.
Moreover
q

.

on

.

.

They are the only cases to study, because

and

This completes the proof of Theorem
