.

∎

Generalization to partial gluing maps

The paragraph is devoted to state an easy generalization of Theorem
A
for “partial gluing maps”, a similar construction but where we only want to glue a
part
of the boundary components.
More precisely

Definition 6.3.8

(Partial gluing map)
.

A
partial gluing map
of a block
(P,X)
is an involution
\varphi
of an union
{\partial_{+}P}
of connected components of
\partial P
, such that
\varphi
satisfies the condition of a gluing map on
{\partial_{+}P}
, i.e. it pairs connected components of
{\partial_{+}P}
, maps
P^{\mathrm{out}}\cap{\partial_{+}P}
on
P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\partial_{+}P}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}\cap{\partial_{+}P}
on
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}\cap{\partial_{+}P}
respecting the flow orientation.
We say equivalently that
(P,X,\varphi:{\partial_{+}P}\to{\partial_{+}P})
is a partial triple.

We generalize the Gluing Theorem
1
with the following statement.
The proof is essentially the same, except that we deal with the hyperbolicity of the orbit of the new maximal invariant set of
(P/\varphi,X_{\varphi})
which is not the entire set of orbits.

Theorem 6.3.9
.

Let
(P,X,\varphi:{\partial_{+}P}\to{\partial_{+}P})
be a filled building block, equipped with a
partial
strongly quasi-transverse gluing map.
Then there exists a partial triple
(P^{\prime},X^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})
strongly isotopic to
(P,X,\varphi)
such that
P^{\prime}/\varphi^{\prime}
is a smooth manifold with boundary equipped with a vector field
X^{\prime}_{\varphi^{\prime}}
induced by
X^{\prime}
, and the pair
(P^{\prime}/\varphi^{\prime},X^{\prime}_{\varphi^{\prime}})
is a building block, whose boundary is
{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\partial_{+}P}
.

6.4.
Criterion of transitivity

Recall that a building block
(P,X)
is said to be
transitive
if the maximal invariant set
\Lambda
is a transitive set for the flow of
X
.
An Anosov vector field
Y
on a closed manifold
{\mathcal{M}}
is said to be
transitive
if
{\mathcal{M}}
is a transitive set for the flow of
Y
.
In this subsection, we give a criterion analogous to
[
BBY17
, Proposition 1.6]
, which allows us to determine the transitivity of an Anosov flow, or a building block, obtained by gluing the boundary of a building block under the hypothesis of Theorem
1
.
Let
(P,X)
be a building block and
\Lambda
be the maximal invariant set of
X
in
P
, and let
\Lambda_{1},\dots,\Lambda_{n}
be the decomposition of the non-wandering set of
\Lambda
into
basic pieces
(
[
Sma67
]
).

Definition 6.4.1

(Graph of a triple)
.

Let
G=G(P,X,\varphi)
be the directed graph having as vertex the basic pieces
i=\Lambda_{i}
and a oriented edge
(i,j)
if :

–

either
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}(\Lambda_{i})\cap{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(\Lambda_{j})\neq\varnothing
,

–

either
\varphi({\mathcal{W}}^{u}(\Lambda_{i}))\cap{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(\Lambda_{j})\neq\varnothing
,

–

either
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}(\Lambda_{i})\cap\varphi({\mathcal{W}}^{s}(\Lambda_{j}))\neq\varnothing
.

We have the following criterion.

Proposition 6.4.2
.

(1)

If
(P_{0},X_{0},\varphi_{0})
and
(P_{1},X_{1},\varphi_{1})
are two strongly isotopic (partial) triples, then the graphs
G(P_{0},X_{0},\varphi_{0})
and
G(P_{1},X_{1},\varphi_{1})
are isomorphic.

(2)

Let
(P,X,\varphi)
be a triple such that
\varphi
is a strongly quasi-transverse gluing map and
X_{\varphi}
is an Anosov vector field on
P/\varphi
.
If
G(P,X,\varphi)
is
strongly connected
11
11

11

each pair of vertex can be connected by a oriented path of edges
, then
X_{\varphi}
is transitive.

(3)

Let
(P,X,\varphi)
be a partial triple such that
\varphi
is a strongly quasi-transverse gluing map and
(P/\varphi,X_{\varphi})
is a building block.
If
G(P,X,\varphi)
is strongly connected, then
X_{\varphi}
is transitive.

Notice that it is not assumed in the criterion of Proposition
6.4.2
that the building block is saddle or filled.

Proof.

Up to make an orbit equivalence of one of the two blocks,
(P_{0},X_{0})
and
(P_{1},X_{1})
have a common minimal continuation (Proposition
1.4.4
, Item
1
).
It follows that the maximal invariant set
\Lambda_{X_{1}}
of
X_{1}
and
\Lambda_{X_{2}}
of
X_{2}
is the same.
Moreover, there exists a homeomorphism
h:{\partial P}_{1}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}\to{\partial P}_{0}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
which maps the pair
({\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{X_{1}},(\varphi_{1})_{*}({\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{X_{1}}))
to the pair
({\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{X_{0}},(\varphi_{0})_{*}({\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{X_{0}}))
and the pair
({\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{X_{1}},(\varphi_{1})_{*}({\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{X_{1}}))
to the pair
({\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{X_{0}},(\varphi_{0})_{*}({\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{X_{0}}))
(Definition
1.4.9
, Item
3
).
This shows that the graphs
G(P_{1},X_{1},\varphi_{1})
and
G(P_{0},X_{0},\varphi_{0})
are isomorphic and the first item is true.

Let us show the second item.
Let
P_{\varphi}:=P/\varphi
be the quotient manifold,
\pi_{\varphi}\colon P\to P_{\varphi}
be the projection, and
\Lambda_{\varphi}:=\pi_{\varphi}(\Lambda)
.
It is a hyperbolic invariant compact for the flow of
X_{\varphi}
in
P_{\varphi}
.
Note
{\mathcal{L}}={\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}\cup{\mathcal{O}}_{*}\cup{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
the boundary lamination on
{\partial P}
.
Let
\Lambda_{1},\dots,\Lambda_{n}
be the basic pieces of the Smale decomposition of the non wandering set of
\Lambda
.
These are the maximal transitive hyperbolic compact sets of
\Lambda
.
Let
\Lambda_{\varphi,i}=\pi_{\varphi}(\Lambda_{i})
be the projection of the basic pieces of
\Lambda
onto
\Lambda_{\varphi}
.
These are transitive hyperbolic sets for the flow of
X_{\varphi}
.
The stable manifold of
\Lambda_{\varphi,i}
contains the projection of

{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(\Lambda_{i})\cup\varphi({\mathcal{W}}^{s}(\Lambda_{i}))

and its unstable manifold contains the projection of
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}(\Lambda_{i})\cup\varphi({\mathcal{W}}^{u}(\Lambda_{i}))
.
The strong connectedness of the graph
G(P,X,\varphi)
is equivalent to the fact that the
\Lambda_{\varphi,i}
are all connected by a cycle: for all
i,j
, there exists a sequence
i_{0}=i,\dots,i_{n}=j
such that

{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(\Lambda_{\varphi,i_{k}})\cap{\mathcal{W}}^{u}(\Lambda_{\varphi,i_{k+1}})\neq{\varnothing}
(
i_{n+1}=i_{0}
).
We deduce that they form a single basic piece (see
[
Sma67
]
), in other words
\Lambda_{\varphi}
is transitive.
Finally, let
O
be an orbit of the flow of
X_{\varphi}
which is not contained in
\Lambda_{\varphi}
.
Then

Claim 6.4.3
.

The stable manifold
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(O)
intersects
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{\varphi}
and the unstable manifold
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}(O)
intersects
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\varphi}
.

Indeed, if
O
is not contained in
\Lambda_{\varphi}
, then it intersects transversally
P^{\mathrm{out}}_{\varphi}
.
The surface
P^{\mathrm{out}}_{\varphi}
is foliated by a pair
(f^{s},f^{u})
of transverse foliations which are the intersections of the stable
{\mathcal{F}}^{s}
and unstable
{\mathcal{F}}^{u}
laminations of the Anosov vector field
X_{\varphi}
on
P_{\varphi}
.
Moreover,
f^{u}
contains the lamination
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{\varphi}
and
f^{s}
contains
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\varphi}
.
The stable manifold
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(O)
intersects
P^{\mathrm{out}}_{\varphi}
along a leaf
l\in f^{s}
.
The leaf
l
intersects the closure of a connected component
R
of the complementary
P^{\mathrm{out}}_{\varphi}{\smallsetminus}({\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\varphi}\cup{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{\varphi})
, i.e. the projection of
P^{\mathrm{out}}{\smallsetminus}(\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}})
.
By strong transversality of the gluing map
\varphi
, the closure of
R
is a compact rectangle bounded by two horizontal segments of leaves of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{\varphi}
and two vertical segments of leaves of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{\varphi}
.
It follows that the segment of leaf of
l
crosses
R
horizontally, and therefore intersects
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{\varphi}
.
The proof for the unstable manifold of
O
is symmetric.
Therefore, each orbit
O
of
X_{\varphi}
has its stable manifold intersecting
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}(\Lambda_{\varphi})
and its unstable manifold intersecting
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(\Lambda_{\varphi})
.
We conclude that the manifold
P_{\varphi}
is a unique basic piece, and therefore the Anosov flow
X_{\varphi}
on
P_{\varphi}
is transitive.

An analogous argument works for the last Item, by considering the orbits in the new maximal invariant set of the building block.
∎

7.
Building block with prescribed boundary lamination

In
[
BBY17
, Theorem 1.10]
, it is shown that any Morse-Smale foliation (in other words, a quasi-Morse-Smale foliation without marked leaves) on the torus can be realized (up to topological equivalence) as a boundary foliation of an attracting transitive orientable Béguin-Bonatti-Yu block
(P,X)
, in other words such that the maximal invariant set
\Lambda
is a transitive attractor for the flow of
X
.
The building blocks considered in this section will be connected and orientable.

Definition 7.0.1

(Topological equivalence of laminations)
.

Two laminations
{\mathcal{L}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{L}}_{2}
on oriented surfaces
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
are said to be topologically equivalent if there exists a homeomorphism
h:S_{1}\to S_{2}
which preserves the orientation and such that
h_{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{1}={\mathcal{L}}_{2}
.

In this section we show the following analogous main:

Proposition 7.0.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
be two quasi-Morse-Smale foliations on an oriented torus
S_{1}
and an oriented torus
S_{2}
respectively, such that
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
have the same nonzero number of marked leaves.
Then there exists
(P,X)
a transitive filled building block, with
P
oriented and connected, such that
\partial P
is the union of two torus
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
quasi-transverse to
X
, and the boundary lamination
{\mathcal{L}}_{X}
restricted to
T_{i}
extends into a foliation topologically equivalent to
{\mathcal{F}}_{i}
.

Remark 7.0.3
.

Recall that an orientation of
P
induces a canonical orientation on the boundary
{\partial P}
.
The proposition states that there exists a homeomorphism
h_{i}:S_{i}\to T_{i}
which maps
{\mathcal{F}}_{i}
to a foliation containing the boundary lamination and preserves the orientation for
i=1,2
.

Recall that the boundary lamination
{\mathcal{L}}_{X}
of a filled block is, by definition, filling, so it extends to a foliation which is unique up to topological equivalence (the connected components of the complementary of the lamination are strips, Definition
1.2.12
).
We will also show the main Theorem
C
, which states that any pair of quasi-transverse foliations on the torus can be realized as the trace of the stable and unstable foliation of a transitive Anosov flow on a quasi-transverse embedded torus.

Section summary

The section is organized as follows.

•

In a preliminary Subsection
7.1
, we give a simple result which allow us to build new building blocks from partial gluing of building blocks, without the condition of being
filled
and equipped with a
strongly quasi-transverse
gluing map. It is the particular case where the gluing pattern has “no cycle”.

•

In Subsection
7.2
, we study quasi-Morse-Smale prefoliation (Definition
1.2.4
).
We associate to every quasi-Morse-Smale prefoliation a combinatorial type (Definition
7.2.9
and
7.2.9
)
It is a complete invariant of the topological equivalence class of a quasi-Morse-Smale foliation (Proposition
7.2.13
).

•

In Subsection
7.3
, we show a preliminary result which states that any quasi-Morse-Smale foliation can be realize (up to topological equivalence) as the boundary foliation of a filled
non-transitive
building block (Proposition
7.3.1
).
We first construct a building block
(P,X)
whose boundary consist of the union of two quasi-transverse tori
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
, containing periodic orbits whose number and orientation is compatible with the given combinatorial type and which constitute the boundary lamination on
T_{i}
, and a collection of tori
T^{\mathrm{in}}
and
T^{\mathrm{out}}
transverse to the vector field
X
(Lemma
7.3.2
).
We then glue attracting and repelling building blocks along the boundary tori
T^{\mathrm{in}}
and
T^{\mathrm{out}}
with prescribed boundary foliation (Lemma
7.3.4
).
This gluing operation must be done in a way to induce foliations on the quasi-transverse tori whose combinatorial type is the one prescribed.
We use properties of building blocks obtained by partial gluing with no cycle (Subsection
7.1
).

•

In Subsection
7.4
we show a general result which allows, from a non-transitive building block
(P,X)
satisfying a condition on its Smale’s graph, to create a filled transitive building block
(P^{\prime},X^{\prime})
and such that the boundary laminations of
(P,X)
and
(P^{\prime},X^{\prime})
extends to topologically equivalent foliations.
We use the
Blow-up – Excise – Glue
surgeries coming from
[
BBY17
, Section 8]
.
The idea is to create a collection of transverse boundaries by bifurcation
Derived from Anosov
on periodic orbits, then excising small tubular neighborhoods whose boundaries are transverse to the flow, and this in each of the non-trivial basic pieces of
(P,X)
.
We then glue these boundaries back together so as to create cycles in the graph
G
associated to the triple (Definition
6.4.1
).
We then use the transitivity criterion (Proposition
6.4.2
).
We show that Proposition
7.0.2
follows from this general result and the result of the previous subsection.

•

In Subsection
7.5
, we show Theorem
C
which allows us to realize any pair of quasi-transverse foliations on the torus as the trace of the stable and unstable foliation of a transitive Anosov flow on a quasi-transverse embedded torus.
It is a corollary of Proposition
7.0.2
and of the Gluing Theorem
1
.

7.1.
Partial gluing without cycle

There is an easy way to construct blocks from partial gluing (Definition
6.3.8
) of building blocks without the condition of being
filled
, and equipped with a
strongly quasi-transverse
gluing map.
This is the particular case where there is
no cycle
in the gluing pattern, and the connected component of the boundary glued together are transverse to the vector field.
It is
[
BBY17
, Proposition 1.1]
, that we reformulate here.
The argument is then elementary, and relies essentially on the
\lambda
-lemma.

Proposition 7.1.1
.

Let
(P,X)
be a building block and
\varphi:{\partial_{+}P}\to{\partial_{+}P}
a partial gluing map of
(P,X)
, such that:

(1)

{\partial_{+}P}
is a union of connected components of
\partial P
transverse to
X
,

(2)

each orbit of the flow of
X
in
P
intersects at most once
{\partial_{+}P}
(we say that the partial gluing map
\varphi
has
no cycle
),

(3)

\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{L}}
is transverse to
{\mathcal{L}}
on
{\partial_{+}P}
.

Then up to modify the partial triple
(P,X,\varphi)
by strong isotopy, the vector field
X
induces a vector field
X_{\varphi}
on the manifold
P_{\varphi}:=P/\varphi
, such that the couple
(P_{\varphi},X_{\varphi})
is a building block.

Before showing the proposition, let us state a number of properties for such building block in the following lemma.
We express the boundary, maximal invariant set, and lamination of a block
(N,Y)
obtain by a partial gluing of a block
(P,X)
without cycle.

Lemma 7.1.2
.

Let
(P,X,\varphi:{\partial_{+}P}\to{\partial_{+}P})
be a partial triple without cycle.
Suppose the manifold
N:=P/\varphi
is provided with a vector field
Y
of class
{\mathcal{C}}^{1}
induced by
X
. Then

(1)

(boundary)

\partial N={\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\partial_{+}P}

(2)

(maximal invariant set)

\Lambda_{Y}=\pi_{\varphi}\left(\Lambda_{X}\cup\underset{t\in{\mathbb{R}}}{\bigcup}X^{t}({\mathcal{L}}_{X}\cap\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{X})\right)

Moreover, if
(N,Y)
is a building block and if
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{Y}
,
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}_{Y}
denotes the stable and unstable manifolds of
\Lambda_{Y}
and,
{\mathcal{L}}_{Y}
the boundary lamination of
(N,Y)
, then

(3)

(invariant manifolds)

•

{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{Y}=\pi_{\varphi}\left({\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{X}\,\cup\,\underset{t\leq 0}{\bigcup}X^{t}\left(\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{X}\right)\right)

•

{\mathcal{W}}^{u}_{Y}=\pi_{\varphi}\left({\mathcal{W}}^{u}_{X}\,\cup\,\underset{t\geq 0}{\bigcup}X^{t}\left(\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{X}\right)\right)

(4)

(boundary lamination)

{\mathcal{L}}_{Y}={\mathcal{L}}_{X}\cup f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}{}_{*}(\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{X})\cup f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}^{-1}{}_{*}(\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{X})

For simplicity, we omit the domain where the previous expressions make sense. In other words

•

\varphi(*)
means
\varphi(*\cap{\partial_{+}P})
,

•

f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}(*)
means
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}(*\cap P^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}))
,

•

f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}^{-1}(*)
means
f_{\mathrm{out},\mathrm{in}}(*\cap(P^{\mathrm{out}}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}))
, etc.

Proof.

(1)

It is obvious.

(2)

It is sufficient to see that if
\tilde{p}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{X}\cap P^{\mathrm{in}}={\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{X}
, then the positive orbit by the flow
X^{t}
is defined for all
t\geq 0
, and if
\tilde{p}\in(\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{X})\cap P^{\mathrm{in}}
, then
\tilde{q}=\varphi(\tilde{p})\in{\mathcal{L}}_{X}\cap P^{\mathrm{out}}={\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{X}
and the negative orbit of
\tilde{q}
by the flow
X^{t}
is defined for all
t\leq 0
.
We deduce the inclusion
\supset
by projection in
N=\pi_{\varphi}(P)
.
Suppose now that there is an orbit
\gamma
of
\Lambda_{Y}
which is not in
\pi_{\varphi}(\Lambda_{X})
.
Such an orbit crosses transversely the surface
\pi_{\varphi}({\partial_{+}P})
in a point
p
.
Let
\tilde{p}
be the lift of
p
in
P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\partial_{+}P}
.
By the no cycle assumption, the positive
X
-orbit of
\tilde{p}
never intersects
{\partial_{+}P}
again.
If it intersects
\partial P
, by projection in
N
we conclude the same for
\gamma
, which contradicts that
\gamma\in\Lambda_{Y}
.
Hence
\tilde{p}\in{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{X}
, and the positive
X
-orbit of
\tilde{p}
is the positive
Y
-orbit of
p
(up to trivial lift in
N
).
Analogously, if
\tilde{q}=\varphi(\tilde{p})
is the lift of
p
in
P^{\mathrm{out}}\cap{\partial_{+}P}
, the negative
X
-orbit of
\tilde{q}
cannot intersects
\partial P
, hence belongs to
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{X}
, and the negative
X
orbit of
\tilde{q}
is the negative
Y
orbit of
p
(up to trivial lift in
N
).

(3)

It is sufficient to use the expression of the maximal invariant set from the previous item.

(4)

It is sufficient to use the expression for invariant manifolds from the previous item.

∎

Proof of Proposition
7.1.1
.

Item
1
ensures that
\varphi
is a dynamical gluing map and
P_{\varphi}
is a compact manifold of dimension 3 with a vector field
X_{\varphi}
induced by
X
.
Let
(N,Y):=(P_{\varphi},X_{\varphi})
and denote
\Lambda_{Y}
the maximal invariant set.
Let
p\in\Lambda_{Y}
.
Then either
p\in\pi_{\varphi}(\Lambda_{X})
, or the orbit of
p
by the flow of
Y
transversely intersects the set
\pi_{\varphi}({\partial_{+}P})=:({\partial_{+}P})_{\varphi}
(Lemma
7.1.2
).
By Item
2
of Proposition
7.1.1
, this intersection is unique.
In the first case, we have a decomposition of the tangent space given by the projection of the hyperbolic splitting of
\Lambda_{X}
for
X
.
In the second case,
p\in\Lambda_{Y}\cap({\partial_{+}P})_{\varphi}
, and let
\tilde{p}\in P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\partial_{+}P}
be a lift of
p
in
P
.
Then
\tilde{p}\in{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}_{X}\cap\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}_{X}
(Lemma
7.1.2
, Item
2
) and we define the following tangent planes on
\tilde{p}
:

(7.1)

E^{cs}(\tilde{p})=T{\mathcal{L}}_{X}^{s}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X,\noindent\quad\mbox{ and }\noindent\quad E^{cu}(\tilde{p})=T\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{X}^{u}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X

We obtain by projection in
P_{\varphi}
the data of two tangent planes
E^{cu}_{Y}
,
E^{cs}_{Y}
on the set
\Lambda_{Y}\cap({\partial_{+}P})_{\varphi}
tangent to the vector field
Y
.
By Item
3
,
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
is transverse to
\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
on
{\partial_{+}P}
so the planes
E^{cu}
and
E^{cs}
are transverse to each other.
We push these tangent planes by the flow of
X_{\varphi}
on
\Lambda_{Y}{\smallsetminus}(\Lambda_{X})_{\varphi}
in the following way.
If
q\in\Lambda_{Y}{\smallsetminus}(\Lambda_{X})_{\varphi}
, there exists a unique
p=Y^{t}(q)\in({\partial_{+}P})_{\varphi}
and we define

(7.2)

E_{Y}^{cu}(p)=Y^{-t}_{*}E_{Y}^{cu}(Y^{t}(q)),\noindent\quad\mbox{ and }\noindent\quad E_{Y}^{cs}(p)=Y^{-t}_{*}E_{Y}^{cs}(Y^{t}(q))

This defines an
Y^{t}
-invariant collection of tangent planes of
TN
on
\Lambda_{Y}{\smallsetminus}(\Lambda_{X})_{\varphi}
.
Let us show that the area of
E^{cu}_{Y}
is exponentially expanded by
Y^{t}
for
t\geq 0
.
Let
p\in({\partial_{+}P})_{\varphi}\cap\Lambda_{Y}
, and
v\in E^{cu}_{Y}(p)
be a vector tangent to
E^{cu}
and transverse to
X
(uniformly in
p
).
Let
\tilde{p}\in P^{\mathrm{in}}\cap{\partial_{+}P}
and
\tilde{v}\in E^{cu}(\tilde{p})
be lifts in
P
.
Note that
\tilde{p}\in{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
.
By definition of
E^{cu}(\tilde{p})
in Equation (
7.1
),
\tilde{v}
is tangent to
\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
and transverse to
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
.
The positive
X
-orbit of
\tilde{p}
accumulates on the maximal invariant set
\Lambda
, hence enters in uniformly bounded time a neighborhood
{\mathcal{U}}
of
\Lambda
where the flow is hyperbolic (we extend the hyperbolic splitting by continuity on
{\mathcal{U}}
) and the vector
\tilde{v}
is (uniformly) transverse to the weak stable direction
E^{ss}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X
in
\tilde{p}
.
According to the
\lambda
-lemma,
X^{t}
exponentially expands the norm of the vector
\tilde{v}
for
t\geq 0
.
Projecting into
P_{\varphi}
it follows that
Y_{t}
exponentially expands the norm of
v
for
t\geq 0
.
Let us now look at the effect on the norm of the vector pushed backward by the flow of
Y
.
The vector
\varphi_{*}(v)
is tangent to
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
, so to
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}_{X}
in
\varphi(\tilde{p})\in P^{\mathrm{out}}
.
Note that
\varphi(\tilde{p})\in{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
.
The negative orbit of
\varphi(\tilde{p})
accumulates on the maximal invariant set
\Lambda
, hence enters in uniformly bounded time the neighborhood
{\mathcal{U}}
, and the vector
\tilde{v}
is transverse to the weak stable direction
E^{ss}\oplus{\mathbb{R}}.X
in
\varphi(\tilde{p})
.
According to the
\lambda
-lemma,
X^{t}
exponentially contracts the norm of the vector
\tilde{v}
for
t\leq 0
.
Projecting into
P_{\varphi}
it follows that
Y^{t}
exponentially contracts the norm of
v
for
t\leq 0
.
Since
Y^{t}
acts isometrically on the direction
{\mathbb{R}}.Y\subset E^{cu}
, we deduce that the area of the plane field
E^{cu}_{Y}
is exponentially expanded by
Y^{t}
for
t\geq 0
.
We show in the same way that the area of
E^{cs}_{Y}
is exponentially contracted in the future, and we conclude with Lemma
6.3.2
.
∎

7.2.
Combinatorial type of quasi-Morse-Smale prefoliation

We refer to Subsection
1.2
for general facts about quasi-Morse-Smale laminations and boundary lamination of building block.
Let
{\mathcal{L}}
be a quasi-Morse-Smale lamination on a closed surface
S
,
\Gamma
the compact leaves and
\Gamma_{*}
the marked compact leaves with
{\mathcal{L}}
, and
S{\smallsetminus}\Gamma_{*}=S^{\mathrm{in}}\cup S^{\mathrm{out}}
the (in,out)-splitting of
{\mathcal{L}}
(Definition
1.2.4
).

Definition 7.2.1

(Dynamical orientation)
.

There exists an orientation of the elements of
\Gamma
such that
the holonomy of each compact leaf is expanding on
S^{\mathrm{in}}
and contracting on
S^{\mathrm{out}}
.
We say that this is a
dynamical orientation
of
\Gamma
.
We say that the lamination
{\mathcal{L}}
equipped with a dynamical orientation of the compact leaves
\Gamma
is a
dynamically oriented lamination
.

Remark 7.2.2
.

In the case where the set
\Gamma_{*}
is empty,
we have either
S=S^{\mathrm{in}}
, or
S=S^{\mathrm{out}}
.
In the first case, the dynamical orientation is such that the holonomy of all compact leaves is expanding.
In the second case, the dynamical orientation is such that the holonomy of all compact leaves is contracting.

Remark 7.2.3
.

Such a lamination can a priori contain isolated compact leaves, and there are then several dynamical orientations.
In the following two cases, there is a canonical dynamical orientation.

•

If
{\mathcal{L}}
is a filling lamination (Definition
1.2.12
) or a foliation, then the dynamical orientation of the compact leaves
\Gamma_{*}
is unique, because each compact leaf
\gamma
is accumulated on both sides by non-compact leaves of
{\mathcal{L}}
.

•

If
{\mathcal{L}}={\mathcal{L}}_{X}
is the boundary lamination of a building block
(P,X)
, then it has a canonical dynamical orientation.
Recall that
{\mathcal{L}}={\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}\cup{\mathcal{O}}_{*}\cup{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
, with
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}={\mathcal{W}}^{s}\cap P^{\mathrm{in}}
, and
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}={\mathcal{W}}^{u}\cap P^{\mathrm{out}}
.
If
\gamma
is a compact leaf of
{\mathcal{L}}
, then:

–

Either
\gamma={\mathcal{O}}_{i}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
is a periodic orbit of
X
, and it is oriented by the flow.

–

Either
\gamma\in{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{in}}
, then it is a circle transverse to the orbits of
X
contained in a stable separatrix of a periodic orbit
O
.
There exists a unique orientation of
\gamma
such that
\gamma
is freely homotopic to the oriented orbit
O
in
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(O)
.

–

Either
\gamma\in{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{out}}
, then it is a circle transverse to the orbits of
X
contained in an unstable separatrix of a periodic orbit
O
.
There exists a unique orientation of
\gamma
such that
\gamma
is freely homotopic to the oriented orbit
O
in
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}(O)
.

We refer to the proof of Lemma
2.1.11
.
This orientation is a dynamical orientation of
\gamma
as a compact leaf of a quasi-Morse-Smale lamination in the sense of Definition
7.2.1
(see the proof of Proposition
1.2.6
).

Lemma 7.2.4
.

Let
{\mathcal{L}}
be a dynamically oriented quasi-Morse-Smale prefoliation on a torus
T
.
Let
\Gamma_{\mathcal{L}}
be the compact leaves,
\Gamma_{{\mathcal{L}},*}
the marked compact leaves, and
T^{\mathrm{in}}_{\mathcal{L}}\cup T^{\mathrm{out}}_{\mathcal{L}}
the (in,out)-splitting of
T
for
{\mathcal{L}}
.
There exists a dynamically oriented quasi-Morse-Smale foliation
{\mathcal{F}}
on
T
, which contains
{\mathcal{L}}
as a sublamination and such that, if we denote
\Gamma_{\mathcal{F}}
the compact leaves,
\Gamma_{{\mathcal{F}},*}
the marked compact leaves, and
T^{\mathrm{in}}_{\mathcal{F}}\cup T^{\mathrm{out}}_{\mathcal{F}}
the (in,out)-splitting of
T
for
{\mathcal{F}}
, then

(1)

\Gamma_{\mathcal{F}}=\Gamma_{\mathcal{L}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathcal{F}},*}=\Gamma_{{\mathcal{L}},*}
(as a set of oriented closed curves)

(2)

T^{\mathrm{in}}_{\mathcal{L}}=T^{\mathrm{in}}_{\mathcal{F}}
and
T^{\mathrm{out}}_{\mathcal{L}}=T^{\mathrm{out}}_{\mathcal{F}}

Proof.

According to Proposition
1.2.11
, a connected component of
T{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}
is either homeomorphic to an annulus bounded by compact leaves (possibly the same one), or it is a strip (Definition
1.2.12
), in other words homeomorphic to
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
and bordered by two noncompact leaves asymptotic to each other at both end (Figure
10
).
Let
C
be a connected component of
T{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}
.
If
C
is a strip, then we extend the lamination
{\mathcal{L}}
on
C
by a trivial foliation
{\mathcal{F}}
on
C
by (non-compact) leaves going from one end of the strip to the other.
If
C
is an annulus, let
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
be the compact leaves of
{\mathcal{L}}
which border
C
, equipped with the dynamical orientation.
The component
C
is either contained in
T^{\mathrm{in}}_{\mathcal{L}}
or in
T^{\mathrm{out}}_{\mathcal{L}}
.
Extend
{\mathcal{L}}
by a foliation
{\mathcal{F}}
on
C
, without compact leaf, such that each leaf of
{\mathcal{F}}
accumulates at both ends on a compact leaf
\gamma_{i}
, and such that the holonomy of the oriented compact leaf
\gamma_{i}
of the boundary of
C
for this foliation is expanding if
C
is contained in
T^{\mathrm{in}}_{\mathcal{L}}
, contracting if
C
is contained in
T^{\mathrm{out}}_{\mathcal{L}}
.
The foliation
{\mathcal{F}}
thus constructed satisfies Lemma
7.2.4
.
∎

Using the Poincaré-Hopf theorem, we deduce:

Corollary 7.2.5
.

Let
{\mathcal{L}}
be a quasi-Morse-Smale prefoliation on the torus
T
.
The compact leaves of
{\mathcal{L}}
are non-contractible in
T
, and they are pairwise freely homotopic in
T
as
non-oriented
closed curves.

In particular, there is a cyclic order of the elements of
\Gamma_{\mathcal{L}}
on
T
.
If
T
is oriented, then giving a dynamically oriented compact “first” leaf determines a cyclic order.

Definition 7.2.6

(Geometric enumeration)
.

Let
{\mathcal{L}}
be a dynamically oriented prefoliation on the oriented torus
T
.
We will say that
\{\gamma_{0},\dots,\gamma_{n-1}\}
is a
geometric enumeration
of the compact leaves of
{\mathcal{L}}
if the leaf
\gamma_{i+1}
is the next leaf of
\gamma_{i}
for the order determined by the dynamical orientation of
\gamma_{0}
and the orientation of
T
.

There are as many geometric enumerations as there are compact leaves.

Definition 7.2.7

(Abstract combinatorial type)
.

An abstract combinatorial type
\sigma
is a map

\sigma\colon{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}}\;\longrightarrow\;\{\rightarrow{},\leftarrow{}\}\times\{\uparrow{},\downarrow{}\}\times\{\rightarrow{},\leftarrow{}\}

satisfying the following two conditions:

(1)

\sigma(0)=(*,\uparrow,*)

(2)

\sigma(i)=(*,*,\rightarrow{})\iff\sigma(i+1)=(\leftarrow{},*,*)
for all
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Notation 7.2.8
.

Define the following alphabets by
{\mathscr{A}}_{H}:=\{\rightarrow{},\leftarrow{}\}
and
{\mathscr{A}}_{O}:=\{\uparrow{},\downarrow{}\}
.
If
\sigma\colon{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\mathscr{A}}_{H}\times{\mathscr{A}}_{O}\times{\mathscr{A}}_{H}
is an abstract combinatorial type, we denote its three components by
\sigma=(\sigma_{g},\sigma_{o},\sigma_{d})
,
12
12

12

for left, orientation, right (see Remark
7.2.11
)

and we denote
-\sigma:=(\sigma_{g},-\sigma_{o},\sigma_{d})
where the sign
-
means that we reverse the direction of the arrow.

Definition 7.2.9

(Combinatorial type of a quasi-Morse-Smale prefoliation)
.

Let
{\mathcal{L}}
be a dynamically oriented quasi-Morse-Smale prefoliation on the oriented torus
T
.
Let
\Gamma=\{\gamma_{0},\dots,\gamma_{n-1}\}
be a geometric enumeration of the compact leaves and
T{\smallsetminus}\Gamma_{*}=T^{\mathrm{in}}\cup T^{\mathrm{out}}
be the (in,out)-splitting of
T
for
{\mathcal{L}}
.
Let the map

\sigma=\sigma_{\mathcal{L}}\colon{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}}\;\longrightarrow\;\{\rightarrow{},\leftarrow{}\}\times\{\uparrow{},\downarrow{}\}\times\{\rightarrow{},\leftarrow{}\}

defined by:

(1)

\sigma(i)=(*,\uparrow,*)
if and only if the oriented leaf
\gamma_{i}
is freely homotopic to the oriented leaf
\gamma_{0}
.

(2)

\sigma(i)=(\rightarrow,*,*)
if and only if the left (local) side of
\gamma_{i}
is contained in
T^{\mathrm{out}}

13
13

13

the dynamical orientation of
\gamma_{0}
and the orientation of
T
determines a left and a right side for each compact leaf

(3)

\sigma(i)=(*,*,\leftarrow)
if and only if the right (local) side of
\gamma_{i}
is contained in
T^{\mathrm{out}}

We say that
\sigma
is the
combinatorial type of
{\mathcal{L}}
on
T
.

Example 7.2.10
.

For example, a combinatorial type of the foliation of Figure
5
is

\leftarrow\uparrow\rightarrow;\noindent\quad\leftarrow\downarrow\rightarrow;\noindent\quad\leftarrow\uparrow\leftarrow;\noindent\quad\rightarrow\uparrow\rightarrow

where we give the list of the values of
\sigma
ordered from
i=0
to
i=3
following the figure from left to right.

Remark 7.2.11
.

If the leaf
\gamma_{i}
is accumulated on the left by the lamination
{\mathcal{L}}
, then Item
13
is equivalent to saying that the holonomy of
{\mathcal{L}}
to the left of
\gamma_{i}
is contracting for the dynamical orientation of
\gamma_{i}
.
Similarly, if the leaf
\gamma_{i}
is accumulated on the right by the lamination
{\mathcal{L}}
, then Item
3
is equivalent to saying that the holonomy of
{\mathcal{L}}
to the right of
\gamma_{i}
is contracting for the dynamical orientation of
\gamma_{i}
.
This justifies the use of arrow symbols, and the notation
{\mathscr{A}}_{H}
for Holonomy and
{\mathscr{A}}_{O}
for Orientation.

Claim 7.2.12
.

The map
\sigma_{\mathcal{L}}
is an abstract combinatorial type.
We have the following relations, via the identification
\Gamma\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}}

(1)

\Gamma_{*}=\{\sigma_{g}=\sigma_{d}\}

(2)

\Gamma\cap T^{\mathrm{in}}=\{\sigma=(\leftarrow{},*,\rightarrow{})\}

(3)

\Gamma\cap T^{\mathrm{out}}=\{\sigma=(\rightarrow{},*,\leftarrow{})\}

Proof.

It is clear that
\sigma_{\mathcal{L}}(0)=(*,\uparrow,*)
by definition.
Let
A
be a connected component of
T{\smallsetminus}\Gamma_{\mathcal{L}}
.
It is an annulus bounded by two compact leaves
\gamma_{i}
and
\gamma_{i+1}
, such that
\gamma_{i+1}
is to the right of
\gamma_{i}
for the orientation of
A
induced by the orientation of
T
.
The annulus
A
is either contained in
T^{\mathrm{in}}
or in
T^{\mathrm{out}}
.
It follows that
\sigma(i)=(*,*,\rightarrow)
if and only if
\sigma(i+1)=(\leftarrow,*,*)
.
So
\sigma_{\mathcal{L}}
is an abstract combinatorial type.
The following items follow directly from the definition.
∎

For an abstract combinatorial type
\sigma
, we will note
\Gamma_{*,\sigma}:=\{\sigma_{g}=\sigma_{d}\}
, and we will call it by use of language the set of
marked leaves
of
\sigma
.
It follows from Definition
7.2.7
that this set is of even cardinal.
Recall that a quasi-Morse-Smale lamination
{\mathcal{L}}
whose set of marked leaves is empty is said to be a Morse-Smale lamination (Remark
1.2.5
).
Similarly, an abstract combinatorial type
\sigma
whose set of marked leaves is empty will be a Morse-Smale combinatorial type.
If it is the combinatorial type of a Morse-Smale lamination
{\mathcal{L}}
on the torus
T
, then there are two possible cases:

•

either
\sigma(i)=(\rightarrow,*,\leftarrow)
for all
i
, this case corresponds to
T=T^{\mathrm{in}}
,

•

either
\sigma(i)=(\leftarrow,*,\rightarrow)
for all
i
, this case corresponds to
T=T^{\mathrm{out}}
.

The combinatorial type determines the foliation

For what follows, we fix an orientation on the torus
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}
.

Proposition 7.2.13
.

Any abstract combinatorial type
\sigma
is the combinatorial type of a dynamically oriented foliation
{\mathcal{F}}
on the oriented torus
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}
, and
{\mathcal{F}}
is unique up to topological equivalence.

Proof.

Let
\sigma=(\sigma_{g},\sigma_{o},\sigma_{d})\colon{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\mathscr{A}}_{H}\times{\mathscr{A}}_{O}\times{\mathscr{A}}_{H}
be an abstract combinatorial type.
For any
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}}
, let
A_{i}=[0,1]\times{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
be an annulus, and let
\gamma^{i}_{0}=\{0\}\times{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}\subset
and
\gamma^{i}_{1}=\{1\}\times{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
the boundaries of
A_{i}
.
We orient
\gamma^{i}_{0}
by the increasing orientation of
{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
if
\sigma_{o}(i)=\uparrow
, and by the decreasing orientation otherwise.
Similarly, we orient
\gamma^{i}_{1}
by the increasing orientation of
{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
if
\sigma_{o}(i+1)=\uparrow
, and by the decreasing orientation otherwise.
We have the following polychotomy.

(1)

\sigma_{o}(i)=\sigma_{o}(i+1)
, and
\sigma_{d}(i)=\,\rightarrow

(2)

\sigma_{o}(i)=\sigma_{o}(i+1)
, and
\sigma_{d}(i)=\,\leftarrow

(3)

\sigma_{o}(i)=-\sigma_{o}(i+1)
, and
\sigma_{d}(i)=\,\rightarrow

(4)

\sigma_{o}(i)=-\sigma_{o}(i+1)
, and
\sigma_{d}(i)=\,\leftarrow

Let
{\mathcal{F}}_{i}
be a foliation on
A_{i}
, with no compact leaves other than
\gamma_{0}^{i}
and
\gamma_{1}^{i}
, and such that each noncompact half-leaf of
{\mathcal{F}}_{i}
accumulates at both ends on
\gamma^{i}_{0}
or
\gamma^{i}_{1}
in a expanding way if
\sigma_{d}(i)=\rightarrow
, and contracting otherwise.
We obtain a cyclic sequence of closed annuli
A_{i}
indexed by
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}}
, equipped with a foliation
{\mathcal{F}}_{i}
.
We identify the oriented boundary
\gamma^{i}_{1}=\{1\}\times{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
of
A_{i}
with the oriented boundary
\gamma^{i+1}_{0}=\{0\}\times{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
of
A_{i+1}
via a diffeomorphism of the circle
\varphi_{i}
, equal to the identity in coordinate
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
of
A_{i}
and
A_{i+1}
.
We obtain a cyclic sequence of gluing diffeomorphisms
\varphi_{i}
indexed by
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}}
.
The quotient
T=\cup_{i}A_{i}/\varphi
, where
\varphi
denotes the product of
\varphi_{i}
, is a torus oriented by the canonical orientation on each
A_{i}
, and the foliations
{\mathcal{F}}_{i}
come together in a foliation
{\mathcal{F}}
on the torus
T\simeq{\mathbb{T}}^{2}
of combinatorial type
\sigma
.

For uniqueness, we refer to the proof of
[
BBY17
, Proposition 7.7]
which deals with the case of Morse-Smale foliation for a given abstract Morse-Smale combinatorial type, i.e. when the set of marked leaves is empty.
Reducing to uniqueness on the closure of each connected component of the complement of compact leaves, it is clear that the proof is completely similar for quasi-Morse-Smale and for Morse-Smale foliations.
∎

Remark 7.2.14
.

If
{\mathcal{L}}
and
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
are two filling laminations on the oriented torus, it is equivalent to say that
{\mathcal{L}}
and
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
have the same combinatorial type to say that
{\mathcal{L}}
and
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
complete each other in foliations
{\mathcal{F}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
which are topologically equivalent (in the sense of Definition
7.0.1
).
This remark follows from Lemma
7.2.4
and from Proposition
7.2.13
.

Definition 7.2.15

(Restriction)
.

Let
\sigma
be a combinatorial type on
{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}}
and let
I=\{k_{1},\dots,k_{m}\}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}}
a subset of cardinal
m
, where the
k_{i}
are cyclically ordered.
The restriction
\sigma|_{I}
is the map

\sigma|_{I}\colon{\mathbb{Z}}/m{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\mathscr{A}}_{H}\times{\mathscr{A}}_{O}\times{\mathscr{A}}_{H}
defined by

\sigma|_{I}(i)=\sigma(k_{i})

A restriction of a combinatorial type is not a priori a combinatorial type.
This definition allows us to compare restrictions to subsets of the same cardinal of any two combinatorial types
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
.

7.3.
Non-transitive building block with prescribed boundary lamination

In this section, we show

Proposition 7.3.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
be two foliations on the oriented torus
S_{1}
and the oriented torus
S_{2}
respectively, with the same non-zero number of marked leaves, i.e.
\operatorname{Card}\Gamma_{*,{\mathcal{F}}_{1}}=\operatorname{Card}\Gamma_{*,{\mathcal{F}}_{2}}=2N\neq 0
.
Then there exists
(P,X)
a oriented connected building block such that :

(1)

(boundary)

\partial P
is the union of two tori
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
quasi-transverse to
X
.

(2)

(boundary lamination)

The boundary lamination
{\mathcal{L}}_{X}
restricted to
T_{i}
is a foliation topologically equivalent to
{\mathcal{F}}_{i}
.

(3)

(Smale’s graph)

The basic pieces of the maximal invariant set
\Lambda_{X}
are the
4N
periodic orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{i,j}
contained in
T_{i}\subset{\partial P}
,
N
attractors
\Lambda_{k}^{+}
, and
N
repellers
\Lambda_{k}^{-}
, each containing infinitely many periodic orbits with negative multipliers.
The Smale’s graph of
\Lambda_{X}
is given by Figure
61
.

Figure 61
.
Smale’s graph of
\Lambda_{X}

For the rest of the proof, let
\Gamma_{i}
be the set of compact leaves of
{\mathcal{F}}_{i}
,
\Gamma_{*,i}
the set of marked compact leaves.
We fix a geometric enumeration
\Gamma_{i}=\{\gamma_{i,0},\dots,\gamma_{i,{n_{i}}}\}
such that
\gamma_{i,0}\in\Gamma_{i,*}
, in other words the first compact leaf is a marked leaf.
Let us denote
\sigma_{i}\colon{\mathbb{Z}}/n_{i}{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\mathscr{A}}_{H}\times{\mathscr{A}}_{O}\times{\mathscr{A}}_{H}
the corresponding combinatorial type of
{\mathcal{F}}_{i}
.
Let us show the following lemma.

Lemma 7.3.2
.

There exists an oriented connected building block
(P,X)
which satisfies the following properties.

(1)

(boundary)

the boundary
\partial P
is the union of two tori
T_{1}^{q}
,
T_{2}^{q}
quasi-transverse to
X
each containing a collection
{\mathcal{O}}_{i,*}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
of
2N
periodic orbits,
N

T^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}\subset P^{\mathrm{in}}
and
N

T^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}\subset P^{\mathrm{out}}
transverse to
X
;

(2)

(maximal invariant set)

the maximal invariant set
\Lambda
is the union of the
4N
isolated saddle periodic orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
of
X
contained in the boundary
{\partial P}
;

(3)

(boundary lamination on transverse tori)

the boundary lamination
{\mathcal{L}}
on
T^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
and on
T^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}
is the union of 4 compact leaves;

(4)

(boundary lamination on quasi-transverse tori)

the boundary lamination
{\mathcal{L}}
on

is reduced to the periodic orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{i,*}
, and one of its combinatorial types coincides with the restriction of
\sigma_{i}
on the set of marked leaves
\Gamma_{i,*}
;

(5)

(crossing map)

the crossing map
f:P^{\mathrm{in}}\to P^{\mathrm{out}}
pairs the connected components of
{\partial P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{L}}
according to the diagram given by Figure
62
.

Remark 7.3.3
.

The manifold
P
is a circle bundle on an annulus minus
2N
disks, and the fiber is homotopic to a periodic orbit of
X
.

Figure 62
.
Block
(P,X)
and crossing map
f

Proof.

Let us note
\{p_{i,0},\dots,p_{i,2N-1}\}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}/2N{\mathbb{Z}}
the ordered set of marked leaves of
\sigma_{i}
, which we also denote (by use of notation)
\Gamma_{i,*}
.
To fix the ideas we suppose that
p_{i,0}=0
, and that the left side of
\gamma_{i,0}
is contained in
S_{i}^{\mathrm{out}}
and the right side of
\gamma_{i,0}
in
S_{i}^{\mathrm{in}}
, which is equivalent to the following relation

(7.3)

\sigma_{i}(p_{i,0})=\sigma_{i}(0)=(\rightarrow,\uparrow,\rightarrow)

Let
A={\mathbb{R}}\times[-1,1]
be the band of
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
with coordinates
(x,y)
.
Let
Y
be a Morse-Smale gradient vector field on
A
described by Figure
63
,
2
-periodic along the variable
x
, i.e.
Y(x+2,y)=Y(x,y)
, and whose non-wandering set consists of

•

hyperbolic fixed points of index
(1,1)
on
(i,\pm 1)
for
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,

•

attracting fixed point on
(i+\frac{1}{2},0)
for
i
even integer,

•

repelling fixed point on
(i+\frac{1}{2},0)
for
i
odd integer,

Figure 63
.
Morse-Smale vector field
Y
on the band
{\mathbb{R}}\times[-1,1]

We cut in
A
disks
D_{i}=D((i+\frac{1}{2},0),\epsilon)
of center
(i+\frac{1}{2},0)
and radius
\epsilon
, such that
Y
is transverse to the boundary of
D_{i}
.
Let
\hat{A}:=A{\smallsetminus}(\cup_{i}D_{i})\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
, and let
\tilde{P}:=\hat{A}\times{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
be the product on the circle, given coordinates
(x,y,\theta)
, and oriented by the orientation of the canonical basis in these coordinates.
Let
\tilde{X}
be the vector field on
\tilde{P}
defined by

\tilde{X}=Y+g(x,y)\partial_{\theta}

where
g\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is a function of class
{\mathcal{C}}^{1}
, which satisfies the following conditions:

(1)

the support of
g
is contained in disks
{\mathbb{D}}((i,\pm 1),\frac{1}{4})
centered in
(i,\pm 1)
of radius
\frac{1}{4}
, its value depends only on the radius
r
, and is monotonic in
r
.

(2)

the absolute value of
g
is
1
-periodic along the variable
x
.

(3)

g
is constant equal to
\pm 1
on the disk
{\mathbb{D}}((i,\pm 1),\frac{1}{8})
centered at
(i,\pm 1)
and of radius
\frac{1}{8}
.

•

g=1
on
{\mathbb{D}}((i,1),\frac{1}{8})
if and only if
\sigma_{1}(p_{1,i})=(*,\downarrow,*)

•

g=1
on
{\mathbb{D}}((i,-1),\frac{1}{4})
if and only if
\sigma_{2}(p_{2,i})=(*,\uparrow,*)

See Figure
64
.
The choice on the sign of
g
(Item
3
) will allow us to obtain the orientations of the periodic orbits compatible with the combinatorial type, which will be detailed at the end of the proof.
The reason for the mirror effect is that the canonical orientation of the boundary
\{y=1\}
and
\{y=-1\}
induced by the orientation of
\tilde{P}
is “reversed”: the first one coincides with the orientation given by the basis field
(-\partial_{x},\partial_{\theta})
and the other one by the basis field
(\partial_{x},\partial_{\theta})
.

Figure 64
.
Support of
g

The
g
-map is
2N
-periodic along the variable
x
and the vector field
\tilde{X}
quotients into a field
X
on the manifold

P:=\tilde{P}/(x,y,\theta)\sim(x+2N,y,\theta).

This manifold is compact, connected, with boundary, oriented by the basis field
(\partial_{x},\partial_{y},\partial_{\theta})
.
Let us show that
(P,X)
satisfies the lemma.

(1)

The boundary
\partial P
is the union of two tori
T_{1}^{q}=\{y=1\}
and
T_{1}^{q}=\{y=-1\}
, of
N
tori
T^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}=\partial D_{2k}\times{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
and of
N
tori
T^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}=\partial D_{2k+1}\times{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
with
k\in\{0,\dots,N-1\}
.
The vector field
X
is transverse to
T^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
and
T^{\mathrm{out}}_{i}
, and points inward along
T^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
and outward along
T^{\mathrm{out}}_{i}
.
Each torus
T_{i}^{q}
contains a finite collection
{\mathcal{O}}_{i,*}
of hyperbolic periodic orbits of index
(1,1)
of
X
induced by the saddle hyperbolic fixed points
{\mathbb{Z}}\times\pm 1\}
of the vector field
Y
.
The vector field
X
is transverse to the boundary on the complementary
T_{i}^{q}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{i,*}
, alternately inward and outward on two connected components of
T_{i}^{q}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{i,*}
adjacent along a periodic orbit of
{\mathcal{O}}_{i,*}
, in other words the tori
T_{i}^{q}
are quasi-transverse to
X
in the sense of Definition
1.1.1
.
Item
1
is true.

(2)

All orbits of
X
which are not in
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}={\mathcal{O}}_{1,*}\cup{\mathcal{O}}_{2,*}
intersect the boundary of
P
in the future or in the past.
It follows that the maximal invariant set
\Lambda
coincides with the set
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
, and is therefore provided with a hyperbolic structure of index
(1,1)
for
X
.
We conclude that
(P,X)
is a building block in the sense of Definition
1.1.2
.
Item
2
is true.

(3)

Let
{\mathcal{L}}
be the boundary lamination of
(P,X)
.
Each orbit of a point of
T_{i}^{q}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
intersects a torus
T^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
or
T^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}
.
It follows that the boundary lamination
{\mathcal{L}}
restricted to
T_{i}^{q}
is reduced to the periodic orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{i,*}
, the boundary lamination
{\mathcal{L}}
on a torus
T^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}
is the union of 4 compact leaves which are the intersection of the free stable separatrices of two consecutive periodic orbits of
T_{1}^{q}
and two consecutive periodic orbits of
T_{2}^{q}
.
Similarly, the boundary lamination
{\mathcal{L}}
on a torus
T^{\mathrm{out}}_{i}
is the union of 4 compact leaves which are the intersection of the free unstable separatrices of two consecutive periodic orbits of
T_{1}^{q}
and two consecutive periodic orbits of
T_{2}^{q}
.
Item
3
is true.

(4)

The lamination
{\mathcal{L}}
is a dynamically oriented prefoliation (Remark
7.2.3
) and the boundary
\partial P
is provided with the orientation induced by the orientation of the canonical basis
(\partial_{x},\partial_{y},\partial_{\theta})
in coordinates
(x,y,\theta)
on
P
.

We enumerate the periodic orbits of
T_{1}^{q}
by
{\mathcal{O}}_{1,i}:=\{(i,1)\}\times{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
in coordinates
(x,y,\theta)
.
Let
\sigma({\mathcal{L}}|_{T_{1}^{q}})
be the combinatorial type of
{\mathcal{L}}
on
T_{1}^{q}
associated with the first compact leaf
{\mathcal{O}}_{1,0}
.
The orientation of
T_{1}^{q}
coincides with the orientation defined by the base field
(-\partial_{x},\partial_{\theta})
.
The choice made between
+1
or
-1
when defining the map
g
at the point
(i,1)
determines the orientation of the vector field
\tilde{X}
in the direction
\partial_{\theta}
or
-\partial_{\theta}
along the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{1,i}
.
By construction
g(0,1)=-1
, so the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{1,0}
is oriented in the decreasing direction of
\theta
, and its left side for the orientation of
T_{1}^{q}
coincides with the side of the lower
x
.
The vector field
X
points outward on this side of
{\mathcal{O}}_{1,0}
(Figure
63
), so
\sigma({\mathcal{L}}|_{T_{1}^{q}})(0)=(\rightarrow,\uparrow,\rightarrow)
.
According to Equation (
7.3
), we check that we have
\sigma({\mathcal{L}}|_{T_{1}^{q}})(0)=\sigma_{1}(p_{1,0})=\sigma_{1}(0)
.
Finally, the enumeration of the collection
{\mathcal{O}}_{1,*}
coincides with the geometric enumeration determined by the choice of the first leaf
{\mathcal{O}}_{1,0}
.
Consequently, the sign of
g
has been chosen so that
\sigma({\mathcal{L}}|_{T_{1}^{q}})(i)=\sigma_{1}(p_{1,i})=\sigma_{i}|_{\Gamma_{i,*}}(i)
for all
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}/2N{\mathbb{Z}}
.

The argument is the same for the combinatorial type of
{\mathcal{L}}
on
T_{2}^{q}
, except that the orientations are in “mirror”.
Let us explain.
We enumerate the periodic orbits of
T_{2}^{q}
by
{\mathcal{O}}_{2,i}:=\{(i,-1)\}\times{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
in coordinates
(x,y,\theta)
.
Let
\sigma({\mathcal{L}}|_{T_{2}^{q}})
be the combinatorial type of
{\mathcal{L}}
on
T_{2}^{q}
associated with the given first compact leaf
{\mathcal{O}}_{2,0}
.
The orientation of
T_{2}^{q}
coincides with the orientation defined by the base field
(\partial_{x},\partial_{\theta})
.
The choice made between
+1
or
-1
when defining the map
g
at the point
(i,-1)
determines the orientation of the vector field
\tilde{X}
in the direction
\partial_{\theta}
or
-\partial_{\theta}
along the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{2,i}
.
By construction
g(0,-1)=1
, so the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{2,0}
is oriented in the increasing direction of
\theta
and its left side for the orientation of
T_{2}^{q}
therefore coincides with the side of the lower
x
.
The vector field
X
points outward on this side of
{\mathcal{O}}_{2,0}
(Figure
63
), so
\sigma({\mathcal{L}}|_{T_{2}^{q}})(i)=(\rightarrow,\uparrow,\rightarrow)
.
According to Equation (
7.3
), we check that we have
\sigma({\mathcal{L}}|_{T_{2}^{q}})(0)=\sigma_{2}(p_{2,0})=\sigma_{2}(0)
.
Finally, the enumeration chosen on the collection
{\mathcal{O}}_{2,*}
coincides with the geometric enumeration determined by the choice of the first leaf
{\mathcal{O}}_{2,0}
.
Consequently, the sign of
g
has been chosen so that
\sigma({\mathcal{L}}|_{T_{2}^{q}})(i)=\sigma_{2}(p_{2,i})=\sigma_{i}|_{\Gamma_{i,*}}(i)
for all
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}/2N{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Item
4
is true.

(5)

By construction.

Finally, let us explain what to do if we change the choice made in Equation (
7.3
) for the value of the combinatorial type of the first marked leaf.
There are two possible cases (up to reversing
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
):

•

either
\sigma_{1}(0)=\sigma_{2}(0)=(\leftarrow,\uparrow,\leftarrow)
, then it is sufficient to construct the vector field
Y
on the strip
A
by exchanging the attracting fixed points and the repelling fixed points, which exchange the components of the entrance boundary and the components of the exit boundary on the two quasi-transverse tori
T_{1,2}^{q}
.

•

either
\sigma_{1}(0)=(\leftarrow,\uparrow,\leftarrow)
and
\sigma_{2}(0)=(\rightarrow,\uparrow,\rightarrow)
, then we change the definition of the map
g
so that we have
g(i,1)=1\iff\sigma_{1}(n-i)=(*,\uparrow,*)
at Item
3
, which reverse the left and right sides of the first orbit on
T_{1}^{q}
as well as the geometric enumeration, but not on
T_{2}^{q}
.

∎

We say that a building block
(P,X)
is
attracting
if the maximal invariant set
(P,X)
is an attractor for the flow of
X
, and
repelling
if the maximal invariant set is a repeller.
The rest of the proof consists of gluing together attracting transitive building blocks
(U_{k}^{+},X_{k}^{+})
and repelling transitive building blocks
(U_{k}^{-},X_{k}^{-})
along the boundary components
T^{\mathrm{out}}_{k}
and
T^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
of the block
(P,X)
which are transverse to the vector field
X
, so as to obtain a new building block whose boundary lamination on
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
has a prescribed combinatorial type.
We will use a result of
[
BBY17
]
to obtain attracting and repelling Béguin-Bonatti-Yu blocks realizing a given Morse-Smale boundary lamination on the torus.
We recall that a Béguin-Bonatti-Yu block
(U,Y)
is a building block such that the vector field
Y
is transverse to the boundary
\partial U
, or equivalently such that the collection of periodic orbits of
Y
contained in
\partial U
is empty (Remark
1.1.4
).
A Morse-Smale lamination is a quasi-Morse-Smale lamination such that the set of marked leaves is empty (Remark
1.2.5
).

Lemma 7.3.4

(
[
BBY17
, Theorem 1.10]
)
.

For any Morse-Smale foliation
{\mathcal{F}}
on the torus
{\mathbb{T}}^{2}
, there exists a connected transitive attracting orientable Béguin-Bonatti-Yu block
(U,Y)
, and a homeomorphism
h:\partial U\to{\mathbb{T}}^{2}
such that
h_{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{Y}={\mathcal{F}}
, where
{\mathcal{L}}_{Y}
denotes the boundary lamination of
(U,Y)
.
Moreover, we can choose
(U,Y)
such that the maximal invariant set
\Lambda_{Y}
contains infinitely many periodic orbits with negative multipliers.
14
14

14

See
[
BBY17
, Lemma 9.8]
in the proof of
[
BBY17
, Theorem 1.10]

Remark 7.3.5
.

We have a completely analogous result where
(U,Y)
is a repelling block. We just need to reverse the vector field of the block given by Lemma
7.3.4
.

Proof of Proposition
7.3.1
.

Let
(P,X)
be the oriented building block given by Lemma
7.3.2
.
Let
(P,X)
be a geometric enumeration of the periodic orbits

of

contained in

to the marked leaves

of

(Item

).
Recall that

is the ordered collection of marked leaves with
\sigma_{i}
, with
p_{i,0}=0
.

Step 1: Gluing attractors.

Let
0\leq k\leq N-1
be an integer, and let

be a torus of

along which

is transverse outward.
According to Item

of Lemma
7.3.2
, and Figure

, there exists a connected component

of

and a connected component

such that
f(A_{i})\subset T^{\mathrm{out}}
for

.
The component

is an annulus bounded by the periodic orbits

and

of
{\mathcal{O}}_{1,*}
, and the annulus
f(A_{1})
is bounded by the trace of the unstable manifolds of
{\mathcal{O}}_{1,j}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{1,j+1}
.
Let
B_{1}
be the connected component of
S^{\mathrm{in}}_{1}
bounded by the marked leaves
p_{1,j}
and
p_{1,j+1}
of
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
.
Similarly, the component
A_{2}\subset P^{\mathrm{in}}
is a annulus bounded by the periodic orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{2,l}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{2,l+1}
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{2,*}
, and the oriented annulus

is bounded by the trace of the unstable manifolds of
{\mathcal{O}}_{2,l}
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{2,l+1}
.
Let

be the annulus of

bounded by the marked leaves

and

of

.

Proof.

such that

The diffeomorphism

which reverses the orientation, associated to a subset

of

7.1.1

Indeed,

is transverse to
Y

Y
intersects
\partial_{1}M
at at most one point, and
\varphi
maps the boundary lamination of
(M,Y)
to a transverse lamination by construction.
We deduce that the vector field
Y
induces on the (compact, boundary oriented) manifold

a vector field
X^{\prime}
, such that
(P^{\prime},X^{\prime})
is a building block.
Items

The basic pieces of the maximal invariant set

are the periodic orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{*}
and the attractors

and the Smale’s graph follow naturally from the gluing graph

There are four possible cases, depending on the sign of

Claim 7.3.9
.

There exists a Morse-Smale foliation
{\mathcal{G}}={\mathcal{G}}_{k}

Proof.

The sets
f^{\prime{-1}}(A_{i})\subset T^{\mathrm{in}}

on the annuli
f^{\prime{-1}}(A_{i})
in a similar way to the proof of Claim
7.3.6
: we place the compact leaves in the annuli
f^{\prime{-1}}(A_{i})
parallel to the boundary and enumerated in the right way, orient them in a way compatible with the combinatorial type of
{\mathcal{F}}_{i}
on
B_{i}
and complete with non-compact leaves that accumulate on the compact leaves oriented for the expanding orientation.
We complete the foliation thus constructed on
T^{\mathrm{in}}
by a transverse foliation on
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
.
We can choose for example the foliation generated by the orthogonal vector field
Z^{\perp}
, for a certain metric, to a vector field
Z
generating
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
on
T^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}\cup_{i}f^{\prime{-1}}(A_{i})
.
By density of Morse-Smale vector fields, up to make a small perturbation on
T^{\mathrm{in}}{\smallsetminus}\cup_{i}f^{\prime{-1}}(A_{i})
, we obtain a Morse-Smale foliation
{\mathcal{G}}
on
T^{\mathrm{in}}
, transverse to
{\mathcal{L}}
, and which satisfies the fact.
∎

By Lemma
7.3.4
and Remark
7.3.5
, there exists an orientable connected block
(U_{k}^{-},X_{k}^{-})
such that the maximal invariant set
\Lambda_{k}^{-}
is a transitive repeller which admits infinitely many periodic orbits with negative multipliers, and there exists a diffeomorphism
\varphi_{k}^{-}\colon\partial U_{k}^{-}\to T_{k}^{\mathrm{in}}
such that
(\varphi_{k}^{-})_{*}({\mathcal{L}}_{k}^{-})=:{\mathcal{G}}_{k}
satisfies Claim
7.3.6
.
Finally, we define

P^{\prime\prime}:=\left(P^{\prime}\cup\left(\bigcup_{k}U_{k}^{-}\right)\right)/\varphi^{-}

where
\varphi^{-}
is the product of
\varphi^{-}_{k}\colon\partial U_{k}^{-}\to T^{\mathrm{in}}_{k}
.

Claim 7.3.10
.

The vector fields
X^{\prime}
and
X_{k}^{-}
induce on the manifold
P^{\prime\prime}
a vector field
X^{\prime\prime}
such that
(P^{\prime\prime},X^{\prime\prime})
is a building block which satisfies Proposition
7.3.1
.

Proof.

The proof is the same as the proof of Claim
7.3.8
.
∎

This completes the proof of Proposition
7.3.1
.
∎

7.4.
Transitivity by the
Blow-up – Excise – Glue
surgery

Proposition
7.3.1
gives us the existence of a building block
(P,X)
which satisfies Proposition
7.0.2
for a given pair of quasi-Morse-Smale foliations, except transitivity.
We show the following general

Proposition 7.4.1
.

Let
(P,X)
be a filled oriented connected building block, we denote
\Lambda
the maximal invariant set.
Assume that
for any basic piece
\Lambda_{i}
of
\Lambda
, there exists a basic piece
\Lambda_{k}
and
\Lambda_{j}
containing infinitely many periodic orbits with negative multipliers, and such that there exists as oriented (possibly trivial) path in the Smale’s graph connecting
\Lambda_{j}
to
\Lambda_{i}
and
\Lambda_{i}
to
\Lambda_{k}
.

Then there exists a
transitive
filled oriented connected block
(P^{\prime},X^{\prime})
such that the boundary lamination
{\mathcal{L}}_{X^{\prime}}
and the boundary lamination
{\mathcal{L}}_{X}
complete into topologically equivalent foliations.

Let us first show that Proposition
7.0.2
is an immediate corollary.

Proof of Proposition
7.0.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
be two quasi-Morse-Smale foliations on the oriented torus
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
, and let
(P,X)
be the block given by Proposition
7.3.1
.
It suffices to check that
(P,X)
satisfies the hypotheses of Proposition
7.4.1
.
The basic pieces of
\Lambda
are attracting and repelling basic pieces
\Lambda_{k}^{\pm}
and saddle periodic orbits
{\mathcal{O}}_{i,j}
.
Each basic piece
\Lambda_{k}^{\pm}
contains infinitely many periodic orbits with negative multipliers (Item
3
, Proposition
7.3.1
).
According to the Smale’s graph on Figure
61
each orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{i,j}
is connected by an oriented edge to an attracting basic piece
\Lambda_{k}^{+}
and by a reversed oriented edge to a repelling basic piece
\Lambda_{k}^{+}
.
The hypotheses are thus satisfied, there exists a block
(P^{\prime},X^{\prime})
associated by Proposition
7.4.1
which satisfies Proposition
7.0.2
.
∎

Let us now show Proposition
7.4.1
.
The idea is to perform surgeries on the block
(P,X)
to obtain a transitive block while keeping the combinatorial type of boundary lamination.
We use the so-called
Blow-up – Excise – Glue
surgery of
[
BBY17
, Section 8]
.
We will need the following lemma.

Lemma 7.4.2
.

Let
(P,X)
be a connected oriented filled building block.
Let
O
be a periodic orbit in the interior of
P
, without free separatrices and with negative multiplier.
Denote
\Lambda
the maximal invariant set and
{\mathcal{L}}
the boundary lamination of
(P,X)
.
There exists a connected oriented block
(\hat{P},\hat{X})
such that,

(1)

(manifold)

\hat{P}=P{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}
where
{\mathcal{V}}
is a tubular neighborhood of the orbit
O
.

(2)

(boundary)

\partial\hat{P}=\partial P\cup T
, where
T=\partial{\mathcal{V}}
and
\hat{X}
is transverse outward on
T

(3)

(semi-conjugation)

There exists a continuous surjective map
\pi\colon\hat{P}{\smallsetminus}\hat{T}\to P
which maps the oriented orbits of the flow of
\hat{X}
onto the oriented orbits of the flow of
X
, where
\hat{T}
is the flow-saturated set of
T
.
There exists a periodic orbit
O^{\prime}\subset\operatorname{int}\hat{P}
of
\hat{X}
such that the restriction

\pi\colon\hat{P}{\smallsetminus}(\hat{T}\cup{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{\hat{X}}(O^{\prime}))\to\hat{P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{X}(O)
is a homeomorphism.

(4)

(lamination)

The boundary lamination
\hat{\mathcal{L}}
is filling,
\hat{\mathcal{L}}
and
{\mathcal{L}}
completes on
{\partial P}
as topologically equivalent foliations on
{\partial P}
, and
\hat{\mathcal{L}}
has a single compact leaf on
T
.

(5)

(Smale’s graph)

The Smale’s graphs of
(\hat{P},\hat{X})
and
(P,X)
are isomorphic.
15
15

15

here, we speak of the Smale’s graph of the maximal invariant set of the block

We say that
(\hat{P},\hat{X})
is a building block obtained by an
attracting bifurcation Derived from Anosov (DA)
on the orbit
O
.

Proof idea.

This block is obtained by an attracting DA bifurcation on the periodic orbit
O
.
We refer to
[
GHS97
, Subsection 2.2.2]
for a detailed description of this bifurcation, and to Figure
69
.
Let us also mention
[
BBGRH21
, Section 3.3]
, where the authors give an explicit construction of a DA bifurcation on a codimension 2 geodesic flow, but which can be adapted in our case.

Figure 69
.
attracting DA bifurcation on a periodic orbit
O
with negative multipliers

This bifurcation consists in “opening” the stable manifold of
O
, which creates an attracting periodic orbit
O^{+}
instead of
O
, and transforms
O
into a saddle periodic orbit
O^{\prime}
with positive multipliers (corresponding to the boundary of the local unstable manifold of
O
which is a Moebius strip).
More precisely, there exists a small perturbation
X^{\prime}
of
X
on
P
such that
X^{\prime}
coincides with
X
outside a linearizing neighborhood
{\mathcal{V}}_{0}
of the orbit
O
, and such that the vector field
X^{\prime}
on
{\mathcal{V}}_{0}
admits an attracting periodic orbit
O^{+}
with negative multipliers, and a periodic orbit
O
with positive multipliers.
We remove a small tubular neighborhood
{\mathcal{V}}={\mathcal{V}}_{\delta}
of size
\delta
from the orbit
O^{+}
, in the basin of attraction of
O^{+}
and whose boundary is transverse to the vector field
X^{\prime}
.
Denote
\hat{P}=P{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{V}}
and
\hat{X}
the restriction of
X^{\prime\prime}
on
\hat{P}
.
Since everything takes place far from the boundary components of
(P,X)
, we can use
[
BBY17
, Propositions 8.1 and 8.3]
, which states that if
\delta
is small enough, the pair
(\hat{P},\hat{X})
is a building block which satisfies Items
1
,
2
, and
4
.

Moreover (see
[
GHS97
, Subsection 2.2.2]
) there exists a continuous surjective map
\pi\colon P\to P
, which maps the oriented orbits of the flow of
X^{\prime}
onto the oriented orbits of the flow of
X
, in other words it is a semi-conjugation between the flow of
X^{\prime}
and a reparametrization of the flow of
X
.
Let
B
be the basin of attraction of the orbit
O^{+}
for the flow of
X^{\prime}
, and
\hat{B}=B\cup{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(O^{\prime})
.
Then
\pi
maps
\hat{B}
onto
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(O)
, and the pre-image of a point
x\in{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(O)
is an arc through
B
whose ends are on the manifold
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(O)
.
The restriction
\pi\colon P{\smallsetminus}\hat{B}\to P{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(O)
is a homeomorphism, hence an orbit equivalence.
Since the set of orbits of
X^{\prime}
which intersects the boundary of
{\mathcal{V}}
coincide with the basin of attraction of
O^{+}
, Item
3
follows.

Let us show the last item, by summarize the proof of
[
BBY17
, Propositions 8.1]
.
Let
\Lambda_{i}
be the basic piece of the maximal invariant set
\Lambda
of
(P,X)
containing the orbit
O
.
The piece
\Lambda_{i}
is nontrivial because the orbit
O
is assumed to have no free separatrices, and there exists
\gamma\in\Lambda_{i}
a periodic orbit dense in
\Lambda_{i}
and disjoint from
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(O)
.
Since
\pi\colon P{\smallsetminus}\hat{B}\to P{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(O)
is a conjugation, the pre-image of
\Lambda_{i}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(O)
contains a dense orbit, hence is a transitive set for the flow of
X^{\prime}
.
Let
\hat{\Lambda}_{i}
be the basic piece of
\hat{\Lambda}
containing this set.
Since the unstable separatrices of
O
are not free, the stable manifold
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(O)
is accumulated by leaves of
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(\Lambda_{i})
, and it follows by orbit equivalence of
X
on
P{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(O)
and
X^{\prime}
on
P{\smallsetminus}\hat{B}=P{\smallsetminus}(B\cup{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(O^{\prime}))
that
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(O^{\prime})
is accumulated by
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(\hat{\Lambda}_{i})
.
It follows that
O^{\prime}\subset\hat{\Lambda}_{i}
, and the map
\pi
induces an isomorphism of Smale’s graphs.
∎

Remark 7.4.3
.

There exists an analogue of Lemma
7.4.2
where
(\hat{P},\hat{X})
is such that
\partial\hat{P}=\partial P\cup T
, and
\hat{X}
is transverse inward on
T
.
We say that
(\hat{P},\hat{X})
is a building block obtained by
repelling DA bifurcation
on the orbit
O
.
In this case, in Item
3
, we have an orbit equivalence between
X
on
P{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{W}}_{X}^{u}(O)
and
\hat{X}
on
\hat{P}{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{W}}_{\hat{X}}^{u}(O^{\prime})
.

Proof of Proposition
7.4.1
.

Let
(P,X)
be a block satisfying the hypothesis of Proposition
7.4.1
.
Denote
\Lambda_{1},\dots,\Lambda_{n}
the basic pieces of
\Lambda
containing infinitely many periodic orbits with negative multipliers.
For any
\Lambda_{i}
, choose
2(n-1)
periodic orbits
O_{i,j}^{+}
and
O_{i,j}^{-}
in
\Lambda_{i}
for
j=1,\dots,n
and
j\neq i
, without free separatrices, with negative multipliers.
Such orbits exist according to Proposition
7.3.1
, Item
3
, and because the periodic orbits with a free separatrix are in finite number (Lemma
2.1.11
).
Let
(\hat{P},\hat{X})
be the oriented connected block obtained by a repelling DA bifurcation on the orbits
O^{+}_{i,j}
and an attracting DA bifurcation on the orbits
O^{-}_{i,j}
by iteration of Lemma
7.4.2
and Remark
7.4.3
for every
i,j
.
It satisfies the following properties, deduced from the previous lemma.
The boundary
\partial\hat{P}
is the union
\partial P\cup_{i,j}(T^{\mathrm{in}}_{i,j}\cup T^{\mathrm{out}}_{i,j})
, with
T^{\mathrm{in}}_{i,j}
the torus created by repelling bifurcation on
O_{i,j}^{+}
where
\hat{X}
is transverse inward, and
T^{\mathrm{out}}_{i,j}
the torus created by attracting bifurcation on
O_{i,j}^{-}
where
\hat{X}
is transverse outward (Item
2
).
The lamination
\hat{\mathcal{L}}
is filling, completes on
{\partial P}
into a foliation topologically equivalent to
{\mathcal{L}}
, and contains a unique compact leaf on each torus
T_{i,j}^{\mathrm{in}}
and
T_{i,j}^{\mathrm{out}}
(Item
4
).
By composing each semi-conjugation obtained by iterating the construction (Item
3
), we obtain a semi-conjugation
h
between the vector field
\hat{X}
on the complementary of the flow-saturated set
\hat{T}
of the tori
T^{\mathrm{out}}_{i,j}
and
T^{\mathrm{in}}_{i,j}
, and a renormalization of the vector field
X
, which is a homeomorphism on the complementary of the union of the stable manifolds
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(O^{+}_{i,j})
and the unstable manifolds
{\mathcal{W}}^{u}(O^{-}_{i,j})
.
The Smale’s graph of
(\hat{P},\hat{X})
is isomorphic to the Smale’s graph of
(P,X)
(Item
5
).
Let
\hat{\Lambda}_{1},\dots,\hat{\Lambda}_{n}
be the basic pieces of
\hat{\Lambda}
associated to
\Lambda_{1},\dots,\Lambda_{n}
.

Claim 7.4.4
.

for all
(i,j)
we have

\hat{\mathcal{L}}\cap T^{\mathrm{out}}_{i,j}={\mathcal{W}}^{u}(\hat{\Lambda}_{i})\cap T^{\mathrm{out}}_{i,j}
,
and

\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{Y}\cap T^{\mathrm{in}}_{i,j}={\mathcal{W}}^{s}(\hat{\Lambda}_{i})\cap T^{\mathrm{in}}_{i,j}
.

Proof.

It follows from Item
3
of Lemma
7.4.2
that the pre-image of
\Lambda\cap W^{s}(O_{i,j}^{-})=\Lambda_{i}\cap W^{s}(O_{i,j}^{-})
by
h
is the set of orbits of
\hat{\Lambda}
whose unstable manifold intersects the torus
T^{\mathrm{out}}_{i,j}
, and is contained in
\hat{\Lambda}_{i}
.
Similarly, the pre-image of
\Lambda\cap W^{u}(O_{i,j}^{+})=\Lambda_{i}\cap W^{u}(O_{i,j}^{+})
by
h
is the set of orbits of
\hat{\Lambda}
whose unstable manifold intersects the torus
T^{\mathrm{in}}_{i,j}
, and is contained in
\hat{\Lambda}_{i}
.
∎

Let us go to the gluing operation.
We want a strongly quasi-transverse partial gluing map
\varphi
of
(\hat{P},\hat{X})
so that the graph
G=G(\hat{P},\hat{X},\varphi)
contains a cycle between
\Lambda_{i}
and
\Lambda_{j}
for any pair
(i,j)
.
Let
\varphi_{i,j}\colon T^{\mathrm{out}}_{i,j}\to T^{\mathrm{in}}_{j,i}
be a strongly (quasi)-transverse diffeomorphism which maps the single compact leaf of the lamination
\hat{\mathcal{L}}|_{T^{\mathrm{out}}_{i}}
to a leaf parallel (disjoint) to the single compact leaf of the lamination
\hat{\mathcal{L}}|_{T^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}}
(Figure
70
).
By symmetry of the lamination, we can choose a diffeomorphism which reverses the orientation.
Let
\varphi
be the product of diffeomorphisms
\varphi_{i,j}
(made involutive).
Then
\varphi
is a strongly quasi-transverse partial gluing map of the filled building block
(\hat{P},\hat{X})
which reverses the orientation.
Moreover, there is now a cycle between
\hat{\Lambda}_{i}
and
\hat{\Lambda}_{j}
in the graph
G=G(\hat{P},\hat{X},\varphi)
for every pair
(i,j)
.
Indeed by the gluing operation and Claim
7.4.4
, the intersection
\varphi({\mathcal{W}}^{u}(\Lambda_{i}))\cap{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(\Lambda_{j})\supset\varphi_{i,j}(\hat{\mathcal{L}}|_{T^{\mathrm{out}}_{i}})\cap\hat{\mathcal{L}}|_{T^{\mathrm{in}}_{j}}
is non-empty.
Similarly, the intersection
\varphi({\mathcal{W}}^{u}(\Lambda_{j}))\cap{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(\Lambda_{i})\supset\varphi_{j,i}(\hat{\mathcal{L}}|_{T^{\mathrm{out}}_{j}})\cap\hat{\mathcal{L}}|_{T^{\mathrm{in}}_{i}}
is non-empty.
By assumption of Proposition
7.4.1
), and isomorphism of Smale’s graphs, every basic piece of
\hat{\Lambda}
is connected by an oriented path and a reverses oriented path to a basic piece with infinitely many negative multipliers
\hat{\Lambda}_{i}
, and every such pair
\hat{\Lambda}_{i},\hat{\Lambda}_{j}
is connected by a cycle in
G(\hat{P},\hat{X},\varphi)
.
It follows that any two basic pieces are connected by a path of oriented edges in
G(\hat{P},\hat{X},\varphi)
, in other words the graph is strongly connected.

According to Theorem
6.3.9
(generalization of Theorem
1
for partial gluing maps), up to make a strong isotopy of the triple
(\hat{P},\hat{X},\varphi)
,
the quotient
P^{\prime}:=\hat{P}/\varphi
is a compact oriented manifold with boundary equipped with a vector field
Z
induced by
\hat{X}
, such that the pair
(P^{\prime},X^{\prime})
is a connected oriented building block.
The boundary of
P^{\prime}
is
\partial P^{\prime}=\partial P
, and the boundary lamination
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
contains the filling lamination
\hat{\mathcal{L}}
(Item
4
, Lemma
7.1.2
), so it is a filling lamination of the same combinatorial type.
It follows that these two laminations complete each other in topologically equivalent foliations on
\partial P^{\prime}
(Lemma
7.2.4
and Proposition
7.2.13
), so the same occurs for
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
and
{\mathcal{L}}
.
By strong connectedness of the graph
G(\hat{P},\hat{X},\varphi)
, the block
(P^{\prime},X^{\prime})
is transitive (Proposition
6.4.2
).
We conclude that
(P^{\prime},X^{\prime})
satisfies Proposition
7.4.1
.

.
Pair of the strongly transverse Morse-Smale laminations
(\hat{\mathcal{L}},(\varphi_{i})_{*}\hat{\mathcal{L}})
on a torus
T^{\mathrm{in}}

∎

7.5.
Realizing quasi-transverse bifoliation in an Anosov flow

In this subsection we show that we can realize any pair of quasi-transverse foliations as the trace of the stable and unstable foliations of a transitive Anosov flow on an embedded quasi-transverse torus (Theorem
C
)
Recall that a pair of laminations
({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})
on torus
T
is said to be
quasi-transverse
if it is a pair of two quasi-Morse-Smale foliations which coincide along their oriented marked leaves, are transverse on the complementary of the marked leaves, and such that
T^{\mathrm{in}}_{{\mathcal{F}}_{1}}=T^{\mathrm{out}}_{{\mathcal{F}}_{2}}
(Definition
1.3.5
and Figure
71
).

Figure 71
.
A quasi-transverse bifoliation on the torus

Lemma 7.5.1
.

Let
({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})
be a pair of quasi-transverse foliations on torus
T
, such that the set
\Gamma_{*}
of marked (common) compact leaves is
non-empty
.
Let
\Gamma=\Gamma({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\cup{\mathcal{F}}_{2})=\gamma_{0},\dots,\gamma_{n-1}
be the collection of compact leaves of
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\cup{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
.
Then the compact leaves
\Gamma
of
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\cup{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
are non-contractible in
T
and pairwise freely homotopic as non-oriented closed curves in
T
.
The compact leaves of
\Gamma{\smallsetminus}\Gamma_{*}
are pairwise disjoint.

Proof.

This is a consequence of the fact that the foliations
{\mathcal{F}}_{i}
have a non-empty set of marked leaves, and coincide along these leaves.
It is then sufficient to apply the corollary
7.2.5
.
By transversality of the laminations on the complementary of the marked leaves, the compact unmarked leaves are pairwise disjoint.
∎

There is thus a cyclic order on the elements of
\Gamma
.
We provide the elements of
\Gamma
with the dynamical orientation induced by the dynamical orientation of the respective compact leaves of
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
.
It coincides along the common marked leaves.
Let be a geometric enumeration (Definition
7.2.6
)
\Gamma=\{\gamma_{0},\dots,\gamma_{n-1}\}
of the compact leaves of
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\cup{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
.
Denote
\Gamma_{*}\subset\Gamma
the collection of common marked leaves.
The following definition allows us to characterize the pattern of the quasi-transverse intersection of the two foliations.

Definition 7.5.2

(Combinatorial type of a bifoliation)
.

Define the map

\sigma=\sigma({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\colon{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}}\;\longrightarrow\;\{1,2\}\times\left({\mathscr{A}}_{H}\times{\mathscr{A}}_{O}\times{\mathscr{A}}_{H}\right)

by :

(1)

\sigma(k)=(1,*)
if and only if
\gamma_{k}
belongs to the foliation
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
, and
\sigma(k)=(2,*)
if and only if
\gamma_{k}
belongs to the foliation
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}{\smallsetminus}\Gamma_{*}
;

(2)

\sigma(k)=(*,(*\uparrow*))
if and only if the leaf
\gamma_{i}
is freely homotopic to
\gamma_{0}
as oriented paths;

(3)

\sigma(k)=(i,(\rightarrow**))
if and only if the holonomy from
{\mathcal{F}}_{i}
on the left
16
16

16

the orientation of the first leaf and of the torus
T
determines a left and a right for each compact leaf
of
\gamma_{k}
is contracting;

(4)

\sigma(k)=(i,(**\leftarrow))
if and only if the holonomy of
{\mathcal{F}}_{i}
on the right of
\gamma_{k}
is contracting;

We say that
\sigma
is a
combinatorial type of the bifoliation
({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})
on
T
.

This definition is not symmetrical because we make the arbitrary choice to privilege
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
, and
\sigma({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})
is not equal to
\sigma({\mathcal{F}}_{2},{\mathcal{F}}_{1})
.
Indeed, we consider the marked leaves as compact leaves of
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
.
If
\sigma_{1}
is a combinatorial type quasi-Morse-Smale of
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
, then there exists an injection
\phi_{1}\colon{\mathbb{Z}}/n_{1}{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}}
such that

\sigma({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\circ\phi_{1}=(1,\pm\sigma_{1})
.
This is not true for
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
.

Example 7.5.3
.

We give the combinatorial type of the bifoliation of Figure
71
, where the geometrical enumeration on the compact leaves is the one from left to right, the green refers to a “in” component, the red refers to a “out” component,
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
is the full line foliation and
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
the dashed line foliation

\displaystyle\sigma({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})=

\displaystyle\{(1,(\leftarrow,\uparrow,\leftarrow));\ (1,(\leftarrow,\downarrow,\rightarrow);\ (2,(\rightarrow,\uparrow,\leftarrow);\ (1,(\leftarrow,\downarrow,\rightarrow);\ (1,(\rightarrow,\uparrow,\rightarrow);\

\displaystyle(2,(\rightarrow,\downarrow,\leftarrow);\ (1,(\leftarrow,\uparrow,\rightarrow);\ (1,(\leftarrow,\downarrow,\rightarrow);\ (2,(\rightarrow,\uparrow,\leftarrow);\ (1,(\leftarrow,\downarrow,\rightarrow)\}

Theorem 2

(Theorem
C
)
.

Let
\sigma
be a combinatorial type of a quasi-transverse bifoliation.
There exists a transitive Anosov vector field
Z
on an oriented
3
-manifold
{\mathcal{M}}
and an incompressible torus
T
embedded in
{\mathcal{M}}
, quasi-transverse to
Z
, such that the trace of the stable and unstable foliation
{\mathcal{F}}^{s}
and
{\mathcal{F}}^{u}
on
T
induces a bifoliation
({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})
on
T
of combinatorial type
\sigma
.

Proof.

Let
({\mathcal{G}}_{1},{\mathcal{G}}_{2})
be a bifoliation of combinatorial type
\sigma
on the oriented torus
T
.
Denote by
\check{T}
the torus
T
with the opposite orientation.
Let
(P,X)
be the transitive filled block given by Proposition
7.0.2
associated to the foliations
{\mathcal{G}}_{1}
on the oriented torus
T
and
{\mathcal{G}}_{2}
on the oriented torus
\check{T}
.
Denote
{\mathcal{L}}
the boundary lamination of
(P,X)
.
The boundary
{\partial P}
is the union of two tori
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
such that the lamination
{\mathcal{L}}
on
T_{1}
is filling and extends into a topologically equivalent foliation
{\mathcal{G}}_{1}
on
T
and the lamination
{\mathcal{L}}
on
T_{2}
is filling and extends into a topologically equivalent foliation
{\mathcal{G}}_{2}
on
\check{T}
.
By construction, there exists an involution
\varphi\colon\partial P\to\partial P
which pairs
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
, which reverses the orientation and such that
(\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{X},{\mathcal{L}}_{X})
is a pair of strongly quasi-transverse filling laminations, and completes on
T_{1}
into a pair of strongly quasi-transverse foliations topologically equivalent to the pair
({\mathcal{G}}_{1},{\mathcal{G}}_{2})
.
This diffeomorphism induces a strongly quasi-transverse gluing map of the block
(P,X)
.
By the Gluing Theorem
1
,
there exists a strongly isotopic triple
(P^{\prime},X^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})
such that the quotient
{\mathcal{M}}:=P^{\prime}/\varphi^{\prime}
is an oriented closed 3-manifold with a vector field
Z
induced by
X^{\prime}
which is Anosov.
As
(P,X)
is transitive, so is
(P^{\prime},X^{\prime})
and the Anosov flow
Z
is transitive (Proposition
6.4.2
).
The projection of
\partial P^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{M}}
is an oriented torus
T
quasi-transverse to the Anosov vector field
Z
so it is incompressible (
[
Bar95
,
Bru93
]
).
The stable and unstable foliations
({\mathcal{F}}^{s},{\mathcal{F}}^{u})
of the Anosov flow induce on
T
a pair of foliations
({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})
, which contains the projection of the pair of filling laminations
(\varphi^{\prime}_{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{X^{\prime}},{\mathcal{L}}_{X^{\prime}})
.
The pair
({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})
is not a priori topologically equivalent to the pair
({\mathcal{G}}_{1},{\mathcal{G}}_{2})
(this is lost by strong isotopy, see Remark
1.4.13
